# Design notes

Groundwork for running the Xbox Series build of GTA VI natively on Windows. The work that does not need the
executable is in place; booting can start as soon as `game_scarlett_master.exe` is available.

## Ground rules

- Everything here runs from **your own copy** and stays on **your PC**. Game files, generated code, shader caches and
  reports that quote game data are never committed, uploaded or shared. `reports/` is git-ignored for this reason.
- Nothing in this folder decrypts, unpacks or works around Xbox package protection. The tools only read files that
  are already plain on disk.
- The store keeps the game as one packaged container (`.xvc`): an XVD file whose contents are encrypted, so the
  build can't use it and needs the folder that holds `MicrosoftGame.Config`. Given one as `--game`, finding one
  with `--find`, or finding one alone in the folder given, `make_game.py` reads only its plain 4 KiB header to say
  what it is instead of "not found". Fields read, at file offsets (xvdtool's `XvdHeader` + 0x200 for the
  signature in front of it), checked on one 1.0.2.1 package: `msft-xvd` magic at 0x200; VolumeFlags at 0x208
  (`0x41`, ReadOnly and RegionIdInXts; EncryptionDisabled `0x2` is clear); ContentType at 0x284 (1, Title);
  XvcDataLength at 0x290 (non-zero for an XVC); PackageVersion at 0x3BC, four u16 stored lowest part first
  (1, 2, 0, 1 is 1.0.2.1). No key field is read.

## Why a loader before a recompiler

The Xbox Series CPU is x86-64, the same as a PC. Unlike Hookshot, where ARM64 code has to be
translated, most of the game's code can run as-is. The work is everything around it:

| Layer | Approach | Folder |
|---|---|---|
| CPU code | Map the PE image and run it natively. Patch only what can't run on desktop (privileged instructions, console-only intrinsics). Each mapped image's `.pdata` is checked (in bounds, sorted, non-overlapping; zero-length entries allowed, as in System32's `symcryptk.dll`) and registered with `RtlAddFunctionTable`, so SEH/C++ exceptions and stack walks unwind through its frames; the host MSVC runtime's `RtlPcToFileHeader` import is redirected so an MSVC `throw` in a mapped image finds its image base. The loader installs static TLS for the calling thread and, through `gta6rt_tls_host.dll`'s thread events, for every thread started after the image was loaded. | `runtime/src` |
| Imports (GameOS / GDK / Xbox DLLs) | Every imported `dll!function` goes to a shim. Unimplemented ones log once and return a failure, so the missing surface shows up at runtime. | `runtime/shims` |
| Graphics | Xbox D3D12 (`d3d12_xs`) to Vulkan. Shaders ship as compiled RDNA2 machine code; `tools\rdna2spirv.py` translates all 26,503 to SPIR-V. The D3D12 layer replays on Vulkan and is still in progress ([d3d12_vulkan.md](d3d12_vulkan.md)). | `tools\` (translator), `runtime\shims\d3d12_*`, `runtime\src\vulkan_*` |
| Assets | `.rpf` archives (RPF9) read through the runtime's file layer. | later |

## Layout

```
tools/       Python analysis tools (no game data inside)
  pe_inspect.py   PE headers, sections, entropy, imports sorted by where they resolve, .pdata, TLS
  rpf_probe.py    RPF header survey of a game folder
  manifest.py     SHA-256 manifest of a game folder (create), plus a verify subcommand
  inline_scan.py  inline command-list writes in an image, and whether each checks the cursor limit first
scripts/
  first_look.ps1  run once the exe arrives: the full report set for it
runtime/     C++ native loader (CMake + Ninja, MSVC)
  src/            PE mapper, relocations, import binding, TLS
  shims/          per-DLL shim tables
reports/     generated output (git-ignored)
docs/        notes
```

## Day one, when the exe is here

```powershell
.\scripts\first_look.ps1 -Exe "<game>\game_scarlett_master.exe"
```

This writes `reports/exe/` (JSON plus a readable summary). Check it in this order:

1. **Section entropy.** Sections near 8.0 bits/byte are still encrypted or compressed. If `.text` is one of them,
   nothing else can be done yet.
2. **Import table.** Each DLL is marked `bundled` (in the game folder), `system` (present on this Windows), or
   `shim` (needs a reimplementation). The `shim` list is the real to-do list.
3. **.pdata function count.** This gives function boundaries for patching and later analysis.
4. **Inline command-list writes.** `python -I tools/inline_scan.py <game>\game_scarlett_master.exe --sites` (a few
   minutes; it streams `dumpbin /disasm`; it exits 2 when it reads no instructions). "UNCHECKED 0" means every
   inline write checks the cursor limit, so a layer that keeps the cursor at the limit receives every command
   through the vtable; any unchecked site is a write that layer never sees. The package's DLLs check at every site
   ([d3d12_command_objects.md](d3d12_command_objects.md), "Every inline write in the package's DLLs checks first").
5. **GDK runtime classes.** The exe imports no GDK function by name; `<exe>.gdk_calls.txt` lists the runtime classes
   and vtable slots it calls through `xgameruntime.lib`'s thunks, and the classes the runtime's
   `runtime/shims/xgameruntime*.cpp` don't serve yet
   ([runtime_imports.md](runtime_imports.md#measuring-which-runtime-classes-a-binary-calls)).
6. **What to confirm.** `checklist.md` (`tools/exe_checklist.py`) gathers every `ASSUMPTION`, `UNVERIFIED` and
   `VERIFY` note of the runtime and the docs, with file and line. Notes in a shim whose class the exe calls are
   marked **called** and come first: those are the ones its first boot reaches, such as the `XStoreGameLicense`
   sizes.

Then `runtime\build\gta6rt.exe --dry-run <exe>` maps the image and binds every import to the shim table without
running any game code. It reports what it couldn't bind.

## Reproduce the shader tests

Build the hand-written tests where LLVM supports AMDGPU and SPIRV-Tools is available. Run the generated shaders
with the Vulkan test tools on the target GPU. Use the same output folder in both commands.

```text
python -I runtime/test/shaders/build.py <out>
python -I runtime/test/shaders/check.py <out> [runtime-build-directory]
```

The wave-mask fixture checks every lane and the wave64 ballot fixture checks mask counts and first-set lanes
across both halves of a wave. Rebuild `vk_compute_test` before running `check.py`. It uses a full 64-wide compute
subgroup for these two checks, and the summary reports them as skipped if the device cannot provide one.
`cs_exec64` still checks per-lane EXEC writes on 32-wide subgroups. With `LEONIDA_W64_EMULATE=1` in the environment of both commands, `build.py` translates with `--wave64-emulate` and `check.py` runs every wave64 check without asking for a 64-wide subgroup, so a 32-wide GPU runs them all (`scripts/run_gpu_tests.py` honours the same variable). Pick one of several GPUs with the Vulkan loader, e.g. `VK_LOADER_DRIVERS_SELECT='*nv*'` or `'*amd*'`. The flat-input fixture checks both integer
IDs and interpolated UVs. The corpus comparison is a separate test and uses shader files from the local build.

On macOS the test tools default to MoltenVK. To test another Vulkan driver, set
`LEONIDA_VULKAN_LOADER` to the standard loader dylib and `VK_DRIVER_FILES` to the driver's ICD manifest, for example
`LEONIDA_VULKAN_LOADER=/path/to/libvulkan.1.dylib VK_DRIVER_FILES=/path/to/lvp_icd.json`.
These tools do not request portability enumeration, so use the default for MoltenVK.

Run `python -I scripts/run_gpu_tests.py --filter ms_v5_workgroup` to check the mesh workgroup index. The synthetic
shader exports its incoming `v5` from lanes 0, 1 and 31 in the RGB channels of a full-screen triangle. One `vkCmdDrawMeshTasksEXT(2, 3, 4)`
produces 24 triangles. A stencil countdown lets pixel *i* record only triangle *i* in Vulkan rasterization order.
For `g = x + 2*y + 6*z`, the CPU compares each pixel with `(32*g, 32*g + 1, 32*g + 31, 1)` (the 32-thread fixture's `g * NumThreads + lane` values). This checks the translator's register mapping on the host GPU.
SPIRV-Tools 2026.1 validates this fixture. Version 2025.1 reports a known error on `CullPrimitiveEXT`.
[Vulkan mesh rasterization order](https://docs.vulkan.org/features/latest/features/proposals/VK_EXT_mesh_shader.html) · [Validator fix](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/SPIRV-Tools/pull/6155).

## Build the runtime

```powershell
.\scripts\build_runtime.ps1
```

It sets up MSVC through `vcvars64.bat` and builds at BelowNormal priority on half the cores, writing to `runtime\build\`.
If VS's "C++ CMake tools" component is installed, it uses CMake and Ninja. Otherwise it calls `cl` directly. That
component may not be installed, and the devkitPro MSYS `cmake` on PATH can't build MSVC projects. When you
add a source file, add it to `runtime\CMakeLists.txt` and to the file list in `build_runtime.ps1`.

## Asset status (no exe needed)

| Asset | State | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Archive data | Carved without the encrypted tables of contents. Most archives are 93–99% plain zlib (`levels.rpf` 98.3%, 1.1M files; full census **23,535 placement maps / 1,670,442 entities**). Not zlib: `audio.rpf` (99.9%), about half of `story000.rpf` and `shared_minigame000.rpf`. | `<work>\carve\<archive>\`, `coverage.json`; [world_census.md](world_census.md) |
| Videos | 797 Bink 2 files (`KB2n`), cut out whole. | `*.bk2` in the carve folders |
| Shaders | 26,503 carved from `shaders.rpf`, native RDNA2 code unpacked (`FSSM`), disassembled, and translated to SPIR-V; all validate (see *Translator status*). | `<work>\shaders\` (`.dxbc`, `fssm\`, `disasm\`, `spirv\`) |
| Textures | **Solved.** Headers are in CPU segments: GPU pointer at q[k], u16 width/height/depth and DXGI format at q[k+4], mip count at byte 2 of q[k+5]. Data is AMD `SW_4K_S` tiled, largest mip first. The GPU segment is the blob run after the CPU blob. On 200 samples, 173 decode at least 1.5× smoother detiled than untiled (median 2.56×). Exports the top mip, slice 0, as DDS. **22,342 exported (1.23 GiB)**: every header whose GPU data follows its CPU blob. World and model textures are handled by the TXD and TTPF exporters below. GPU offsets must be 256-byte aligned, which drops the false headers in `navmeshes`/`anim`. | `tools\tex_headers.py`, `tools\tex_export.py`, `<work>\textures\` |
| Models | **Done: 382,781 models measured at the `e293917` pin** (`levels.rpf` 343,327, `packs` 26,585, `story000` 7,461, `data` 3,175, `shared_minigame000` 1,294, `core_restofworld` 936, `story000_restofworld` 3; 0 decode failures across all 382,781 models; **159,511 drawable-dictionary children**, **223,270 standalone**; the other archives measure **4,686 fragments**, **30,343 bone-bound**, **126,301,454 triangles** and 512 embedded textures, while the `levels.rpf` fragments/bone-bound/triangles/embedded cells are **not independently reproduced** (**†**) (no `levels.rpf` manifest; the count harness emits no such fields); 72 unrecovered scan candidates vs 1,282 at `917e9b9`). Earlier baselines: 350,251 at `917e9b9` (159,486 dictionary children, 190,765 standalone) and 190,792 at `c3708fb`; the +32,530 gain at `e293917` comes from upstream `v1.2` PRs #8–#12 (optional fragment pointers, texture-dictionary filtering, probe-cap parity, and zero-radius drawables; see [models.md](models.md)). Exported with `tools\rpf9dump` (built on breadbbbb/rpf9-explorer's Core; see below). Resources are found at page offset +16 after 16 zero bytes, followed by a zlib system blob with a drawable/fragment header and then the following zlib blocks as graphics data. Positions are quantized: `bias + scale * ((q >> 8) & 0xFFFFFF)`. Output is OBJ (positions, UVs, indices) plus a manifest with real model names (e.g. `cvar_int_grg2b_toys_1`), bounds, per-mesh shader index and material texture references (parameter hash, name hash, 64-bit asset key). My earlier `0x1380`/`0x1601` descriptor notes stand, but the "data not located" blocker was wrong. Character skeletons are separate: the shared 597-name `.#ft` table and its model-side bone-hash mapping are in [character_skeleton.md](character_skeleton.md). | `<work>\assets\<archive>\models\` |
| Texture dictionaries | Same tool: TXD resources become DDS per texture. | `<work>\assets\<archive>\textures\` |
| World textures (TTPF) | **Done (all 57 pools, 357,115 catalog entries, 0 failures):** the original exporter baseline exported **308,895** (98,603 complete, 210,292 partial) and skipped **48,220** (33,507 BCPack, 14,030 compact mapping, 678 arrays/volumes, 5 unsupported format, 0 unmapped). The compact-tail tool resolves 14,011 of the 14,030 layouts (305,264 complete, 1,100,865 mips, 4.59 GiB), the multi-slice pass resolves all 697 arrays/cubemaps/volumes ([textures_arrays.md](textures_arrays.md)), and DXGI 11 resolves the last 5 format skips, bringing the measured zlib/raw split to **305,959 complete, 16,423 partial, and 34,733 skipped** (0 remaining layout/format skips; all 34,733 are BCPack). Clean-room `tools/bcpack.py` decodes all six active BCPack modes (BC1, BC3, BC4, BC5, BC6H, BC7), giving a `tools/ttpf_mips.py scan` codec-availability projection of **357,115 complete / 0 partial / 0 skipped**; on full-corpus chunk decode with `--allow-provisional --history <path>`, BC6H decodes 268 / 268 chunks (0 strict) and BC7 decodes 5,150 / 5,167 chunks (583 strict), while **17 / 5,167 BC7 chunks (0.33%)** still cleanly refuse in provisional mode. `rpf9dump`'s C# `TtpfReader` (`e293917` pin, `breadbbbb/rpf9-explorer#7`) decodes compact tails, multi-slice layouts, DXGI 11, and strict BCPack BC1/BC3/BC4/BC5 directly; BC6H/BC7 and provisional-history chunks remain skipped by `rpf9dump` and can be completed by `tools/ttpf_assets.py` ([textures.md](textures.md)). 46 pools are in `data\ttdb\**`, 11 in `pack\<name>\data\ttdb\**` (exported under `ttpf_pack\<pack>\`). `data\ttdb\**\*.ttpf` (57 pools, 58 GiB) are loose RDS5 texture pools, **not** inside RPFs. rpf9-explorer's `TtpfReader` reads the catalog and detiles them; checked visually (`ttdb_graphics`: scope reticles, clean lines). Names are generated (`texture_N_<assetkey>`); models reference textures by asset key. Measured breakdowns and decode caveats are in [textures.md](textures.md). | `<work>\assets\ttpf\<pool>\` + `manifest.json` |
| Animation | Indexed without the TOC: 130,470 zlib streams (4,203,762,662 stored / 11,593,460,132 inflated), 95.4% readable. New RAGE page directories (`0DRP`, version 122), `MoVE` (version 19), 273 `pack:/` name tables (2,298 paths), and a tail `RBF0` rig-set manager (`rage__crRigSetManager`: 1,126 rig sets, 6,677 source→rig entries, 3,669 distinct rigs; 56 empty). Bone data: one standalone 28-name table and 3 motion-matcher streams with embedded bone tables (30-name union, 19 joaat32 hashes present, 4 motionmatcher paths); no rig→skeleton link was found. Clip dictionaries are system-only RAGE resources (page map at the `+0x08` pointer). `tools/anim_clips.py` decodes 191,042 of 191,042 animations in 61,505 of them to per-frame values (channel types 0–5, 7, 8 and the GTA VI type 10) and resolves 111,948 of 159,584 `.motion` children to a clip. For each `.motion` it reads the per-child durations and time curves and, on 10,171 of 17,247, the one-axis parameter node (axis name hash, sorted sample values, range). All 1,781 second pages labelled `clip-stream` begin at the parent `0DRP` auxiliary table (+0x40), with two or three tagged payloads followed by schema data. The 2/3 packed streams are second pages of `0DRP` resources, which are not decoded. No `YCD`/`RSC7` at any stream start. The `rpf9_carve` tail overread is measured in `docs/animation.md`. | `tools\anim_index.py`, `<work>\anim-index\` |
| Audio | `audio.rpf` holds raw (not zlib) **AWC** banks: Opus (codec 0x0B) and XMA2 streams. Exported as whole `.awc` files plus `audio_manifest.json` (stream ids, codec, channels, rate, duration). **Done: 7,162 banks, 27 GiB, 1,603,231 streams, about 1,324 hours** (codec 0x0B Opus 1,601,167; 5: 1,818; 2: 227; 0 PCM: 19). `tools/awc_opus.py` repackages every Opus stream **losslessly** into a standard Ogg Opus file (no re-encode; PCM streams become .wav). The AWC framing is 2048-byte blocks of [u16 length][packet], with a zero length padding the block (as rpf9-explorer's OpusPreview reads it). The final granule is `pre-skip + declared samples`; the EOS page is emitted at the packet that reaches that target and later padding packets are dropped, so decoders stop at the declared end. Checked: 20 real streams at 48 and 32 kHz decode to exactly their declared counts; 300 random streams decode with ffmpeg with no errors. XMA2 streams (codec 5, 1,818) are written as RIFF XMA2 files by `rpf9dump xma` (rpf9-explorer's `AwcRecovery.WriteXmaWave`) and decoded to PCM WAV by ffmpeg: all 1,818 decode. Streamed music (tag 0x48: chunks with a per-channel record table, each channel's 2048-byte subblocks after the aligned header, and a repeated-prefix skip per chunk) becomes one Ogg Opus per descriptor channel (3,008 banks contain music). The Opus TOC stereo bit gives the packet channel count; a 1-channel descriptor can carry stereo packets, whose interleaved sample count is halved for the end granule. Checked: a 4-channel bank gives four 42.0 s mono channels; 1-channel stereo banks export at the config duration. Output: `<work>\assets\audio\ogg\<bank>\<stream id>.ogg`. **Done: 1,598,051 Opus streams + 3,116 music channels as Ogg Opus (22 GB), 19 PCM16 and 227 codec-2 streams (32-bit float PCM, 4 bytes per sample; values can exceed ±1) as WAV, 1,818 XMA2 as WAV. Every audio stream is converted; all WAVs decode.** `make_game.py --wav` also decodes the Ogg Opus output to 16-bit PCM WAV with `tools/audio_wav.py` (ffmpeg required; existing WAVs are skipped on resume). | `<work>\assets\audio\awc\` |
| World placement | **Full census of `levels.rpf`:** 23,535 map resources / 1,670,442 entities; all maps/entities exact, 36/36 shards, 98.32% stream coverage. Map files occupy a continuous band from ~7.5 to ~62.9 GiB; the earlier 9,918 / 680,228 sample was a 55% lower bound. `0x7a` resources: `CMapData` root, 0x90-byte `CEntityDef` with position/quaternion/scale/archetype/flags/per-entity id/per-archetype key/LOD fields; names and most archetypes are hash-only. Archetype and map names are lower-cased joaat, confirmed by a MurmurHash3 x64 low-64 key; see [world_names.md](world_names.md). | `tools\world_index.py`, `tools\world_census.py`, `tools\world_names.py`; [world.md](world.md), [world_census.md](world_census.md), [world_names.md](world_names.md) |
| Navmeshes | `navmeshes.rpf`: 90,295 zlib streams (6,540,238,392 stored / 20,551,068,624 inflated), 97.5% readable; 9,603 `0DRP` pages and 27,644 `0x00010015` pages, no `NAVM`/`CNavMesh`/`NVMS` magic. `0DRP` is generic RPF paging, not a navmesh container. | `tools\navmesh_index.py`; [navmesh.md](navmesh.md) |
| BVH | `core_bvh4.rpf`: 565,596 streams, 271,627 complete BVH4 streams + 10 eight-MiB split continuations, 269,106 instance records. Header `0x81020006` at +0x100, 64-byte node units, 208-byte instances with a 3x4 transform; prebuilt acceleration data, not source meshes or shaders. | `tools\bvh_index.py`; [bvh.md](bvh.md) |
| RBF0 | 1,263 `data.rpf` resources, 475,461 elements, 44,887 descriptors and 340,859 strings. Inline record stream with no section table; UI/Scaleform (`rage__fwui*`, screens, subtitles, settings). The `anim.rpf` tail uses the same format (`rage__crRigSetManager`). `tools/rbf0_tree.py` decodes every resource to a strict JSON/CSV/XML element, value and text tree. | `tools\rbf0_index.py`, `tools\rbf0_tree.py`; [rbf0.md](rbf0.md) |
| Gamedata | `common.rpf`: 954 streams (5,647,255 stored / 50,231,123 inflated), 85.5% readable; 266 XML documents / 118 roots (time/weather, vehicles/handling, peds/schedules, tuning, manifests, world/navigation); `tools/gamedata_xml.py` catalogs 267 payloads / 259 well-formed / 118 roots / 428,611 elements. No RBF0/PSIN. | `tools\gamedata_index.py`, `tools\gamedata_xml.py`; [gamedata.md](gamedata.md) |
| PSIN | `script.rpf`: 671 streams start with `PSIN`, 670 complete; sections `LSCH`, `LENM`, `LSNS`, `LGID`, `LRSC`, `CHKS`. `data.rpf`: 2,281 streams, 1,045 complete after joining. `LSCH` (type schema: structs and members) and `LENM` (enums) are decoded on all 17,664 / 17,641 whole sections across every archive, names as case-sensitive joaat. The instance data before `LSCH` is a block table plus 16-byte-aligned blocks with 20-bit-block pointers; read from the `LRSC` root through the schema, it reaches 129,100,004 of 129,117,812 block bytes on 17,507 containers. `LSNS` is a block table of 16-byte records (5,217 sections, 0 layout errors); the records are uniformly random, match no tested name hash and may be encrypted, so they stay unread. No plaintext scripts or names. Hash-typed members name other resources (u32: lower-cased joaat; u64: MurmurHash3 x64 low 64 bits): 228,427 values in 5,355 of 18,404 containers resolve against `rpf9_names.py` names, 299 control hits. | `tools/psin_index.py`, `tools/psin_data.py`, `tools/psin_names.py`; [psin.md](psin.md) |

Names still need the TOC. | `tools/page_index.py`; [page_directory.md](page_directory.md) |

| Rest-of-world / script | Six archives, 1.57 GB total: `core_restofworld` 1.21 GB (11 Bink 2 streams / 1.24 GB, 37 decoded models, 958 graphics pages, 6 PSIN), `story000_restofworld` 189 MB, `story000_script` 74 MB (2,309 PSIN, 2,118 complete), small `appdata0`/licences. No maps; no validated script container. | `tools/restofworld_index.py`; [restofworld.md](restofworld.md) |

Queue/list/fence creation and barriers are scoped; `PresentX` signature/slot and exact swapchain/plane parameters remain open until the executable is available. | [d3d12_command_objects.md](d3d12_command_objects.md) |

### rpf9dump (bulk export)

`tools\rpf9dump` is a small CLI over the Core library from https://github.com/breadbbbb/rpf9-explorer (a friend's tool; source at `third_party\rpf9-explorer`, audited: no keys, no network, no custom build targets). It decrypts nothing. It carves validated resources, as `rpf9_carve.py` does, but with working model and texture decoders.

```
dotnet build tools\rpf9dump\rpf9dump.csproj -c Release -o <work>\tools\rpf9dump
rpf9dump models <archive.rpf> <work>\assets
rpf9dump textures <archive.rpf> <work>\assets
rpf9dump ttpf <pool.ttpf> <work>\assets\ttpf
rpf9dump audio <audio.rpf> <work>\assets
rpf9dump xma <audio.rpf> <work>\assets
python -I make_game.py --stages assets   # everything, in order; skips what is done
```

Full-scan indexes are also saved to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\RpfPreview\Indexes`, so the rpf9-explorer GUI opens these archives with complete listings instead of its 16 MiB preview scan.

## Other prepared pieces

- `python -I tools\shader_profile.py <work>\shaders\dxbc\disasm` writes the instruction profile to
  `<work>\shaders\dxbc\profile.json` by default. Use `--out` to choose a different file. An earlier corpus run
  reported 551 distinct mnemonics, with 204 accounting for 99% of instructions. Those counts predate the filter
  for assembler directives and comments.
- `tools\shader_ir.py`: parses every disassembled instruction into opcode, typed operands (s/v registers and
  ranges, NSA register lists, constants, literals, export targets, interpolation attributes), modifiers and
  encoding bytes. On 1,000 shaders, 12 of 2.2M operands were left untyped before the last fix. This is the front end
  for an RDNA→SPIR-V translator.
- `tools\rtti_scan.py`: MSVC x64 RTTI scanner (class names, bases, vtables). Tested on the bundled DLLs; run it on
  the exe first.
- `tools\hashdict.py`: joaat hash→name dictionary (`<work>\hashes.json`, 1.9M entries, checked against
  `adder` = 0xB779A091). On day one, rerun with `--extra <exe>` so PSO field and type names resolve. Without the exe
  only about 1% do.
- Runtime shims: all of `xmem.dll` is shimmed with the measured argument shapes (`XMemAlloc`, `XMemFree`,
  `XMemVirtualQuery(process, address, out64)`, `XMemGetWorkingSetStatistics(kind, out)`, with `kind` 0x20000001 at all seven FSR call sites). The `d3d12_xs` root-signature
  serializer forwards to the PC `d3d12.dll` after clearing the Xbox flag byte; the deserializer forwards unchanged.
  `D3D12XboxCreateDevice` returns an Xbox-ABI proxy over the PC device: every PC vtable slot is forwarded with
  `this` adjusted, Xbox slot 62 is `CreateComputePipelineState`, and Xbox slot 76 maps resource creation to
  `CreateCommittedResource` with the Xbox `0x101`/`0x1` layout word replaced by the PC layout. Slots 9/10 are
  `ID3D12DescriptorHeap` methods, not device methods. The PC heap getters take a hidden return pointer (RDX),
  while Xbox expects the 8-byte handle in RAX, so slot 14 wraps the heap and slots 9/10 convert it. The heap holds
  a reference to the device proxy and its `GetDevice` returns that same proxy. Queues, fences, command lists and
  `PresentX` extensions remain unimplemented.
- PSO metadata is a new variant (`PSIN`, `LSCH` schema, `LENM`, `LSNS`, `LGID`, `LRSC`, `CHKS`, little-endian).
  CodeWalker's parser reads the container but not the new schema; dump it once the exe gives the field names.

## Shader translation (RDNA → SPIR-V)

`tools\rdna2spirv.py` (needs `spirv-val` on PATH — Vulkan SDK, or `spirv-tools` in WSL — and `spirv-as` too
unless `--assembler python` assembles in process with `tools\spirv_asm.py`) translates parsed RDNA code to
SPIR-V assembly, assembles it (`spirv-as` by default), and validates it with `spirv-val` (Vulkan 1.2). It runs one
lane per invocation and keeps VGPRs and SGPRs as uint arrays with exact bit patterns. Version 0 treated EXEC as
all-ones and covered only branch-free vertex and pixel shaders with no memory loads: common float and integer ALU
ops and exports to `gl_Position`, params and MRTs. Unsupported instructions are reported by name, never guessed.
In that version, `VS_Passthrough` and `VS_BlurFilter` translated and validated. Coverage across the corpus
and the blocking instructions are in `<work>\shaders\spirv_run.log`; the former next steps, structured branches
(EXEC masks) and the descriptor/binding model for `s_load`, `s_buffer_load`, `buffer_*` and `image_*`, are now
implemented. Current coverage is in *Translator status* below.

Also handled: 64-bit scalar moves, per-invocation EXEC reads, whole-quad mode on EXEC, `m0`, SCC, vector and scalar
compares, `v_mul_hi_u32`, `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` (now exact round-toward-zero; the earlier round-to-nearest version
could overflow to inf), and pixel interpolation (`v_interp_p1/p2/mov` become fragment inputs at their attribute's
location). MODE writes (`s_setreg`) model the f16/f64 rounding field, FP_ROUND[3:2], the only MODE field the
corpus writes; other MODE writes are refused (see Known approximations).

`--opt` is off by default and runs a basic-block peephole pass, `tools/spirv_opt.py`, over the emitted
module before it is assembled: register store-to-load forwarding, `OpAccessChain` CSE, dead-store
elimination, `OpSelect`/`OpCopyObject` folding and DCE. Only Function-scope variables that do not escape
are tracked, and a variable with any dynamic index is excluded from whole-function dead-slot elimination.

Measured on five hand-written pixel fixtures from `tests/semantic/fixtures/disasm`, with every result still
passing `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.2`: `ps_ops` 76→33 instructions and 7,876→4,516 bytes, `ps_flat`
60→22 and 4,560→1,676, `ps_sdwa` 61→19 and 5,720→2,192, `ps_vcc_x2` 63→26 and 5,260→2,552, and
`ps_fnan_med3` 76→26 and 8,060→4,264.

### GPU tests (RTX 4060 Ti)

`runtime\test\vk_harness.cpp` (CMake target `vk_harness`, built by `scripts\build_runtime.ps1` or
`cmake --build runtime\build --target vk_harness`; see "Build the test programs" in `docs/testing.md`. It loads
`vulkan-1.dll` at runtime.) draws one triangle with a given VS + FS into an RGBA32F target and dumps the floats.
Test shaders are in `<work>\gputest`.

- **`VS_Passthrough` (translated) is bit-exact.** All 64×64 pixels are written, and `param0.xy` = (u, 1 − v) with max
  error **0**, with zw = (0, 1) as exported. This covers the VertexIndex input, integer/float ALU, inline constants,
  and position/param exports.
- **400 arithmetic-only translated pixel shaders** (no buffers, descriptors or subgroup ops), driven by a generic
  full-screen VS feeding locations 0–15: the driver compiled every pipeline, every draw completed, and every output
  was finite. This is a stability check, not a value check (there's no reference for these).
- **Texture path is exact** (`runtime\test\vk_texture_test.cpp`): translated `PS_PassthroughCopy` gives max error
  **0.0** over all 64×64 pixels against the expected nearest-sampled 8×8 test texture. That covers push-constant
  user data, a heap-relative `s_load_dwordx8` of the T#, the hash-table lookup of the T# and of the shader's
  literal S#, bindless sampling, `v_interp`, and the f16 compressed export. **It also caught a translator bug**
  that validation can't: compressed exports read the wrong register pair (LLVM prints `v4, v4, v5, v5`), giving
  (r,g,r,g). That's fixed.
- After the export fix, 2 of the 400 smoke-test shaders (`PS_ImposterShadow` variants) gave non-finite red values.
  The cause was round-to-nearest `pkrtz` overflowing to inf, fixed with exact RTZ. One variant is now fully finite.
  The other was reported with 1 inf and 4 NaN in red, put down to the shader's own maths on the synthetic inputs.
  **That doesn't reproduce, and the shader has no maths to produce it** (TEST-643): both variants are the same
  nine-instruction body, whose red is `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` of v2 and 1.0. Both read only `SV_Position.z` (input
  signature rw mask 4), so v2 is POS_Z_FLOAT, which the translator maps to `gl_FragCoord.z`; red is f16(depth),
  finite for every rasterized fragment. Redrawn with `vk_harness` (64×64, a full-screen triangle with depth 0, 0.5
  and 1 at its vertices, synthetic values on locations 0–15) on an AMD RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, driver 32.0.21043) and
  an RTX 5090: 4,096 of 4,096 pixels written, 0 inf and 0 NaN in red, red in [0.0059, 0.744], for both variants as
  translated by `main` and by the initial commit's translator; all eight images are bit-identical. **Measured on
  the hardware** with the pixel-shader oracle (#3061's `ps_oracle.py`, not on `main` yet; same iGPU, wave64): with a
  template PS that reads only `FragCoord.z` and front facing, the driver enables PERSP_CENTER, POS_Z_FLOAT and
  FRONT_FACE, so v0/v1 are the barycentrics, v2 the depth and v3 the facing, as the translator assumes. The raw
  body (its export made uncompressed, so the packed words come back as they are) over the same draw gives 0 inf
  and 0 NaN in 4,096 of 4,096 pixels; each red half is the round-toward-zero f16 of that pixel's v2 (4,096 of
  4,096, checked on the CPU), and the translated shaders' red equals it bit for bit on both GPUs.

### Translator status and runtime contract

Full corpus (verified pixel-shader inputs incl. front face, ACO-confirmed 16-bit semantics, `vcc_hi` as an SGPR,
code-embedded data via `s_getpc_b64`, global memory, exact packed-f16 modifiers): **All 26,503 shaders translate and pass `spirv-val`** (Vulkan 1.2 target, `--allow-offset-texture-operand`): 2,236 VS, 12,101 PS, 2,689 CS, 9,282 mesh, 195 geometry. `spirv/index.json` lists each one's requirements. Output:
`<work>\shaders\spirv\` (`.spvasm` + `.spv`), and the log is `<work>\shaders\spirv_run.log`.

How the generated SPIR-V expects the runtime to work:

| Piece | Contract |
|---|---|
| Null sources in 64-bit integer VALU compares | `v_cmp` and `v_cmpx` relations `eq`, `ne`, `lt`, `le`, `gt`, `ge` on `u64` and `i64` read `null` as the 64-bit value 0 in either source, like immediate `0`. Register pairs and immediates retain their existing semantics; `v_cmp` writes the active lane's comparison to VCC/SGPR, while `v_cmpx` narrows EXEC without changing VCC. Source: RDNA2 ISA special operand `null` reads zero; LLVM `llvm-mc -mcpu=gfx1013` accepts the encoding. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_vcmp64_null_src.py` (both sources, signed/unsigned ordering, equality, e32/e64, EXEC and inactive lanes); **not GPU-verified, no corpus count**. |
| Execution model | One invocation = one lane. Compute and mesh waves derive lanes from the workgroup-local index; other stages use the subgroup lane. A wave = a subgroup, so cross-lane ops (`ds_swizzle`, `v_permlane*`, `v_readlane`/`v_writelane`, `v_mbcnt`) become subgroup shuffles. Wave32 pixel shaders split a 64-lane physical subgroup into two logical waves: lane IDs are `SubgroupLocalInvocationId & 31`, shuffle sources retain the current half (`& 32`), and ballots/votes use that half's 32-bit word. A value the translator knows is uniform (the bindless descriptor index) is read from the half's first running lane, never from a fixed lane, which need not be running (SHADER-1016). Other stage/wave combinations still require subgroup size == wave size or wave64 emulation. **Requires subgroup size == wave size** (wave32 maps directly onto NVIDIA GPUs; wave64 shaders need a 64-wide subgroup or emulation). `v_readlane_b32`/`v_writelane_b32` mask the lane select with 63 in wave64 (`S1[5:0]`) and 31 in wave32 (`S1[4:0]`); until SHADER-52 both used 63, so a wave32 select ≥ 32 pointed past a 32-wide subgroup. GPU-checked with `cs_w32_lane_wrap.asm` (lane 40 → 8). For the fragment-half rule, `ps_bitmask_perm` produced 4096/4096 exact pixels on an AMD Radeon RX 5700 XT; Vulkan reported a default subgroup size of 64 (range 32..64), but does not permit pinning fragment subgroup size on this device. CPU semantic tests compare a 64-lane wave32 pixel subgroup against two 32-lane subgroups for reads/writes, permutations, ballots, and votes. |
| DPP | VOP1/VOP2 `_dpp` applies to src0. DPP16 controls (`quad_perm`, `row_shl/shr/ror/share/xmask/mirror/half_mirror`) and DPP8 (`dpp8` + `fi`) select the source lane, then `row_mask` (one bit per 16-lane row) and `bank_mask` (one bit per 4-lane bank) decide which destination lanes update. A lane whose row/bank is off is not written. A source lane outside the row is invalid: `bound_ctrl` 1 uses 0 as src0, otherwise the lane is not written. A source lane inactive under EXEC is used when `fi` is 1; otherwise `bound_ctrl` decides. DPP8 always writes an enabled lane: `fi` 1 reads the inactive source, `fi` 0 uses 0. DPP16/8 never leaves its 16/8-lane group, so this is exact on every subgroup at least that wide; under `--wave64-emulate` the exchange goes through the workgroup buffer like the other cross-lane ops. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_dpp*.asm` on an RTX 3070 and hardware-measured on an RX 6800. On an RX 6700 XT (gfx1031), `tools/crosslane_fuzz.py --dpp-sweep --oracle hw` checks every DPP16 control × `bound_ctrl` × `fi`, every `row_mask`/`bank_mask` pair and the DPP8 selects in wave32 and wave64: 14,412 cases, all matching these rules (BUILD-162, `docs/shader_validation.md` FUZZ-7). The ballot of the source lanes' EXEC bits and the shuffle are each stored to a Function variable of their own (`Gen.eager`): SPIRV-Cross (MoltenVK) inlines a single-use result into the expression that uses it, which put the ballot on the right of a short-circuiting `&&` and the shuffle in a ternary's arm, so lanes out of their row skipped the ballot and row_shr:1's lane 1 found lane 0 inactive (`cs_dpp_ops` on MoltenVK; SHADER-529). A store is a statement every lane runs. Checked in SPIRV-Cross 1.3.239's MSL for the `cs_dpp*` tests (each op is now its own statement) and by `tests/semantic/test_dpp_eager_subgroup.py`; not yet run on a Mac. Semantics from the RDNA2 ISA, AMD LLVM modifier syntax and the GPUOpen DPP notes. |
| EXEC | A per-invocation `active` flag. VALU writes and exports are masked by it, scalar ops aren't, and `v_cmpx`, saveexec and EXEC writes update it. A pixel lane still inactive at the end is discarded. Measured on native gfx1100 (8 threads): a `v_mov_b32` under `EXEC=0x0F` leaves the other lanes untouched, `EXEC=0` writes nothing, a store writes only active lanes and `v_cmpx_gt_u32` narrows EXEC (4 of 4 cases); note that AMD clang 20 rejects `v_add_co_u32`, `v_sub_co_u32`, `v_add_co_ci_u32` and `v_mad_u64_u32` for `gfx1100`, `gfx1101` and `gfx1102`, so this toolchain could not reproduce the RX 7600 result below, which leaves open whether those parts support the opcodes and says only that they could not be assembled here. **A pixel is written when its lane was on in EXEC at the last `exp ... vm` the path executed (if it executed one) and at the last export to the color target; a path with no export at all goes by EXEC at the end.** The `vm` half is the RDNA2 ISA reference guide's rule (30-November-2020, §11.1 and §11.2.1: EXEC at the export is the valid mask and the final one sent is used; an `exp null ... vm` carries only the mask). The color half is measured: an export sends data only for the lanes that are on, a later export to the same target replaces the earlier one for the whole wave, and a lane with no data from the last one is not written even when a later `exp null done vm` marks it valid. So `exp mrt0 done` (no `vm`) with every lane on, followed by an EXEC write, still writes every pixel, and EXEC off at that export and back on afterwards writes none. **Measured on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon iGPU, gfx1036, driver 26.10.1) with the pixel-shader oracle of #3061 (`ps_oracle.py`), hand-written ISA as the pixel shader of a real draw (wave64 stage): 18 programs that cut EXEC to half of the target before, between or after `exp mrt0` (with and without `vm` and `done`) and `exp null done vm`; all 18 fit the rule, 6 of them differed from the translator, which until 2026-11-23 discarded by EXEC at the end on a path without `vm` and kept the older export's value (or the output's initial value) for a lane that was off at the last color export. The latches (`%vm_mask`, `%col_mask_mrtN`) are only emitted where a path can write EXEC after a `vm` or color export; everywhere else EXEC at the end is the same mask and the SPIR-V is unchanged. With several color targets a pixel stays when any target got an export from its lane (Vulkan cannot skip one attachment per pixel; not measured, the oracle's pipeline has one target); `mrtz` is not part of the color mask (not measured). **Measuring exports needs `s_waitcnt expcnt(0)` after each one**: an export is asynchronous, and without the wait the hardware takes the data and the EXEC of a later moment (`v_mov v9, A; exp mrt0 v9 done vm; v_mov v9, B` shows B, and an EXEC write right after the export decides which pixels are written, which looks like "EXEC at the end decides"); 64 `s_nop 15` have the same effect as the wait. Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_export_valid_mask.py` and on the GPU by `runtime/test/shaders/ps_exp_color_mask.asm` (RTX 5080 and the Radeon). VALU scalar lane-mask destinations (`v_cmp_*`, `v_add_co_u32`, `v_add_co_ci_u32`, `v_sub_co_u32`, `v_sub_co_ci_u32`, `v_subrev_co_u32`, `v_subrev_co_ci_u32`, `v_mad_u64_u32`, `v_mad_i64_i32`) write 0 for lanes off in EXEC (`SDST[lane] = EXEC[lane] ? bit : 0`, AMD RDNA2 ISA). Until 2026-11-22 the six `*_co_*` ops and `v_mad_u64_u32`/`v_mad_i64_i32` did not AND the carry/sign bit with `active`, so inactive lanes leaked 1 bits into `VCC`/SGPR masks (`s_bcnt1`, `s_cbranch_vccz`, `v_cndmask_b32`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_carry_exec_mask.py` (wave32, wave64, and `--wave64-emulate` structure), and **hardware-verified on an AMD RX 7600 (gfx1102)** with `v_add_co_u32` and `v_add_co_ci_u32` carrying on every active lane: VCC reads `0xffffffff` with full EXEC, `0x0000ffff` for the mask `0x0000ffff` and `0xaaaaaaaa` for `0xaaaaaaaa` in wave32, `vcc_lo = 0x0000ffff` with `vcc_hi = 0x0000000f` for the same partial mask in wave64, and `0` when EXEC is 0 even with VCC preloaded to all ones; with one carrying lane under mask `0x0000ffff` VCC is `0x00000001`, and `v_sub_co_u32`/`v_subrev_co_u32` with a borrow under that mask give `0x0000ffff` too. The `*_co_ci_` forms, `v_mad_u64_u32` and `v_mad_i64_i32` share the same path but were not measured separately; it is also GPU-verified on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, AMD Radeon Graphics) with partial EXEC masks (inactive lanes write 0 in sdst for `v_add_co_u32` and `v_mad_u64_u32`). In a shader that writes EXEC, a run of two or more lane-local VALU instructions (no cross-lane read, no lane-mask, SCC, carry or EXEC write) sits inside one `if (active)` and writes its VGPRs directly, instead of an `OpSelect(active, new, old)` per write: the same result, but the driver predicates the run with its own EXEC instead of keeping both values live. On an RX 5700 XT (RADV) a pixel shader with 8 divergent if/else regions of `v_fma`/`v_mul`/`v_add`/`v_min`/`v_max` went from 0.85 ms to 0.69 ms (the same program as native SPIR-V: 0.65 ms). Not under `--wave64-emulate`. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_active_runs.py`. |
| `s_*_saveexec_b32/b64`, `s_andn1/andn2_wrexec_b32/b64` | RDNA2 ISA SOP1. saveexec: `D = EXEC; EXEC = f(S0, EXEC); SCC = (EXEC != 0)`. wrexec: `EXEC = f(S0, EXEC); D = EXEC`. `f` is one of `and`, `or`, `xor`, `andn1` (`~S0 & EXEC`), `andn2` (`S0 & ~EXEC`), `orn1` (`~S0 \| EXEC`), `orn2` (`S0 \| ~EXEC`), `nand`, `nor`, `xnor`, the table in `Gen.EXEC_LOGIC`. The definitions match `tools/cf_ref.py` `_SAVEEXEC`, the FUZZ-8 model. Each invocation holds its own lane's bit (0 or ~0), so `f` is the bitwise op on that bit. A lane EXEC had off becomes active when `f` gives 1 for it (`nand`, `nor`, `orn1`, `orn2`, `xnor`, ...), as on hardware, and SCC is the wave vote. Until 2026-11-22 only `and`/`or`/`andn2_saveexec` and `andn2_wrexec` were translated. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_saveexec_variants.py` (all ten saveexec kinds in wave32 and wave64, both wrexec kinds, partial EXEC and S0, an empty result, every lane against `cf_ref._SAVEEXEC`) and by `tools/cf_diff.py` (300 programs × wave32/64 still pass). Hardware-verified on native RDNA2 (`gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21045.29003`, wave32 & wave64, 0 mismatches): all 10 `saveexec` kinds and both `wrexec` kinds (`andn1`/`andn2`) match bit-for-bit on non-symmetric masks (`src0 = 0x0F0F0F0F`, `exec = 0x33333333` in wave32; `0x0F0F0F0F_00FF00FF` / `0x33333333_55555555` in wave64), zero-result `SCC = 0`, wave64 upper-32-bit-only `new_exec = 0x00000001_00000000` setting `SCC = 1`, and `sdst == src0` register aliasing (`s4, s4` and `s[4:5], s[4:5]`) reading original `src0` before writing old `EXEC` to `sdst`. **Not GPU-verified** for the seven new kinds. Measured on native gfx1100 (single 8-lane workgroup, 36 programs): all ten `saveexec` kinds and both `wrexec` forms follow `f` for three `S0`/`EXEC` pairs, including lanes `EXEC` had off being switched back on (`nand` with `S0=0x03`, `EXEC=0x0F` leaves `0xFFFFFFFC`, activating lanes 4-7), `D` holding the old `EXEC` for `saveexec` and the new one for `wrexec`, and `SCC` the `EXEC != 0` vote. |
| Control flow | Reducible graphs use structured branches and natural loops. Irreducible graphs use the dispatch-loop form, which can also be forced with `--dispatch-loop` for comparisons. The control-flow unit test checks 18,228 generated graphs. Irreducible graphs use node splitting (`cfg_structure.split_irreducible`, up to `min(2*N, N+64)` blocks) and fall back to the dispatch-loop form when capped; it can also be forced with `--dispatch-loop` for comparisons. `cfg_structure.structure`, `cfg_structure.run`, and `rdna2spirv.assemble`'s structured emitter are iterative and hoist each guarded member's dominated tail continuation out of its enclosing guard (exact by strict dominance: no block outside the guard's subtree can target the continuation), keeping `OpSelectionMerge` nesting O(1) on sequential diamond/loop chains instead of nesting O(N) deep and hitting Python's recursion limit on multi-thousand-block shaders (e.g. 2,000 sequential diamonds nest to depth 2 instead of 2,000). The control-flow unit test (`tests/test_cfg_structure.py`) checks 20,000 generated graphs plus 2,000-diamond, 1,200-loop, and 1,500-early-exit chains. AMD A/B checks compare the two forms for selected pixel shaders with defined test inputs. For shaders that never export colour, the comparison checks pixel coverage. **Nesting is bounded (SHADER-299):** a tree deeper than `MAX_STRUCTURED_DEPTH` (16) is emitted in the dispatch-loop form instead, since `spirv-val` time grows roughly with the cube of the measured nesting depth. **Nesting is bounded (SHADER-299, SHADER-427):** a tree deeper than `MAX_STRUCTURED_DEPTH` (64; `--max-structured-depth N`, 0 = no bound) is emitted in the dispatch-loop form instead, since `spirv-val` time grows roughly with the cube of the measured nesting depth. Before #1904, over the 1.0.2.1 corpus (26,503 modules, `spirv-val` 2026.4): the deepest block of a module is nested 118 constructs deep, 12,963 modules are structured (median 15) and 6,000 of those are deeper than 16; an estimated 85% of the corpus's `spirv-val` time is in modules nested 20 or deeper. Median `spirv-val` time for the same module in each form: depth 20 181 -> 129 ms (1.4x), 30 507 -> 371 (1.4x), 50 1,712 -> 738 (2.3x), 64 2,692 -> 1,163 (2.3x), 77 5,122 -> 1,130 (4.5x), and the deepest (118) 13,683 -> 1,352 ms (10.1x). Depth 5-12 shows no gain (at depth 6 the flat form is slightly slower), which is why the bound first sat at 16. Since #1904 (`cfg_structure` hoists tails) the deepest tree in the corpus is 29: of 12,944 structured modules, 1,031 are deeper than 16 (747 CS, 284 PS), 57 deeper than 20, 24 deeper than 24. For those 1,031 the flat form saves only 12% of `spirv-val` time (1,235 -> 1,091 s summed, slowest module 8.3 -> 5.9 s), while on an RTX 5070 Ti (NVIDIA 621.24) it compiles slower: the 284 pixel shaders 1.67x (827 -> 1,382 s summed, two rounds), four compute modules at depth 19-24 2.0x (297 -> 591 s, 1.6-2.7x each), so the bound now sits above the corpus (SHADER-427). Both forms pass `spirv-val` on a 200-shader slice (200/200 each), and `gen.nesting` records the tree depth. AMD's Windows driver adds a reason to keep the structured form (SHADER-995): on a Radeon gfx1036 iGPU (32.0.21042.62) the dispatch-loop module of `XboxBvhBuilder.dll`'s `LeafRehydration` (tree depth 18, a 581-case switch) takes 34 minutes to create (XXDESIGN), where its structured module is created in 2.9 s; ten other programs of that DLL compile in the dispatch-loop form there in 0.4-40 s, 2.5-5x their structured time ([embedded_shaders.md](embedded_shaders.md), section 7). |
| User data | **Push constants, 144 bytes:** dwords 0–31 = initial s0–s31 (the user SGPRs the console driver loads from the root signature: descriptor-table pointers, constants and so on), dwords 32–33 = the 64-bit **heap base**, dwords 34–35 = the **code base**: the Xbox VA where the runtime uploaded this shader's whole FSSM blob. `--mesh-compute` modules add dwords 36–37, the output buffer address (152 bytes; see "Mesh shaders as compute"). `--pc-tail-ubo` keeps only dwords 0–31 here (128 bytes) and moves the rest to a uniform block (see "Push-constant tail as a uniform block"). The runtime has to reproduce the console driver's root-signature-to-user-SGPR mapping; that comes from the exe or driver behaviour. |
| Xbox GPU addresses | Shaders build Xbox GPU virtual addresses, and some force the high dword to 0 (descriptor tables in the low 4 GB). Every memory access goes to **heap base + Xbox VA**, so the runtime maps the console's GPU address window into one host buffer-device-address allocation. |
| Code-embedded data | 155 VS/PS had "undecodable words". They're **constant tables inside the code range** (float values like 0x3EF7…, plus zeros), read through `s_getpc_b64` + `s_add_u32`/`s_addc_u32` + `s_load` (147 of 155). The translator re-anchors each instruction to its true FSSM offset by matching encodings, translates only code reachable from entry (a branch into data is rejected), and turns `s_getpc_b64` into code base + next-instruction offset. **The runtime uploads each shader's FSSM blob into the heap and passes that VA as the code base.** Re-anchoring compares bytes exactly, except that VOP3P `op_sel_hi[2]` (bit 14) is ignored on two-source packed ops: the compiler stores 0 there, and LLVM prints its default of 1. Unused VOP3/VOP3P `SRC1` (bits 49:41) and `SRC2` (bits 58:50) fields are also ignored when stored as 0 and printed as `0x80`: LLVM 23 re-encodes a source it doesn't read as inline constant 0 (one-source `v_rcp`/`v_log`/`v_sqrt_f32_e64`, two-source `v_sub_f32_e64`/`v_cmp_*_e64`/`v_mul_lo_u32`/`v_pk_mul_f16`). Both checks for clean listings and re-anchoring use the parsed operand count to apply this exception only to unused sources. A used source encoded as 0 reads `s0` and is never treated as inline zero. `tests/semantic/test_printed_encoding.py` checks used `SRC1`/`SRC2` and an unused field with synthetic listings. Confirmed by a count over the corpus with LLVM 23.1.5: without it, 1,471 of the 1,473 shaders with embedded data are rejected ("reachable instruction with normalized encoding"); with it, all 1,473 translate and pass `spirv-val`. Runs with LLVM 21 already translated all of them, so older LLVMs presumably print the stored 0 (not re-checked). Data words that decode with ignored fields set are printed re-encoded (e.g. `0xbe8d2242` as `s_rfe_b64` `0xbe802242`), so they can never match. Since the decoder consumed the same length, they are anchored in sequence and marked. A shader that can *reach* a marked word is rejected; none of the corpus does. All 155 now translate. A reachable instruction that falls through into words llvm-mc skipped (it printed no instruction there) is rejected too, as `fall-through into undecodable words`: until SHADER-813 the reachable path ended there without a word, so everything after the gap (exports, stores) was dropped from a module that still passed `spirv-val`. No VS/PS of the corpus has such a gap on a reachable path; in the FidelityFX FSPX programs it is the `v_dot4_i32_i8 ... op_sel` words LLVM 23 cannot decode (`docs/fspx.md`), so those programs are now refused instead of translated without their stores, until those words are decoded (#2038, #2323). `tests/test_rdna2spirv_decode.py` (`test_fall_through_into_undecodable_words_is_refused`). |
| VOP3 opcode 0x140 | LLVM disassembly of the synthetic words in `tests/test_rdna2_decode.py` gives `v_mad_legacy_f32` on gfx1010 through gfx1013 and `v_fma_legacy_f32` on gfx1030. The direct decoder defaults to gfx1013, matching the disassembler CLI. Checked by the unit test `test_vop3_legacy_mad_fma_target` in `tests/test_rdna2_decode.py`, which decodes the same words directly and runs the Python disassembler backend for gfx1013 and gfx1030. |
| Printed encodings | A listing is LLVM's text for what it decoded, not the bytes it read, so the translator checks the two against each other: the re-anchoring above for listings with undecodable words, and, for every other listing whose header names its code range and whose blob sits next to it (`fssm/`, or `fspx/` for FSPX), each instruction's printed encoding against the bytes at its offset, with the same exceptions (`same_encoding`). A difference is `Unsupported`, naming the instruction, its offset and both byte strings, instead of a translation of the field as 0. The case that prompted it: llvm-mc's gfx1013 decoder (LLVM 21.1.8) drops the VOP3 `op_sel` bits (word 0, bits 14:11) of a 16-bit VOPC compare: `0a 08 a9 d4 01 05 02 00` disassembles as `v_cmp_lt_u16_e64 s10, v1, v2` and re-encodes with the bits cleared (its assembler rejects `op_sel` on VOPC for gfx1013 and gfx1030 alike). Counted over the 1.0.2.1 corpus: the 25,030 clean listings hold 30,335,381 instructions, and none differs from its bytes beyond those exceptions (754); the 166 16-bit VOPC compares (154 shaders, all `f16`) have `op_sel` clear. Hand-written listings, `run_gpu_tests.py`'s `code 0x0..0x0 (synthetic)` listings and a blob that doesn't hold the listing's code range (the tests' header-only FSSM) are not checked. `tests/semantic/test_printed_encoding.py` (a dropped VOPC `op_sel`, a later instruction, the SRC2 exception, and the unchecked cases). One difference is repaired instead of refused: a `v_fma_mix*` literal that llvm-mc prints cut to its low 16 bits (and re-encodes with a zero high half). llvm-mc 23.1.5 prints some of these literals in full as `lit(V)` (an f32 source, or a low half that is an inline constant; LLVM 21 printed the inline constant and dropped the literal word) and still re-encodes only the low half: `tools/shader_ir.py` reads `lit(V)` as V, and the same repair applies (5,733 such operands in the pipeline library, [shader_library.md](shader_library.md)). When the 8 instruction bytes match (`same_encoding` with all three sources counted as used, so a live `s0` can't pass as an inline 0) and the printed literal is the stored one's low half, every source the bytes encode as the literal (SRC field `0xff`) takes the stored 32 bits, so a source with `op_sel_hi` and `op_sel` set reads the high half instead of +0.0. Seen in one program of the FidelityFX DLL's `FSPX` code ([fspx.md](fspx.md)): src0 the low half, src2 the high half; the game's clean listings have no such difference (the count above). That the hardware gives op_sel's high half of the literal to that source is not measured; it is what Mesa's ACO compiler relies on (public: `optimize_constants()` in `src/amd/compiler/aco_optimizer.cpp`, Mesa `main` at `bb986060`, packs two constants as the two f16 halves of one literal, read through `v_fma_mix`'s `op_sel_hi` with the second source extracting the high half), and what the console compiler emitted here. `tools/fma_mix_literal_probe.py --oracle hw` measures it on an AMD GPU (the instruction's exact bytes through `tools/hw_oracle`; not run on one yet). `tests/semantic/test_fma_mix_literal.py` (the value through the interpreter, the re-anchoring path, an inline constant with the literal's printed value left alone, and the refused cases, a live source printed as inline 0 among them). |
| Global memory | `global_load/store_dword*`: address = (64-bit SGPR base + zero-extended 32-bit VGPR offset, or a 64-bit VGPR address) + the instruction offset, translated through the heap base. Inactive lanes don't access memory, and loads merge their previous values with OpPhi. The offset is the **signed 12-bit** byte value as llvm-mc prints it, -2048..2047 (`offset:-4` is the field 0xffc; llvm-mc rejects `offset:0xffc` and disassembles that field as `offset:-4`, so no repo path delivers the raw unsigned field), added to the address as is; a tool that printed the raw field (`0xffc`) would mean the same offset, and a value outside 12 bits is rejected. Measured on an AMD Radeon RX 6800 (gfx1030) running `global_load_dword` as a raw compute kernel, wave32 and wave64 alike, with the dword at byte n = 0x10000000+n and the pointer at 0x1000: offset 0 reads 0x10001000, -4 0x10000ffc, -2048 0x10000800, 2044 0x100017fc, -1024 0x10000c00. The translator used to subtract 0x1000 from an already negative value (4096 bytes too low). Checked by `tests/semantic/test_global_mem_offset.py` (both addressing modes, dword/x2/x4, byte/short loads and stores at -4, -2048, 2047 and 0) and the GPU test `runtime/test/shaders/cs_global_neg_offset.asm` (`vk_compute_test ... 40 dump`). |
| Global atomics (`global_atomic_*`, 32-bit) | `add`, `sub`, `smin/umin`, `smax/umax`, `and`, `or`, `xor`, `swap` and `cmpswap` at the `global_load/store` address (same `Gen.global_address`: saddr pair plus zero-extended vaddr, or a 64-bit vaddr with `off`, plus the signed 12-bit offset), as one `OpAtomic*` on the PhysicalStorageBuffer dword, device scope, guarded so inactive lanes don't touch memory. `fmin` and `fmax` (32-bit float) lower to a structured `OpAtomicCompareExchange` CAS loop using GLSL.std.450 `NMin`/`NMax` (no Vulkan float-atomic extension needed; NaN returns the non-NaN operand, `-0.0 < +0.0`). `inc` and `dec` (32-bit unsigned wrapping against `DATA`: `inc` gives `(M >= DATA) ? 0 : M + 1`, `dec` gives `(M == 0 \|\| M > DATA) ? DATA : M + 1`, `dec` gives `(M == 0 With `glc` the pre-op value lands in `vdst`, the first operand; without `glc` there is no `vdst`, and the first-read analysis counts operand 0 as the address (a source). `cmpswap` takes `vdata` as the pair {DATA, CMP}: `M = (M == CMP) ? DATA : M`, as `buffer_atomic_cmpswap`. Still `Unsupported`: the `_x2` 64-bit forms, `inc`/`dec`, the float atomics, and `slc`/`dlc`. `and/or/xor/swap_x2` work on a 64-bit element (two dwords, low first) as one 32-bit atomic per dword, with no Int64 atomics: a bitwise op leaves the same memory that way, since its bits are independent. **Approximation:** an exchange can tear, since two lanes swapping into one element at once can leave one's low dword with the other's high dword (A.lo, B.lo, B.hi, A.hi), and the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done (as for `ds_and/or/xor_b64`). **Approximation:** the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done (as for `ds_and/or/xor_b64`). Still `Unsupported`: the other `_x2` forms (`add/sub/min/max/fmin/fmax_x2` carry or compare across the dwords, and `cmpswap_x2` compares all 64 bits), `inc`/`dec`, `fcmpswap`, and `slc`/`dlc`. Until 2026-11-22 every global atomic was rejected. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_global_atomics.py` (all ten ops on a value grid with and without `glc`, `cmpswap`, offsets from -2048 to 2044, `off` addressing, seeded values, rejected forms, the first-read analysis). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_global_atomics.py` (all ten integer ops plus `fmin`/`fmax` on a value grid with and without `glc`, NaN/±0/±inf, multi-lane CAS contention, `cmpswap`, offsets from -2048 to 2044, `off` addressing, seeded values, rejected forms, the first-read analysis) and `tests/semantic/test_global_atomics_x2.py` (the four `_x2` forms with and without `glc`, an offset, seeded values, the carrying forms rejected), and validated with `spirv-val` (native and `--wave64-emulate`). **Not GPU-verified.** \|\| M > DATA) ? DATA : M - 1`, RDNA2 ISA) also lower to a structured `OpAtomicCompareExchange` CAS loop via `Gen.atomic_inc_dec_cas` for both `global_atomic_{inc,dec}` and MUBUF `buffer_atomic_{inc,dec}` (preserving MUBUF bounds checks: OOB/inactive lanes leave memory untouched and return 0 with `glc`). With `glc` the pre-op value lands in `vdst`, the first operand; without `glc` there is no `vdst`, and the first-read analysis counts operand 0 as the address (a source). `cmpswap` takes `vdata` as the pair {DATA, CMP}: `M = (M == CMP) ? DATA : M`, as `buffer_atomic_cmpswap`. `and/or/xor/swap_x2` work on a 64-bit element (two dwords, low first) as one 32-bit atomic per dword: a bitwise op or an exchange leaves the same memory that way, with no Int64 atomics. **Approximation:** the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done (as for `ds_and/or/xor_b64`). Still `Unsupported`: the other `_x2` forms (`add/sub/min/max/inc/dec/fmin/fmax_x2` carry or compare across the dwords, and `cmpswap_x2` compares all 64 bits), `fcmpswap`, and `slc`/`dlc`. Until 2026-11-22 every global atomic was rejected. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_global_atomics.py` (all ten integer ops plus `fmin`/`fmax` on a value grid with and without `glc`, NaN/±0/±inf, multi-lane CAS contention, `cmpswap`, offsets from -2048 to 2044, `off` addressing, seeded values, rejected forms, the first-read analysis), `tests/semantic/test_atomic_inc_dec.py` (`global_atomic_{inc,dec}` and `buffer_atomic_{inc,dec}` edge grid, wraparound, `glc` vs non-`glc`, signed offsets, `off` addressing, MUBUF bounds/OOB, inactive lanes, multi-lane CAS contention, first-read analysis, rejected `_x2`/`slc`/`dlc` forms), and `tests/semantic/test_global_atomics_x2.py` (the four `_x2` forms with and without `glc`, an offset, seeded values, the carrying forms rejected), and validated with `spirv-val` (native and `--wave64-emulate`). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `global_atomic_csub`, `buffer_atomic_csub` (GFX10.3) | `tmp = M; M = (DATA > tmp) ? 0 : tmp - DATA`, unsigned: a subtract clamped at 0 that never wraps; with `glc` the old M comes back. Measured on gfx1036 by @Vonsek (#3193): 162/162 (memory, data) pairs over both forms. Same `OpAtomicCompareExchange` loop as `inc`/`dec` (`Gen.atomic_inc_dec_cas`), so exact under contention; operands, inactive lanes, buffer bounds and `glc` as `inc`/`dec`. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_atomic_csub.py` (a 9×9 value grid, no `glc`, buffer bounds, 32 lanes on one dword against 32 sequential subtracts, seeded values). The translation is not GPU-verified. |
| Sub-dword MUBUF loads and `GFX10_FORMAT_8_UINT` | `buffer_load_ubyte`, `buffer_load_sbyte`, `buffer_load_ushort` and `buffer_load_sshort` read 1 or 2 bytes at `Gen.buffer_address(..., elem=1 \| 2)` (same `OOB_SELECT` end check with element size 1 or 2 bytes; out-of-bounds returns 0 without reading memory; inactive lanes preserve `vdst`), zero- or sign-extending to 32 bits; 16-bit loads at byte offset 3 within a dword merge byte 0 of the next dword (`idx1 = (b3 == 3) ? 1 : 0`) without over-reading past the buffer. `FORMATS` adds code 5 (`GFX10_FORMAT_8_UINT`, 1-byte unsigned integer) for `tbuffer_load_format_*` / `buffer_load_format_*` (with `DST_SEL` integer 1). Needed by `XboxBvhBuilder.dll` (#1813 follow-ups: `Box16Rehydration`, `Box32Rehydration`, `ReloadTriangles`, `LeafRehydration`). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_load_sub.py` and validated with `spirv-val` (native and `--wave64-emulate`). GPU-checked (TEST-510) through Vulkan by `runtime/test/shaders/cs_buf_load_sub.asm` (ubyte/sbyte/ushort/sshort zero- and sign-extended, the dword-crossing ushort at offset 3, and the RAW end check by element size: a ushort at 7 of 8 bytes reads 0, where a start-only check would read the next bytes) and `cs_buf_fmt8.asm` (`BUF_FMT_8_UINT` at offsets 0..3, out of range, an INVALID V# format, `DST_SEL` integer 1), both in `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`: pass on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3070 Ti (driver 610.88) and, with the same eight words, on an AMD RX 7600 (`gfx1102`) and a Ryzen 5 9600X iGPU (`gfx1036`, RDNA2, RADV Mesa 27.0.0-devel), so the `vdst` preservation, the sign/zero extension, the ushort load across a dword and the element-size end check are GPU-checked on AMD too. `tools/hw_oracle` was not used for this; the Vulkan path above is what the translator runs. Not measured on AMD hardware (`tools/hw_oracle`). **Not GPU-verified.** Measured on an AMD Radeon RX 7900 XTX (`gfx1100`, driver 26.10.1) by running the instructions as a raw compute kernel (`tools/hw_oracle`, wave32, seeded synthetic bytes): the four loads zero-/sign-extend at byte offsets 0..7, the ushort at byte offset 3 merges byte 0 of the next dword, and with `NUM_RECORDS` = 8 the end check uses the element size (a ubyte at 7 reads where one at 8 returns 0, a ushort at 6 reads 0x5eee where one at 7 returns 0, an sshort at 7 returns 0 where an sbyte at 7 reads). `buffer_load_format_x` with `FORMAT` code 5 and `tbuffer_load_format_x format:[BUF_FMT_8_UINT]` return that byte zero-extended with the same 1-byte end check (at `NUM_RECORDS` 8, offset 7 reads 0x5e and 8 returns 0), and a V# whose `DST_SEL`s are 1/2/3/4 returns the integer 1 in all four components, not the fetched byte. The inactive-lane clause rests on the NVIDIA run. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| 8/16-bit global stores and d16 loads | `global_store_byte`/`short` write the low 8/16 bits of the data VGPR (the `_d16_hi` forms its high half). `global_load_{u,s}byte_d16` and `global_load_short_d16` write the low 16 bits of `vdst` (the `_hi` forms the high 16) and keep the other half; `ubyte` zero-extends and `sbyte` sign-extends the byte to 16 bits. The address is the `global_load/store` one (`Gen.global_address`). The access goes one byte at a time, so a 16-bit access that crosses a dword (at byte 3) is exact too. A store merges each byte into its dword with atomic and/or, which keeps neighbouring bytes even when another lane writes them, as buffer byte stores do. A load reads each byte. Inactive lanes neither store nor load. The first-read analysis counts a d16 load's `vdst` as read, for the half it keeps. `global_load/store_dword_addtid` (lane-id and M0 addressing) raise `Unsupported`; until 2026-11-22 they crashed with `IndexError`, and the ten sub-dword ops raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_global_sub_dword.py` (every byte offset 0 to 7 for each op, an inactive lane, rejected forms, the first-read analysis). **Not GPU-verified.** Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle`, 16 cases that use the output buffer as scratch memory (seven stores, nine d16 loads), all matching: `b8`/`b16` change only the addressed bytes (`0x11223344` with `0xbeef` stored at offset 2 reads back `0xbeef3344`), `_d16_hi` stores take the data VGPR's high half (`0x12340000` at offset 0 gives `0x11221234`), a 16-bit store at offset 3 splits across the dword exactly (`0xfe223344` then `0x556677ca`), `ubyte`/`sbyte` d16 loads fill the low 16 bits of `vdst` and keep the other half (`0x85` gives `0xbeefff85` from a `0xbeef0000` vdst, `0x7f` gives `0xbeef007f`), `short`/`short_hi` d16 loads fill the low/high 16 and keep the rest, and both dword-crossing loads at offset 3 are exact (`0xbeef8811`, `0x88110000`). Not measured: an inactive lane. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| Literal samplers | Some shaders build their S# from immediates (for example `0x92, 0xC00000, 0x14500000, 0`). The runtime registers those in the descriptor hash table too, by scanning the translated shaders' constant S# values, not only S#s the game binds. The translator lists them per shader (`literal_samplers` in the index). `CreateGraphicsPipelineState` and `CreateComputePipelineState` register each stage's list in the descriptor hash table, since no heap slot names these S#s. Until 2026-11-22 nothing called `register_samplers` (#1169) outside its test, so such a shader's lookup found the fallback sampler and sampled with the wrong filter. The game's `$PS_PassthroughCopy` drew a coordinate ramp point-sampled: worst error 0.0078, against 0.00049 once registered. Checked by `d3d12_m2_textured_test` (`ps_m2_literal.asm`: no key before the PSO, a sampler index after it). |
| Scalar/buffer memory | `s_load*`, `s_buffer_load*`, `buffer_load*` and `buffer_store*` use buffer device addresses (`PhysicalStorageBuffer64`). LLVM `VCCTuple` scalar register lists ending in `vcc_lo,vcc_hi` (e.g. `[s100,s101,s102,s103,s104,s105,vcc_lo,vcc_hi]`, `[s92,...,s105,vcc_lo,vcc_hi]`, seen in 6 FidelityFX FSPX shaders) are parsed as `slist` operands in `tools/shader_ir.py`, clear `vcc` from the lane-mask state in `tools/lane_masks.py`, and route `sN` slots to `%sgpr` and `vcc_lo`/`vcc_hi` to `%vcc`/`%vcc_hi` across SMEM loads, T#/S#/V# descriptor reads, and `global_*` `saddr`. `src_scc` reads `%scc` (0 or 1, RDNA2 inline constant 253), matching `scc`. `image_atomic_*` and `image_atomic_cmpswap` accept `a16` packed 16-bit unsigned coordinates (low half first, matching `image_store`). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_fspx_rules.py` and validated with `spirv-val` (native and `--wave64-emulate`). **Not GPU-verified.** The base comes from the 64-bit SGPR pointer or the V# (dword0 \ \| dword1[15:0] << 32), and the stride is V# dword1[29:16]. **The runtime must put the game's GPU heap in buffer-device-addressable memory at the same addresses the game writes into descriptors** (or remap them). `s_load*` and `s_buffer_load*` take an immediate or an SGPR offset, or both at once (llvm-mc prints `sOFF offset:IMM`). Rules, measured on AMD Radeon RX 6800 (gfx1030, Windows driver 32.0.21045.29003) by running the instructions as a raw compute kernel, wave32 and wave64 (155 runs, every result either 0 or the stored data, wave32 = wave64 in every dword): **address** = (base & ~3) + (sOFF & ~3) + (IMM & ~3) + 4·i for dword i: base, sOFF and IMM are each truncated to a multiple of 4, then added (`s_buffer_load_dword s9, s[24:27], 0x3` with base & 3 = 1 reads +0, not bytes 4..7; sOFF 3 + IMM 3 reads +0 and sOFF 2 + IMM 6 reads +4, where truncating the sum gives +4 and +8; sOFF 8 + IMM 4 reads +12; the same for `s_load*` with pointer & 3 = 1 or 3). **`s_buffer_load*` bound**: dword i is 0, and memory is not read, when (sOFF & ~3) + (IMM & ~3) + 4·i + 4 > limit, with limit = NUM_RECORDS (V# dword2) bytes when the stride is 0 and NUM_RECORDS × stride bytes when it is not. Per dword (x4 at 0xc with NUM_RECORDS 21: data, data, 0, 0; x8 at 0 with 21: five data, three 0), on the truncated offset (IMM 0x10 with NUM_RECORDS 18: 0; IMM 0x13 with 21: the dword at +0x10; sOFF 0xe + IMM 2 with 16: the dword at +0xc; sOFF 0x10 + IMM 4 with 20: 0), not seeing base & 3 (base & 3 = 3, IMM 0x13, NUM_RECORDS 20: data); with a stride the test is still on bytes (stride 4, NUM_RECORDS 5: IMM 0x10 data, 0x14 0; stride 1, NUM_RECORDS 17: IMM 0x10 is 0, so it is not an index test). IMM is unsigned (0xffffc reads +0xffffc). OOB_SELECT (V# word3[29:28]) 0, 1, 2 and 3 gave the same pattern (7 probes per value). **`s_load*`**: no bound (+0x1ffffc reads the dword there); IMM is sign-extended from 21 bits and added in 64 bits (sOFF 8 + IMM −4 reads +4 where zero-extension reads 4 GiB further; sOFF 0xb + IMM −1 reads +4, so the negative IMM is truncated on its own: −1 → −4). **Negative sums** (SHADER-336), measured on the integrated GPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D (gfx1036, RDNA2, Windows driver 32.0.21045.16010) with `tools/smem_neg_probe.py --oracle hw`, wave32 = wave64: `s_load` with IMM −4 or −0x40 and no sOFF, sOFF 4 + IMM −8, sOFF 0xc + IMM −0x20 and the x2 form with sOFF 4 + IMM −0x10 read below the pointer at the 64-bit signed sum, and sOFF 6 + IMM −1 reads +0 (4 + −4, each term truncated on its own). `s_buffer_load` adds sOFF and IMM without wrapping at 32 bits: sOFF 0xfffffffc + IMM 8 and sOFF 0xfffffff0 + IMM 0x20 give 0 (past NUM_RECORDS 0x100), and sOFF 0xfffffffc, 0x80000000 and 0x7ffffffc alone give 0 (the translator has the same rule in both offset forms since #1686). An `s_buffer_load` IMM with bit 20 set (llvm-mc rejects it; −4, −0x10, 0x100000 and 0x100004 were encoded by hand) reads data that is neither 0 nor the buffer's own words, differently in wave32 and wave64: the bound does not hold for that encoding, so no compiler output uses it and the translator does not model it. `SmemHardwareRuleTests` in `tests/semantic/test_scalar_mem_smem.py` runs these rows with sOFF in a register and with sOFF known. **Measured since** on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 7 9800X3D, Windows driver 32.0.21042.62) through `tools/hw_oracle`, one raw kernel, wave32 = wave64 in every dword (22 cases): **`x16`** takes the per-dword bound like x8 (`s_buffer_load_dwordx16` at offset 0 with NUM_RECORDS 0x24 gives nine data dwords and seven 0; offset 0x20 with 0x40 gives eight and eight; offset 4 with 0x3d gives fourteen and two; stride 4 with NUM_RECORDS 9 gives nine and seven), and `s_load_dwordx16` reads all sixteen; **NUM_RECORDS ≥ 2^31** is compared unsigned (0x80000000 and 0xffffffff with offset 0x10, and 0x80000000 with an x4 at 0x2f0, all read data); **NUM_RECORDS × stride ≥ 2^32** does not wrap (stride 4 × 0x40000001, stride 0x100 × 0x01000001 and stride 0x40 × 0x04000004 wrap to 4, 0x100 and 0x100 in 32 bits, yet offsets 0x10, 0x200 and 0x2f0 read data), as the translator's 64-bit product assumes; **V# dword1[31:30]** = 1, 2 or 3 changes nothing (a dword at 0x10, an x4 at 0xf8 with NUM_RECORDS 0x100 giving two data and two 0, and the same with stride 8 and NUM_RECORDS 4 at 0x18). Not measured: gfx1013 (PS5) itself. **Bound edges**, measured on the same GPU and driver with `tools/hw_oracle` (wave32 = wave64, every read inside a seeded region): `s_buffer_load_dwordx16` is bounded per dword like x8 (NUM_RECORDS 0x14 gives d0..d4 and 0 for d5..d15; 0x20 with IMM or sOFF 8 gives d0..d5); NUM_RECORDS × stride is not cut to 32 bits (stride 0x1000 × 0x100000 and 0x2000 × 0x80000 = 2^32, stride 8 × 0x20000001 = 2^32 + 8: all four dwords of an x4 at 0 are data; stride 8 × 1 gives two); NUM_RECORDS 0x80000000 and 0xFFFFFFFF are unsigned (an x4 at 0 reads all four). These are the rules the translator already used; `SmemHardwareRuleTests` pins them too (a 32-bit product, a signed NUM_RECORDS or one bound for the whole load each fail a test). Not measured: `x16` (gets the x8 rule), NUM_RECORDS ≥ 2^31 and NUM_RECORDS × stride ≥ 2^32, V# dword1[31:30], and gfx1013 (PS5) itself. `s_load`/`s_buffer_load` with sOFF + IMM < 0 (a negative IMM without sOFF included; the translator uses the 64-bit signed sum, an inference), an `s_buffer_load` IMM with bit 20 set (llvm-mc rejects it) or a negative one (kept as the 32-bit value: for stride 0 that is past any NUM_RECORDS below 4 GiB), `x16` (gets the x8 rule), sOFF ≥ 2^31, NUM_RECORDS ≥ 2^31 and NUM_RECORDS × stride ≥ 2^32, V# dword1[31:30], and gfx1013 (PS5) itself. The translator checks each `s_buffer_load` dword with its own structured `if` and an `OpPhi` with 0, so an out-of-range dword issues no load (when descriptor limit and offset are compile-time constants, in-range dwords fold to straight-line loads and out-of-range dwords emit constant zero without branches); `s_load` stays straight-line. Before this the translator added base + sOFF + IMM unaligned, had no `s_buffer_load` bound and zero-extended a negative `s_load` IMM. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_scalar_mem_smem.py` (`SmemHardwareRuleTests`, the measurement's rows, values and the addresses read; `SmemSoffsetImmTranslatorTests`) and by the GPU tests `runtime/test/shaders/cs_smem_soffset_imm.asm`, `cs_smem_bounds.asm`, `cs_smem_stride_align.asm` and `cs_smem_sload_neg.asm` (never run that one with a translator that zero-extends the immediate: it reads 4 GiB past the heap), all in `check.py` and `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`, which pass on the RX 6800 (Vulkan, driver 26.11.2). See *Buffer bounds checking* below for the MUBUF `num_records` checks. **A constant SGPR offset** (`s_mov_b32 s12, C`, folded at translate time) is added as the run-time path adds `sOFF`: zero-extended, `& ~3`, after the immediate has been normalised (unsigned for `s_buffer_load`, 21-bit signed for `s_load`), with no 32-bit wrap. Until 2026-11-22 (#1686) the fold added it before the truncation, so `s12 = -4` with `offset:20` read byte 16 instead of being out of range, and an `s_load` `sOFF` of 2^20 or more was sign-extended from 21 bits (found by @Impre-visible; hardware measured by @Pierre-Kookis on gfx1036: 0xFFFFFFFC + 20 reads 0). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_smem_const_offset.py` (each case constant vs run-time offset, and against the measured rule). |
| 2D array resource dimensions | For `image_get_resinfo` on a 2D array, X/Y are the width/height at the requested mip, Z is the view layer count and W is its mip count. The query reads the sampled array at binding 3. A native gfx1036 kernel returned (19,11,5,3), (9,5,5,3) and (4,2,5,3) for the three mips of a synthetic array. On RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics the translated query matched those tuples and returned (4,2,3,2) at relative mip 1 with a view starting at mip 1 and covering three layers. The registered `ps_array_resinfo` draw checks all 4096 pixels against (8,8,12,1). |
| Images and samplers | `image_sample_l` on 2D arrays used to read its LOD from the array layer; it now reads the LOD after all coordinates. `image_get_lod` and `image_gather4_lz` also work on 2D arrays. With `d16` every sample result is f16, packed two to a VGPR from the low half (the packed D16 of GFX10, as LLVM returns D16 image data); `image_sample_c_lz`/`_c_lz_o` follow it too, and until 2026-11-21 their compare result was written as f32 bits, which read as 0 in the low half. Checked by `tests/test_translate.py` (`test_depth_compare_sample_result_width`) and GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_sample_c_lz.asm` (`vk_texture_test dref`: a D32 texture and a LESS_OR_EQUAL compare sampler) on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2060 SUPER (Linux, driver 620.70.06): 4096/4096 pixels exact for f32 and `d16`; with the old translator the `d16` result read 0 in the 2048 pixels whose compare passes. |
| Images and samplers (core) | Bindless. Set 0: binding 0 = 2D sampled images, 1 = samplers, 2 = descriptor hash table (storage buffer), 3 = 2D-array images (**cube textures are bound here as 6-layer 2D-array views**, cube arrays as 6N layers: the shader already selected the face with `v_cube*`), 4 = r32ui storage images, 5 = 1D, 6 = 3D. A cube sample's s and t come in [1, 2] and its third coordinate is 8·slice + face, as the hardware takes them (Mesa `ac_nir_lower_tex_coords.c`, LLPC `ImageBuilder.cpp`); the view is read at (s − 1, t − 1), layer 6·slice + face. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_cube_sample.asm` (`vk_texture_test cube`). For a native Metal path, `tools/spirv_metal_set0.py` sizes set 0's runtime arrays from `vulkan_bindless.h` and moves each binding to its own argument-buffer ID range (binding b starts at the capacities of bindings 0..b-1 added up), to convert with SPIRV-Cross `--msl-decoration-binding`: SPIRV-Cross 1.4.363.0 otherwise puts every runtime array at one ID and comments all but one out as "Overlapping binding" (SHADER-161). `cs_imgstore` converted this way writes all 3×64 texels correctly in a native Metal dispatch on an Apple A18 Pro. |
| Typed buffer loads | The format is V# word3[18:12] (GFX10_FORMAT, from Mesa's `gfx10-rsrc.json`, saved as `tools\gfx10_formats.json`). Supported: f32 ×1–4, u32 ×1–4, i32 ×1–4 (formats 21/63/73/76), f16 ×1/2/4, unorm8 ×1/2/4, snorm8 ×1/2/4, uint8 ×1/2/4, sint8 ×1/2/4, unorm16 ×1/2/4, snorm16 ×1/2/4, uint16 ×1/2/4, sint16 ×1/2/4, 10-10-10-2 and 2-10-10-10 unorm, and, since 2026-11-22, the other GFX10 buffer codes 1–77 (every USCALED/SSCALED, 10_11_11 and 11_11_10 FLOAT, the 10_10_10_2 and 2_10_10_10 families, 8_8_8_8 and 16_16_16_16 SINT and SCALED; see *Typed buffer formats*). Others (code 0 and 78 up) give 0. The 8- and 16-bit UINT/SINT formats 5, 6, 11, 12, 18, 19, 28, 61 and 70 were missing until 2026-11-22 and read 0: UINT channels zero-extend, SINT channels sign-extend, d16 packs them as 16-bit integers, measured natively on gfx1036 (wave32 and wave64) with `tools/hw_oracle` and checked word for word by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_format_narrow_int.py`; GPU-checked by `runtime/test/shaders/cs_fmt_int_narrow.asm`, whose 136 expected words are the same program run natively on gfx1036 (exact on an RTX 5080 and on the gfx1036 iGPU through Vulkan). Full-width SINT accesses preserve integer bits. D16 SINT loads saturate to signed 16-bit and D16 stores sign-extend, covered by the synthetic `cs_fmt_sint32.asm` readback. DST_SEL swizzle is applied (0→0, 1→1.0f for float/norm or integer 1 for UINT/SINT, 4..7→X..W). Missing channels repeat the format's own (component *c* = *c* mod *n*), measured on an RX 6700 (SHADER-86). UNORM/SNORM give the correctly rounded quotient by (2ⁿ−1), as the hardware does (0 mismatches vs hardware over 395,776 conversions on that GPU). It is built with integer ops (`Gen.norm_bits`): x/(2ⁿ−1) in binary is x's bits repeated, so a 64-bit multiply by the repeat pattern, `FindUMsb` and the round bit give the f32 exactly (the tail never ends, so there are no ties). `OpFDiv` is not enough on the GPU: Vulkan allows it 2.5 ULP, and on an RX 6700 (AMD Windows 26.11.2) and an RTX 5090 it returned the reciprocal-multiply bits (8_UNORM 130/256 exact, #599).
