Checked against exact values in `tests/semantic/test_norm_decode.py` (every value up to 8 bits, edges and the reciprocal-multiply misses for 10, 15 and 16 bits). 8_SNORM (2), 16_SNORM (8) and 8_8_SNORM (15) were missing until 2026-11-22 and decoded to 0. Format names list the channel widths from the top bit, so `2_10_10_10` (50) is R10G10B10A2 and `10_10_10_2` (44) has a 2-bit x (Mesa `gfx10_format_table.py`, `ac_formats.c`). Until 2026-11-22 the two were swapped, in stores too. Until 2026-11-21 an element that started mid-dword re-read dword 0 for its tail (`need` was `ceil(size/4)` and only the first dword was shifted); loads now take `need = ceil((byte_shift + size) / 4)` dwords and shift the whole window so the packed stream starts at the element (FUZZ-5, leftover from #234). The 10:10:10:2 order is checked by `cs_fmt_1010102` in `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py` (the shader decodes the format, so a GPU run can't check it). Mid-dword spanning elements are checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_load_format.py` (`CrossingDwordBoundary`); repeat / UNORM / DST_SEL 1 by the SHADER-86 cases in the same file. The `_xy` and `_xyz` forms (buffer and tbuffer) are GPU-checked by `cs_fmt_xy_xyz.asm`, whose words were measured natively on a Radeon 680M (gfx1035, RDNA2): they write only their 2 or 3 VGPRs, and missing channels repeat there too (TEST-185). |
| Descriptor hash table | `uint mask; entries[mask+1] of 10 uints: key[0..7], index, pad`. The key is a T# or a padded S#. FNV-1a over the eight key words selects the first slot. The shared SPIR-V lookup probes up to eight slots and returns index 0 on a miss. The runtime supplies a fallback at index 0 and maps live descriptor keys to bindless indices. Outside the wave64 emulation, the lookup's result goes through `OpGroupNonUniformBroadcastFirst` before it indexes the image or sampler array. The lookup key words come from SGPRs, so every invocation in a subgroup has the same index; the broadcast is an identity on all drivers and marks the value uniform for the compiler. It declares `GroupNonUniform` and `GroupNonUniformBallot`, without a shuffle capability or subgroup-lane input. The shader index records the capabilities; its `features` field lists optional Vulkan device features. Mesa ANV 26.2.7 on an Intel Alder Lake-S UHD (Xe-LP, Linux) segfaults in `vkCreate*Pipelines` on a sampler index taken straight from the probe loop when that sampler serves both a float and an integer image (`cs_sample_lz_int_d16`, `ps_ssharp`); with the broadcast both pass. Checked by the unit test `test_lookup_result_is_broadcast_before_use` in `tests/test_desc_cache.py` (standard library only: no GPU, LLVM or SPIRV-Tools). |
| Run-time format decode cost | A `buffer_load_format_*` whose format comes from the V# runs only that format's decode: `Gen.decode_any_format` is a binary search over the codes in `FORMATS` (`OpSelectionMerge` with `DontFlatten`, plain conditional branches), and a code outside the table gives zeros. The element size is selected from the format alone, and the per-lane byte offset is added once. Confirmed on an RTX 4090 (621.01) against the decode-everything translation: identical output buffers for all 128 codes in four configurations (four byte alignments, out-of-range records, constant DST_SEL), xyzw and d16_xyzw. Regression test: `tests/semantic/test_format_decode_branch.py`. |
| 3D explicit-gradient texture samples | For `image_sample_d` and `image_sample_d_cl` on a 3D view, the first six address VGPRs contain dP/dx.xyz and dP/dy.xyz. XYZ and the optional minimum LOD follow. With `a16`, the six derivative VGPRs still hold f32; the following two words contain packed f16 coordinates and the optional clamp. The hand-written `tests/semantic/fixtures/volume_gradients.asm` checks the source registers of both gradients, coordinates and clamp. LLVM gfx1030 assembles all four forms and their modules validate for Vulkan 1.2. [LLVM GFX10 address sizing](https://reviews.llvm.org/D102231) and [SPIR-V Grad operands](https://registry.khronos.org/SPIR-V/specs/unified1/SPIRV.html#Image_Operands) |
| `image_sample_c_l` | Scalar 2D depth comparison (`dmask:0x1`) takes the f32 reference, XY coordinates and explicit mip in that order. Under A16 the reference stays full-width; XY share a VGPR and LOD uses the next low half. D16 returns the comparison in the low half of the destination. Confirmed against LLVM AMDGPU's `llvm.amdgcn.image.sample[.a16].dim.ll` lowering and synthetic Vulkan 1.2 modules in `test_explicit_mip_depth_compare`. |
| Packed sample addresses | GFX10 `image_sample_d` with `a16` keeps the non-G16 gradients as f32 and packs the following coordinates as f16 pairs, following LLVM's GFX10 operand layout. `test_a16_sample_gradients_use_full_vgprs` checks the emitted gradient operands, and `ps_texture_grad_a16` checks coordinate selection with a single-mip texture on RTX 5080. The same holds for every extra argument before the coordinates (LLVM `getAddrSizeMIMGOp`: one word each, then the gradients, then the packed halves; llvm-mc takes `v[0:2]` for a 2D `image_sample_c_b ... a16`): an `image_sample_b` bias keeps its VGPR with the f16 in the low half, an `image_sample_c_lz` z-compare its f32 VGPR, and `image_sample_d_cl` its four f32 gradients, with the clamp the half right after the coordinates (an f16 pad may follow it). Until 2026-11-21 those were unpacked as pairs too, and the clamp was taken from the last half. GPU-checked on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2060 SUPER (Linux, driver 620.70.06) with `ps_texture_bias_a16`, `ps_sample_c_lz_a16` (`vk_texture_test dref_a16`) and `ps_texture_grad_cl_a16`, which draws on the `--two-mip` texture with the clamp 0 on the left half and 1 on the right: 4096/4096 pixels exact each. The old reading got 4,032 bias pixels and both depth-compare results in 2,048 pixels wrong, a clamp taken from the pad read mip 1 on the left half, and the sample without its clamp mip 0 on the right half (2,048 pixels each). |
| Depth-compare samples with implicit LOD (`image_sample_c`, `image_sample_c_b`, and the `_o` forms) | In a pixel shader `image_sample_c` is `OpImageSampleDrefImplicitLod` and `image_sample_c_b` adds `Bias` (the LOD comes from the quad derivatives; the sampler is the comparison sampler `image_sample_c_lz` already uses, and the result is one value, f32 bits or with `d16` an f16 in the low half). The address order after the optional texel-offset dword is **bias, z-compare, coordinates** (GFX10 MIMG: offset, bias, zcompare, derivatives, coordinates, LOD, clamp): `image_sample_c_b` read the compare first until this change, the one place that order was wrong, which `ps_sample_c_b` catches (a bias of 1.0 read as the reference compares false everywhere). Outside the pixel stage there are no derivatives: `image_sample_c` samples LOD 0 (like `image_sample`), `image_sample_c_b` is `Unsupported`. `_o` takes the offset as the other `_o` forms do (`Offset`, or with `--texel-offset-fallback` the coordinate shift and a `Grad` sample of the unshifted coordinate's derivatives, scaled by `2^bias` for `_c_b`). Refused still: `_cl`, `_c_d`, `_c_cd` and the gradient forms of the compare samples; `image_sample_c_l` is #2685. GPU tests: `ps_sample_c`, `ps_sample_c_b` (`vk_texture_test dref_c`, `dref_c_b`: the D32 8x8 depth texture of `ps_sample_c_lz`, all 4096 pixels exact for the f32 result and the d16 one); semantic: `tests/semantic/test_sample_c.py` and the forms in `tests/test_texel_offsets.py`. `vk_texture_test` now takes the GPU `LEONIDA_VK_DEVICE` names, as the other harnesses do (it ignored it before). |
| Min-LOD clamp forms (`image_sample_cl`, `_b_cl`, `_c_cl`, `_c_b_cl`, and the `_o` forms) | The clamp is the component after the coordinates (address order: offset, bias, z-compare, coordinates, clamp), and becomes the `MinLod` image operand of the implicit-LOD sample (`OpImageSampleImplicitLod` / `OpImageSampleDrefImplicitLod`, with `Bias` and `Offset` before it in mask order), the same as the `image_sample_d_cl` that already existed; the module declares `MinLod` (`shaderResourceMinLod`, the harnesses skip a device without it). Outside the pixel stage there are no derivatives and the LOD is 0, so the clamp raises it: explicit `Lod = max(0, clamp)` and no `MinLod`. Under `a16` the clamp is packed with the coordinates (its own f16, with a pad after it in the last VGPR), as for `_d_cl`. With `--texel-offset-fallback` the Grad sample takes `Grad\|MinLod`. A clamp on a form that has no `_cl` instruction (`_l`, `_lz`, `_c_lz`) stays `Unsupported`. GPU tests: `ps_texture_cl` and `ps_texture_b_cl` on `vk_texture_test --two-mip` (coordinates / 4 so the unclamped LOD is -1; clamp 0.0 left of x = 32 reads mip 0 red, 1.0 from there on reads mip 1 cyan; dropping the MinLod operand turns the right half red); semantic: `tests/semantic/test_sample_cl.py` and the `_cl_o` forms in `tests/test_texel_offsets.py`. The compare forms (`_c_cl`, `_c_b_cl`) have no GPU test: the depth fixture has one mip level. Not translated: `_c_d`, `_c_cd`, `_d_cl` with compare, the g16 forms. |
| Flat and scratch store operands | Register analysis reads the full address and data VGPR ranges and defines neither. A store after a block terminator can still appear in the listing scanned for PS live-ins. The CPU branch regression in `tests/semantic/test_store_liveins.py` checks both exported barycentric weights. LLVM gfx1013 confirms operand order. Reachable flat and scratch stores still raise `Unsupported`. |
| Vertex fetch | `s_swappc_b64` at the start of a VS calls a driver-generated fetch program that isn't in the blob. It's replaced by Vulkan vertex inputs: each `ISG1` input-signature element (DXBC container) becomes an input at **location = its D3D input register**, typed by its component type, and is written to **v(4 + 4·register + component)**. That rule was checked over all 788 fetch-calling shaders: in 783 every read-before-write VGPR is predicted by it (715 exact). Reads of components the element doesn't declare (e.g. `.w` of an `xyz` position, 3 VS) are allowed: the fetch returns the format defaults (0, 0, 1), and Vulkan fills a `vec4` input from a narrower format the same way. Inputs are declared as `vec4`. **The runtime must bind vertex buffers by the game's D3D12 input layout, location = input register.** |
| `v_swap_b32` and pixel inputs | The swap reads and writes both VGPRs (AMD RDNA2 ISA, VOP1). A barycentric coordinate in the first operand is therefore a live input, and a coordinate or copy in the second operand is overwritten. When the swap changes an interpolation weight, the other incoming weight is still available to the pair. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_interp_computed_ij.py`: `test_swap_reads_both_incoming_barycentrics`, `test_swap_overwrites_interpolation_weight_in_second_operand` and `test_swapped_copies_swap_the_weights` run synthetic fragment exports through the SPIR-V interpreter. |
| PS system inputs | v0–v1 = barycentrics (consumed by `v_interp`, which becomes attribute inputs). **v2.. = the `SV_Position` components the shader reads, packed in order**. Counted over all 12,101 PS listings of 1.0.2.1 (names starting with `.` included; `rdna2spirv.read_before_write_vgprs`, with `v_interp`'s I/J operands left out as the translator does, against the ISG1 read masks from `input_signature_full`; an earlier sample gave 2,531 of 3,000): in 12,098 the VGPRs they read before writing inside the system-input range (v2 up to the last hardware input) equal exactly the position components their input signature reads, then front face when its read mask is non-zero. The 1,639 PS that declare `SV_IsFrontFace` with read mask 0 never read that slot; the translator still places it, last of the inputs this layout models, so no other modelled input moves. The other 3 read fewer position components than their read mask says. Verified on 2,531 of 3,000 PS: the VGPRs they read before writing equal exactly the position components their input signature reads. Filled from `FragCoord`: xy/z directly, and **w directly too**, because AMD's position-w register holds 1/w (Mesa lowers `load_frag_coord_w_rcp` straight to `frag_pos[3]`, `ac_nir_lower_intrinsics_to_args.c`), the same as Vulkan's `FragCoord.w`. **Front face** follows the used position components when the signature declares `SV_IsFrontFace`, as ±1.0 (Mesa: `load_front_face` = `front_face > 0`). Only enabled inputs are packed, in hardware order (Mesa `radv_shader_args.c`: barycentric pairs, POS_X/Y/Z/W, FRONT_FACE, ANCILLARY, SAMPLE_COVERAGE, POS_FIXED_PT). That explains the earlier "unverified" shaders: of the 345 PS that read front face, 130 read pos.xy + front face and 141 pos.x/y/w + front face. That explains the earlier "unverified" shaders: 102 read pos.xy + front face, and 76 read pos.x/y/w + front face. **Raw barycentric reads** (321 PS, e.g. `v_readfirstlane_b32 s41, v1`): AMD interpolates P = P0 + i(P1 − P0) + j(P2 − P0), so i/j are the weights of vertices 1/2, which is `BaryCoordKHR.y`/`.z` (`VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric`). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_bary.asm`: raw i/j equal the interpolated weights on every pixel. `ps_persp_bary.asm` (the same code drawn by `vs_persp.asm`, clip w = 1, 2, 4) checks them, and the interpolated attributes, against perspective-correct CPU weights; the affine weights differ by at least 0.006 on every covered pixel (all 1158 judged), so `NoPerspective` interpolation fails it. The pair is perspective-center, the only pair this layout models; that holds for every corpus PS (row "PS interpolation modes"). A PS that reads any other unexplained register inside the system-input range is still rejected (none left in the corpus). Reads past the last hardware input are values undefined on some path, so zero stands in for them. 373 PS have one (and 85 more whose raw listing holds embedded data words). In each of the 373 a branch instruction comes earlier in listing order than the first such read; in 364 the register is written later in listing order, which suggests a loop-carried value (inferred from listing order, not measured on the control flow), in 9 never after the read. All register arrays start at zero, so translated shaders are deterministic. |
| Partial pixel color exports | Each MRT retains channels from earlier exports when the current `exp` disables them. Uncompressed exports enable individual RGBA channels and compressed exports enable the RG and BA pairs. Partial MRT outputs start at zero so a channel the shader never writes keeps its existing translator default. The CPU test in `tests/semantic/test_export_valid_mask.py` checks the eight RGBA words from two interleaved targets in `partial_color_exports.asm`. [AMD RDNA 2 ISA, sections 11.1 and 11.2.1](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) |
| Inputs and outputs | VS: **legacy (non-NGG) hardware VS, measured** over all 2,236 (`scripts/vs_input_survey.py`): no `exp prim`, no `s_sendmsg` (so no `GS_ALLOC_REQ`) and no `v_mbcnt` in any of them, and every one exports `pos0` and `param`. The input VGPRs are Mesa's GFX10 legacy-VS layout (`radv_shader_args.c`, `declare_vs_input_vgprs`, not `is_ngg`): `v0` = vertex id, `v1` unused, `v2` = primitive id, `v3` = instance id. On entry no VS reads `v1` or `v2`; 788 start with the fetch call (see *Vertex fetch*) and read the fetched attributes from `v4` up, and of the other 1,448, 1,052 read `v0` only, 386 `v0` and `v3`, and 10 neither (NGG would have put the VS inputs at `v5`/`v8`, after five GS VGPRs). **Both ids are zero-based on the hardware**: `v0` excludes the draw's base vertex and `v3` its first instance (Mesa `ac_nir_lower_intrinsics_to_args.c`: `vertex_id` is `load_vertex_id_zero_base`, `instance_id` is `load_instance_id`; the bases come in the `base_vertex` / `start_instance` SGPRs). Vulkan's `VertexIndex` / `InstanceIndex` include `firstVertex`/`vertexOffset` / `firstInstance`, so the translator seeds `v0 = VertexIndex - BaseVertex` and `v3 = InstanceIndex - BaseInstance` (`DrawParameters`, device feature `shaderDrawParameters`, in every VS's `features`). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/vs_base_ids.asm` (`VKH_DRAW=16,7`): `0x101` on every pixel, where `VertexIndex`/`InstanceIndex` alone gave `0x7111`. `exp pos0` → `Position`, `exp pos1..pos3` → `ClipDistance` / `CullDistance` (and `Layer`/`ViewportIndex` in VS when `pos1` is the misc vector), `exp paramN` → location N. RDNA2/GFX10 packs clip distances before cull distances contiguously across 4-component `pos` exports (up to 8 floats total), starting at `pos2` when a misc vector (`PointSize`, `EdgeFlag`, `ShadingRate`, `Layer`, `ViewportIndex`; GFX9+ packs `Layer` in `z[10:0]` and `ViewportIndex` in `z[19:16]`) occupies `pos1`, and at `pos1` otherwise (Mesa `src/amd/common/nir/ac_nir_prerast_utils.c`: `ac_nir_export_position`; AMD LLPC `lgc/lowering/LowerInOut.cpp`: `LowerInOut::visitReturnInst`). Slot assignment follows the DXBC `OSG1` signature (`SV_ClipDistance` = 2, `SV_CullDistance` = 3, `SV_RenderTargetArrayIndex` = 4, `SV_ViewportArrayIndex` = 5, `SV_ShadingRate` = 23), ordered by `(semantic_index, register, component)` with clip before cull; when `OSG1` is absent (or declares none of those system values), `pos1..pos3` exports are discarded. PS: `v_interp_*` attribute N → input location N. `exp mrtK` → the location of the K-th declared `SV_Target` (**Pixel colour exports**): colour exports are packed, so a shader that declares `SV_Target0` and `SV_Target2` exports `mrt0` and `mrt1`, and `mrt1` is render target 2. Counted over the 1.0.2.1 corpus (SHADER-210): of 10,500 pixel shaders with `SV_Target` outputs (OSG1 system value 64, register = semantic index in all 29,290), the 221 that declare non-consecutive targets all export `mrt0`..`mrt(k-1)`, never `mrtN` at register N. Without an OSG1 (hand-written listings, `run_gpu_tests.py` fixtures) `mrtK` is location K. An export past the declared targets is `Unsupported`. `tests/semantic/test_sparse_sv_target.py`. `exp mrtN` → location N, `exp mrtz` x → `FragDepth` only when x is on in an uncompressed export; the 16-bit `compr` form holds stencil and sample mask, never Z (Mesa `ac_nir_lower_ps_late.c` `emit_ps_mrtz_export`), and an export without Z must not declare `DepthReplacing`. `exp mrtN` → location N, `exp mrtz` x → `FragDepth`. |
| VS input layout, per PAL hardware stage | `scripts/vs_layout_census.py` joins every tag-0x0D payload's PAL stage record (`tools/pal_stages.py`) with its code markers over all 26,698 payloads of the 26,503 1.0.2.1 containers: the 2,236 stage-4 (VS) payloads are **all** legacy non-NGG — no `exp prim`, no `s_sendmsg`, no `v_mbcnt`, and every one exports `pos0` and `param` — so the Mesa GFX10 legacy-VS layout of *Inputs and outputs* holds for every VS the corpus translates, and `v0` = `VertexIndex` (zero-based, `DrawParameters`) is confirmed rather than NGG. The NGG markers belong to the 9,282 stage-7 payloads (every one has `exp prim`, `s_sendmsg MSG_GS_ALLOC_REQ` and `v_mbcnt`), the 195 stage-3 payloads are the geometry shaders (`s_sendmsg` + `v_mbcnt`, no position export), and the 195 stage-2 payloads are the ES variants of the next row; the PS (12,101) and CS (2,689) payloads carry `v_mbcnt` (332 and 266) and, in ten PS, an `s_sendmsg`, but no `exp prim`. |
| VS as ES (two-payload VS containers) | 195 `$VS_VoxelizeGeneric_*` containers carry **two** tag-0x0D payloads: besides the legacy VS above, a stage-2 (`es`) payload — the vertex shader compiled for the export-shader stage of a legacy geometry-shader pipeline. It has **no `exp`** (the vertex outputs go to LDS with `ds_write*` for the GS to read), takes its lane id from `v_mbcnt`, and on entry its read-before-write VGPRs are `v5`, `v9`, `v10`, `v11`, `v13` and `v14` (never `v0`/`v3`); none of the 195 starts with the fetch call. `fssm_unpack.py` defaults to the **last** payload, so the corpus translates the VS variant; the ES variant is untranslated, and which payload a GS-bound pipeline selects is open (`docs/fssm_crosscheck.md` §6). |
| 16-bit results | From Mesa ACO (`aco_ir.cpp`: `instr_is_16bit`, `can_use_opsel`) for GFX10: **f16 VOP1/VOP2 ops** (`v_add/sub/subrev/mul/max/min_f16`, `v_fmac_f16`, `v_cvt_f16_f32`, f16 unary) and **opsel-capable VOP3 ops** (`v_fma_f16`, `_e64` forms of the 16-bit integer ops) are **partial writes that keep the high half**. The **VOP2 (`_e32`) 16-bit integer ops** (`v_lshrrev_b16`…) define the whole register, so the high half is modelled as zero. An op_sel destination bit writes the high half. The translator used to clear the high half on f16 ops; that's fixed. **op_sel on f16 VOP3 ops** (`v_fma_f16`, `v_med3_f16`, `v_add/sub/subrev/mul/max/min_f16`, f16 unary, `v_fmac_f16`): bit i (i < number of sources) reads the high half of source i, the last bit writes the high half of the destination; neg/abs apply to the selected half. Source: Mesa ACO `can_use_opsel` and the RDNA2 ISA VOP3 `OP_SEL` field. Checked by the structural tests in `tests/test_f16_op_sel.py` (which half is read, where the result goes) and the value tests in `tests/test_f16_op_sel_values.py`. **Hardware-verified** on an AMD RX 6800 (gfx1030), the instructions run as a raw compute kernel in wave32 and in wave64 (IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals kept, round to nearest; the bytes assemble identically for gfx1013 and gfx1030): **`v_med3_f16`** with all 16 `op_sel` patterns, with neg/abs on a selected high half, with `clamp`, and with the destination also a source; the **plain forms** `v_add/sub/mul/max/min_f16` (`_e32`, and `_e64` with neg/abs/`clamp`), `v_fmac_f16` (`_e32`, and `_e64` with neg/abs; the accumulator is the low half of the destination), `v_rndne/floor/ceil/trunc/fract/sqrt/rcp/rsq_f16` (`_e32` and `_e64`) and `v_cvt_f16_f32`: low halves in, low half out, high half of the destination kept. 336 words per wave size (plus `v_rsq_f16_e64` and `v_ceil_f16_e64` in a later run), all as the rule says and as the translator computes. **Vulkan** (AMD Radeon RX 6800, Windows, driver 26.11.2; the nine `ps_f16_*` fixtures also pass on an NVIDIA RTX 4050 Laptop and an AMD Radeon 740M (RADV), both Linux): `ps_f16_med3_opsel`, `ps_f16_med3_mods`, `ps_f16_vop2`, `ps_f16_vop2b`, `ps_f16_vop1`, `ps_f16_vop1b`, whose expected words are the hardware ones (`tests/test_f16_hw_words.py` checks them against the rule and the translator without a GPU). **Not expressible, so not verified:** `op_sel` on the VOP2 and VOP1 ops in VOP3 encoding (`v_add/sub/mul/max/min_f16_e64`, `v_fmac_f16_e64`, the unary `_e64` forms, `v_cvt_f16_f32_e64`): llvm-mc 21 rejects the modifier for gfx1013 and gfx1030, and its disassembler prints bytes that have those bits set without it (`03 08 32 d5 01 05 02 00` comes back as `v_add_f16_e64 v3, v1, v2`). The translator's `op_sel` handling for these forms is therefore never reached from a disassembly listing and rests on the ISA field description alone; whether the hardware honours the bits there is unknown, and a blob that set them would be translated as if they were clear. Also unverified: NaN and denormal operands, other rounding and IEEE modes, outside the f16 transcendentals (measured, see the f16 transcendentals row). **Driver quirk (AMD Windows driver 26.11.1, RX 9070):** `valu_fuzz` f16 seed 3 programme 38 loses the high half of v26: `v_add_f16_e32 v26, v29, v32` then `v_sub_nc_u16_e64 v28, v32, v27 op_sel:[1,1,0]` then `v_add_f16_e64 v26, v26, v28` — the final add should keep `0xCD52` in the high half, but the GPU returns `0xEEEEEEEE` (native) / `0x00000000` (`--emulate`), all 64 threads. Bisected to the pair: the add's two sources must both have been produced by earlier partial writes; either alone, a NaN input alone, or `dst==src0` alone all pass. The emitted SPIR-V was checked with `spirv-dis` in both modes: the keep (load old v26, `AND 0xFFFF0000`, `OR` new low half, store to index 26) is present and correctly addressed, so the translator is clear and this is the driver miscompiling the pattern. Minimal reproducer: the three ops above with programme 38's inits (v26 `0xCD524000`, v28 `0xC692D243`, v29 `0xBC004200`, v32 `0xB35ABD4B`, v27 `0xB48ABA06`). Pinned by `tests/test_values.py` (`v_add_f16_e64_keep_hi`, `v_add_f16_e32_keep_hi`), which check the translator's keep, not the driver. Found and bisected by 1TrickOmen, reported on #634. |
| 16-bit SDWA result with `dst_sel:DWORD` | A 16-bit op in SDWA form (`v_add/sub/subrev/mul/min/max_f16_sdwa`, the f16 unary ops, `v_cvt_u16_f16`, `v_cvt_i16_f16`, `v_cvt_f16_u16`, `v_cvt_f16_i16`) with `dst_sel:DWORD` writes the whole VGPR: the 16-bit result zero-extended, **whatever `dst_unused` says**. The keep-the-high-half rule of the row above is for the non-SDWA forms only; with `dst_sel:WORD_0 dst_unused:UNUSED_PRESERVE` the high half is kept as usual. **Until 2026-11-22 the translator kept the high half for `dst_sel:DWORD`** (`Gen.insn` marked an SDWA destination only for a `dst_sel` other than DWORD). Measured natively on an AMD Radeon iGPU (gfx1036, RDNA2, Windows driver 26.10.1) with `python -I tools/sdwa_diff.py --oracle hw` (#2113) and with the program of `runtime/test/shaders/ps_sdwa_dword_hi.asm` through `tools/hw_oracle`: destinations 0xdeadbeef give 0x00004000 (`v_add_f16`, PRESERVE), 0x00000003 (`v_cvt_u16_f16`, PAD), 0x00004700 (`v_cvt_f16_u16`, PRESERVE), 0x0000c000 (`v_mul_f16`, SEXT); the same ops with `dst_sel:WORD_0 UNUSED_PRESERVE` give 0xdead4000, 0xdead0003, 0xdead4700, 0xdeadc000. GPU-checked through Vulkan with `ps_sdwa_dword_hi.asm` on an RTX 5080 (driver 616.92) and the same Radeon iGPU (4096/4096 pixels each); CPU-checked in `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`. |
| f16 inline constants | An inline float constant (`0.5`, `1.0`, `2.0`, `4.0`, their negatives, `0.15915494`) in an operand of a 16-bit float op is the **f16 value in the low half with a zero high half**, not f32 bits (the translator used to unpack the f32 pattern, whose low half is 0, so `v_add_f16 d, 1.0, v` added 0.0). Measured on an AMD RX 6800 (gfx1030) raw kernel, wave32 and wave64: `v_add/mul/max_f16`, `v_sub_f16_e64`, `v_fma_f16`, `v_fmac_f16`, `v_rcp_f16`, `v_med3_f16`, `v_cmp_lt_f16`, `v_cvt_f32_f16` (`0.5` gives `0x3f000000`), `v_cos_f16`, `v_pk_add_f16`/`v_pk_mul_f16` (the high result half sees 0.0), `v_pack_b32_f16`; `0.15915494` is `0x3118`; the high half `op_sel` selects from a constant reads +0.0 (`v_med3_f16`, `v_fma_f16`, `v_pack_b32_f16`, `v_pk_add_f16` with swapped halves). GPU tests `ps_f16_const`, `ps_f16_const2` and `ps_f16_pack` (Vulkan: AMD Radeon RX 6800, Windows, driver 26.11.2; all three fail without the fix). Constants in `v_fma_mix_f32`/`v_fma_mixlo/mixhi_f16` operands follow the same rule per operand (measured on an RDNA2 iGPU, gfx1036, SHADER-135): f32 bits where `op_sel_hi` makes the operand f32, the f16 constant with a zero high half where it makes it f16, so `op_sel` with `op_sel_hi` reads +0.0; an integer constant is its 32-bit integer. The translator gives the same words for the float constants in every `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` combination and for the integer constants as f16 halves, but not for `1` and `64` as f32 operands: their bits are f32 denormals, which gfx1036 flushes to a zero of their sign (`denorm_mode_32 = 0`, the MODE the translator assumes for the game) and the translator keeps (difference 4 in docs/shader_validation.md, "f16 output modifiers, clamp and `v_fma_mix*` operands on RDNA2"). |
| `v_fmamk_f16`, `v_fmaak_f16` | VOP2 with a literal K: `fmamk` computes `D = S0 * K + S1` (printed `vdst, src0, K, vsrc1`), `fmaak` computes `D = S0 * S1 + K` (printed `vdst, src0, vsrc1, K`). In print order both are `a[1] * a[2] + a[3]`, as for `v_fmamk/fmaak_f32`. K is the f16 in the literal's low 16 bits; a float literal is converted to f16 (`src_h`). One rounding to f16, as `v_fmac_f16` (SHADER-55). The GFX10 f16 VOP2 write puts the result in the low half and keeps the high half. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_valu_fmamk_fmaak_f16.py`, against `v_fma_f16` with K in a VGPR (value grids, a float literal, the operand order, seeded values). **Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle`, 17 cases: the print order, the one-rounding rule and the kept high half hold (`1*2+3`, `1.5*2+0.5`, both orders, `(1+2^-10)*3` rounding the exact 3.0029296875 to the tie's even `0x4202`, `65504*2` overflowing to `+inf`); f16 VALU keeps denormals, unlike the LDS float adder, so `0x0001*1+0x0001` gives `0x0002`; an sNaN is quieted with its payload kept (`0x7c01` gives `0x7e01`) and `inf + -inf` gives the negative default `0xfe00`. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| SDWA select on an f16 inline constant | In an `_sdwa` 16-bit float op, an inline float constant is first the f16 value over a zero high half (row above) and `src0_sel`/`src1_sel` then narrows those 32 bits like a register's, zero-extended, before neg/abs and the op: `1.0` under `WORD_1` reads +0.0, `2.0` under `BYTE_1` reads the f16 denormal `0x0040`. An SGPR source, and an integer op reading the f32 bits of a float constant (`v_cvt_f16_u16_sdwa d, 1.0 src0_sel:WORD_1` converts 0x3f80), are narrowed the same way. **Until 2026-11-22 the translator used the whole f16 constant** (`v_add_f16_sdwa d, 1.0, v src0_sel:WORD_1` with v = 0.5 gave 1.5). Measured on an AMD Radeon iGPU (gfx1036, Ryzen 9 9950X3D, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle` (raw HIP kernel, wave32): the four words of `runtime/test/shaders/ps_sdwa_f16_const.asm`; found with `python -I tools/sdwa_diff.py --oracle hw` (#2113). GPU test `ps_sdwa_f16_const`, 4096/4096 pixels on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 (driver 616.92) and the same Radeon iGPU through Vulkan; both fail without the fix. Also executed by `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`. |
| `v_pack_b32_f16` input `neg` | A VOP3 `neg` on an operand of this instruction flips the sign bit of **the 16-bit half that operand contributes**, not of the whole 32-bit source, and only that half: measured on gfx1036 (RDNA2 iGPU, AMD Windows driver 32.0.21044.64) with 192 raw kernels, `0x7c01` gives `0xfc01` under IEEE mode 0 and `0xfe01` under mode 1, in either half and with `op_sel`. The translator moved the bits unchanged and refused every modifier; it now XORs `0x8000` into the selected half. `abs` is still refused, because its rule on this instruction is unmeasured (SHADER-260), and the mode-1 quieting of a signalling-NaN half is not modelled while SHADER-190 is open; under mode 0, where the bits move unchanged, the translation is exact. Checked on the CPU by `tests/test_values.py` and `tests/semantic/test_vop3_const_mods.py`; the gfx1036 measurement above is @Sanonymous01's, not repeated here. |
| Packed 16-bit integer ops (`v_pk_add/sub_u16/i16`, `v_pk_mul_lo_u16`, `v_pk_mad_u16/i16`, `v_pk_max/min_u16/i16`) | VOP3P: result half 0 takes each source's half from `op_sel[i]`, result half 1 from `op_sel_hi[i]` (defaults `[0,..]` and `[1,..]`), as for `v_pk_lshlrev_b16`. Each half is computed on its own and wraps to 16 bits. `clamp` (add, sub, mad) saturates the half to its type's range (the exact value fits in 32 bits), whereas on `v_pk_mul_lo_u16` and `v_pk_max/min_u16/i16` VOP3P `clamp` is a hardware no-op (`v_pk_mul_lo_u16 clamp` wraps modulo 2^16 rather than saturating to `0xFFFF`, and `max`/`min` results are unchanged; until 2026-11-23 `clamp` on those five ops raised `Unsupported`). `neg_lo`/`neg_hi` are float-only and unsupported modifiers (such as `mul:*`/`div:*`) raise `Unsupported`. `clamp` (add, sub, mad) saturates the half to its type's range; the exact value fits in 32 bits. `neg_lo`/`neg_hi` are float-only and raise `Unsupported`, as does `clamp` on the other ops. A constant operand is treated as a 32-bit dword `C`: `op_sel[i] == 0` selects bits `[15:0]`, `op_sel[i] == 1` selects bits `[31:16]`. Positive inline constants zero-extend (`5` → `0x00000005`), negative inline constants sign-extend (`-1` → `0xFFFFFFFF`, `-16` → `0xFFFFFFF0`), and 32-bit literals retain their full dword (`0x12345678`). Lowered via `OpBitField*Extract` at `16 * sl[i]` (the packed 16-bit *shifts* read their halves with a shift and a mask instead, like every other high-half reader: see the 16-bit high-half reads row). Lowered via `OpBitField*Extract` at `16 * sl[i]`. Until 2026-11-22 reading the high half of a constant raised `Unsupported`. A constant operand reads as its low 16 bits. Its high half under `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` is **not measured** for integer ops (for f16 constants it reads +0.0, see the row above), so a constant whose high half would be read raises `Unsupported` instead of guessing; the compiler's usual form, `op_sel_hi:[1,0]` with the constant second, reads only the low half. Until 2026-11-22 all eleven ops raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_pk_int16.py` (value grids, seeded `mad`, `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` combinations, `clamp` including the unsigned `sub` floor, inline and literal constants, rejected forms) and `tests/semantic/test_pk_int16_clamp.py` (all 26 probe cases plain vs `clamp`, SPIR-V non-saturation assertion, inactive `EXEC` preservation, `spirv-val` Vulkan 1.2 validation, rejected modifiers), plus `tests/test_pk_int16_clamp_probe.py`. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pk_int16`, and independently on `gfx1036` desktop APU, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21045.29003`, across 380 raw compute kernels covering all 11 ops, constants under `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`, `clamp`, and wave32/wave64 parity with 0 wave diffs and 0 mismatches, and via `tools/pk_int16_clamp_probe.py --hw-arch gfx1036` across 624 wave32 & wave64 evaluations with `mismatches=0, wave_diffs=0, target_clamp_diffs=0, control_clamp_diffs=34`, plus a 96-word wave32 Vulkan compute check under `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` passing with 0 mismatches on `AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT` driver `26.11.2` and `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` driver `26.11.2`): all eleven ops (wrap, `clamp` saturation on add/sub/mad, `clamp` no-op on `mul_lo`/`max`/`min`, and constant `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`) match bit-for-bit. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_pk_int16.py` (value grids, seeded `mad`, `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` combinations, `clamp` including the unsigned `sub` floor, inline and literal constants, rejected forms). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pk_int16`, and independently on `gfx1036` desktop APU, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21045.29003`, across 380 raw compute kernels covering all 11 ops, constants under `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`, `clamp`, and wave32/wave64 parity with 0 wave diffs and 0 mismatches): all eleven ops (wrap, `clamp` saturation on add/sub/mad, and constant `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`) match bit-for-bit. **Not GPU-verified.** **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pk_int16`): all eleven ops (wrap, `clamp` saturation on add/sub/mad, and `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`) match bit-for-bit. **`v_pk_mul_lo_u16` low 16 bits (found with `pk16_diff --oracle hw,vk`, GPU test `cs_pk_mul_lo_const`):** each half is `((x << 16) * y) >> 16`, not `(x * y) & 0xffff`: with a constant half of 0 in the other half of the result the AMD Windows driver (Radeon iGPU gfx1036, 26.10.1) dropped the mask (`0x1234 * 0xaaaa` gave `0x0c229e88`, hardware `0x00009e88`); the RTX 5080 was right either way. Words measured on the same gfx1036 in `runtime/test/shaders/cs_pk_mul_lo_const.asm`. |
| `v_sin_f32`, `v_cos_f32`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_cos_f16` | The operand is an angle in turns (the ISA says valid over +-256; the measurement below finds no such limit on gfx1030). `turn_angle` reduces it in turns first, `x - RoundEven(x)` (exact in binary32, in [-0.5, 0.5]), then scales by `2*pi` and calls `Sin`/`Cos`, so the angle stays in [-pi, pi], the only range where GLSL guarantees them (2^-11 absolute). Scaling first rounded the fractional turn away (about 1.2e-4 rad near 255 turns, #594); the first fix (#1283) reduced with `Fract`, which rounds `1 - \| x \| ` for a negative x and lost `-2^-26` (it returned +0; absolute errors up to ~1.7e-7 at -0.1 turns), so the reduction is `RoundEven`-centred. `RoundEven` rather than `Round` so +-0.5 turn reduce symmetrically. inf and NaN still give NaN, and a zero operand is passed through unreduced so `sin(-0)` stays -0. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_sincos.py` against `sin`/`cos(2*pi*x)` in double (large angles, tiny arguments, whole turns of either sign, +-0, inf/NaN, modifiers, f16) and `tests/semantic/test_valu_sin_cos_turns.py` (#1283). GPU test `cs_w32_sincos` (large angles within 1e-3, since the driver `Sin` is only good to 2^-11) is a regression guard, not proof the old form failed on a given driver. Measured on an AMD Radeon RX 6800 (gfx1030, not the console's gfx1013) by @shelbykb2 (#616): whole and half turns of either sign give +0 (only `sin(-0)` is -0, so no sign needs copying onto a zero result, as here); there is no +-256 limit on that card (any finite x reduces exactly: `sin(300.25)` = 1, `sin(2^24)` = +0); the f16 forms reduce the same way as the f32 ones; +-inf gives a NaN and a NaN passes through, quieted with IEEE mode on. Re-measured natively on gfx1036 (RDNA2, SHADER-134) for f32 and f16 over every quarter turn from 0.25 to 2^24 of either sign and around +-256: the same rules. At an odd half turn `sin`, and at an odd quarter turn `cos`, is exactly +0 (either sign of x), where `Sin`/`Cos` of the rounded 2*pi give about -+8.7e-8 (f32) or a +-1-ulp f16 denormal; since 2026-11-22 the translator selects +0 there (`Gen.trig_exact_zero`), checked against 500 measured words by `tests/semantic/test_alu_sincos_turns_hw.py`. Known differences from this translation: for Known differences from this translation: at half turns `Sin` of the rounded pi gives about -+8.7e-8 where the hardware returns exactly 0, likewise `cos` at quarter turns; for \| x \| <= 2^-12 the hardware multiplies by `0x40c90fd5`, not the rounded 2*pi; the inf/NaN words are whatever the host driver returns. Not checked on gfx1013. |
| `v_pack_b32_f16` source modifiers | A VOP3 `abs` or `neg` on an operand of this instruction acts on **the 16-bit half that operand contributes**, not on the whole 32-bit source, and only on that half: `abs` clears bit 15 and `neg` flips it, `abs` first, both as plain bit moves — the operand is not converted to a float. Until 2026-11-22 any such operand was rejected as `Unsupported`. **`neg` is measured** on gfx1036 (RDNA2 iGPU, AMD Windows driver 32.0.21044.64) with 192 raw kernels: `0x7c01` gives `0xfc01` under IEEE mode 0 and `0xfe01` under mode 1, in either half and with `op_sel`, so the sign move does not depend on the mode. **`neg`, `abs`, and `neg_abs` are hardware-verified on RDNA2** across both `gfx1035` (Windows 11, driver 26.5.1, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pack_b32_f16_abs` and raw `ieee=0` kernels) and `gfx1036` (Windows 11, driver 32.0.21045.29003, 1,088 raw kernels across wave32 and wave64, 0 mismatches): **`abs` and `- **`neg`, `abs`, and `- **`abs` rests on the ISA's definition of ABS on a float operand as the sign clear, applied before `neg`; no hardware run of this instruction with `abs` exists yet**, and `abs` on a NaN half is therefore unmeasured too. **Not modelled: IEEE mode 1 also quiets a signalling-NaN half** (`0x7c01` → `0x7e01`); which mode the game's shaders run in is SHADER-190, and under mode 0, where the bits move unchanged, the translation is exact. Checked by `tests/test_values.py`, `tests/semantic/test_vop3_const_mods.py`, `tests/semantic/test_pack_b32_f16_mods.py` (exact bits through the interpreter) and the GPU fixture `ps_f16_pack_mods` (words derived from the rule, not a readback); the gfx1036 measurement above is @Sanonymous01's, not repeated here. \| x src \| ` are hardware-verified on RDNA2** across both `gfx1035` (Windows 11, driver 26.5.1, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pack_b32_f16_abs` and raw `ieee=0` kernels) and `gfx1036` (Windows 11, driver 32.0.21045.29003, 1,088 raw kernels across wave32 and wave64): ` (neg+abs) are measured** on gfx1036 (RDNA2 iGPU, AMD Windows driver 32.0.21045.29003) with 1,088 raw kernels across wave32 and wave64 (0 mismatches): `abs` clears bit 15 (`& 0x7FFF`), `neg` flips it (`^ 0x8000`), and `neg_abs` sets bit 15 across all normal numbers, zeros, infinities, subnormals, quiet NaNs, and signaling NaNs, in either half and with `op_sel`. Under IEEE mode 0, signaling NaN payloads are preserved untouched (`0x7c01` gives `0xfc01` under `neg` and `0x7c01` under `abs`); under IEEE mode 1, a signaling-NaN half is quieted (bit 9 set: `0x7c01` gives `0xfe01` under `neg` and `0x7e01` under `abs`). ` are hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_pack_b32_f16_abs` and raw `ieee=0` kernels): On `gfx1035`, `abs` clears bit 15 and applies before `neg` on `-1.0`, `-0.0`, `-qNaN` (`0xfe05` → `0x7e05`), and `-sNaN` (`0xfc01` → `0x7c01` under `ieee=0`), with and without `op_sel`. **Not modelled: IEEE mode 1 also quiets a signalling-NaN half** (`0x7c01` → `0x7e01`); which mode the game's shaders run in is SHADER-190, and under mode 0, where the bits move unchanged, the translation is exact. Checked by `tests/test_values.py`, `tests/semantic/test_vop3_const_mods.py`, `tests/semantic/test_pack_b32_f16_mods.py` (exact bits through the interpreter) and the GPU fixture `ps_f16_pack_mods` (words derived from the rule, not a readback); the gfx1036 measurement above is @Sanonymous01's, not repeated here. |
| Packed integer dot products (`v_dot2_i32_i16`, `v_dot2_u32_u16`, `v_dot4_u32_u8`, `v_dot8_i32_i4`, `v_dot8_u32_u4`, plus VOP2 accumulating `v_dot2c_i32_i16`, `v_dot2c_u32_u16`, `v_dot4c_u32_u8`, `v_dot8c_i32_i4`, `v_dot8c_u32_u4`), `v_dot2_f32_f16` / `v_dot2c_f32_f16`, and `v_pk_fmac_f16` | RDNA2 ISA: `v_dot2_i32_i16`/`u16` (2×16-bit), `v_dot4_u32_u8` (4×8-bit unsigned), and `v_dot8_i32_i4`/`u4` (8×4-bit) — and their VOP2 accumulating `v_dot*c_*` forms (reading old `vdst` as `acc`) — extract each element with `OpBitFieldSExtract`/`OpBitFieldUExtract`, sum the elementwise products with `S2` (or old `vdst`) wrapping at 32 bits, and saturate to `[INT32_MIN, INT32_MAX]` / `[0, UINT32_MAX]` under `clamp` (signed carry tracking handles the `v_dot2_i32_i16` `(-32768)^2 + (-32768)^2 + acc` overflow/cancellation edge). For VOP3P `v_dot*_*`, the hardware default `op_sel:[0,0,0]` and `op_sel_hi:[1,1,1]` is accepted and any other `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`, `neg_lo`/`neg_hi`, DPP/SDWA, or source `neg`/`abs` raises `Unsupported`; for VOP2 `v_dot*c_*`, only optional `clamp` and VGPR `vdst` are accepted. `v_dot2_f32_f16` (VOP3P) and `v_dot2c_f32_f16` (VOP2) compute `a0*b0 + a1*b1 + acc` in f32 (`f16_in` per half, `flush_f32` on `acc`, optional `clamp`, and for `v_dot2_f32_f16` `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`/`neg_lo`/`neg_hi` on the inputs and `neg_lo[2]` on `acc`). All 6 new `v_dot*c_*` ops mark `vdst` as read-before-write in `read_before_write_vgprs` and `reg_reads_writes`. (`v_dot4_i32_i8` and `v_dot4c_i32_i8` are covered separately in the Signed-byte dot products row, #1754.) `v_pk_fmac_f16` (VOP2) unpacks `src0`, `vsrc1` and `vdst` as `f16x2` (`Gen.src_h`, so inline float constants apply in the low half with `+0.0` high), computes `fma(src0[k], vsrc1[k], vdst[k])` per half with single f16 rounding (`Gen.fma_f16_odd`, SHADER-55), and packs with `Gen.f16_pack2`; any modifier outside `_dpp` or non-VGPR `vdst` raises `Unsupported`, and `v_pk_fmac_f16` marks `vdst` as read before write in `read_before_write_vgprs` and `reg_reads_writes`. **Hardware-confirmed on RDNA2** for the 5 VOP3P integer forms and `v_pk_fmac_f16`: on `gfx1036` (Ryzen 9 7950X3D iGPU, `tools/hw_oracle`, @aslan2142), all 60 integer dot-product sets and all 22 `v_pk_fmac_f16` default-MODE words match; independently confirmed on `gfx1035` (Radeon 680M, @Youssefrah16) across 18 cases × wave32/wave64 (36/36 bit-exact), pinned by `tests/semantic/test_alu_dot_pk_fmac.py`. The 5 VOP2 `v_dot*c_*` integer forms and `v_dot2_f32_f16` / `v_dot2c_f32_f16` are CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_dot_complete.py` and not yet hardware-measured on RDNA2. Non-default f16 MODEs (denormal flush, RTZ) are not modelled on `v_pk_fmac_f16` while the translator has no MODE input. |
| Packed integer dot products (`v_dot2_i32_i16`, `v_dot2_u32_u16`, `v_dot4_u32_u8`, `v_dot8_i32_i4`, `v_dot8_u32_u4`) and `v_pk_fmac_f16` | RDNA2 ISA: `v_dot2_i32_i16`/`u16` (2×16-bit), `v_dot4_u32_u8` (4×8-bit unsigned), and `v_dot8_i32_i4`/`u4` (8×4-bit) extract each element with `OpBitFieldSExtract`/`OpBitFieldUExtract`, sum the elementwise products with `S2` wrapping at 32 bits, and saturate to `[INT32_MIN, INT32_MAX]` / `[0, UINT32_MAX]` under VOP3P `clamp` (signed carry tracking handles the `v_dot2_i32_i16` `(-32768)^2 + (-32768)^2 + S2` overflow/cancellation edge). The hardware default `op_sel:[0,0,0]` and `op_sel_hi:[1,1,1]` (whether omitted or written explicitly) is accepted; any other `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` (such as `op_sel_hi:[0,0,0]` or `[0,1,1]`, which select the low half for lane 1 on `v_dot2_*`), `neg_lo`/`neg_hi`, DPP/SDWA forms, and source `neg`/`abs` raise `Unsupported`. (`v_dot4_i32_i8` and `v_dot4c_i32_i8` are covered separately in the Signed-byte dot products row, #1754.) `v_pk_fmac_f16` (VOP2) unpacks `src0`, `vsrc1` and `vdst` as `f16x2` (`Gen.src_h`, so inline float constants apply in the low half with `+0.0` high), computes `fma(src0[k], vsrc1[k], vdst[k])` per half with single f16 rounding (`Gen.fma_f16_odd`, SHADER-55), and packs with `Gen.f16_pack2`; any modifier outside `_dpp` or non-VGPR `vdst` raises `Unsupported`, and `v_pk_fmac_f16` marks `vdst` as read before write in `read_before_write_vgprs` and `reg_reads_writes`. Until 2026-11-22 all six ops raised `Unsupported`. **Hardware-confirmed on RDNA2:** on `gfx1036` (Ryzen 9 7950X3D iGPU, `tools/hw_oracle`, @aslan2142), all 60 integer dot-product sets (plain, swapped/SGPR sources, and `clamp`) match bit-for-bit, all measured `op_sel`/`neg_*` variants are refused, and all 22 `v_pk_fmac_f16` default-MODE (`0x000003C0`: round-to-nearest-even, f16 denormals kept, IEEE and DX10_CLAMP on) words match in both source orders (all 34 non-NaN halves bit-exact; the 10 NaN halves yield quiet f16 NaN, as SPIR-V does not preserve NaN payload bits or sign); independently confirmed on `gfx1035` (Radeon 680M, `tools/hw_oracle`, @Youssefrah16) across 18 cases × wave32/wave64 (36/36 bit-exact). Pinned by `tests/semantic/test_alu_dot_pk_fmac.py` (all 82 `gfx1036` measured tables plus hand-written `.asm` fixture, boundary/clamp cases, default/non-default `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi`, `read_before_write_vgprs`/`reg_reads_writes`, DPP, seeded sweeps, and rejected forms). Non-default f16 MODEs (denormal flush, RTZ) are not modelled on `v_pk_fmac_f16` while the translator has no MODE input. |
| `v_med3_f32` output modifiers | The translator applies `mul:2`, `mul:4` or `div:2` to the median before clamping to [0, 1], following [LLVM’s modifier order](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUModifierSyntax.html#omod). The finite-input fixture `ps_med3_mods.asm` produced 4,096 exact pixels on an Apple M3 Max with MoltenVK and on Mesa llvmpipe (software Vulkan); it also passes (4,096/4,096) on an NVIDIA RTX 4050 Laptop and an AMD Radeon 740M (RADV), both Linux, and on an RTX 2060 SUPER per #634. This uses the existing float MODE assumptions described above. |
| `v_div_fmas_f32`, `v_div_fmas_f64` | Scaled FMA for IEEE-754 division (VOP3): `D = fma(S0, S1, S2)` when `VCC[lane] == 0`; when `VCC[lane] == 1`, `D = (exp(S2) > bias ? 2^+S : 2^-S) * fma_exact(S0, S1, S2)` where `(bias, S)` is `(127, 64)` for f32 and `(1023, 128)` for f64 (`OpCapability Float64`, no `Int64`), i.e. `\|S2\| >= 2.0` scales up and `\|S2\| < 2.0` scales down. Scale-up uses `fma(S0, S1, S2) * 2^S` (any non-zero cancellation residual against `\|S2\| >= 2.0` stays well above normal minimum). Scale-down pre-scales `S0`/`S1` only when `\|S0*S1\|` can overflow (`\|S0\| >= 2^S` and `\|S1\| >= 1`, symmetric, or both `>= 2^(S/2)`), with a raw-bit sticky value `copysign(min_normal, S2)` whenever `0 < \|S2\| < 2^(Emin + S)` so half-ULP tie-breaking stays exact; otherwise it post-scales `fma(S0, S1, S2) * 2^-S`, with a TwoSum + product-error half-ULP tie adjustment in f64 when `\|fma\|` falls in `[2^-947, 2^-894)` so f64 subnormal outputs single-round. For f32 under the game MODE (`denorm32=0`), subnormal inputs and intermediate products are kept while subnormal outputs flush to signed zero before (and after) output modifiers; for f64 (`denorm16_64=3`), subnormals are kept throughout. VOP3 input (`abs`/`neg`) and output (`mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2`, then `clamp`) modifiers apply via `clamp_mods` / `clamp_mods_f64`. **Until 2026-11-22 the translator scaled unconditionally by `2^32` (f32) / `2^64` (f64) whenever `VCC[lane] == 1`, omitting the `exp(S2) > bias` branch and using half the hardware exponent shift.** **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, AMD Radeon Graphics, Windows driver 32.0.21042.62, `tools/hw_oracle`, wave32 and wave64, game MODE `ieee=0`/`dx10_clamp=1`/`denorm32=0`/`denorm16_64=3`): 150/150 f32 and 153/153 f64 stress cases match bit-for-bit, including the `\|S2\| = 2.0` vs `nextbelow(2.0)` threshold, scale-down overflow prevention, sticky-bit tie-breaking, f32 output denormal flushing, and f64 subnormal single rounding. Checked bit-for-bit on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp` for f32, SSA `%double` evaluator for f64) and assembled with `tools/spirv_asm.py` (plus `spirv-val` when present) by `tests/semantic/test_alu_div_fmas.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| POPS | Primitive-ordered pixel shading (10 PS, `src_pops_exiting_wave_id` wait loop, `s_sleep`, `MSG_ORDERED_PS_DONE`) becomes **fragment-shader interlock** (`PixelInterlockOrderedEXT`). It begins at the end of the entry block and ends at shader exit: a longer critical section with the same ordering. `src_pops_exiting_wave_id` reads 0, and **the runtime must put 0 (no collision, bit 31 clear) in the POPS collision SGPR**, so the hardware wait loop is skipped. Needs `VK_EXT_fragment_shader_interlock` (`fragmentShaderPixelInterlock`). |
| Coherent image accesses (`glc`) | On GFX10 a device-scope (coherent) load is `glc dlc` and a device-scope store `glc`: Mesa sets exactly these bits for `ACCESS_COHERENT` ([`ac_get_hw_cache_flags`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/common/ac_shader_util.c), GFX10–10.3 branch). In the corpus only the 10 POPS pixel shaders use them: 30 `image_load ... glc dlc` and 50 `image_store ... glc`, all 2D, and in each shader the 3 T#s it loads are the 3 it stores, a read-modify-write of its ordered images. A `glc` `image_load` therefore reads the **storage view the stores write** (`OpImageRead` on binding 8 + 3·dim + class, the class picked from the T# FORMAT as for stores), not the sampled array of "Typed image loads": a sampled read has no coherent form in SPIR-V, and between critical sections the fragment-shader interlock only makes coherent memory visible ([Vulkan memory model](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/appendices/memorymodel.html): non-atomic accesses are private unless `Coherent`; [ARB_fragment_shader_interlock](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/ARB/ARB_fragment_shader_interlock.txt): stores to `coherent` memory in one critical section are visible to reads in the next). A shader with a `glc` image load or store decorates its storage views **`Coherent`**, as vkd3d-proton's dxil-spirv does for rasterizer-ordered views (`dxil_converter.cpp`: "ROV implies coherent"). These 10 modules need `shaderStorageImageReadWithoutFormat` (listed in their index features); every other `image_load` keeps the sampled path and needs no storage feature (SHADER-151). `dlc` alone is a CU-scope load and stays non-coherent; `glc` on a sampled read (`image_sample*`, `image_gather*`, `image_load_mip`) or on a dimension without storage views (cube) is `Unsupported`. Until 2026-11-22 the coherent loads read the sampled array and nothing was `Coherent`, so a POPS read could miss the previous fragment's store. Confirmed by these references, `tests/semantic/test_image_coherent.py` (each read and write's view and type, decorations, capabilities, index features; 7 of its 10 tests fail on the old translator) and a corpus count; **not GPU-checked**: a stale read is a cache race no deterministic test forces. |
| 2D `image_sample_lz` | The T# FORMAT selects the float, UINT or SINT sampled view at LOD zero. With nearest sampling, native gfx1036 returned the original 32-bit integer components. RTX 5080 and gfx1036 Vulkan readbacks matched all four channels on RGBA8 UINT, SINT and UNORM for full-width and D16 results. The R32 UINT/SINT saturation-boundary fixture also matched all 64 sampled sums on both adapters. |
| 2D `image_sample_l` | The T# FORMAT selects the sampled image type, and every type uses the LOD following the two coordinates. Vulkan requires the sampled type and signedness to match the view. The unit test `test_2d_lod_samples_use_matching_typed_views` in `tests/test_image_load_typed.py` checks the R32 UINT, SINT and FLOAT descriptor branches, the float fallback on a lookup miss and the LOD register with full-width and D16 results. [Vulkan sampling validation](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/textures.html#textures-input-validation) |
| Implicit 2D pixel-shader integer samples | For `image_sample`, take the UV derivatives before branching on the T# format, then sample the matching float, UINT or SINT view with those gradients. In `ps_sample_implicit_sint` a 128×128 signed texture minifies onto 64×64 pixels, and `ps_sample_implicit_uint` samples the same image through the unsigned view. Clamping the sampler to level 0 returned four color quadrants; allowing level 1 returned cyan. All four signed D16 channels matched at every pixel on RTX 5080 and gfx1036; both fixtures are 4096/4096 pixels exact on an Intel UHD Graphics 630 (CFL GT2), Mesa 25.2.8. |
| Texel offsets | `image_sample*_o`: the first address dword packs signed 6-bit offsets (x [5:0], y [13:8], z [21:16]), which become the SPIR-V `Offset` operand. A non-constant `Offset` outside gather needs **`VK_KHR_maintenance8`**, and is validated with `spirv-val --allow-offset-texture-operand`. The operand is always a run-time value: the translator extracts the fields from the register, also when the shader loaded the dword from an immediate, so every `_o` sample needs the extension. `image_gather4_lz_o` / `_l_o` read x and y the same way; a gather `Offset` needs `shaderImageGatherExtended`, not maintenance8. **GPU-tested** with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_texoff.asm` on `vk_texture_test` (in `check.py` and `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`; SKIP on a device without the extension), on an AMD Radeon RX 6800, Windows driver 26.11.2, maintenance8 enabled: an 8×8 texture whose texels are all different, a point sampler that clamps to the edge, 2D targets, mip 0. `image_sample_lz_o` with an offset computed per pixel (all 64 pairs of x, y in −4..3, none clamped), `image_sample_l_o` with (−3, +2) and `image_sample_lz_o` with (+16, −32) from immediates, and `image_gather4_lz_o` of red and of green with the per-pixel offset gave, on all 4096 pixels, the texels worked out by hand from this rule (derivation in the `.asm` header). Run with a sabotaged translator, the test fails when the sample offset is dropped, has x and y swapped, takes y from bit 6, or uses 4-bit or 5-bit fields, and when the gather offset is dropped, has x and y swapped or takes y from bit 6; 4-bit and 5-bit gather fields pass, because the gather offsets stay in −4..3. What it shows is that the translated SPIR-V implements this rule on Vulkan; it does not measure the field layout on the hardware. **It cannot see the sign extension on this driver**: with the fields read unsigned the same texels come back: `Offset` 60..63 sampled as −4..−1 and +32 as −32 (the device reports texel offset limits −64..63, gather −32..31). The sign extension and the gather field width are therefore pinned by the structural test `tests/test_texel_offsets.py` (signed, bit 8k, 6 bits, for `_lz_o`, `_l_o`, `_o`, 3D and gather) and are not GPU-tested through Vulkan. **The x field is measured on hardware:** `image_sample_lz_o` run as a raw compute kernel on the same RX 6800 (gfx1030, wave32 and wave64), on a 64×1 `R32_FLOAT` image whose texel i holds i, with a point sampler that clamps to the last texel: from texel 40, offset dword `0x20` reads texel 8 and `0x3c` reads texel 36 (unsigned would clamp both to 63); from texel 10, `0x1f` reads 41 and `0x20` clamps to 0; `0x40`, `0x80` and `0xc0` read the centre texel (bits 6–7 are ignored); and a non-zero y field, z field or top byte leaves x alone. All 36 rows fit x = sign-extended bits [5:0]. **The y and z fields are measured too** (SHADER-337): `tools/texoff_probe.py --oracle hw` on the integrated GPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D (gfx1036, RDNA2, Windows driver 32.0.21045.16010), wave32 and wave64, samples a linear `R32_SFLOAT` texture whose texel holds its index (2D 64×64 for y, 3D 64×1×64 for z) with `image_sample_lz_o` and a point sampler that clamps to the last texel. From row 40, y = `0x20` reads row 8 and `0x3c` row 36, `0x21` row 9 (unsigned would clamp all three to 63); from row 10, `0x1f` reads 41 and `0x20` clamps to 0 (unsigned: 42); `0xc0` (bits 14–15 set, field 0) reads row 40 and `0x7f` row 39, so those bits are ignored; x −2 with y −4 in one dword reads both. The z field at bits [21:16] gives the same 7 results on the 3D image. All 15 rows fit each field = sign-extended 6 bits, the bits above it ignored, which is the translator's rule; `tests/test_texoff_probe.py` checks that every row with a negative field tells the signed reading from the unsigned one. The (+16, −32) sample is outside Vulkan's guaranteed −8..7 and is judged only where the device limits cover it. **`image_sample_o`, `image_sample_b_o` and `image_gather4_l_o` are GPU-tested** with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_texoff_forms.asm` (the same texture, sampler and per-pixel offset as `ps_texoff`, all 64 offset pairs in −4..3; in `check.py` and `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`): 4096/4096 pixels exact on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 (driver 621.24) and on the integrated GPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D (gfx1036, driver 32.0.21045.16010), both with maintenance8 enabled. Not GPU-tested: `_c_lz_o`, the z field (3D), 1D, 2D-array and cube targets (offset next to an array layer), wrap or mirror addressing, linear filtering, mips other than 0, other vendors' drivers, and a device without maintenance8 (the test skips there; the translator has no fallback for it). **Without maintenance8 (`--texel-offset-fallback`, NVIDIA Pascal for one):** an `image_sample*_o` gets no `Offset`; the offset is folded into the coordinate instead, as coordinate + offset ÷ the size of the sampled level. The level is 0 for `_lz` / `_c_lz`, the given LOD for `_l`, and `OpImageQueryLod`'s for implicit samples (plus the bias for `_b`), rounded the way the S#'s `MIP_FILTER` (word 2 [27:26], literal or read at run time) picks a level, and clamped to the image's levels: floored for linear, nearest with a tie rounding up for point (floor(LOD + 0.5), computed as floor + (fraction ≥ 0.5) so binary32 rounding of the sum can't move it; Vulkan allows either tie rule, and RTX 5070 GPUs (drivers 616.56, 621.01) sample LOD 0.5 and 0x3f000001 at mip 1, as `tools/image_query_probe.py` records RDNA2 doing) and for the value 3, and 0 for none, whose LOD the runtime holds in [0, 0.25] (SHADER-699: a point-mip sample at LOD 0.75 reads mip 1, so flooring shifted it by mip-0 texels; checked in `tests/test_texel_offsets.py` by evaluating the emitted level chain). A texel offset applies before wrapping and clamping, and an integer offset commutes with both, so the shift is exact for every address mode at that level. The one case that differs is a trilinear blend of two levels, where the coarser level moves by offset ÷ 2 of its texels. Implicit samples become `Grad` samples with the derivatives of the unshifted coordinate (× 2^bias for `_b`), so the LOD and anisotropy are those of the original sample even when the offset varies per pixel. Gathers keep their `Offset` (`shaderImageGatherExtended`). Only 4 of the 12,101 PS need it, all through `image_sample_o`. **GPU-tested** on an NVIDIA GeForce GTX 1060 6GB (no maintenance8; driver 560.94, Windows 10) with `LEONIDA_TEXOFF_FALLBACK=1`: `ps_texoff` and `ps_texoff_implicit` (the per-pixel sample as `image_sample_o`) are 4096/4096 pixels exact, with the +16/−32 sample judged too, since a coordinate shift has no device offset limit. Checked in `tests/test_texel_offsets.py`, including `spirv-val` without `--allow-offset-texture-operand`. |
| A16 sample offsets | For the supported `image_sample*_o` forms, the first address VGPR retains the signed texel-offset fields. The following coordinates and any trailing LOD use packed binary16 halves, low half first. LLVM counts extra operands as whole words before packing coordinates and LOD ([getAddrSizeMIMGOp](https://llvm.org/docs/doxygen/AMDGPUBaseInfo_8cpp_source.html#l00342)). On RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics, `ps_texoff.asm` checked 64 distinct offsets applied to packed U/V at texel (3,5) against the expected texel for each of 4096 pixels. Swapping the halves or dropping the offset changed 4032 pixels per GPU. |
| `s_memtime`, `s_memrealtime` | Behind `--shader-clock khr` (default `off`: both stay `Unsupported`, as before). `s_memtime` is the per-CU shader clock: `OpReadClockKHR` with Subgroup scope; `s_memrealtime` is the constant-rate REFCLK counter: Device scope. The 64-bit result goes to the destination SGPR pair, low word first; other destinations are `Unsupported`. Each module declares `ShaderClockKHR` + `SPV_KHR_shader_clock` and lists `shaderSubgroupClock` and/or `shaderDeviceClock` in the index `features`, only for the scopes it reads. The runtime enables `VK_KHR_shader_clock` with whichever feature the GPU has (`VulkanDeviceInfo::shader_subgroup_clock` / `shader_device_clock`, checked by `d3d12_vulkan_device_test`); the shader library does not read `features`, which is why the rule is opt-in: a module with a scope the device lacks fails `vkCreateShaderModule`. Values are not comparable with a console GPU's (different clock and frequency), so time differences work and fixed-frequency assumptions don't. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_smem_clock.py` (scopes, capability/extension, features, spirv-val, off by default). GPU: a PS reading both clocks creates its pipeline (`vk_pipeline_check`) on an RTX 6000 Ada (Linux) and an RX 9060 XT (RADV, #2817). The values themselves are not measured. |
| A16 gather offsets | On GFX10, `image_gather4_lz_o` and `image_gather4_l_o` take the packed signed texel offset in a full VGPR before the packed binary16 coordinates and optional LOD. LLVM [counts the extra operand before packing coordinate words](https://llvm.org/docs/doxygen/AMDGPUBaseInfo_8cpp_source.html#l00342). The synthetic `gather_offset_a16.asm` checks an offset of (−1,+1) with distinct U and V and a nonzero mip. Checked by the unit test `test_a16_offset_gathers_preserve_offset_and_unpack_uv_lod` in `tests/test_image_load_typed.py`, which translates that fixture and its `gather4_lz_o` variant and follows the offset fields and the packed U, V and LOD halves back to their source VGPRs. |
| Optional device features | Shaders may need `fragmentShaderBarycentric`, `fragmentShaderPixelInterlock` and `maintenance8`. All three are present on an RTX 4060 Ti (and an RTX 2060 SUPER), and `runtime/test/vk_harness.cpp` enables them when available, as does the runtime's `create_vulkan_device_standalone`, each with its extension (`VulkanDeviceInfo::fragment_shader_barycentric`, `fragment_shader_interlock`, `maintenance8`; checked by `d3d12_vulkan_device_test`). All three are present on an RTX 4060 Ti, and `runtime/test/vk_harness.cpp` enables them when available. (It is guarded by the header macro `VK_KHR_MAINTENANCE_8_EXTENSION_NAME`, so older Vulkan headers build without it. `vk_texture_test` enables it too, and `shaderImageGatherExtended`.) A device without `fragmentShaderBarycentric` (NVIDIA Pascal) runs the raw-barycentric PS with `--bary-fallback` (below). Shaders translated with `--fma khr` need `VK_KHR_shader_fma` and its `shaderFmaFloat32` feature (`VkPhysicalDeviceShaderFmaFeaturesKHR`): `create_vulkan_device_standalone`, `runtime/test/vk_test_device.h` and the `vk_*` test programs enable it when the device offers it (`VulkanDeviceInfo::shader_fma`), and a test program refuses (exit 77) a module with `OpCapability FMAKHR` on a device without it. |
| Wave size | **Most of the game's shaders are wave64: 23,841 of 26,503** (`fssm_header.wave_size()`: FSSM header flags word `u32[0xD4]` bit 5, `0x20` = wave32, `0` = wave64; 0 contradictions against GFX10 code-proven wave32/wave64 instructions across all 26,503 blobs): 10,974 of 12,101 PS, 9,282 of 9,282 mesh shaders, 2,236 of 2,236 VS, 1,154 of 2,689 CS and 195 of 195 GS, against 2,662 wave32 (1,535 CS and 1,127 PS). A full-corpus translation at `ec8ec4c9` marks exactly these 23,841 `wave64` (`subgroup_size` 64) in the index. Before the FSSM flag (#1900) the wave size came from code markers (64-bit EXEC/mask writes, `exec_hi`, `*_saveexec_b64`): at `6a6704df` they made 21,538 of the 26,487 shaders translated then wave64 (the figure quoted here was 21,545). 10,031 PS, 9,282 mesh shaders, 1,179 VS, 851 CS and 195 GS (the 16 shaders refused then are all wave64 CS by the flag; the figure quoted here was 21,545). Against the flag they call 2,287 wave64 shaders wave32 (943 PS, 1,057 VS, 287 CS), whose code has no 64-bit EXEC/mask marker, and none the other way. An older marker scan (`s_mov_b64 exec, -1`, `exec_hi`, `s_and/or_saveexec_b64` and 64-bit masks) counted 9,930 PS, 8,666 mesh shaders, 1,115 VS and 923 CS, but it left out the 769 listings whose names start with `.` (616 of them mesh shaders; Python's `glob` skips such names). Over all 26,503, the translator's own marker test (`WAVE64_RE`, or a 64-bit op on EXEC/VCC) on the code `translate()` keeps finds 21,554 wave64: 10,031 PS, 9,282 mesh shaders, 1,179 VS, 867 CS and 195 GS. With the 2,287 above that is exactly the flag's 23,841, and none contradicts it; read raw, 104 more wave32 listings (80 CS, 24 PS) seem to carry a marker, all from embedded data words decoded as instructions. The first marker scan (`s_mov_b64 exec, -1`, `exec_hi`, `s_and/or_saveexec_b64` and 64-bit masks, written into this row at `0082ad53`) counted 9,930 PS, 8,666 mesh shaders, 1,115 VS and 923 CS: 20,634 without the GS, so not the 21,545 total either. The translator maps a wave to a subgroup, and NVIDIA subgroups are 32 wide. What that means: (1) lane-independent code, which is most pixel-shader math, is exact; (2) wave-wide branch decisions and reductions are made per 32-lane half, which is exact unless scalar state computed for one half is later consumed by the other; (3) lane indices from `v_mbcnt_lo/hi` and wave IDs from the merged-wave SGPR must be 64-lane values. Mesh and compute shaders build thread IDs as wave_id·64 + mbcnt, so those need a mapping from `LocalInvocationIndex` (lane = index & 63, wave = index >> 6). Full fidelity needs either 64-wide subgroups (AMD GPUs, via `VK_EXT_subgroup_size_control` requiredSubgroupSize = 64) or emulating cross-half ops (ballot, readlane, shuffles across halves) through workgroup memory. |
| Mesh shaders | NGG wave64 programs → `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` (`MeshEXT`). **PSV0** (DXBC) gives NumThreads (128×1×1 in the corpus), MaxOutputVertices/Primitives (128/128) and the topology (triangles). System inputs, from Mesa's RADV setup for mesh as NGG on GFX10.3: v5 = first launch vertex ID of the lane (the "vertex_id" slot), `linearGroupIndex * NumThreads + LocalInvocationIndex` with the linear index from WorkgroupId/NumWorkgroups; all 9,282/9,282 corpus mesh shaders read `v5` once with `v_readfirstlane_b32 sX, v5` and use `sX` only via `s_lshr_b32 D, sX, 7` to recover the group index (NumThreads is 128 in the corpus), matching TomBox's mesh ABI (`gid.x * numThreads + gi`) on 1D launches. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ms_v5_workgroup.asm` (24/24 groups exact on RTX 4060 Ti and RTX 3080, and on an RTX 4050 Laptop and an AMD Radeon 740M (RADV)); still unverified on Xbox hardware: the low 7 bits of `v5` on lanes 1..127 and whether 3D `DispatchMesh` flattens XYZ or uses X only. The merged-wave-info SGPR (s3 in this ABI) = wave ID << 24, from LocalInvocationIndex >> 6; `v_mbcnt` lanes = LocalInvocationIndex & 63; user data in s16.. as usual. All 9,282 mesh-shader listings of 1.0.2.1 (names starting with `.` included) read exactly v5 before writing anything (`rdna2spirv.read_before_write_vgprs` over the code `translate()` keeps). An earlier sample of 1,500 put 27 aside as adding loop-carried registers; over the corpus no extra read is loop-carried. Read raw, 266 of the 309 listings with embedded data words seem to read more VGPRs, each after a branch (in 101 the register is written after the read); they come from data words decoded as instructions and are gone once the listing is re-anchored to the FSSM code and cut to reachable code, as `translate()` does. 1,473 of 1,500 sampled mesh shaders read exactly v5, and the rest only add loop-carried registers. Exports: on NGG, thread t exports vertex t (`pos0`, `paramN`) and primitive t (`prim`). They're staged per invocation; an all-`off` export is the dummy a wave with no vertices must issue, and writes nothing. `s_sendmsg GS_ALLOC_REQ` (m0 = prims << 12 \| verts, sent by thread 0) stores the counts in a workgroup variable. At shader end, in uniform control flow: workgroup barrier, `OpSetMeshOutputsEXT(min(verts, max), min(prims, max))`, then each thread writes its staged vertex (Position + Location N) and primitive (`PrimitiveTriangleIndicesEXT` = bits [8:0], [18:10], [28:20]; `CullPrimitiveEXT` = bit 31). `pos1..pos3` clip/cull distances are staged per invocation and emitted in a per-vertex `Block` output struct (`Position` + `ClipDistance`/`CullDistance` arrays, `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`) when used, keeping the standalone `%ms_out_pos` layout unchanged for shaders without `pos1+`. The distances are copied to that struct one float at a time, not as a whole array: SPIRV-Cross 1.4.363.0 turns a whole-array store into a mesh output into a C array assignment that Metal rejects (SHADER-155; `ms_clip` then compiles and draws its 2048/4096 pixels in a native Metal mesh draw on an Apple A18 Pro). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ms_tri.asm` (`VKH_MESH=1 vk_harness`), built from the game's own idioms (thread ID from s3 and mbcnt across two waves, thread-0 ALLOC_REQ, per-thread exports): 0 error on every pixel. |
| Mesh shaders as compute (`--mesh-compute`) | For devices without `VK_EXT_mesh_shader` (NVIDIA Pascal, for one), and on by itself in `make_game.py` on Intel's Windows Vulkan driver (`VK_DRIVER_ID_INTEL_PROPRIETARY_WINDOWS` with mesh shaders, `runtime_gpu_mesh_draws_crash`): there `vkCmdDrawMeshTasksEXT` crashes (0xC0000409 in `igvk64.dll`) for a mesh shader with a live push-constant load at byte 32 or later, which every corpus mesh shader has, while `--mesh-compute` is exact (BUILD-2202, Arc A770, driver 32.0.101.9175; no fixed version known; `--native-mesh` keeps native mesh shaders). Each mesh shader becomes a `GLCompute` module with the same workgroup size and code; only the epilogue changes. Instead of `OpSetMeshOutputsEXT` and the mesh outputs, after the same barrier lane 0 writes the vertex and primitive counts, lane i < vertex count writes its vertex and lane i < primitive count its primitive into this workgroup's **slot** of an output buffer: slot = linear workgroup index (x + y·nx + z·nx·ny) × `slot_bytes`, computed in 64 bits. The buffer's device address is in push-constant dwords 36–37, so these modules declare 152 bytes. Slot layout (`rdna2spirv.mesh_compute_layout`, recorded per shader as `mesh_compute` in the index), all 16-byte aligned: a header (vertex count, primitive count), then `max_vertices` vertices, each holding the position, the clip and then cull distances padded to whole vec4s, and one vec4 per param location; then `max_primitives` primitives of three vertex indices plus 1 if culled. The modules need no mesh capability, extension or `shaderClipDistance`. `tools/mesh_compute_vs.py` writes the vertex shader that draws the slots with a non-indexed `vkCmdDraw(groups × max_primitives × 3)`: vertex v is workgroup v / (3·max_primitives), primitive (v / 3) mod max_primitives, corner v mod 3. A primitive past the count, or one culled, gets one position outside the clip volume at all three corners and is clipped. Otherwise the corner's index, clamped to `max_vertices` − 1, selects the vertex, and its position, distances and params go out at the mesh shader's locations, so the pixel shader is unchanged. One draw rasterizes in vertex order, which is workgroup then primitive order, as with mesh workgroups. Corpus: all 9,282 mesh shaders are 128×1×1 with 128/128 outputs, wave64, 0–18 params, clip distances in 1,906, cull distances and per-primitive attributes in none, no task shaders. On a 32-wide device combine it with `--wave64-emulate`. GPU-checked on an NVIDIA GeForce GTX 1060 6GB (Pascal: no mesh shaders; driver 560.94, Windows 10) with `LEONIDA_MESH_COMPUTE=1 LEONIDA_W64_EMULATE=1`: `ms_tri`, `ms_w64x_ballot` and `ms_wave_info_s3` 4096/4096 pixels exact, `ms_clip` 2048/4096 covered as expected, `ms_v5_workgroup` 24/24 workgroup coordinates, zero Khronos validation messages. Checked in `tests/test_mesh_compute.py`. The runtime side, turning a `DispatchMesh` into that dispatch, a barrier and the draw, is the follow-up (it needs the graphics pipelines of #291). Unused primitives still run the vertex shader; compaction with an indirect draw would avoid that. |
| Geometry shaders | On output stream zero, `GS_OP_EMIT_CUT` emits the staged vertex and ends its strip under the same EXEC guard. The synthetic `gs_emit_cut.asm` checks that six vertices form two separate triangles. [RDNA 2 ISA, S_SENDMSG](https://docs.amd.com/api/khub/documents/Et~wpu9g~Ffl7d9q0QZ~Og/content). All 195 are `$GS_VoxelizeGeneric` permutations: legacy (merged ES/GS) wave32 programs on LDS rings, with no `exp`. All 195 are `$GS_VoxelizeGeneric` permutations: legacy (merged ES/GS) programs on LDS rings, with no `exp`, written for wave64 (the FSSM header's wave-size flag says wave64 in 195 of 195, see *Wave size*, and the translator's index marks all 195 `wave64` with `subgroup_size` 64 at `f847748c`; the code agrees: the EXEC prologue that `gs_tri.asm` mirrors compares the thread count with 64 and writes a 64-bit EXEC, the count in #94 finds `v_mbcnt_hi_u32_b32` in all 195, and the translator marks all 195 `wave64`; an earlier version of this row said wave32, TEST-251). Inputs: vertex i sits at the dword offset in v0 lo / v0 hi / v1 lo; an LDS read at vertex base + 4·(4·reg + comp) is input-signature register reg, component comp (checked: SV_Position xyz in dwords 0–2 and TEXCOORD0.xy in 4–5, as ISG1 declares). Outputs: emitted vertex k is dwords [k·4·nreg, (k+1)·4·nreg) of the lane's GSVS area, dword = 4·reg + comp of the output signature (20 dwords = 5 OSG1 registers; every write matches a declared mask). Translation: synthetic offsets (vertex i at dword 64·i), LDS reads → `gl_in[i]` inputs, LDS writes → staged slots, `MSG_GS EMIT` → copy slot (emit count) to the outputs + `OpEmitVertex`, `CUT` → `OpEndPrimitive`, `GS_DONE` → nothing. The prologue's `s_bfm_b64`/`s_cselect` EXEC setup is fed s3 = 64 << 8. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/gs_tri.asm` (`VKH_GS=… vk_harness`): 0 error. Without a geometry stage (Apple GPUs: Metal, MoltenVK), `tools/spirv_gs_to_compute.py` lowers the translated module to a compute capture pass, one invocation per input primitive: inputs come from a buffer, each `OpEmitVertex` guards its record/meta stores and `%gsc_n` increment on `n < OutputVertices` (dropping excess vertices rather than overflowing into the next primitive's slots, and updating `OpPhi` predecessors across split blocks, and rejecting (`Unsupported`) an `OpEmitVertex` in a loop header before its `OpLoopMerge`, which the split would move; SHADER-617, checked on the CPU by `tests/test_spirv_gs_to_compute_bounds.py`), and a meta buffer holds each primitive's vertex count (`<= OutputVertices`) and strip ends, for an ordinary draw afterwards (SHADER-159). Metal, MoltenVK), `tools/spirv_gs_to_compute.py` lowers the translated module to a compute capture pass, one invocation per input primitive: inputs come from a buffer, each `OpEmitVertex` writes the outputs to a record buffer, and a meta buffer holds each primitive's vertex count and strip ends, for an ordinary draw afterwards (SHADER-159). `gs_tri` lowered this way, converted with SPIRV-Cross and run natively on an Apple A18 Pro: 195/195 records exact for 65 random triangles, and the captured fullscreen triangle drawn from the records matches all 4096 gradient pixels. Not done: wiring it into a draw path, and GS that use cross-lane operations or more than one invocation. |
| Integer clamp | The VOP3 `clamp` bit on `v_add_nc_u32`, `v_sub_nc_u32`, `v_subrev_nc_u32` saturates the result to [0, 0xFFFFFFFF], and on `v_add_nc_i32`/`v_sub_nc_i32` to [0x80000000, 0x7FFFFFFF]; without it they wrap. GFX9+ semantics: LLVM selects `uaddsat`/`usubsat` and `saddsat`/`ssubsat` to exactly these ops with clamp set. **Until this change clamp was silently ignored on the unsigned ops** (and the signed ones were `Unsupported`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_alu_add_nc_clamp.py`. The carry-out unsigned ops (`v_add_co_u32`, `v_sub_co_u32`, `v_subrev_co_u32`, `v_add_co_ci_u32`, `v_sub_co_ci_u32`, `v_subrev_co_ci_u32`) with VOP3 `clamp` likewise saturate D to `0xFFFFFFFF` on add carry or `0` on sub borrow while still reporting the real carry/borrow bit in `sdst` (**until 2026-11-22 the translator ignored `clamp` on all six**); confirmed natively on RDNA2 (AMD Radeon 680M `gfx1035` wave32 & wave64 via `tools/hw_oracle`, and independently on `gfx1036` in #1746) and CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_add_co_clamp.py`. The 16-bit unsigned add/sub (`v_add_nc_u16`, `v_sub_nc_u16`, and the `v_add_u16`/`v_sub_u16`/`v_subrev_u16` `_e64` names) saturate to [0, 0xFFFF] with `clamp` (llvm-mc 21 gfx1030 encodes the bit on them, and LLVM selects i16 `uaddsat`/`usubsat` to them); **until 2026-11-22 the translator wrapped instead**. `clamp` on any other 16-bit integer op raises `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_u16_clamp.py` (the high half of the destination is kept), **not GPU-verified**.
