CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_u16_clamp.py` (the high half of the destination is kept), and **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon 680M, `gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137): all 32-bit and 16-bit `*_nc_*` `clamp` cases (including `op_sel` and kept destination half on u16) match hardware bit-for-bit. **Open divergence measured on the same hardware:** `v_add_co_u32`, `v_sub_co_u32`, `v_subrev_co_u32`, `v_add_co_ci_u32`, `v_sub_co_ci_u32` and `v_subrev_co_ci_u32` with `clamp` saturate D (`0xFFFFFFFF` / `0`) while still reporting the real carry/borrow in `sdst`, whereas the translator currently ignores `clamp` on these carry-out ops. |
| Push-constant tail as a uniform block (`--pc-tail-ubo`) | For devices whose `maxPushConstantsSize` is 128, Vulkan's minimum, which can't take the 144-byte block (152 with `--mesh-compute`): AMD's older Windows drivers on RDNA2 iGPUs (Radeon 680M on 22.40.84.06, 610M on 24.10.16.14, the Ryzen 5 7600's iGPU on 24.10.24; a 610M on 26.11.2 reports 256) and some Intel Arc drivers (D3D-14, `push_constants_need_fallback` in `d3d12_vulkan_device.cpp`). The push-constant block keeps dwords 0–31 (s0–s31, 128 bytes). The tail that follows them by default, the heap base and code base and, with `--mesh-compute`, the output address, becomes `%pc_tail`: a uniform block at **set 1, binding 0** with the same members in the same order at offsets 0, 4, … (16 bytes, 24 with `--mesh-compute`). Set 1 is otherwise unused, so set 0's update-after-bind table doesn't change. The block is declared, and listed in the entry point's interface, only when the shader reads the tail (a memory access through the heap base, `s_getpc_b64`, or the `--mesh-compute` epilogue); the index then records `"pc_tail": {"set": 1, "binding": 0, "bytes": 16}`, and `"push_constant_bytes"` is 128 for every module. `"bindings"` still lists set 0 only. Without the flag nothing changes. **Checked:** `tests/test_pc_tail_ubo.py`; all 185 hand-written GPU test shaders pass `spirv-val` with the flag (109 of them read the tail) with set-0 bindings unchanged. The GPU harnesses and the D3D12 layer that bind the block are the follow-ups. |
| `v_mad_u32_u16` / `v_mad_i32_i16` op_sel on S2 and D, and clamp | D = S0.half · S1.half + S2, the halves zero- (u16) or sign-extended (i16), op_sel bit 0 / 1 reading the high half of S0 / S1. The other bits and `clamp` were refused (`Unsupported: op_sel/clamp`) until their results were measured on RDNA2 (AMD Radeon iGPU, gfx1036, Windows driver 26.10.1, raw kernels through `tools/hw_oracle`, wave32 and wave64 alike; EGEN-HW-MAD-U32-U16, 44 words): **op_sel bit 2 adds S2[31:16] zero-extended, in the i16 op too** (`v_mad_i32_i16 d, 3, 3, 0xffff8000 op_sel:[0,0,1,0]` gives `0x00010008`, not 8); an inline float constant there is its f32 pattern (`1.0` adds `0x3f80`); **op_sel bit 3 writes the result shifted left by 16** (low half 0, the old D not kept); **`clamp` saturates the 32-bit sum** to [0, 0xffffffff] (u16) or [0x80000000, 0x7fffffff] (i16), before that shift (the 16-bit product itself never overflows). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_valu16_mad_sel.asm` (4096/4096 on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080, driver 616.92, and the gfx1036 iGPU); CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_mad_32_16.py` (40 seeded cases against a Python reference) and `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`. |
| `v_add_nc_i16`, `v_sub_nc_i16` | GFX10 VOP3-only signed 16-bit add and subtract. Without `clamp` they are bit-identical to `v_add_nc_u16`/`v_sub_nc_u16` (the low 16 bits wrap) and take the same path: `op_sel:[s0,s1,dst]` picks each source's half and the destination half, and the other half of D is kept. With `clamp`, the exact sum or difference of the sign-extended halves saturates to [-32768, 32767] (`SClamp`), where the u16 forms saturate to [0, 0xFFFF]. RDNA2 (GFX10.3) dropped reversed 16-bit integer subtraction (`v_subrev_nc_u16` / `v_subrev_nc_i16` do not exist on hardware; `v_subrev_f16` is the only 16-bit reverse subtract opcode). Checked by `tests/semantic/test_valu_add_sub_nc_i16.py` (a value grid with and without `clamp`, `op_sel`, literals, SGPR sources, and untouched half preservation) and **Hardware-verified on AMD RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py`, `valu_add_nc_clamp/v_add_sub_nc_i16_clamp`): signed saturation to [-32768, 32767], `op_sel` source selection, and untouched destination half preservation match hardware bit-for-bit. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_valu_add_sub_nc_i16.py` (a value grid with and without `clamp`, `op_sel` including the destination half, and equality with the u16 forms without `clamp`). **GPU-verified** on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 7 9800X3D, Windows driver 32.0.21042.62) through `tools/hw_oracle`, one-thread raw kernels: 112 of 112 directed cases (both ops; 0x7FFF+1, 0x8000+1, 0x8000+0xFFFF, 0x7FFF+0x7FFF, 0x8000+0x8000 and others, each with and without `clamp` and with `op_sel` `[0,0,0]`, `[1,0,1]`, `[0,1,0]` and `[1,1,1]`, the destination preset to 0xA5A5A5A5). For example `v_add_nc_i16(0x7FFF, 1)` gives 0x8000 wrapped and 0x7FFF with `clamp`, `v_sub_nc_i16(0x8000, 1) clamp` gives 0x8000, and the destination half `op_sel` doesn't pick keeps its 0xA5A5. `tools/pk16_diff.py --oracle translator,hw --ops v_add_nc_i16,v_sub_nc_i16 --programs 3000 --seed 1`: 3000 of 3000 programs agree with the hardware and the translator. **Not GPU-verified.** Measured on native gfx1100: `clamp` saturates the exact sign-extended sum to [-32768, 32767] and the other half of D is kept. |
| Gather sampler LOD | Before choosing the mip for the four fetches in `image_gather4_l(_o)`, clamp the requested LOD to S# word 1's MIN_LOD [11:0] and MAX_LOD [23:12] (unsigned 4.8). `ps_gather_sampler_lod.asm` reads the S# from the heap. On RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics, a maximum of 0.25 selects the base quadrants and a minimum of 0.75 selects the cyan mip across the 64×64 draw. Without these bounds half of each frame reads the wrong level. Radeon also agreed with native `OpImageGather Lod` (`VK_AMD_texture_gather_bias_lod`) for those bounds. At exactly 0.5, with bounds [0,1], that native draw selected mip 1; the emulation now computes the mip as `Floor(LOD + 0.5)`, ties up, and also selects mip 1 (before SHADER-152 it used `RoundEven`, which selected mip 0). It computes `floor(LOD) + (LOD - floor(LOD) >= 0.5)`, which is exact in binary32, not `Floor(LOD + 0.5)`: that sum rounds `0x3effffff` up to 1.0, and on an RTX 5070 (621.24) the gather then read mip 1 where native `image_sample_l` at the same LOD reads mip 0. |
| `s_absdiff_i32`, `s_mul_hi_i32` | `s_absdiff_i32`: D = \|S0 − S1\| with the 32-bit difference wrapped first (0x7FFFFFFF − 0x80000000 → 1; a difference of −2³¹ stays 0x80000000), SCC = D ≠ 0. `s_mul_hi_i32`: high word of the signed 64-bit product, SCC kept. From the RDNA2 ISA pseudocode; checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_salu_absdiff_mul_hi.py`. **Hardware-verified** on an AMD Radeon RX 7900 XTX (gfx1100, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1) as raw wave32 kernels through `tools/hw_oracle`: `s_absdiff_i32(0x7FFFFFFF, 0x80000000)` = 1 and `s_absdiff_i32(0x80000000, 0)` = 0x80000000, both with SCC = 1, and equal operands give 0 with SCC = 0; `s_mul_hi_i32(0x7FFFFFFF, 0x7FFFFFFF)` = 0x3FFFFFFF and `s_mul_hi_i32(-1, 1)` = 0xFFFFFFFF, with SCC left as it was. Also verified on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, AMD Radeon Graphics): 500 programs in `tools/salu_diff.py` (`--oracle translator,hw`) matched bit-for-bit with 0 mismatches, and the high product word and SCC retention confirmed across edge values. |
| SCC from scalar ALU | `s_and/or/xor/andn2/orn2/nand/nor/xnor_b32` and `s_lshl/lshr_b32` set SCC = result ≠ 0, `s_add/sub_i32` and `s_addk_i32` set it to signed overflow, `s_min_u32`/`s_max_u32` to S0 < S1 / S0 > S1, and `s_min_i32`/`s_max_i32` to the same compared signed: SCC is 1 when S0 is the one chosen, and a tie chooses S1 (RDNA2 ISA, S_MIN_I32: D = S0 < S1 ? S0 : S1, SCC = 1 if S0 is chosen). **Before this fix these ops left SCC unchanged**, so a following `s_cbranch_scc*` read a stale flag, and until 2026-11-21 `s_min_i32`/`s_max_i32` set SCC on a tie. 32-bit scalar shifts now mask the amount to 5 bits. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_scc.asm` and `ps_scc_minmax.asm` (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2060 SUPER, Linux, driver 620.70.06: 4096/4096 pixels exact, and all 4096 differ with the old translator). **On lane masks in a wave32 program** (a source is EXEC, VCC or a mask SGPR, or the result goes to EXEC) the 32-bit bitwise ops' result is the whole wave's mask, so SCC is a subgroup vote (`OpGroupNonUniformAny`), as for the `_b64` forms. Until 2026-11-22 each lane took its own bit: `s_and_b32 s0, vcc_lo, exec_lo; s_cbranch_scc0` skipped the block in lanes whose bit was 0, and a loop closed by `s_andn2_b32 exec_lo, ...; s_cbranch_scc1` let each lane leave early. Found and checked by `tools/cf_diff.py` (FUZZ-8, CPU differential against the ISA model); GPU test `runtime/test/shaders/ps_scc_mask32.asm` passes (s5 == 8 on 2,048 pixels, 192 of them with bit 0, and s5 == 7 on 2,048) on an NVIDIA RTX 4050 Laptop (Linux, driver 620.73.04, natively and under `LEONIDA_W64_EMULATE=1`) and an AMD Radeon 740M (RADV, Mesa 26.3.1), and on an RTX 2060 SUPER per #634. A 32-bit op on one half of a wave64 mask still takes the lane's bit (its SCC would be a vote of that half). |
| Chained scalar constant folding (PERF) | Multi-step constant calculations on SGPRs (`s_mov_b32`, `s_lshl_b32`, `s_lshr_b32`, `s_mul_i32`, `s_add_u32`, `s_sub_u32`, `s_lshl1..4_add_u32`, `s_bfe_u32/i32`, `s_min/max_u32/i32`, `s_andn2/orn2/nand/nor/xnor_b32`, `s_abs_i32`, `s_absdiff_i32`, `s_sext_i32_i8/i16`, `s_pack_*_b32_b16`, `s_bfm_b32`) fold completely at translation time and record folded literal values in `self.sgpr_imm`, allowing downstream instructions within the basic block to propagate constants. `s_buffer_load` offset expressions computed via constant SALU fold statically into 64-bit addresses, and in-bounds reads eliminate runtime bounds check branches and Phi nodes. `s_bfe` with constant control words emits static offset/width operands directly. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_chained_const_prop.py` and `tests/semantic/test_scalar_alu_and_smem_folding.py`. |
| Far branches | `s_getpc_b64; s_add_u32 K; s_addc_u32 0/−1; s_setpc_b64` on the same pair is a branch beyond `s_branch`'s ±32K-dword range. It's folded into one branch to (address after `s_getpc`) + K before CFG construction. The `s_sub_u32 K; s_subb_u32 0` form is the backward jump (a loop back-edge), as is `s_add_u32 -K; s_addc_u32 -1`. K is a byte distance, so the fold needs the instructions' `; encoding:` bytes; a listing without them is `Unsupported` ("without encoding"), not a crash. GPU-checked by `ps_far_branch.asm` (two forward jumps, a `sub`/`subb` loop, an `add`/`addc -1` loop and a conditional one run twice, K and the expected words derived by hand; RTX 5080 and Radeon gfx1036, 4096/4096 exact), CPU side `tests/semantic/test_far_branch.py`. |
| No-op SOPP/SMEM (`s_icache_inv`, `s_ttracedata`, `s_ttracedata_imm`, `s_code_end`, `s_version`, `ds_nop`, `v_clrexcp`) | Instruction-cache invalidate, thread-trace data, end-of-code padding, the ISA version marker and the DS no-operation have no register, memory, SCC, VCC or EXEC effect, so they are in `NOOPS` with `s_nop` / `v_nop` / `v_pipeflush`. `v_clrexcp` clears the TRAPSTS exception bits, which nothing translated reads (`s_getreg_b32` is unsupported and there are no traps), so it is a no-op too (since 2026-11-22; before, both it and `ds_nop` raised `Unsupported`). The data-cache invalidations (`buffer_gl0_inv`, `buffer_gl1_inv`, `s_dcache_inv`, `s_gl1_inv`) concern coherence between waves and stay `Unsupported`. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOPP/SOPK/SMEM/DS/VOP1. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_sopp_noops.py` (values, SCC and VCC survive; the data-cache invalidations are still rejected). |
| Buffer format stores | `buffer_store_format_*` and `_d16_*` encode every format the loads decode (the same FORMATS table: f32/u32, f16, unorm/snorm 8/16/10-10-10-2, uint/sint 8/16, and the formats of the *Typed buffer formats* row). 1–2-byte elements are merged into their dword with atomic and/or so neighbours are kept. The atomics use element 0 of the array at the aligned dword address, which also compiles on MoltenVK 1.4.0 (checked with `cs_store` on an Apple M3 Max and Mesa llvmpipe). d16 data is unpacked first (f16 → float for float kinds, 16-bit integers for integer kinds). Before this, only four f32 formats, rgba16f and rgba8 unorm were stored, and everything else silently stored nothing. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_store.asm` (rgba8 unorm, R10G10B10A2 (`2_10_10_10`), two r16f halves of one dword, d16 rgba16f: exact, neighbours untouched). `buffer_load_format_d16_*` packs pairs the same way. UNORM/SNORM stores convert NaN to zero before clamping. On native gfx1036, `buffer_store_format_d16_xyzw` into RGBA8_UNORM/SNORM matched clamp, scale by 255/127 and round to nearest even for all 63,488 finite binary16 encodings. Both infinities saturated and all 2,046 NaNs stored zero in both wave32 and wave64. The translated sweep matched all 65,536 bytes per format on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics. For normalized stores with format 56 (8/8/8/8) or 50 (10/10/10/2), a Z,Y,X,W descriptor exchanges the red and blue memory fields after filling absent input components with zero. Native gfx1036 confirmed both channel orders at all four instruction widths for packed and ordinary stores in wave32 and wave64. A store with fewer components than its format writes the whole element, with 0 for the components it doesn't give (`_xy` into 32_32_32_32 leaves z = w = 0, `_xyz` into 16_16_16_16 leaves w = 0); into a one-channel format only x is written. Measured natively on a Radeon 680M (gfx1035, RDNA2), and GPU-checked by `cs_fmt_xy_xyz.asm` for 32_32_32_32, 16_16_16_16, 8_8_8_8 UNORM and UINT and 32 (TEST-185). Unsigned integer stores saturate to the destination channel width. On native gfx1036, RGBA8_UINT, RG16_UINT and RGBA16_UINT clipped overflowing inputs to 255 or 65535, with RGBA32_UINT as a full-width control. For normalized stores with format 56 (8/8/8/8) or 50 (10/10/10/2), a Z,Y,X,W descriptor exchanges the red and blue memory fields after filling absent input components with zero; native gfx1036 confirmed both channel orders at all four instruction widths for packed and ordinary stores in wave32 and wave64. `tbuffer_store_format_*` is not translated. 8- and 16-bit SINT stores clamp the signed 32-bit value to [-2^(w-1), 2^(w-1) - 1] (300 stores 127 into 8_SINT, -129 stores -128), and the 8/16-bit UINT formats 5, 11 and 18 saturate like the others; measured natively on gfx1036 for x, xy, xyzw and d16_xyzw stores (a d16 half is read as a signed 16-bit integer for SINT, so 0xff80 stores -128) with the runtime's selectors (X001, XY01, XYZW) and checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_format_narrow_int.py` (until 2026-11-22 those formats stored nothing). `tbuffer_store_format_*` and its `_d16_*` forms take the instruction's format instead of the V#'s, as the tbuffer loads do: only that format is encoded, and its element size decides the bounds check (an 8_UNORM byte is stored where the V#'s 16-byte element would not fit; a 16-byte element is dropped where the V#'s 4-byte one would fit). A V# whose FORMAT is 0 (INVALID) still disables the store. No `format:` modifier is GFX10's default, BUF_FMT_8_UNORM; a format FORMATS has no encoder for stays `Unsupported`. Measured natively on a Radeon 680M (gfx1035, wave32 = wave64) and GPU-checked by `cs_tbuffer_store.asm` (13 stores: f32, f16, 8_8_8_8 UINT and UNORM, 2_10_10_10, d16, the default format, more and fewer components than the format has, both bounds cases, a V# with FORMAT 0; exact on an RTX 3050 and the 680M); CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_tbuffer_store.py`. |
| Typed buffer formats | `Gen.FORMATS` holds all 77 GFX10 codes a V# or MTBUF can carry (a buffer's `FORMAT` is 7 bits; codes from 128 are image-only, and there are no 5_6_5, 1_5_5_5, 5_5_5_1 or 4_4_4_4 buffer formats): the 8/16-bit UINT, SINT, USCALED and SSCALED families (USCALED/SSCALED: the integer converted to float, not divided), 10_11_11 and 11_11_10 in all seven numeric formats (the FLOAT ones are unsigned floats: 5 exponent bits, bias 15, 6 or 5 mantissa bits, no sign; the first number is the top field, so 36 has x in bits 10:0 and a 10-bit z on top), and the 10_10_10_2 (44–49) and 2_10_10_10 (50–55) families. Codes outside the table decode to 0 and store nothing. **Stores**: UINT and SINT saturate; UNORM and SNORM round the exact product once (`Gen.encode_norm_bits`: 0.3 · 65535 stores 19661; nearest even, but a half rounds away from zero in a 2-bit SNORM field); a SCALED store converts as the matching NORM format; float stores (16_FLOAT and the small floats) **round toward zero, saturate to the largest finite value (65504), keep a NaN a NaN and give 0 for a negative number in an unsigned float** (`Gen.ufloat_encode_bits`); **a two-component format stored through a V# takes channel y as its second component when DST_SEL_Y is Y and DST_SEL_Z a constant (the runtime's X,Y,0,1), channel w otherwise (identity XYZW: `_xy` writes (x, 0))**, and `tbuffer_store_format_*` takes (x, y) whatever the V# says. The typed-store encode is emitted once per module (`Gen.format_encode_call`: one encode function and one element-size function, each store site a call, like the shared decode), because inlined every site carried every format. **How confirmed**: `tools/buffer_format_fuzz.py --oracle hw` runs raw `buffer_load_format_xyzw` / `buffer_store_format_xyzw` / `_xy` / `tbuffer_*_format_*` kernels and compares every result word with a CPU model (a distinct value in every register). **Radeon 880M (gfx1150, RDNA3.5; GFX11 numbers its formats differently, so formats are mapped by name)**: 1,390,400 elements over the 63 formats GFX11 has, 0 mismatches. **Real RDNA2, gfx1036, by @AwkGrep256 on the Linux DRM backend ([#2022](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/2022))**: the same totals, 1,330,469 validated and 26,523 elements of the (x, w) routing, 0 mismatches, and 199,144 elements of the 14 codes GFX11 lacks (10_11_11 and 11_11_10 non-FLOAT, 10_10_10_2 USCALED/SSCALED), 0 mismatches. Per-format counts in [buffer_formats.md](buffer_formats.md); `--negative-control` catches a wrong V# format on all but one or two elements. The translated SPIR-V is held to the same model by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_formats_all.py` (every added format, loads through the V# and tbuffer, stores, every older format's store, hand-worked values) and by the GPU test `cs_fmt_all.asm` (32 loads and 20 stores, on an AMD Radeon 880M and an NVIDIA RTX 5070); `cs_store`, `cs_fmt_*`, `cs_oob_*` and `cs_tbuffer_store` pass on both too. Three older stores used to differ from the hardware (f16 format stores rounded to nearest and overflowed to infinity, NORM stores rounded an f32 product twice, a two-component V# store always took (x, y)) and follow it now. **Unmeasured**: the console's gfx1013, d16 forms of the new kinds and of the (x, w) routing, `_xyz` into a two-component format, selector sets that reorder channels. |
| No-op SOPP/SMEM (`s_icache_inv`, `s_ttracedata`, `s_ttracedata_imm`, `s_code_end`, `s_version`, `ds_nop`, `v_clrexcp`, `s_atc_probe`, `s_atc_probe_buffer`) | Instruction-cache invalidate, thread-trace data, end-of-code padding, the ISA version marker and the DS no-operation have no register, memory, SCC, VCC or EXEC effect, so they are in `NOOPS` with `s_nop` / `v_nop` / `v_pipeflush`. `v_clrexcp` clears the TRAPSTS exception bits, which nothing translated reads (`s_getreg_b32` is unsupported and there are no traps), so it is a no-op too (since 2026-11-22; before, both it and `ds_nop` raised `Unsupported`). `s_atc_probe` and `s_atc_probe_buffer` (SMEM) probe, that is prefetch, an address in the address translation cache: no data comes back and nothing is written, so they are no-ops too (since 2026-11-22). The data-cache invalidations (`buffer_gl0_inv`, `buffer_gl1_inv`, `s_dcache_inv`, `s_gl1_inv`) concern coherence between waves and stay `Unsupported`. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOPP/SOPK/SMEM/DS/VOP1. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_sopp_noops.py` (values, SCC and VCC survive; the data-cache invalidations are still rejected). |
| No-op SOPP/SMEM (`s_icache_inv`, `s_ttracedata`, `s_ttracedata_imm`, `s_code_end`, `s_version`, `ds_nop`, `v_clrexcp`, `s_atc_probe`, `s_atc_probe_buffer`, `s_incperflevel`, `s_decperflevel`, `s_wakeup`) | Instruction-cache invalidate, thread-trace data, end-of-code padding, the ISA version marker and the DS no-operation have no register, memory, SCC, VCC or EXEC effect, so they are in `NOOPS` with `s_nop` / `v_nop` / `v_pipeflush`. `v_clrexcp` clears the TRAPSTS exception bits, which nothing translated reads (`s_getreg_b32` is unsupported and there are no traps), so it is a no-op too (since 2026-11-22; before, both it and `ds_nop` raised `Unsupported`). `s_atc_probe` and `s_atc_probe_buffer` (SMEM) probe, that is prefetch, an address in the address translation cache: no data comes back and nothing is written, so they are no-ops too (since 2026-11-22). `s_incperflevel` and `s_decperflevel` increment or decrement a performance counter (RDNA2 ISA SOPP, SIMM16[3:0]) and `s_wakeup` pings the other waves of the threadgroup out of an `s_sleep`, which is itself a no-op here; none writes a register, memory, SCC, VCC or EXEC, so they are no-ops too (since 2026-11-22). The data-cache invalidations (`buffer_gl0_inv`, `buffer_gl1_inv`, `s_dcache_inv`, `s_gl1_inv`) concern coherence between waves and stay `Unsupported`. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOPP/SOPK/SMEM/DS/VOP1. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_sopp_noops.py` (values, SCC and VCC survive; the data-cache invalidations are still rejected). |
| Buffer format store rounding and 2-component `DST_SEL` (SHADER-364) | UNORM/SNORM stores (`Gen.encode_norm_bits`) round the exact product `clamp(x) * scale` once in 64-bit integer arithmetic with ties away from zero, instead of double-rounding through `OpFMul` + `RoundEven` (`UNORM16(0.3)` stores 19661, not 19660; `SNORM2(±0.5)` stores ±1). MUBUF `buffer_store_format_*` (and `_d16_*`) into 2-component formats infer `COMP_SWAP` from V# `DST_SEL_X/Y/Z`: `use_y = (sy >= 4) and (sz < 4)`, `rev = (sx != 4) and (sy >= 4 or sz >= 4)` (`XY01` → `(x, y)`, `YX01` → `(y, x)`, `XYZW` → `(x, w)` so `_xy` writes `(x, 0)`, `YXZW`/`ZYXW` → `(w, x)`); MTBUF `tbuffer_store_format_*` ignores `DST_SEL` and always stores `(x, y)`. Measured natively on RDNA2 (`gfx1036`, AMD Ryzen 7 9800X3D iGPU, Linux AMDGPU DRM via `tools/hw_oracle`, wave32 and wave64) with `tools/buffer_store_probe.py`: 0 mismatches across 5 UNORM/SNORM formats (2,474 inputs × MUBUF/MTBUF × wave32/64 = 9,896 runs) + SNORM2 ties, all 12 2-component formats (`XY01` vs `XYZW`), and all 216 `(sx, sy, sz)` triples on `32_32_UINT`. Checked on CPU + `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_store_rdna2.py` and `tests/test_buffer_store_probe.py`. |
| `_d16_hi_` typed buffer forms (SHADER-755) | Only the single-component forms are instructions. `buffer_load_format_d16_hi_x` and `buffer_store_format_d16_hi_x` are the `_x` forms of the d16 load and store with their one component in bits 31:16 instead of 15:0; #1885 (SHADER-320) adds them. The multi-component spellings (`buffer_load_format_d16_hi_xy`, `_xyz`, `_xyzw`, and `buffer_store_format_d16_hi_*`) name no RDNA instruction and stay `Unsupported`: AMD's machine-readable [RDNA1 and RDNA2 ISA XML](isa_coverage.md) lists `BUFFER_LOAD_FORMAT_D16_HI_X` and `BUFFER_STORE_FORMAT_D16_HI_X` (MUBUF opcodes 0x026 and 0x027) as the only format D16 high-half instructions, and the packed widths `BUFFER_*_FORMAT_D16_X` to `_XYZW` (0x080-0x087) have no high-half counterpart; LLVM's gfx10 to gfx12 MUBUF tables define the same two, and the console's gfx1013 decodes through the gfx10 table, so no listing can carry one (the 25,735-shader corpus has none, reported in #4084). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_format_d16_hi_absent.py`: every multi-component spelling raises `Unsupported`, and the packed forms translate as a control. |
| `s_lshl1..4_add_u32` | D = (S0 << N) + S1. SCC = the sum taken in 64 bits is ≥ 2³², so a bit shifted out of S0 sets it as well as a carry out of the add (RDNA ISA pseudocode: `tmp = (64'U(S0) << N) + 64'U(S1)`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_salu_lshl_add.py`. **Hardware-verified** on the same gfx1100 part through `tools/hw_oracle`: `s_lshl1_add_u32(0x80000000, 0)` = 0 with SCC = 1 (the bit shifted out of S0 sets it, with no carry out of the add), `s_lshl2_add_u32(5, 3)` = 0x17 with SCC = 0, `s_lshl4_add_u32(1, 0)` = 0x10. Also verified on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, AMD Radeon Graphics): 500 programs in `tools/salu_diff.py` (`--oracle translator,hw`) matched bit-for-bit with 0 mismatches across shift amounts 1–4, confirming that bits shifted out of S0 set SCC as well as sum overflow. |
| Other additions | `image_gather4_l(_o)`: no explicit-LOD gather in core Vulkan, so four `OpImageFetch` at the mip the "Gather sampler LOD" row chooses, in gather order. `image_gather4_l(_o)`: no explicit-LOD gather in core Vulkan, so four `OpImageFetch` at mip `Floor(clamp(LOD, S# MIN_LOD, MAX_LOD) + 0.5)` (ties up, as native) in gather order. With `d16` those four taps round to binary16 and pack low half first into two consecutive VGPRs. The packed words and the first untouched VGPR were checked with `ps_gather_wrap.asm` on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics. Each of the four texel coordinates goes through the S#'s own address mode, read from S# word0 (`CLAMP_X` = bits [2:0], `CLAMP_Y` = [5:3]; fields from Mesa `src/amd/registers/gfx10-rsrc.json`, values `SQ_TEX_*` in Mesa `gfx6.json`, matching RADV's `radv_tex_wrap` mapping of Vulkan address modes): 0 wrap (`c mod n`), 1 mirror (`c mod 2n`, reflected), 2 clamp to the last texel, 3 mirror-once (`-1-c` for negative `c`, then clamped), as in Vulkan's address-mode definitions. The modulo is `OpSRem` plus the period when the remainder is negative, not `OpSMod`. On a Ryzen 5 7600's iGPU (RDNA2) with AMD's Windows driver 24.10.24, `OpSMod` of a negative coordinate came out with the dividend's sign: the three `ps_gather_wrap` cases whose wrap or mirror coordinate is negative (1, 2 and 6) fetched outside the image and read zeros, while the same module passed on an RTX 4070 SUPER. With `OpSRem` and the fix-up, all 4096 pixels are exact on both. A literal S# (`s_mov_b32` earlier in the block) is decoded at translation time; otherwise the mode is read from the SGPR at run time. Modes 6 (clamp border) and 7 (mirror once border) give a tap whose coordinate, before the clamp, is outside [0, n-1] on either axis the S# border colour's channel instead of the fetched texel: BORDER_COLOR_TYPE = word 3 [31:30] (Mesa `gfx10-rsrc.json` `SQ_IMG_SAMP_WORD3`, `SQ_TEX_BORDER_COLOR_*` in `gfx6.json`), 0 transparent black, 1 opaque black (alpha 1), 2 opaque white; 1 for an integer format, as Vulkan's `INT_OPAQUE_*`. Type 3 (a colour from the border-colour table, `BORDER_COLOR_PTR`) is `Unsupported` on a literal S# and transparent black at run time (SHADER-348: since #1567 made the game's S# words literals, 16 of the corpus's mode-6 gathers failed as `Unsupported`; before that they ran as clamp-to-edge). A literal S# with a half-border mode (4, 5) or `FORCE_UNNORMALIZED` is `Unsupported`; a run-time S# can't be judged, so modes 4 and 5 act as clamp-to-edge there and `FORCE_UNNORMALIZED` (word0 bit 15) is not applied (coordinates are still scaled by the image size). Those two, and a table border colour (BORDER_COLOR_TYPE 3) on a run-time S#, which gives transparent black, are the remaining approximations; the unnormalized rules (texel-unit coordinates, mip 0) weren't confirmed on hardware, so they were not guessed. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_gather_wrap.asm` (`vk_texture_test`; expected values derived by hand in its header; six border cases, three literal and three run-time S#) on AMD (Radeon 880M, wave64) and NVIDIA RTX 5070; the border modes with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_gather_border.asm` (two literal and two run-time S#s, opaque white and transparent black, modes 6 and 7) on NVIDIA RTX 4070 SUPER (driver 621.01), with `tests/test_translate.py`'s `test_gather4_l_*border*`. `image_gather4_lz_o`: `OpImageGather … Offset`. `image_store` on 1D (bindings 17–19, `Image1D`) and with a16 coordinates. `ds_swizzle BITMASK_PERM` ("pippi" masks; GPU-checked). `v_add_f64`, `v_cvt_f32_f64` (`Float64`). `v_mul/mad_i32_i24`, `v_alignbyte_b32`, `v_xnor_b32`, `v_ffbh_i32`, `v_swap_b32`, `s_abs_i32`, `s_bfm_b32/b64`, 64-bit ordered and `v_cmpx` compares, 16-bit compares, 8/16-bit global loads, `neg(…)` constant operands (GPU-checked where listed in `runtime/test/shaders`). |
| `v_mad_u64_u32`, `v_mad_i64_i32` | {sdst, D} = S0 × S1 + S2 as a 65-bit result (RDNA ISA pseudocode), the product unsigned or signed. sdst (a lane mask) gets bit 64 for active lanes and 0 for lanes off in EXEC (`EXEC[lane] ? bit64 : 0`, matching `v_cmp_*` and `*_co_*`): the carry out for u64, and for i64 the sign of the exact sum (bit 64 of a 65-bit two's-complement value), not a signed-overflow flag. `tools/lane_masks.py` tracks sdst as a lane mask (until 2026-11-22 it only matched `*_co_*`, so `s_bcnt1`/`s_ff1`/`v_mbcnt` on sdst read each lane's own mask word as plain data). `clamp` is rejected. Checked on the CPU by `tests/test_lane_masks.py`, `tests/semantic/test_alu_mad64.py` (including 64-lane `s_bcnt1_i32_b64`/`s_ff1_i32_b64` ballot tests), and `tests/semantic/test_carry_exec_mask.py` (EXEC masking on inactive lanes). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon 680M, `gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137): unsigned carry, signed positive overflow `(-2³¹)² + INT64_MAX` giving `sdst = 0`, negative sum giving `sdst = 1`, and inactive EXEC lane leaving D untouched with `sdst = 0` all match bit-for-bit. |
| Ray tracing | `image_bvh_intersect_ray` / `image_bvh64_intersect_ray` (64 CS: RT shadows) are one traversal step against AMD's hardware BVH node format. The traversal loop lives in the game's shader. The step is emulated over buffer reads following Mesa RADV's software path (`radv_nir_rt_common.c`, `intersect_ray_amd_software_box/_tri`; node layouts from `bvh_defines.h`). Node id = address >> 3 \| type (0–3 triangle, 4 box16, 5 box32). Box nodes give the four child ids, with misses and NaN boxes = `0xFFFFFFFF`, sorted by entry distance when descriptor bit 63 (box_sort_enable) is set. **The box test is the native one, measured on RDNA2** (SHADER-650; RX 6900 XT, gfx1030, `tools/hw_oracle` raw kernels, 338 cases: BOX32 and BOX16, four rays including negative directions, NaN in every position, empty boxes, planes on the origin, and every tie order of four children; the translator now matches all 338 bit for bit, `main` before it 43): per axis, the near plane is min and the far plane max when the inverse direction (taken as given, not clamped) is ≥ 0, swapped otherwise; t = (plane − origin) × inverse direction, so `0 × inf` is NaN. tnear = max of the three near t, tfar = min of the three far t, **each skipping NaN**; all three near or all three far NaN is a miss. BOX32 and BOX16, four rays including negative directions, NaN in every position, empty boxes, planes on the origin, and every tie order of four children; the translator now matches all 338 bit for bit, `main` before it 43, and all 811 rounding cases below): per axis, the near plane is min and the far plane max when the inverse direction (taken as given, not clamped) is ≥ 0, swapped otherwise; t = (plane − origin) × inverse direction, so `0 × inf` is NaN. **Both the subtraction and the multiply round toward zero** (811 more cases, 800 randomized slabs at origins 2^18 to 2^27 away plus 11 ties: round-to-nearest predicts 769 of them, round-toward-zero all 811; among the ties, a box with min.x = 1 > max.x = 0 at origin.x = −2^25 is a hit on hardware because both planes round to the same t). The translator emulates this exactly: the subtraction's error comes from TwoSum, the product's error sign from a Dekker split of the two mantissas scaled to [1, 2), and a result rounded away from zero steps one ulp back; a finite overflow gives ±FLT_MAX. tnear = max of the three near t, tfar = min of the three far t, **each skipping NaN**; all three near or all three far NaN is a miss. Hit when max(tnear, 0) ≤ tfar and tnear < extent (the extent boundary itself was not measured). So a lone NaN bound leaves its own plane open (a box with min.x = NaN is hit wherever x ≤ max.x), an empty box (min > max on any axis, ±inf or ±65504 included) is a miss without a separate check, and a plane exactly on the origin along a zero-direction axis is open. The sort key is max(tnear, 0), so boxes around the origin tie at 0, ordered by the network (0,2) (1,3) (0,1) (2,3) (1,2) with a strict `<` swap (all 88 measured tie orders). `tests/semantic/test_image_bvh_box_hw.py` holds measured words. Triangle nodes read their three vertices from the slots the node type picks: TRI0 (0,1,2), TRI1 (1,3,2), TRI2 (2,3,4), TRI3 (2,4,0), 12 bytes each. They use the watertight test with RADV's shared edge/vertex tie-breaks and give (t/\|det\|, sign(det), b_i/\|det\|, b_j/\|det\|), or (inf, 1, 0, 0) on a miss. The weights i and j are chosen by the node type's byte of the triangle-ID word at +60: bits [1:0] and [3:2], 0/1/2 = vertex 0/1/2 and 3 = vertex 0, bits [7:4] ignored; `0x09` gives RADV's (v, w). Vertex slots and remap follow AMD GPURT (`CalcTriangleCompressionVertexIndices`, `SwizzleBarycentrics`) and the public XDXR software path ([bvh.md](bvh.md) §7a), and the hardware agrees. **The triangle step is the native one, measured on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon RX 6800, gfx1030, AMD Windows driver 26.11.2, Windows 11, 2026-11-22; the `tools/hw_oracle` kernels `tools/bvh_tri_probe.py` builds, hand-built nodes and rays: 14 kernels, 308 single-lane cases and 7 cases on 64 lanes, 1,204 results, each in both forms; the 32-bit form with the descriptor base at the node and the 64-bit form with base 0, wave32 and wave64, NSA and contiguous operands all gave the same words). A NaN anywhere in t max, the origin, the direction or the inverse direction, or an infinite origin, returns (+inf, 1.0, +0, +0). Otherwise the watertight test runs with its shear taken from the direction (kz = the axis of the largest \|d\|, ties x, y, z; kx and ky swapped when d[kz] < 0; Sz = 1/d[kz]): **the supplied inverse direction is not used**. With the edge functions U, V, W, det = U + V + W, T = t·det and k = \|d[kz]\|³, a hit returns (k·T, k·det, k·b_i, k·b_j) and a miss (copysign(inf, k·det), k·det, k·b_i, k·b_j): numerators over the denominator k·det, and a miss keeps the plane's barycentric numerators. det = 0 is a miss with word 0 = +inf. The hit needs t ≥ 0 (t = 0 hits); there is no test against t max. Every zero word is +0 and every NaN word 0xFFC00000, so a NaN det gives word 0 = −inf. An edge function of 0 is inside when its edge passes RDNA2's tie rule, which is not RADV's: the edge from P to Q (U: B→C, V: C→A, W: A→B), with (tx, ty) = Q′ − P′ in the sheared frame with the d[kz] < 0 swap of kx and ky undone, passes when ty = 0 ? sign(det)·tx > 0 : sign(d[kz])·sign(det)·ty > 0, and every zero edge must pass. Every shared edge and shared vertex gave exactly one hit. How this rule was found: the first fit (ty = 0 ? tx < 0 : sign(det)·ty > 0, on the 49 tie rows of the first measurement, all z dominant) was confounded, because every one of those rows had det < 0 with d[kz] > 0 or det > 0 with d[kz] < 0, and it was wrong in the cells it never saw. A second measurement (2026-11-23, same part and driver; 8 more kernels, 256 rows: ties with x, y and z dominant, both windings, both signs of d[kz], every edge direction class in each of those cells, tilted triangles with oblique rays, equal \|d\| components) fitted none of the eight rules registered before it ran. The rule above was then read off its x/y rows (post hoc), and it predicted the z-dominant bridge kernel `tri_t8` before that ran: 32 of 32 cases, 10 of them rows where it and the first fit disagree. The translator computes these words without a division, with the shear multiplied through by \|d[kz]\| (a shear divided by d[kz] gives the same words on every measured row but d.z = +inf, and divides by zero at d = 0; this form gives d = 0 → (+inf, 0, 0, 0) and d.z = +inf → (−inf, NaN, −inf, −inf) as measured, a fit after the runs), and fixes zero signs and NaN bits with integer selects, so every measured input is defined SPIR-V. `tests/semantic/test_image_bvh_tri_hw.py` asserts all 308 rows and 84 lane results of the first measurement and the 256 rows of the second bit for bit (the translator before it: 129 of the first 308; the first fit: 210 of the 256); `tests/semantic/test_image_bvh_tri.py` runs every node type and remap byte on the interpreter. **A consumer that divides by word 1** (t = w0/w1, weights w2/w1 and w3/w1) gets t and the weights on a hit (k cancels; word 1 keeps det's sign); +inf for t on a miss of either winding, with the plane's weights; t = +inf/+0 = +inf and weights 0/0 on a zero-area triangle; t = NaN with a NaN or inf vertex, so a `t < t max` test fails as on a miss; t = +inf with a NaN in the ray or an infinite origin. It must compare t with t max itself. The translator's earlier normalised words (t/\|det\|, sign(det), b_i/\|det\|, b_j/\|det\|; a miss (inf, 1, 0, 0)) gave such a consumer the same t on every row and the same weights on every hit except on 14 shared-edge rows (RADV's tie rule), 3 rows whose inverse direction is not 1/d, 3 rows with a NaN in the ray (it reported a hit or other words), the v0.x = −inf and d.z = +inf rows, and 14 rows that were undefined SPIR-V there (zero area, a NaN or inf vertex, a NaN, inf or zero ray). **Not measured:** gfx1013 (the console); node types 6 and 7; the box extent boundary; descriptor words other than w2 = 0xFFFFFFFF, w3 = 0x81000000 (return mode 0 included); the dominant axis on equal \|d\| components except where a measured tie row tells the axes apart: x for \|dx\| = \|dy\| > \|dz\| (only with dx, dy > 0), x for \|dx\| = \|dz\| > \|dy\| (not with dx > 0 > dz), y for \|dy\| = \|dz\| > \|dx\| (only with dy, dz of the same sign) and x with all three equal (in the four of the eight sign patterns that ran), which is the translator's `>=` order (a non-tie row gives the same words whatever the choice, and every sign pattern could show it with the right tie); rounding-sensitive inputs (every input is dyadic, so the divided versus multiplied-through shear and fused versus separate rounding are not told apart); d = 0 and infinite d beyond one row each; the remap's high nibble on TRI1–TRI3. These two references agree; one real pack BLAS is now matched, rehydrated and traversed (§7b), though the rule has not been counted over the whole corpus. The triangle rule has not been measured on the native instruction yet: `tools/hw_oracle` can run it on RDNA2, and box32 nodes were measured that way (gfx1036, address = base × 256 + (node & ~7) × 8 in both instruction widths, §7b), so the triangle counterpart is a possible follow-up rather than an impossibility. These two references agree; no game BVH is decoded yet to count over. The triangle rule has not been run natively yet; the box rule above has (`tools/hw_oracle` on an AMD RDNA2 card runs the instruction itself). `tests/semantic/test_image_bvh_tri.py` runs every node type and remap byte on the interpreter, including the descriptor-base case. The game's code divides by `.y` exactly as for the hardware's numerator/denominator. Address = descriptor base + ((node & ~7) << 3). The descriptor's dword0 is the base in 256-byte units: the game passes `ptr >> 8` there, and the `\| 0x30` its shader ORs is only the temporary address it uses to load `rootNodePtr`, not the descriptor base. Measured at base 0 and at base 0x1000 (structure at VA 0x1000, dword0 `0x10`): the GPU returns the CPU walker's children in the same order at both, so the `<< 8` is correct and a nonzero base needs no masking. `tests/semantic/test_image_bvh_tri.py` runs the nonzero base (`0x10` → `0x1000`). Address = descriptor base + ((node & ~7) << 3), base = descriptor bits [39:0] in 256-byte units (measured on gfx1030: the 32-bit form with a non-zero base and the 64-bit form with base 0 and the full address gave the same words in 71 cases). **The runtime must build the game's acceleration structures in this AMD node format** (RADV's GPU BVH builder produces it) wherever the game's D3D12 calls build them. Far branches with `s_sub_u32`/`s_subb_u32` (backward) are folded too. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_bvh.asm`: sorted box children and the triangle result are exact. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_bvh.asm`: sorted box children and the triangle result are exact; its box with a NaN min.x is entered at t = 0 and sorts first (`0x68 0x48 0x40 0xffffffff`), as native gfx1030 returns for that scene. The 32-bit form (11 address dwords: the node id has no high dword) runs the same scene in `cs_bvh32.asm` and gives the same eight words. Corpus counts (`tools/image_bvh_census.py`, static, [bvh.md](bvh.md) §7): 716 sites, 638 32-bit and 78 64-bit; the descriptor has box_sort_en 1, big_page 1, size all ones and type 8 at every site; on every path from the call, 397 sites take `v_rcp_f32` of word 1, multiply word 0 (335 also words 2 and 3) by it and read `sext(w1[31:16])`, all 716 read the words as integers (397 through shifts and `v_and_or_b32`/`v_lshl_or_b32`/`v_or3_b32`, 319 through `v_cmp_eq_u32` only), and none reads a word undivided as a float; 633 sites also have reads on some paths only, which the census leaves unclassified. |
| Coverage | Every shader in the corpus translates. The remaining fidelity gap is wave64 cross-half code on 32-wide subgroups in VS/PS/GS (tier C of the *Wave64 graphics* row; tiers A and B are exact); compute and mesh shaders are exact there with `--wave64-emulate` (see the wave-size and wave64 rows). |
| Flat pixel inputs | A pixel input location read only by `v_interp_mov_f32` with the p0 select is declared `Flat` (a location a p10/p20 mov reads is `PerVertexKHR`, next row). A pixel input location read only by `v_interp_mov_f32` is declared `Flat`. Locations read with `v_interp_p1/p2` remain interpolated. The `ps_flat` GPU test passes integer IDs through the first vertex alongside interpolated UVs. On the CPU, `test_v_interp_mov_emits_flat_decoration` and `test_attribute_read_both_flat_and_interpolated_is_not_flat` in `tests/semantic/test_interp_mov_flat.py` check the Flat decoration of a p0-only location and that a location also read with p1/p2 is not decorated Flat. |
| v_interp operand guards (SHADER-177) | The single-lane model reads the attribute's own value for `v_interp_p1/p2`, which is only right when the VGPR source really holds the barycentric coordinate that op interpolates with — **p1 reads I = v0 and p2 reads J = v1, per op** — and `v_interp_mov_f32` p0 is the provoking vertex (p10/p20 are the deltas of the vertex-delta row above). A p1/p2 (f32 and f16 forms) whose source is not its own coordinate — v0 for p1, v1 for p2, each either the hardware register or a plain `v_mov_b32` copy rooted at it (the copy's single write in the whole shader, defined before the use, copy chains followed, and its source v0/v1 itself unwritten up to the copy: a copy of an already-overwritten v0/v1 carries a computed value — zeroing the source before copying renders (0,0) on hardware — while a write after the copy is fine, the copy saved the value) — is **Unsupported**, naming the register and the role, so a swapped p1(v1)/p2(v0) pair, a both-v0 pair, or a copy of an overwritten source is refused rather than silently mistranslated; `v_interp_mov_f32` with p10/p20 was **Unsupported** until the vertex-delta row above; any other select still is, naming it. `v_interp_mov_f32` with p10/p20 is **Unsupported**, naming the select. A **direct** v0/v1 read stays accepted even when the shader overwrites v0/v1 first (evaluate-at-offset/snapped barycentrics; up to 213 PS, #1218): modelling that computed value is the separate provenance follow-up. Zero corpus cost: over the 26,503-shader corpus every f32 I/J source is v0/v1 (117,001 / 116,997) except one PS whose four `v_interp_p2_f32` read J through `v_mov_b32 v14, v1` (its v1 unwritten up to the copy; the later overwrites are the saved-copy case), which the copy rule keeps translating (bit-identical .spv), every f16 source is v0/v1 (58,950 / 58,950), and all 6,926 `v_interp_mov_f32` use p0. Confirmed by `tests/semantic/test_interp_guards.py` (refusals name the operand and its role, including swapped and both-v0 pairs, a wrong-root copy, and a copy of an overwritten source; the copy, copy-chain, matching-role and saved-from-later-zero cases translate and compute; a second write or a dpp copy no longer qualifies; a mutation check that the guard consults the copy analysis) and by re-translating a 200-shader corpus slice plus that PS before and after: translated/valid counts and every .spv unchanged. |
| Pixel-shader interpolants | When a sidecar `fssm/<name>.fssm` holds a pixel-input table (`tools/fssm_header.py`: 8-byte records from 0x138 while the first byte is 6; table position = `v_interp attr` number), every position 0..count−1 is declared as a fragment input at that Location, including positions the code never reads (the table overcounts the code's distinct `v_interp` attrs in 775 of 12,101 PS: 300 by one, 475 by two or more; only the 10 records starting with kind 7 undercount). A 32-bit `v_interp` read (`p1/p2/mov`) of a 16-bit-flagged position (flag bit 4; **measured natively on RDNA2**, AMD Radeon 680M `gfx1035` in wave32 and wave64 via `tools/vinterp_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-430: the VINTRP unit reads the same LDS parameter-cache dwords at `M0 + 48·attr + {8·c, 8·c + 4, 32 + 4·c}` with no hidden 16-bit unpacking, so `v_interp_mov_f32` returns the raw 32-bit parameter word and `v_interp_p1_f32`/`v_interp_p2_f32` interpolate those 32-bit words as binary32), and reads past the table still declare an input (the value is undefined on hardware). 11,199 such inputs corpus-wide, matching the `*_f16` interpolation opcodes in 99.7%) sees the value rounded to f16 (round to nearest, f16 denormals flushed to signed zero, back to f32) via a single `OpQuantizeToF16` (a `PackHalf2x16`/`UnpackHalf2x16` round-trip is folded to a no-op by at least one Vulkan driver, RTX 5080); reads through the `*_f16` opcodes already narrow and are unchanged (on a location an f16 op also reads, the f32 read is of the per-vertex raw word, "16-bit interpolation"; on gfx1036 a 32-bit `p1/p2_f32` read of a packed channel takes the raw LDS words as f32 bits, one fma each (SHADER-430, `tools/interp_hw_probe.py`); what P10/P20 words the SPI stores for such a channel is unverified), and reads past the table still declare an input (the value is undefined on hardware). Without a sidecar, or with an empty table, the output is byte-identical to the code-driven path. Confirmed by synthetic fixtures plus mini-interpreter agreement (`tests/test_rdna2spirv_pixelinput.py`: an f32 read of a flagged slot with input 1.1 yields the f16-rounded 1.099609375; unflagged and table-less controls yield exact 1.1) and `tests/test_vinterp_hw_probe.py`. The `index.json` entry records `ps_interpolants` (count) and `ps_fp16_interpolants` (flagged positions). |
| PS interpolation modes (SHADER-181) | Every interpolated generic pixel input in this build is **perspective-correct at the pixel center**, and every other input is flat, so the single v0/v1 pair the code reads is `PERSP_CENTER`. The translator models exactly these two: Flat for `v_interp_mov_f32`, plain (perspective, center) inputs and `BaryCoordKHR` for `v_interp_p1/p2` and the f16 forms. Two records describe each input, and they state **different facts**: PSV0 (DXBC) stores the interpolation **mode**; the FSSM input record's flags byte (byte 7) stores the **read method** — bit 0 (0x01) marks `v_interp_mov_f32` (4,600 records), bit 1 (0x02) 32-bit `v_interp_p1/p2` (51,191; values 0 and 3 never occur), bit 3 (0x08) a Float16-typed parameter (11,619), bit 4 (0x10) the `*_f16` opcodes (11,199; 24 f16-read records lack it). The flag/usage cross-table over all 55,791 records has no contradiction: every bit-0 position the code reads is read only by `v_interp_mov_f32` (4,295 positions), every bit-1 position only by `v_interp_p1/p2` or the f16 forms (49,594), and no position is read both ways (1,902 positions are unread, and 88 reads fall past the table). **The mode is in PSV0, not the FSSM**: `tools/ps_interp_modes.py` decodes it from the PSV0 signature elements (`DXIL::InterpolationMode`, "must match D3D_INTERPOLATION_MODE": 1 Constant, 2 Linear, 3 LinearCentroid, 4 LinearNoperspective, 5 LinearNoperspectiveCentroid, 6 LinearSample, 7 LinearNoperspectiveSample; the 16-byte elements carry row, cols, semantic kind, component type and the mode — layout in the Pixel-shader inputs section) and maps the modes onto the `SPI_PS_INPUT_ENA` barycentric-pair bits (Mesa `gfx103.json` register type SPI_PS_INPUT_ENA: PERSP_SAMPLE/CENTER/CENTROID = bits 0/1/2, LINEAR_SAMPLE/CENTER/CENTROID = bits 4/5/6; the same type serves `SPI_PS_INPUT_ADDR`). **No FSSM word stores ENA/ADDR**: every u16 in bytes 0x00–0x138 of all 12,101 PS was compared against the signature-reconstructed ENA and only constant coincidences matched, and the register addresses (0x286CC/0x286D0, or 0xA1B3/0xA1B4 as context-register indices) appear in no PS blob, so the v2.. position layout of "PS system inputs" (one enabled pair) is the only ENA witness. The #2735 packed-component census matched PSV0, FSSM and disassembly for all 12,101 indexed PS. Of 101,024 declared input rows, 77,245 have a nonzero read mask. Census (12,101 PS; 101,024 declared / 77,245 read inputs): every arbitrary, clip and cull input is Constant (6,333 rows, 4,529 read) or Linear = perspective-centre (81,385 rows, attributes plus 1,806 `SV_ClipDistance` and 2 `SV_CullDistance`); zero centroid, sample or noperspective generics; per shader 6,350 linear only, 4,507 linear and constant, 6 constant only, 1,238 no interpolated input. 101,024 declared / 76,215 read inputs): every arbitrary, clip and cull input is Constant (6,333 rows, 3,652 read) or Linear = perspective-centre (81,385 rows, attributes plus 1,806 `SV_ClipDistance` and 2 `SV_CullDistance`); zero centroid, sample or noperspective generics; per shader 6,350 linear only, 4,507 linear and constant, 6 constant only, 1,238 no interpolated input. ISG1 has no mode field, and the DXIL part carries no bitcode. SV_Position is mode 4 (11,295 rows) or mode 5 (27), both noperspective by the D3D rule. With packed-component read masks, the classifier assigns 10,260 shaders to the perspective-centre pair and 1,841 to no pair. None classify as linear-centre. Hardware delivery of SV_Position is outside the scope of the census. Front face is a system input (1,984 rows, 345 read, at most one per shader). It assigns none to a linear pair. This count measures input classification; it does not directly measure how the hardware delivers SV_Position; front face is a system input (1,984 rows, 345 read, at most one per shader). SV_Position is mode 4 (11,295 rows) or mode 5 (27) — noperspective by the D3D rule — and is delivered through the system VGPRs in every shader except **4**, which interpolate it through the parameter cache with the *linear* pair (`FragCoord` stays at the pixel center, which only differs at partly covered MSAA pixels); front face is a system input (1,984 rows, 345 read, at most one per shader). The code reads **at most one** barycentric pair per shader: v0/v1 in 10,259, none in 1,841, and one shader reads J from v14 (a copy of v1), so the perspective-centre assumption holds for every interpolated generic input. **Mode and read method disagree in thousands of rows** — the residual surface for modelling is: those 4 linear-pair position shaders, Constant rows read via `p1/p2` or `*_f16` (2,190 records at the matching table position; flat semantics in hardware, not modelled), and Linear rows read only by `v_interp_mov_f32` (3,197; already declared `Flat`). The translator's table check (`check_read_methods`, the row below) compares each read's opcode against the record's read-method bits (`fssm_header.read_methods`) — since the bits restate the read method, it rejects 0 of 12,101 PS: a consistency check between the two encodings of the read, not a mode check (a centroid/sample/noperspective input would still read `v_interp_p1/p2` and pass; that every interpolated generic input is perspective-centre is the PSV0 census result above, so keying a refusal on genuinely unmodelled modes means decoding PSV0, not reading these bits). Counted with `tools/ps_interp_modes.py` (stdlib; PSV0, FSSM and disassembly in one pass; #1301 removed the earlier #1246 census tool in favour of it) on the 1.0.2.1 corpus; tests `tests/test_ps_interp_modes.py`, `tests/test_fssm_header.py` and `tests/test_rdna2spirv_pixelinput.py` (synthetic). No GPU test: no shader needs centroid or sample interpolation. **Not covered:** bit 3 (0x08) is set in 11,619 records (all 11,199 16-bit ones, 396 flat, 24 linear). The 213 PS that overwrite v0/v1 before a `v_interp` (SHADER-178) compute their own barycentrics in code; PSV0 gives them no other mode. |
| `v_interp_mov_f32` p10 / p20 | The VINTRP mov returns one of the three values the hardware keeps per attribute for the p1/p2 pair, {P10, P20, P0}[S] (RDNA2 ISA, VINTRP; P = P0 + i·P10 + j·P20). p0 stays the provoking vertex (`Flat`, exact bits). p10 and p20 read the location `PerVertexKHR` (the per-vertex path the `*_f16` interps use, so the PS needs `fragmentShaderBarycentric`) and return P1 − P0 and P2 − P0 as one f32 `OpFSub` of the producer's words, vertices ordered as in the f16 row (BaryCoordKHR .y/.z weight vertices 1/2). Refused (`Unsupported`) under `--bary-fallback` and `--f16-interp-fallback` (no per-vertex inputs) and on a 16-bit-flagged interpolant (FSSM table), where whether the hardware forms the delta in f16 is unmeasured. Until 2026-11-22 both selects were refused; all 6,926 `v_interp_mov_f32` in the corpus use p0, so the corpus translation is unchanged. Confirmed on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_interp_mov_delta.py` (distinct per-vertex words give the deltas, and P0 + i·P10 + j·P20 from the three movs matches the p1/p2 pair bit for bit). **Unverified:** a GPU run, and AMD's rounding of P10/P20 for denormal or NaN words. |
| PS `v_interp` read methods (SHADER-181, translator side) | Bits 0–1 of the FSSM input record's flags byte (byte 7; `fssm_header.read_methods`) give the **read method** of each table position: bit 0 (0x01) = read through `v_interp_mov_f32`, bit 1 (0x02) = read through `v_interp_p1/p2` or the f16 forms. They do not give the D3D interpolation mode, which is PSV0's (row "PS interpolation modes", `tools/ps_interp_modes.py`, #1256). Exactly one bit is set in every corpus record (bit 1 in 51,191, bit 0 in 4,600). In all 12,101 PS every bit-0 position the code reads is read only by `v_interp_mov_f32` (4,295 read positions), every bit-1 position only by `p1/p2` or the f16 forms (49,594), and no position is read both ways (1,902 positions are unread, and 88 reads fall past the table). The translator follows the read method, which is what the code does: `v_interp_mov` → `Flat`, `p1/p2` → a plain input (perspective, pixel center; `BaryCoordKHR` for raw i/j). That this one pair is perspective-center holds because PSV0 lists no centroid, sample or noperspective generic input, and the code reads one barycentric pair (#1256). Table positions don't pair one-to-one with PSV0 rows, since the table keeps only the inputs the compiled code uses, so a PSV0 row's mode and the record's read method can differ at a paired position; #1256 counts those cases. `rdna2spirv.check_read_methods` refuses a PS whose read position disagrees with its record's read method (`v_interp_mov` of a bit-1 position, `p1/p2`/f16 of a bit-0 one), or whose record has neither or both bits, instead of reading it as perspective-center. It refuses 0 of 12,101 PS, and shard 0/12 translates byte-identically to before the check. `index.json` records `ps_flat_interpolants`, the bit-0 positions. Tests: `tests/test_rdna2spirv_pixelinput.py` and `tests/test_fssm_header.py` (synthetic). GPU-checked by @Vonsek on #1246 (RTX 5080 and Radeon integrated graphics, stock `ps_flat` with a hand-built table of read methods 2 and 1): the smooth channels match a per-center perspective reference, the flat channel keeps the provoking value, and flipping either record's read method makes the translator refuse the shader. Bit 3 (0x08, 11,619 records) is a Float16-typed parameter per #1256. |
| Mixed flat and smooth f32 reads | A pixel shader may use `v_interp_mov_f32 p0` and `v_interp_p1/p2_f32` on the same input location. The translator declares it `PerVertexKHR`, takes vertex 0 for the MOV, and interpolates the smooth read with the triangle weights. The fallback modes without per-vertex inputs reject this combination. The synthetic `mixed_f32_varying.asm` fixture and the SPIR-V interpreter check both reads at two points of one triangle, and both fallback modes raise Unsupported (`test_p0_and_interpolated_read_of_one_f32_location` in `tests/semantic/test_interp_mov_flat.py`). [AMD RDNA 2 ISA, VINTRP instructions](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) |
| Computed I/J barycentrics (SHADER-177) | `v_interp_p1/p2` weight the interpolation with the values their operand VGPRs hold at the two ops, not with the hardware's own pair. Vonsek's image evidence on a synthetic triangle (#1224): zeroed sources render P0 = (0,0) on all 4,096 pixels, and copies of the pair read in swapped order render (v,u) — both follow the two VINTRP equations in the RDNA2 ISA guide; the centre interpolation the single-lane model used to emit is wrong for both. When a write of the operand register reaches an interp site (earlier in its own basic block, or in any block a path runs through first — loops included; a write at the site itself, an interp whose destination is its own I/J register, happens after the operand is read), the attribute is read `PerVertexKHR` and both ops interpolate with the operand values loaded from `%vgpr`, which holds whatever the program computed: constants (snapped coordinates, 577 straight-line sites), copies and copy chains (575), arithmetic (8), multi-writer sequences (1,135). The p1 loads its I and keeps it for the p2, which loads its J fresh — the program often sets J up between the two ops (574 sites) — so the final value uses the I of the p1 with the J of the p2; the p1's own full-sum value is dead because nothing may read the partial between the ops. Pipodi's straight-line scan puts the upper bound at 213 PS (#1218 comment 7076614458; 2,290 v0/v1 sites plus one PS reading J through a copy, all branchy); the reaching-write analysis finds 207 with a write that can reach an interp, and 6 whose overwrites sit on paths that cannot (left pristine, `.spv` unchanged). The other 11,888 PS keep the interpolated-input read and a byte-identical `.spv`. Composes with the operand guards (row above, #1224): a pristine plain `v_mov` copy of the pair stays the guards' to model (centre interpolation, bit-identical `.spv`, including the corpus's one copy PS), an operand that is neither v0/v1 nor written stays the guards' role-naming refusal, and a computed site is exempt from `interp_pair_src` and modelled here — which relaxes exactly the guards' copy-based refusals whose operand a program write reaches: a twice-written register (`v_mov` then arithmetic — the load reads the value the later write left; value-proven by `test_copy_written_again_is_computed`), a copy of an already-overwritten v0/v1 (Vonsek's zero-before-copy row: hardware renders P0, which is exactly the loaded zeros), and a modified `v_mov_b32_dpp` source (any write of the operand is a `%vgpr` value; translation-only test). A pristine copy in the wrong role still refuses (no computed value to model). Over the corpus the relaxation is empty — every one of the 213 PS names v0/v1 (or the one qualified copy), so on main and on this branch alike the set translates 213/213 and passes `spirv-val` 213/213, and no shader changes class; the guards' refusals that relax are the synthetic ones the tests cover. Refused (naming the pattern): a computed pair whose p1 partial is rewritten or read between the ops (its hardware value P10·I would be observable), one whose ops are not in one basic block (the captured I would not dominate the p2), a dangling op without its partner, any computed pair under `--bary-fallback` or `--f16-interp-fallback` (neither has PerVertexKHR inputs; the latter's decoded-half read would otherwise drop the computed weights), and a computed 32-bit read of a 16-bit-flagged interpolant — every one empty over the corpus. Confirmed by `tests/semantic/test_interp_computed_ij.py` and `tests/semantic/test_interp_guards.py` (Vonsek's rows, the between-ops J, f16 pairs, the refusals, the relaxed classes) and the `ps_ij_computed` GPU test (RTX 5060 Ti: snapped quarter pair = 0.5, zeroed pair = P0 = 0, (i, j/2) halving the second weight with 0.0 max error over 4,096 pixels; the same fixture built on main is wrong on 4,096 of 4,096). |
| Whole-wave masks | `tools/lane_masks.py` tracks per-lane mask bits through scalar registers, vector operations and saved EXEC spills. Whole-mask counts, first-set-lane queries, the leading-bit scans `s_flbit_i32_b32` / `s_flbit_i32` of `vcc_lo`/`exec_lo` (and `_hi`) and equality use subgroup operations (`tests/semantic/test_salu_flbit_lane_mask.py`; until then the flbit scans saw this lane's 0 / ~0). Whole-mask counts, first-set-lane queries and equality use subgroup operations. `cs_wavemask` checks all 64 lanes with a partially active EXEC mask. A physical 32-lane subgroup cannot directly exchange mask bits with the other half of a wave64, so `cs_wavemask` and the `cs_wave64_ballot` / `cs_wm_*` tests are exact only on 64-wide subgroups (AMD): on an RTX 4090 (subgroup 32) `s_bcnt1_i32_b64` of lanes 16–47 counts 16, one half (#101). `check.py` skips them where the GPU has no 64-wide compute subgroup. A mask copied into a VGPR with `v_mov_b32` goes through the normal EXEC-gated store, so inactive lanes keep the old VGPR (SHADER-21 part 2, #210); per-lane mask computations still store in all lanes, because whole-wave readers (`wave_mask`, `v_readfirstlane`) read every lane's bit. `cs_mask_vgpr_exec` checks it. Vector bitwise mask tracking (`VECTOR_BITWISE`) also includes `v_and_or_b32`, `v_xnor_b32` and `v_bfi_b32` when every source is a lane mask or `0`/`-1` (CPU-checked by `tests/test_lane_masks.py` and `tests/semantic/test_lane_masks_vector_bitwise.py`). |
| Floating-point operations | Separate floating-point instructions carry `NoContraction` so the driver preserves their individual rounding. The instructions that fuse multiply-add (`v_fma_f32`, `v_fmac_f32`, `v_fmaak_f32`, `v_fmamk_f32`, `v_fma_mix*`) round **once** on RDNA2 (measured on gfx1150 by `tools/f32_fuzz.py --oracle hw`: 72 of 72 cancellation probes fused). SPIR-V has two ways to say that. `GLSL.std.450 Fma` is the default (`--fma glsl`), but the Vulkan SPIR-V environment gives it the precision "inherited from `OpFMul` followed by `OpFAdd`", so a driver may legally round twice; `tools/f32_fuzz.py` and `runtime/test/shaders/cs_fma_fused.asm` are the tests that notice — on Mesa RADV/ACO (Linux), `--fma glsl` splits a subset of the 7 f32 FMAs in `cs_fma_fused` depending on GPU, Mesa version, and shader execution modes (measured: 4 of 7 on RX 6800 / Mesa 26.2.6-arch3.1 and RX 7600 / Mesa 27.0.0-devel with `SignedZeroInfNanPreserve 32`, 5 of 7 on RX 6800 before float controls, and 7 of 7 on Radeon Pro V540 RDNA1 / Mesa 25.2.8), while `--fma khr` emits single-rounded `v_fma_f32` (8/8 exact). `tools/f32_fuzz.py` and `runtime/test/shaders/cs_fma_fused.asm` are the tests that notice. `tools/f32_fuzz.py` and `runtime/test/shaders/cs_fma_fused.asm` are the tests that notice — on an AMD Radeon RX 6800 (Mesa RADV 26.2.6-arch3.1, Linux), RADV/ACO actually splits 5 of the 7 f32 FMAs in `cs_fma_fused` under `--fma glsl` (folding/splitting against the live `v_mul_f32` product `a*a`) and emits single-rounded `v_fma_f32` (8/8 exact) under `--fma khr`. `--fma khr` emits `OpFmaKHR` (`SPV_KHR_fma`, capability `FMAKHR`; "correctly rounded"), which needs `VK_KHR_shader_fma` with `shaderFmaFloat32` on the device. The index then lists capability `FMAKHR`, extension `SPV_KHR_fma` and feature `shaderFmaFloat32` for exactly the shaders that use it, and the output of every shader without a fused multiply-add is byte-identical under both options. `khr` is not the default because a device without the extension would fail pipeline creation; a runtime that knows its device has `shaderFmaFloat32` should translate with `--fma khr`. A device that has neither can split `Fma`: the Intel UHD Graphics driver (Xe-LP, Windows 101.7088) does, `cs_fma_fused` words 0, 2, 3 and 5 coming out split, with no `VK_KHR_shader_fma` (SHADER-497). RADV (Mesa 26.3.1, RX 6700 XT) splits the same four words, measured in #3004's review, but it has `VK_KHR_shader_fma` since Mesa 26.2, so `--fma khr` covers it. For it, `--fma exact` (`Gen.fma_exact`, no extension; per multiply-add about 110 instructions more than `glsl`, plus 31 `NoContraction` decorations and 13 constants) builds the single rounding from `NoContraction` f32 operations, after Boldo and Melquiond (IEEE Transactions on Computers 57(4), 2008): Dekker's exact product with the factors split by masking their low 12 mantissa bits, a TwoSum of the addend and the product, the two error terms summed with round-to-odd, and one final rounding. Power-of-two scaling keeps every intermediate in the normal range: by 2^-64 when the product or the addend is at least 2^126, by 2^96 when both are tiny, and 2^64 traded between a factor below 2^-100 and one above 2^-20. Infinite or NaN operands, and a product beyond even the scaled range (the result is ±inf), take the plain `Fma`. Confirmed: `tests/semantic/test_fma_exact.py` runs the emitted instructions in the interpreter with its `Fma` split as on that driver and compares 700 random and 96 edge cases with an exact rational reference; a model of the same steps matched 399,989 of 399,989 cases with a normal or zero result, with f32 denormals kept and flushed; on the GPU, `cs_fma_fused` (and #2211's `cs_w32_fma_mix`) pass with `--fma exact` on that Intel UHD and on an RTX 3050 Laptop (617.42). Not exact: a result in the denormal range (scaled back with a second rounding) and denormal operands, which follow the driver's denormal mode as every f32 operation does here. The f16 forms (`v_fma_f16`, `v_fmac_f16`, `v_pk_fma_f16`) never use either: they are exact round-to-odd arithmetic built from `OpFMul`/`OpFAdd` with `NoContraction` (`Gen.fma_f16_odd`), so a fused f32 `Fma` would not change them (`v_fma_mixlo_f16`/`v_fma_mixhi_f16` under the toward-zero MODE are the exception: `fma_mix_odd` needs the exact product error `Fma(x, y, -p)`, which is only guaranteed with `OpFmaKHR`), and an f16 `OpFmaKHR` (`shaderFmaFloat16`) is not used. The f64 multiply-adds of `v_div_fmas_f64` (the plain `fma`, the pre-scaled one and the exact product error) are `GLSL.std.450 Fma` on doubles by default, and the Vulkan environment lets a driver round that twice too: NVIDIA's does (RTX 4090, driver 621.24, Windows 11: `runtime/test/shaders/cs_div_fmas_f64.asm`, `fma(a, a, c)` with a = 1 + 2^-30 and c = -(1 + 2^-29) gives the split 0 instead of 2^-60, while the f32 `cs_fma_fused` fuses 8/8). `--fma khr` emits them as `OpFmaKHR` on doubles, which needs `shaderFmaFloat64` (the index lists that feature, and `shaderFmaFloat32` only for a module with an f32 FMA; `vk_pipeline_check` checks each); on the RTX 4090 it gives the fused 2^-60. `--fma exact` has no f64 form and keeps `Fma`. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_fma_khr_f64.py` (emission, index features, `spirv-val`). |
| `NoContraction` cost on MoltenVK (SHADER-234) | MoltenVK's SPIRV-Cross turns each `NoContraction` `OpFAdd`, `OpFSub` and `OpFMul` into a call to a `[[clang::optnone]]` helper (`spvFAdd`, `spvFSub`, `spvFMul`), and Metal does not inline it. MoltenVK 1.4.2 and `main` up to at least `5189f8de` are affected. SPIRV-Cross `98cbb7c0` inlines the helpers from MSL 3.2 with bit-identical output, and MoltenVK on macOS 26 or newer compiles MSL 4.0. On an Apple M3 Max, a translated compute shader of `v_mul_f32`, `v_add_f32` and `v_sub_f32` in a loop took 7.6 to 15.7 times as long on the GPU with the optnone helpers as with `98cbb7c0`. With independent operations and no `NoContraction`, it took two thirds of the `98cbb7c0` time, which is the cost of the decoration itself ([measurement](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/issues/1380#issuecomment-7078722086)). The same `spvFMul` helper drops the sign of a -0 product (SHADER-182, #1376). |
| `NoContraction` multiply on MoltenVK (SHADER-182) | On MoltenVK, a multiply whose exact product is a negative zero (`+0 × -1.5`, `-0 × 1.0`) returns +0 in f32, f16 and packed f16 (#1203). MoltenVK's SPIRV-Cross turns every `NoContraction` `OpFMul` into its `spvFMul` helper, which returns `fma(l, r, T(0))`. A -0 product plus +0 is +0. The f16 multiplies are `OpFMul` on `float` or `v2float`, so they take the same path. So does the product inside `fma_f16_odd`: `v_fma_f16`, `v_fmac_f16` and `v_pk_fma_f16` return +0 for a -0 product plus a -0 addend. SPIRV-Cross [300681bb](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/SPIRV-Cross/commit/300681bbcb8365a8d1eacdacdbdd0249bbf1d6b1) changes the addend to `T(-0.0)`. MoltenVK 1.4.2 has the old helper, and so does MoltenVK `main` at `5189f8de` (2026-10-13), which pins the parent commit `aa217aeb`. The translator does not change for this. `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED=0` does not help, because the helper itself adds +0, and per #1203 neither does `SignedZeroInfNanPreserve` (#664). Measured on an Apple M3 Max (macOS 27.3): Metal keeps the sign for `a * b` and for `fma(a, b, -0.0)` with `MTLMathModeFast` and with `MTLMathModeSafe`, and loses it only with the +0 addend. With `valu_fuzz.py --family f16`, MoltenVK `5189f8de` as pinned matches 49/50 programs with `--seed 1 --wave 32`, 98/100 with `--seed 1 --emulate` and 98/100 with `--seed 4 --emulate`, and Homebrew MoltenVK 1.4.2 gives the same counts. Each mismatch is a -0 product read as +0. The same build with SPIRV-Cross `300681bb` matches 50/50, 100/100 and 100/100, as does KosmicKrisp (Mesa 26.3.1). For the FMA path, `--seed 330 --wave 32` with `--skip-op v_mul_f16 --skip-op v_pk_mul_f16` matches 49/50 with `aa217aeb` (a `v_fmac_f16` result) and 50/50 with `300681bb` and on KosmicKrisp. |
| MoltenVK fast-math (SHADER-234, NaN compares) | MoltenVK compiles every shader's MSL with Metal's fast-math on by default (`MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED`, default 1), and with it Metal folds an f32 compare of a NaN with itself as if the NaN were an ordinary number: `v_cmpx_eq_f32` and `v_cmpx_le_f32` on (NaN, NaN) give true where the ISA, AMD and NVIDIA give false (`ps_vcmpx_forms`, #1374: word 1 `0xf05e8e16`, want `0xf05e8a12`, bits 2 and 10; found by @zneel and @DomasUrbonas). The translation is not involved: the compare is `OpFOrdEqual` / `OpFOrdLessThanEqual`, and the MSL MoltenVK generates (`MVK_CONFIG_SHADER_DUMP_DIR`) is byte-identical with fast-math on and off, so it is Metal's compile option. `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED=2` (on demand) does not help, because translated modules don't declare an execution mode that asks for IEEE behaviour; 0 does. The test tools (`runtime/test/vulkan_loader.h`, which every Vulkan test program uses to load MoltenVK) set `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED=0` before MoltenVK reads its configuration, unless the variable is already set, so a user's own value still wins; the variable also reaches MoltenVK through the Vulkan loader. A runtime that loads MoltenVK on a Mac must do the same. Fast-math off does **not** remove the `[[clang::optnone]]` `spvFAdd`/`spvFSub`/`spvFMul` helpers SPIRV-Cross emits for `NoContraction` (11 calls in `ps_ops`'s MSL either way: their cost is the other part of SHADER-234), and it does not fix the row above (SHADER-182): `valu_fuzz.py --family f16 --seed 1` gives the same `v_mul_f16` +0 mismatch with fast-math on and off. Confirmed on an Apple A18 Pro (macOS 26.6.2, MoltenVK 1.4.2): `ps_vcmpx_forms` fails on all 4,096 pixels with fast-math on and matches all 4,096 with it off, natively and with wave64 emulation; no other `run_gpu_tests.py` result and none of the 25 `ctest` tests changes. |
| `v_cmp(x)_lg_f32/f16` on MoltenVK (ordered not-equal) | LG is less-or-greater, false when either source is a NaN. The translator emits it as the `OpLogicalOr` of `OpFOrdLessThan` and `OpFOrdGreaterThan`, not as `OpFOrdNotEqual`. MoltenVK's SPIRV-Cross writes `OpFOrdNotEqual` as a plain `!=`, the same text it gives `OpFUnordNotEqual`, and Metal's `!=` is true on a NaN. That happens with fast-math off too, so it is not the row above: `ps_vcmp_f32_nan`'s SPIR-V has 8 `OpFOrdNotEqual` and 8 `OpFUnordNotEqual`, and the MSL MoltenVK dumps (`MVK_CONFIG_SHADER_DUMP_DIR`) has 16 identical `as_type<float>(a) != as_type<float>(b)`. Confirmed on an Apple M4 (MoltenVK 1.4.2, fast-math off, MoltenVK logs "MathMode Safe"). `ps_vcmp_f32_nan`, `ps_vcmp_f16`, `ps_vcmp_f16_e64`, `ps_vcmpx_f16` and `ps_vcmpx_f16_e64` failed only in the LG bits of their NaN pairs (f16 word 1 `0x4ce9bda2`, want `0x4ce9b1a2`; f32 `0x377c`, want `0x376c`). With this form all five pass, and no other `run_gpu_tests.py` result changes. f64 LG compares integer words and is not affected. `v_cmp(x)_lg_f32/f16` is in 13,795 of the 25,734 listings. The form is pinned on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_vcmp_f32_predicates.py` (`test_lg_is_ordered_without_ord_not_equal`). |
| Denormal flush and special results in `v_rcp_f32`, `v_rsq_f32`, `v_sqrt_f32`, `v_log_f32`, `v_exp_f32`, `v_rcp_iflag_f32` | Measured on an RX 6800 (gfx1030, not the console's gfx1013) with raw kernels in both wave sizes and all four IEEE/DX10_CLAMP combinations: all six read a denormal input as a zero of its sign and flush a denormal result, even with MODE's denormal field set to keep them. `v_rcp_f32(±0)` = ±inf, `v_rsq_f32(-0)` = -inf, `v_sqrt_f32(-0)` = -0, `v_log_f32(±0)` = -inf, `v_rcp_f32(FLT_MAX)` = +0, `v_exp_f32(-127)` = +0, `v_exp_f32(128)` = +inf, and every invalid result (`v_sqrt_f32`/`v_rsq_f32`/`v_log_f32` of a negative, …) is `0xffc00000`. Each is within 1 ulp of correctly rounded on the values tried, and `v_rcp_iflag_f32` is bit-identical to `v_rcp_f32`. **Not modelled yet:** the translator leaves all six on Vulkan's default denormal handling, and the ±0 and negative inputs above stay undefined in the emitted SPIR-V (`OpFDiv` by zero, `Sqrt`/`Log2` of a negative), as listed in the multiply-add and output modifiers row; the `clamp`/`omod` bits on `v_cvt_f32_*` and `v_rcp_iflag_f32` are modelled there. Source: [@shelbykb2's hardware words](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/issues/634#issuecomment-7074348963), not the ISA text. The corpus only ever writes MODE's FP_ROUND field and carries `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` on 102,365 float instructions (a compiler does not emit one that is ignored), so the inferred f32 state, IEEE off with denormals flushed, is the one to model. Corpus count (`tools/shader_profile.py`, 26,503 shaders; the shader count of each op deduplicates its `_e32`/`_e64` forms): `v_rcp_f32` 17,393 shaders / 424,863 occurrences, `v_rsq_f32` 17,221 / 269,310, `v_sqrt_f32` 14,511 / 201,422, `v_log_f32` 8,454 / 90,199, `v_exp_f32` 8,603 / 148,846, `v_rcp_iflag_f32` 7,395 / 34,852; 20,193 shaders (76.2%) use at least one, 1,169,492 occurrences (2.6% of the corpus's 44,406,870 instructions). |
| f16 transcendentals (`v_exp_f16`, `v_log_f16`, `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_cos_f16`) | Measured on an RX 6700 XT (gfx1031, not the console's gfx1013) with `tools/f16_trans_sweep.py`: all 65,536 inputs of each op, wave32 and wave64 (identical on every input), five MODEs (FUZZ-29). With f16 denormals kept (`denorm_mode_16_64 = 3`, the assembler default and what the translator assumes), `v_exp/log/rcp/rsq/sqrt_f16` are **correctly rounded on every input that is not a NaN** (0 ulp). `v_sin/v_cos_f16` take turns and reduce them exactly over the whole f16 range (no ±256 limit): compared with sin/cos(2πx), every result for \|x\| > 1 is bit-exact, and for \|x\| ≤ 1 only 14 (sin) and 4 (cos) of 30,722 are 1 ulp off. So the translator's rule (widen to f32, the GLSL.std.450 function, round to nearest even back to f16) matches the hardware as far as the driver's f32 function is accurate. For sin/cos that holds only once the angle is reduced in turns first (#594): with the f32 product 6.2831855 · x, 16,366 sin and 2,036 cos inputs are more than 1 ulp off, up to 9,944 ulp (sin of 64,640 turns: 0.0267 instead of 0). With `denorm_mode_16_64 = 0`, a denormal operand reads as a zero of its sign, and a denormal result is written as a zero of its sign. That differs from the f32 ops in the row above, which flush in both modes. The f16 field of FP_ROUND is ignored: round toward zero gives the same words as round to nearest on every input. IEEE changes only signalling NaNs. With IEEE off they pass through unchanged; with IEEE on they are quieted (bit 9 set). Payload and sign are kept either way, and quiet NaNs pass unchanged in every mode. Where SPIR-V leaves the result undefined, the hardware gives: log(±0) = -inf, rcp(±0) = ±inf, rsq(+0) = +inf, rsq(-0) = -inf, sqrt(-0) = -0, and `0xfe00` for log/rsq/sqrt of a negative (-inf included) and for sin/cos(±inf). `v_exp_f16`/`v_log_f16` give the same counts on gfx1036 (RDNA2, AMD Windows driver; SHADER-135). Since 2026-11-22 the translator selects those hardware words for `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16` and `v_sqrt_f16` itself (the operand is replaced by 1.0 for the f32 `OpFDiv`/`InverseSqrt`/`Sqrt`, which SPIR-V leaves undefined at 0 or below 0) and `0xfe00` is the negative quiet NaN; checked bit for bit through the interpreter by `tests/semantic/test_valu_rcp_f16.py`, `test_valu_rsq_f16.py` and `test_valu_sqrt_f16.py` against exactly rounded references. The undefined cases of `v_exp_f16`/`v_log_f16`/`v_sin_f16`/`v_cos_f16` are still left to the driver. **Not modelled yet:** `Gen.f16_bits` quiets every NaN (the hardware keeps a signalling NaN with IEEE off), and the undefined cases above are left to the driver. |
| f64 literal sources | For `v_cvt_f32_f64` and either source of `v_add_f64`, a literal dword supplies bits 63:32 and bits 31:0 are zero. For `v_cvt_f32_f64`, `v_rcp_f64`, `v_rsq_f64`, `v_sqrt_f64`, `v_frexp_mant_f64`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f64` and either source of `v_add_f64`, a literal dword supplies bits 63:32 and bits 31:0 are zero. Inline integers are sign-extended and inline floats use their binary64 value. For example, gfx1030 `llvm-mc` encodes 1.5 as the literal word `0x3ff80000`. The synthetic `tests/semantic/fixtures/f64_literals.asm` checks both source words in the translated SPIR-V, including the distinction between the printed literal `0x1` and inline `1`.
