No core SPIR-V atomic adds floats on Workgroup memory, so it is an `OpAtomicCompareExchange` loop with an `OpFAdd` (round to nearest even, `NoContraction`). That is exact under contention: every update is one successful exchange of the value it read. **Not measured:** how the LDS adder treats denormals and NaN payloads; the result is `OpFAdd`'s IEEE sum. The f32 min/max forms are #2495's. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_add_f32.py` (a value grid, the non-returning form, `offset:`, ties to even, -0 + +0, overflow to ±inf, seeded values, against a once-rounded double sum). **Not GPU-verified.** Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle`, 49 cases: the LDS adder **flushes denormal inputs and denormal results to zero** (`0x00800001 + -0x00800000` gives `+0`, not `0x00000001`; `0x007fffff + 0x00800000` gives `0x00800000`, not the tie's `0x00ffffff`; `0x00000001 + 0x00000001` gives `+0`), while the `OpFAdd` the translator emits keeps them, so denormal operands and results are a known divergence. A NaN is quieted with its payload and sign kept (sNaN `0x7f800001` becomes `0x7fc00001`) and when both operands are NaN the DATA operand wins whatever the payloads (`0x7fc00007 + 0x7fc00000` gives `0x7fc00000`); `inf + -inf` gives `0xffc00000`. The f32 min/max forms are #2495's. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_add_f32.py` (a value grid, the non-returning form, `offset:`, ties to even, -0 + +0, overflow to ±inf, seeded values, against a once-rounded double sum). Normal sums match that model on the same run: ties to even (`1.0 + 2^-24` stays `1.0`, `1.0 + 3*2^-25` goes up), `-0 + -0` is `-0`, `+0 + -0` is `+0`, overflow to `±inf`, and the `_rtn_` form returns the raw pre-op dword, denormal or not. |
| `ds_mskor_b32`, `ds_mskor_rtn_b32` | `tmp = M[A]; M[A] = (tmp & ~DATA) \| DATA2`; the `_rtn_` form returns `tmp` (RDNA2 ISA, DS). Emitted as an atomic AND with `~DATA`, whose result is `tmp`, then an atomic OR with `DATA2`, so the returned value is exact. DATA2 bits outside DATA are ORed in too, as the formula says; it isn't a masked insert. **Approximation:** the memory ends as the single op leaves it unless another lane's op on the same dword lands between the two atomics. Until 2026-11-22 both raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_mskor.py` (a value grid, the non-returning form, DATA2 outside DATA, `offset:`, seeded values). Measured on native gfx1100, including DATA2 bits outside DATA being ORed in, so it is not a masked insert. **GPU-verified** on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 7 9800X3D, Windows driver 32.0.21042.62) through `tools/hw_oracle`: 20 of 20 one-thread cases (five M/DATA/DATA2 triples including DATA2 bits outside DATA, both forms, `offset:` 0 and 12) leave `(M & ~DATA) \| DATA2` and return M. **The approximation, measured:** 32 lanes on one dword (M = 0xABCD12FF, DATA = 0xFF, DATA2 = 1 << (lane & 7)) run one lane at a time in lane order on the hardware: each lane returns the word the lane before it left (lane 0 0xABCD12FF, lane 1 0xABCD1201, lane 2 0xABCD1202, …) and the dword ends 0xABCD1280. The two-atomic emission does every AND before every OR, so it would end 0xABCD12FF and return 0xABCD1200 to lanes 1..31 (expected, not run through the translator). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `ds_{inc,dec,rsub}_u32`, `ds_{min,max}_f32` (and `_rtn_`) | 32-bit LDS atomics lowered inside `Gen.lds_op` via a Workgroup-scope `OpAtomicCompareExchange` CAS loop (no shader float atomic extension needed): `ds_inc_u32` gives `(M >= DATA) ? 0 : M + 1` (unsigned), `ds_dec_u32` gives `(M == 0 \|\| M > DATA) ? DATA : M - 1` (unsigned), `ds_rsub_u32` gives `DATA - M` (wrapping), and `ds_{min,max}_f32` use GLSL.std.450 `NMin`/`NMax` (NaN returns the non-NaN operand, `-0.0 < +0.0`, matching `global_atomic_fmin`/`fmax`). The `_rtn_` forms return the pre-op word in `vdst`; inactive lanes skip the op and preserve `vdst`. Source: AMD RDNA2 ISA DS encoding. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_atomics_cas.py` (edge value grids, NaN/±0/±inf, `offset:`, `vdst` aliasing, inactive lanes, sequential wraparound, Workgroup CAS loop structure, outside-compute and bad-operand rejections). Measured on native gfx1100 (single thread, 28 cases): `ds_inc_u32` gives `M + 1` until `M >= DATA` and 0 from there, `ds_dec_u32` gives `M - 1` or `DATA` when `M == 0` or `M > DATA`, `ds_rsub_u32` wraps, the `_rtn_` forms return the pre-op word, and `ds_{min,max}_f32` take the non-NaN operand and rank `-0.0` below `+0.0`. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b32`, `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b32` | Two atomic exchanges, addressed as `ds_write2_b32` addresses its dwords: `vaddr + offset0 * 4` and `vaddr + offset1 * 4` (times 64 for st64). `M[A0] = data0` and `M[A1] = data1`, and `vdst` (a VGPR pair) gets the two old words. Both exchanges run before either result is written, so `vdst` may alias the address or data VGPRs. When both offsets name one dword, the second exchange returns `data0`. Each exchange is atomic on its own, not the pair together, as on hardware (two DS operations). Until 2026-11-22 both ops raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_wrxchg2.py` (offsets, st64 = 256-byte steps, the same dword twice, aliasing, a single-VGPR `vdst` rejected). Measured on native gfx1100: with `vaddr` 16 the offsets 0/4 read `vaddr + 0` and `vaddr + 16`, the `_st64_` form's offsets 1/2 read `vaddr + 256` and `vaddr + 512` (64 dwords per step), `vdst` takes the two old words, and with both offsets on one dword the second exchange returns `data0`; `vdst` may alias the address or the data pair, which still lands both data words in LDS and leaves the old words in `vdst`. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `ds_wrxchg_rtn_b64`, `ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b64`, `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b64` | 64-bit LDS exchanges (two dwords, low first): `M[A] = DATA`, and `vdst` gets the old 64 bits. `wrxchg_rtn_b64` has one element at `vaddr + offset`. The `2` forms have two, at `vaddr + offset0/offset1 * 8` (times 64 for st64), as `ds_write2_b64` addresses them, and `vdst` is four VGPRs. Each dword is its own 32-bit `OpAtomicExchange`, without Int64 atomics on Workgroup memory. All exchanges run before `vdst` is written. **Approximation:** two lanes exchanging into one element at once can tear it, leaving one's low dword with the other's high dword (A.lo, B.lo, B.hi, A.hi), which a 64-bit exchange never does; and the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done (as for `ds_and/or/xor_b64`). Until 2026-11-22 these three raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_wrxchg_b64.py` (values, `offset:`, `vdst` aliasing the data, both 2 forms, st64 = 512-byte steps, bad operands rejected). Measured on native gfx1100: `M[vaddr]` takes the data pair and `vdst` gets the old pair, the `2` form's offsets 0/2 read `vaddr + 0` and `vaddr + 16` with four old dwords in `vdst`, and the `_st64_` form's offsets 1/2 read `vaddr + 512` and `vaddr + 1024` (64 pairs per step). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `ds_and_b64`, `ds_or_b64`, `ds_xor_b64` (and `_rtn_`) | `M = M op DATA` on the 64-bit LDS element at `vaddr + offset` (two dwords, low first); the `_rtn_` form returns the old 64 bits in a VGPR pair. A bitwise op works on each bit alone, so it is emitted as one 32-bit `OpAtomicAnd/Or/Xor` per dword. That leaves exactly the memory a 64-bit atomic would, without Int64 atomics on Workgroup memory (optional in Vulkan). **Approximation:** the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done; the memory result is exact. The other 64-bit LDS atomics (`add/sub/min/max_u64`, `wrxchg_b64`, `cmpst_b64`) carry between dwords or need the pair to move together, and still raise `Unsupported`. Until 2026-11-22 these three were rejected as well. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_bitwise_b64.py` (all six forms, offsets, `vdst` aliasing the data, seeded values). **GPU-verified** on native RDNA2 (gfx1036, the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 7 9800X3D, Windows driver 32.0.21042.62) through `tools/hw_oracle`: 39 of 39 one-thread cases (all six forms on three 64-bit M/DATA pairs, `offset:` 0 and 24, and `vdst` aliasing the data pair for each `_rtn_` op) leave M op DATA in both dwords and return the old pair. 32 lanes `ds_xor_rtn_b64` with (1 << lane) in both dwords on one element ran in lane order: lane i returned (2^i − 1) in both words, so the hardware never shows a half-done pair, unlike the two-read emission under contention. **Not GPU-verified.** Measured on native gfx1100 for all six forms, both dwords of each pair, with the returning forms giving the old pair. |
| `ds_bpermute_b32` subgroup convergence | Native wave32 and wave64 apply bitwise masks to the shuffle result and the destination write so both the ballot and shuffle execute before lane predication. If the ballot has no source lane, the shuffle reads the calling lane before the result is masked to zero. MoltenVK 1.4.2 lowered the scalar selects to nested Metal ternaries, placing the subgroup operations in divergent control flow. The `cs_bpermute32` inactive-source case returned 64 on an Apple M1 Max, and all eight fixture words match with bitwise masking. |
| LDS 2-address b64 (`ds_read2_b64`, `ds_write2_b64`, `ds_read2st64_b64`, `ds_write2st64_b64`) | Two elements at vaddr + offset0·unit and vaddr + offset1·unit, unit = 8 bytes (×64 for st64); each element is two consecutive dwords, low first. `read2_b64` fills v[d:d+3] after all four loads (a vdst overlapping vaddr is safe); `write2_b64` takes two VGPR pairs. The b32 forms share the code with unit 4. Source: RDNA2 ISA DS 2-address forms. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_lds_read2_write2_b64.py` (compute listings, LDS as a Workgroup variable); GPU-checked by `runtime/test/shaders/cs_lds_b64_cmpst.asm` (unit 8 and the 512-byte st64 forms, plus vdst/vaddr overlap, on an RTX 3070, driver 621.01, Windows 11; the same eight words also on an AMD RX 7600 (`gfx1102`) and a Ryzen 5 9600X iGPU (`gfx1036`, RDNA2, RADV Mesa 27.0.0-devel)). |
| Compute workgroup IDs Y/Z | With four user SGPRs and only TGID_Y and TGID_Z enabled (RSRC2 `0x308`), s4 receives Y and s5 receives Z. The synthetic `cs_workgroup_yz.asm` fixture checks a 2 × 3 × 4 dispatch and each of its 24 origins against a CPU reference. |
| Typed image stores | `image_store` (2D, 2D array, 3D) writes the dmask components (missing ones = 0). The image's numeric type lives in the T# (word 1 FORMAT [28:20], 512 GFX10 formats), so it is decided at run time. A 512-bit table built from `tools/gfx10_formats.json` (30 UINT and 15 SINT formats) picks a **sint**, **uint** or **float** view (bindings 8 + 3·dim + class; dim 0 = 2D, 1 = 2D array, 2 = 3D; class 0 float, 1 uint, 2 sint). Views have Unknown format (`shaderStorageImageWriteWithoutFormat`). **The runtime binds every storage image into all three arrays of its dimension, or at least the one matching its format.** The earlier path wrote only component 0 as `r32ui`, which was wrong for every other format. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_imgstore.asm` through `runtime/test/vk_store_test.cpp`: rgba8 unorm, r32 uint and r32 sint images, every texel correct. |
| Typed image loads | A noncoherent cube `image_load` reads the 2D-array sampled binding (3) with integer coordinates and layer. Its T# format selects the float, uint or sint alias, including for D16. The [RDNA2 ISA §8.2.1](https://docs.amd.com/v/u/en-US/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture) assigns D16 conversion to the image format. The synthetic `cube_load_d16.asm` checks the generated views and format choices on the CPU. `image_load` on 1D/2D/2D-array/3D images uses the same T# FORMAT tables as stores, and fetches every format with `OpImageFetch` from the sampled array of its dimension (bindings 0, 3, 6, 5): UINT/SINT formats through **uint/sint aliases of that array** (same set and binding, an `OpTypeImage` of `%uint`/`%int`; Vulkan allows several variables on one binding when the descriptor type fits), everything else through the float array. A lookup miss (index 0) takes the float path, since the fallback there is RGBA8_UNORM. Results are the raw bits of the dmask components. **The runtime binds each image's view in its own format at its index of the sampled array** (what `bind_image` in `runtime/shims/d3d12_vulkan_device.cpp` already does for SRVs and UAVs), so the view's numeric type matches the alias that reads it. No storage image is read, so shaders that only load need neither `shaderStorageImageReadWithoutFormat` (missing on Intel Xe-LP, #619) nor `shaderStorageImageWriteWithoutFormat` (SHADER-151). Until 2026-11-22 integer formats were read with `OpImageRead` from the uint/sint storage arrays (bindings 8–19), which hold only unordered-access views and 256 entries: a read-only integer texture found the fallback there, or an index past the array's end. Like the float path, the fetch goes through the texture path, so it sees writes made before the dispatch, not ones made earlier in the same dispatch. **d16 loads take the same branch** (#1136) and pack the dmask components two per VGPR, low half first: f16 (round to nearest even, integer ops, no `PackHalf2x16`) from the float array, the low 16 bits of each value from the uint/sint aliases (truncated). **d16 loads take the same branch** (#1136) and pack the dmask components two per VGPR, low half first: f16 (round to nearest even, integer ops, no `PackHalf2x16`) from the float array, each value from the uint/sint aliases saturated to the 16-bit range of its signedness (`saturate_int16`). For odd component counts (1 or 3 in `dmask`), the trailing destination VGPR preserves its existing high 16 bits (`write_lo16`), matching native `gfx1036` hardware where a seeded `0x5a5a` high half survived 1-component UINT/SINT `image_load` and `image_load_mip`; until 2026-11-22 the unused high half was zeroed. Before #1136, a d16 load always read the float view, so a `Texture2D<min16uint>` `Load` returned garbage. CPU-checked by `tests/test_image_load_typed.py` (capabilities, bindings and component types for every dimension; the branch for all 512 FORMAT values, found and missed). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_imgload.asm` through `vk_store_test load`, on a device made without `shaderStorageImageReadWithoutFormat`: r32 uint and r32 sint images filled before the dispatch, both read with `image_load` and their sum stored, every texel exact on an RTX 2080 Ti (NVIDIA 621.14, Windows 11), clean under the validation layer with GPU-assisted validation; the test fails a module that declares `StorageImageReadWithoutFormat`. d16: CPU-checked by `tests/test_image_load_d16_typed.py` (the uint/sint fetches go through the aliases, no storage image is read, the SPIR-V validates); GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_imgload_d16.asm` through `vk_store_test load`: both pre-filled texels read with d16 loads (the sint one sign-extended from bit 15 in the shader) and their sum stored, every texel exact on the RTX 2080 Ti with GPU-assisted validation (#1136 checked its first form, a store and d16 read-back in one dispatch, on an RTX 5070, and `cs_imgload_d16` passes unchanged on an AMD RX 7600 (gfx1102, RADV Mesa 27.0.0-devel) in `run_gpu_tests.py`). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_imgload_d16.asm` through `vk_store_test load`: both pre-filled texels read with d16 loads (the sint one sign-extended from bit 15 in the shader) and their sum stored, every texel exact on the RTX 2080 Ti with GPU-assisted validation (#1136 checked its first form, a store and d16 read-back in one dispatch, on an RTX 5070). 2D `image_load_mip` uses the same numeric-type selection and passes the address register's mip to each fetch. Its D16 form packs the selected components in ascending order, two per VGPR. An RGBA8 two-level texture was checked on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics: all four exported halves matched the CPU reference for UINT, SINT and UNORM, and forcing level 0 changed every pixel. Checked on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics with R8G8B8A8 UINT, SINT and UNORM textures: level 1 matched the CPU reference at every pixel with GPU-assisted validation, and forcing level 0 changed all 4096 pixels for each format. Native gfx1036 saturated 32-bit UINT/SINT texels to the unsigned/signed 16-bit range in both D16 load forms; the translated 2D load matched both limits on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics. Native gfx1036 does the same on every dimension (SHADER-387, `tools/image_d16_probe.py`): 32_UINT and 32_32_UINT texels saturate to [0, 0xffff] and 32_SINT and 32_32_SINT texels to [-32768, 32767], never truncate, in 1D, 2D, 2D-array and 3D images, through `image_load` and `image_load_mip` alike; two-channel results pack x low and y high, and a one-channel result keeps the seeded high half. The translated d16 loads on 1D, 2D-array and 3D images still lack GPU checks. |
| Packed 2D mip-load addresses | For `image_load_mip` with `a16`, the first VGPR holds unsigned X (low half) and Y (high half). The low half of the second VGPR selects the mip and its high half is unused. Both ordinary and D16 results use the same address. LLVM gfx1030 assembles both forms in `tests/semantic/fixtures/packed_mip_load_2d.asm`. `tests/test_image_load_typed.py` checks the texel and mip operands of the float, UINT and SINT fetches, including noncontiguous address registers. [AMD RDNA 2 ISA, MIMG A16 field](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) |
| 3D explicit-mip loads | `image_load_mip` reads x, y, z and mip from four address VGPRs. T# FORMAT selects the float, UINT or SINT 3D sampled view at binding 6. D16 narrows the selected components before packing them low half first. The unit test `test_volume_mip_fetch_uses_xyz_level_and_descriptor_type` in `tests/test_image_load_typed.py` checks the four address registers and the RGBA32 format selection on the translated module. Native gfx1036 measured UINT and SINT saturation for 3D `image_load_mip` at mip zero. [Volume load measurements](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/2217) |
| Typed 2D gathers | `image_gather4_lz(_o)` takes one channel from four taps. For `image_gather4_l(_o)` the four manual fetches share the rounded, clamped mip and addressed footprint. The T# FORMAT selects the float, UINT or SINT sampled image at binding 0. For d16, integer taps are packed low half first, two per VGPR; float taps round to f16. \| AMD RDNA 2 ISA §8.2 (MIMG D16). RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics matched a CPU clamp-to-edge gather over all 4096 RGBA32F pixels for 8-bit UINT, SINT and UNORM, both packed and unpacked, with zero offset and with (+1,-1). Selecting red in place of green changed all 4096 pixels. Explicit LOD 1 on two-level UINT, SINT and UNORM images also matched all 4096 pixels for both offset forms on both GPUs. Forcing mip 0 changed every pixel. Native gfx1036 saturates out-of-range integer taps as in the typed image loads below. On the CPU, the unit tests `test_explicit_lod_gather_fetches_four_taps_from_each_typed_view` and `test_2d_gather_picks_the_sampled_type_and_selected_component` in `tests/test_image_load_typed.py` check the typed views at binding 0, the selected component, the shared mip footprint and the d16 packing. |
| Dynamic sampled descriptor indices | Set 0 bindings 0 (2D), 3 (2D array and cube), 5 (1D) and 6 (3D), including their integer sampled aliases, use runtime T# hash results for sampling, gather, texel fetch and image queries. Binding 1 uses runtime S# hash results for sampling, gather and LOD queries. Explicit-LOD gather emulation takes its sampler state directly from S# words and uses sampled-image fetches. Literal descriptor words and the zero-on-miss fallback still go through the runtime lookup. Emit `SampledImageArrayDynamicIndexing` and record `shaderSampledImageArrayDynamicIndexing` in the shader index whenever such an array is indexed. A storage-only shader can declare sampled arrays without indexing them. The hash table is one storage buffer containing an array of words. T# and S# operands are scalar across an RDNA2 wave (AMD RDNA 2 ISA, MIMG fields SRSRC/SSAMP). The Vulkan contract requires the resulting index to be dynamically uniform in the subgroup or invocation group, and in the invocation group when the effective subgroup size is one. If wave and Vulkan execution mappings break that uniformity, the affected accesses need separate non-uniform indexing support. Confirmed by the synthetic `sampled_dynamic_index.asm` regression (`test_sample_with_literal_sampler_still_indexes_both_arrays_dynamically` and `test_fetch_and_query_paths` in `tests/semantic/test_sampled_dynamic_index.py`), `spirv-val`, and the declaration-only GPU positive control in [#2806](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/2806#issuecomment-7083836498). [Vulkan VUID 10128](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/appendices/spirvenv.html#VUID-StandaloneSpirv-SampledImageArrayDynamicIndexing-10128) and [runtime VUIDs 10135 and 10142](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/RuntimeSpirv.html#VUID-RuntimeSpirv-SampledImageArrayNonUniformIndexing-10135). |
| Half-precision coordinates for LOD queries | For `image_get_lod a16` on 2D and 2D-array images, the low and high halves of the first address VGPR supply U and V. The array layer is excluded from `OpImageQueryLod`; the selected clamped and raw LOD outputs are f32. `tests/semantic/fixtures/get_lod_a16.asm` assembles for gfx1030 and checks the query operands and output components through `tests/test_image_load_typed.py`. [AMD RDNA2 ISA, MIMG](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) |
| Dynamic storage image descriptor indices | The runtime T# hash result indexes the r32ui arrays (bindings 4 and 7) used by `OpImageTexelPointer` for image atomics and the typed storage arrays (bindings 8 through 19) used by `OpImageWrite` for stores and `OpImageRead` for coherent loads. Literal T# words still go through the hash table. Emit `StorageImageArrayDynamicIndexing` and record `shaderStorageImageArrayDynamicIndexing` in the shader index when a storage array is accessed this way. Plain typed loads use sampled aliases and follow the sampled indexing rule. The same Vulkan dynamic-uniformity contract applies to storage indices. Confirmed by [Vulkan VUID 10130](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/appendices/spirvenv.html#VUID-StandaloneSpirv-StorageImageArrayDynamicIndexing-10130), the synthetic `storage_dynamic_index.asm` regression (`test_literal_storage_descriptor_and_mixed_sampled_load`, `test_storage_aliases_in_every_dimension` and `test_atomic_texel_pointers_use_storage_indices` in `tests/semantic/test_sampled_dynamic_index.py`) and `spirv-val`. |
| Image atomics, size and LOD queries | `image_atomic_add/sub/and/or/xor/smin/smax/umin/umax` are `OpAtomicIAdd/ISub/And/Or/Xor/SMin/SMax/UMin/UMax` on the texel (`OpImageTexelPointer`) of an r32ui storage image, binding 4 for 2D and 7 for 3D, at the T#'s index (the view `StorageViews::r32ui` of `runtime/src/vulkan_bindless.h`). The data VGPR is the operand; with `glc` it receives the texel's previous value, without it it is not written (RDNA2 ISA, MIMG: the previous value is returned if glc is 1). `image_atomic_cmpswap` takes two data VGPRs, the new value and then the comparand (`OpAtomicCompareExchange`). A lane that EXEC turns off skips the access entirely (`OpAccessChain`, `OpImageTexelPointer`, `OpAtomic*`, and any `glc` write-back sit inside `if (active)`), leaving memory and `vdata` untouched. Two-VGPR `dmask:0x3` forms of the nine standard ops currently still operate on the low 32-bit dword as before; true 64-bit image atomics remain separate follow-up work. `image_get_resinfo` (2D, no sampler) gives, for the mip level in its address VGPR, width, height, depth (0 for 2D; a 2D array's layers minified from BASE_LEVEL, T# word 3 [15:12]) and the level count in VGPRs 0..3, with width, height and depth 0 past the last level (the size isn't queried there) (ISA: {num_mip_levels, depth, height, width} in VGPRs 3..0; `image_get_resinfo` (2D, no sampler) gives, for the mip level in its address VGPR, width, height, depth (1) and the level count in VGPRs 0..3 (ISA: {num_mip_levels, depth, height, width} in VGPRs 3..0; `OpImageQuerySizeLod`, `OpImageQueryLevels`). `image_get_lod` (pixel shaders; 2D and 2D array, the slice ignored) gives the clamped LOD and the calculated LOD in VGPRs 0..1 (ISA: {rawLOD, clampedLOD} in VGPRs 1..0; `OpImageQueryLod`). In both, dmask keeps components and packs them into consecutive VGPRs. GPU-checked (TEST-184) on an RX 7900 XTX and a Ryzen 9 7950X3D integrated GPU (RADV, Mesa 26.3.1) and on Mesa llvmpipe, with the expected words derived by hand in each `.asm` header: `cs_imgatomic.asm` (every op on a 2D image: 0xfffffff0 tells the signed min/max from the unsigned ones, each with a case that goes the other way; cmpswap hit and miss; an op without `glc`; 63 lanes off; 64 threads adding to one texel) and `cs_imgatomic3d.asm` (six cases on a 4x4x4 volume, the three coordinates all different), through `vk_store_test words`; `cs_resinfo.asm` (a 16x4 image with five levels: levels 0, 1 and 3 in full, then dmask 0x3, 0xa and 0x8); `ps_get_lod.asm` and `ps_get_lod_array.asm` through `vk_texture_test` (calculated LOD 1 and 3 on the two-mip texture, the clamped LOD held at the last level, and at 0 by a sampler MAX_LOD of 0.25; 2 and -3 on the one-level array). Measured natively on RDNA2 (gfx1036, SHADER-388: `tools/image_query_probe.py`, 614 results in wave32 and wave64, all as follows). resinfo's depth word is 0 for a 2D image whatever T# WORD4 DEPTH holds; for a 2D array it is the layer count minified like a 3D depth, max(1, layers >> (BASE_LEVEL + level)). Past the last level, width, height and depth are 0. The level count is LAST_LEVEL - BASE_LEVEL + 1 and sizes count from BASE_LEVEL. The translator follows all three (`Gen.resinfo_words`; `cs_resinfo.asm` checks the 2D depth word and level 5 of five on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics, `tests/test_translate.py` the array rule). The translator still differs in the first three: it writes 1 for the 2D depth word and the full layer count for a 2D array, and `OpImageQuerySizeLod` past the last level is undefined. `image_get_lod`'s raw LOD includes the S#'s LOD_BIAS. Its clamped LOD is min(max(min(raw, MAX_LOD), MIN_LOD), levels - 1) under a linear mip filter (MIN_LOD wins when it is above MAX_LOD), that value rounded half up (the level read) under a point mip filter, and 0 with MIP_FILTER none: `OpImageQueryLod`'s definition. u is scaled by the width and v by the height (a 64x4 image). Unverified: resinfo's depth word on hardware (1 is what the T#'s DEPTH field says for a 2D image), the clamped LOD under a linear mip filter (the test's sampler is point-filtered, where 0.25 selects level 0), and the order of u and v in the LOD query (the test textures are square, where it makes no difference). `image_get_resinfo` on other dimensions is `Unsupported`. A host quirk the LOD test steps around: Mesa llvmpipe 26.3.1 does not apply a sampler's LOD range to `OpImageQueryLod` when its minimum and maximum are equal (0..0 gave level 1), so the second draw uses 0..0.25. |
| `image_atomic_{swap,inc,dec,fmin,fmax,fcmpswap}` | The six remaining 32-bit MIMG image atomics on 2D (binding 4) and 3D (binding 7) `r32ui` storage images (including `a16` packed coordinates and optional `glc` return), guarded by `active_b` so inactive lanes skip memory access and preserve `vdata`. `image_atomic_swap` emits `OpAtomicExchange`; `inc`/`dec`/`fmin`/`fmax`/`fcmpswap` emit `OpAtomicLoad` (valid on `OpImageTexelPointer` `Image` pointers) followed by a Device-scope `OpAtomicCompareExchange` CAS loop (`inc`: `(M >= D) ? 0 : M + 1`; `dec`: `(M == 0 \|\| M > D) ? D : M - 1`; `fmin`/`fmax`: GLSL.std.450 `NMin`/`NMax`, no float-atomic extension required; `fcmpswap`: integer IEEE-754 binary32 ordered equality on raw 32-bit words — equal iff neither is NaN and `(cur == cmp) \|\| (both ±0.0)`, preserving subnormals even on Vulkan drivers reporting `shaderDenormPreserveFloat32 = false`, exiting without writing on mismatch or NaN; native RDNA2 subnormal behavior under denormal flush is unmeasured). Source: AMD RDNA2 ISA MIMG `IMAGE_ATOMIC_*`. CPU-checked structurally (including `spirv_mini_interp.check_structure` CFG validation) by `tests/semantic/test_image_atomics_32.py` (all 6 ops on 2D and 3D images, `a16`, `glc` and non-`glc`, no `AtomicFloat` capability, rejected widths/coords/dims). **Not GPU-verified on RDNA2.** |
| Hand-written short assembler forms | `llvm-mc` accepts `v_add_co_ci_u32 vD, src0, src1` (also `v_sub_co_ci_u32`, `v_subrev_co_ci_u32`) with the carry out and in left to VCC, and a modifier list shorter than the operands (`op_sel_hi:[0,0]`, `neg_lo:[1,0]` on a three-source op), whose missing entries are 0; it prints them as `v_add_co_ci_u32_e32 vD, vcc_lo, src0, src1, vcc_lo` and `[0,0,0]`. The GPU runner assembles through `llvm-mc -show-encoding`, so it sees the printed form; `tests/semantic/regen_fixtures.py` copies the `.asm` text as written, so the translator takes the source spelling too (it used to raise `IndexError` for these: the carry forms, `v_pk_fma_f16`, `v_fma_mix_*`). A probe of 42,000 operand and modifier variations of 302 ALU mnemonics, in the source spelling and as `llvm-mc` prints them, found no other `IndexError`, `KeyError` or `TypeError`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_short_asm_forms.py`. |
| SDWA | `_sdwa` forms: each source is narrowed to `src0_sel`/`src1_sel` (BYTE_n/WORD_n, zero-extended, or sign-extended under `sext()`) **before** neg/abs and the op. A VGPR result goes to `dst_sel`, with `dst_unused` setting the other bits: PAD = 0, SEXT = sign copies above the field, PRESERVE = old bits kept. A byte or word destination with PRESERVE reads its incoming VGPR on first use. The synthetic `sdwa_bary_input.asm` checks this with two raw fragment weights, following the [LLVM SDWA destination rules](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUModifierSyntax.html#dst-unused). VOPC has no `dst_sel`, and GFX10 `v_cmpx` has no written destination. **Until 2026-11-20 the selects were silently ignored.** That affected 3,826 of the 13,926 translated shaders: they passed `spirv-val` but computed wrong values. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_sdwa.asm` and `ps_ops.asm` (also `v_pk_fma_f16`, `v_min3_u32`, `v_max3_i32`, `v_bfe_i32`): byte/word source selects, `sext()`, `UNUSED_PRESERVE` into WORD_1 and `UNUSED_SEXT` at BYTE_2 all match on every pixel. **Measured natively on RDNA2** (gfx1036 iGPU, `tools/hw_oracle`, the game's MODE): all 172 distinct SDWA forms of the 26,503 listings (79,307 instructions: opcode, operand kinds, sels, `sext`, neg/abs, `dst_unused`, omod), 12 operand words per source, 21,204 cases. 18,885 give the translator's word. The rest are: a 16-bit result with `dst_sel:DWORD`, where the hardware zeroes the high half and the translator keeps it (1,888, #2187); a NaN result, where the hardware keeps the input's sign and payload (826, of which 556 also fall in the first group, so the groups add up to 21,204; as in the f16 transcendentals row); `div:2` on -0, which gives +0 (1, as in the output-modifier rows); and 160 cases whose translation is undefined in SPIR-V (`FClamp`/`FDiv`/`Sqrt` on NaN, zero or negative operands). `tests/semantic/test_alu_sdwa_hw.py` replays 624 of the matching words, up to four per form. |
| VOP3 output and source modifiers in the game's forms, on RDNA2 | Every distinct float VALU form in the 26,503 listings that carries `clamp`, an omod (`mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2`), `op_sel`/`op_sel_hi` or neg/abs: 572 forms, 1,270,932 instructions (`v_mul_legacy_f32 … clamp` alone is 147,499; `v_fma_mix_f32` has 222 forms), up to 120 random operand triples each, run natively on gfx1036 (`tools/hw_oracle`, the game's MODE) and through the translator: 542 forms, 64,802 cases (the GFX10.1-only `v_mad_f32`/`v_mad_legacy_f32`/`v_mac_f32` and `v_permlanex16` are left out). 51,761 give the translator's word. The rest are differences already on record: NaN sign and payload (5,074); f32 denormal operands or results, and a denormal intermediate that the hardware flushes before the omod or clamp (1,178; SHADER-377); an omod turning a -0 result into +0 (121; the Output modifiers rows, #1281); 1 ulp or the known sin/cos precision in `v_rcp/v_rcp_iflag/v_log/v_sin/v_cos` with an omod (56); and 6,430 whose translation is undefined in SPIR-V, mostly `FClamp` of a NaN, where the hardware gives +0 under DX10_CLAMP (#2437). 182 cases were my harness's (three distinct SGPRs, over GFX10's two-read limit). No other modifier difference. `tests/semantic/test_alu_vop3_mods_hw.py` replays 1,616 matching words, up to three per form; it fails without `clamp` (326 rows) or without the omod (237). |
| Byte permute | `v_perm_b32`: result byte i = BYTE_PERMUTE({src0, src1}, selector byte i), src1 = bytes 0–3 (AMD "RDNA 2" ISA reference, VOP3 opcode 836 V_PERM_B32, `byte_permute` pseudocode, page 189 of the PDF). Selector 0–7 picks that byte; **8, 9, 10, 11 give 0xff if bit 7 of byte 1, 3, 5, 7 is set, else 0x00**; 12 gives 0x00; 13 and above give 0xff. **Until 2026-11-21 selectors 8–11 gave 0x00 regardless of the sign bit**. In the 26,503-shader disassembled corpus (measured via `tools/feature_scan.py`), 0 of 10,500 shaders (11,930 `v_perm_b32` instructions) use selectors 8–11 or >= 13: all 11,258 immediate and 672 constant-register selectors (0 unresolved) use only bytes 0–7 (36,234) and 12 (11,486). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_ops7.asm` (also `v_alignbit_b32` with a shift of 36, `v_med3_i32` vs `v_med3_u32`) and `ps_ops8.asm` (`v_mul_hi_u32/i32`, `v_bcnt_u32_b32`, `v_ffbl_b32`, `v_ffbh_u32` of 0, `v_xad_u32`, `v_add3_u32`), 4096/4096 pixels on an AMD Radeon iGPU (Ryzen 7 9800X3D, RDNA2, AMD driver 25.10.36) and an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 (driver 621.24); before the fix `ps_ops7` gave 0x00000000 for the sign-selector word on both. Also executed by `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`. When S2 is a literal or a currently known SGPR constant, the translator emits fixed byte extracts/inserts (including sign replication), folds the zero and 0xff bytes, and uses the selected source directly for a whole-word identity. `tests/semantic/test_perm_known_selector.py` checks the resulting words and that selector dispatch only remains for a clobbered SGPR. |
| Sum of absolute differences (`v_sad_u8`, `v_sad_hi_u8`, `v_sad_u16`, `v_sad_u32`, `v_msad_u8`) | Unsigned, wrapping at 32 bits, with ABSDIFF(x, y) = `UMax − UMin`: `v_sad_u8` = S2 + the sum over the four bytes; `v_sad_hi_u8` = (that byte sum << 16) + S2; `v_sad_u16` = S2 + the sum over the two halves; `v_sad_u32` = \ \| S0 − S1\ \| + S2; `v_msad_u8` skips a byte where S1's byte is 0. `clamp` raises `Unsupported`: the ISA pseudocode doesn't say what it does to these ops. Source: RDNA2 ISA VOP3 opcodes 346–349 and 369 (pseudocode re-read for this row). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_sad.py` (#544): a 9×9 grid of edge words per op, 40 seeded cases, masked bytes, an SGPR source, `clamp` rejected; the PR also reports a 300-case differential run per op with 0 mismatches. None of them is in the translated corpus, and none is GPU-checked. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137). Measured on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, RDNA2) through tools/hw_oracle across edge byte/half patterns, zero masks, and wrap cases: all match the ISA reference with 0 mismatches. |
| Quad-byte SAD 64-bit source operands | The GFX10.3 `null` operand supplies zero for the 64-bit input window in all three quad-byte SAD operations and for the 64-bit addend of the two packed forms. Confirmed with LLVM gfx1030 assembly and scalar byte-difference references in `tests/semantic/test_alu_qsad.py` (`test_null_pixel_source_and_null_accumulator`) and `tests/semantic/test_alu_mqsad_u32.py` (`test_null_pixel_source`). |
| `v_mqsad_u32_u8` | Four masked byte-SADs with full 32-bit accumulators: D[k] = S2[k] + `v_msad_u8` of the 32-bit window S0[8k+31:8k] of the 64-bit S0 against S1, wrapping at 32 bits (S2 and D are four VGPRs). Every source is read before the first write; `clamp` raises `Unsupported`. Source: RDNA ISA pseudocode. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_mqsad_u32.py`. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137). Measured on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, RDNA2) through tools/hw_oracle across 64-bit sliding window and masked byte values against the 32-bit accumulators: bit-exact with the ISA reference. |
| `s_sext_i32_i8`, `s_sext_i32_i16`, `s_flbit_i32` | The first two sign-extend the low byte or the low half of S0 to 32 bits (`OpBitFieldSExtract`). `s_flbit_i32` counts how many bits in a row from the MSB equal the sign bit, and gives −1 when all bits are the same (0 and −1); it is the scalar form of `v_ffbh_i32` and goes through the same code. None of the three writes SCC. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOP1 opcodes 23, 25 and 26. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_sext_flbit.py` (#549): edge words with SCC starting at 0 and at 1, and 30 seeded values with long runs of sign bits. Not in the translated corpus, not GPU-checked. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137, SCC kept). Measured on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, RDNA2) through tools/hw_oracle and tools/salu_diff.py (500 programs, --oracle translator,hw): 500 pass, 0 mismatches against the reference; confirmed s_flbit_i32 gives 0xFFFFFFFF for 0 and -1. |
| `v_lerp_u8` | Per byte i: `D.b[i] = (S0.b[i] + S1.b[i] + (S2.b[i] & 1)) >> 1`, an unsigned average where bit 0 of S2's byte decides whether 0.5 rounds up. The translator uses the per-byte identity `x + y = 2*(x & y) + (x ^ y)`. It clears each byte's low bit before shifting, then adds the rounding bit only to an odd sum. Each result fits in one byte, so the packed additions do not carry across bytes. The 9-bit sum shifted right by 1 fits back in its byte, so nothing carries between bytes. `clamp` raises `Unsupported` (the ISA doesn't define it for this op). Source: RDNA2 ISA VOP3 opcode 333. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_lerp_u8.py` (#577): a 9×9×5 grid of edge and rounding words, 40 seeded cases, an SGPR source with an inline S2, `clamp` rejected. The synthetic `tests/semantic/fixtures/lerp_packed_bytes.asm` gives exact words across a 64x64 draw on an RTX 3070 (NVIDIA 591.86). Not in the translated corpus, not GPU-checked. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137). Measured on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, RDNA2) through tools/hw_oracle across byte edge values and rounding bit variations: 0 mismatches against the ISA reference. |
| Scalar select and pack (`s_cmov_b32`, `s_cmovk_i32`, `s_cmov_b64`, `s_pack_ll/lh/hh_b32_b16`) | `s_cmov_b32` / `s_cmovk_i32`: D = SCC ? S0 : D (`s_cmovk_i32`'s S0 is the 16-bit immediate, sign-extended); SCC is read, not written; the destination must be an SGPR (a lane-mask destination stays `Unsupported`, #210). `s_cmov_b64`: D[63:0] = SCC ? S0[63:0] : D[63:0] on plain 64-bit data pairs (SGPR pair, immediate or `null`), SCC read and not written; a pair that is or has been a lane mask (EXEC, VCC or a mask SGPR) stays `Unsupported` (#210) — conservatively, because a CFG join can drop a register that is a mask on only one path — while 0/-1 EITHER constants are data; selecting a per-lane bit as data would silently return a bool instead of the wave's mask. D[63:0] on plain 64-bit data pairs (SGPR pair, immediate or `null`), SCC read and not written; a pair that is or has been a lane mask stays `Unsupported` (#210). D[63:0], SCC read and not written, on plain 64-bit data pairs (SGPR pair, immediate or `null`); any dword that is or has been a lane mask (EXEC, VCC or a mask SGPR) makes the pair `Unsupported` — conservatively, because a CFG join can drop a register that is a mask on only one path — while 0/-1 EITHER constants are data; selecting a per-lane bit as data would silently return a bool instead of the wave's mask (#210). `s_pack_*`: the low half of D from S0, the high half from S1, the letters naming the half of each (l = 15:0, h = 31:16), S0 first; GFX10 has no `s_pack_hl`; SCC not written. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2/SOPK and LLVM AMDGPU `SOPInstructions.td` (`S_CMOV_B64 : RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2/SOPK. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_cmov.py` and `test_salu_pack.py`; the translated shaders were run on NVIDIA by reviewers (`s_cmov*`: RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2/SOPK and LLVM AMDGPU [SOPInstructions.td](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/blob/2bfedf3338162e2c841a73cd7cd9e98d00add608/llvm/lib/Target/AMDGPU/SOPInstructions.td) (`S_CMOV_B64 : SOP1_64` under `Uses = [SCC]`). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_cmov.py`, `tests/semantic/test_salu_cmov_b64.py` and `test_salu_pack.py`; no corpus count; **not GPU-verified, no corpus count**; the translated shaders were run on NVIDIA by reviewers (`s_cmov_b32/k_i32`: **no corpus count**; Hardware: on native `gfx1036` (Ryzen 9 9950X3D iGPU, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1) `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --wave both` matches the translator in wave32 and wave64 for all five `salu_cmov_pack` cases and for the 14 `s_cmov_b64` cases (`--group salu_cmov_b64`: GPU-checked for `s_cmov_b32` (`cs_w32_salu64.asm`, SCC = 1 selects S0) and `s_pack_lh_b32_b16` (`cs_w32_int_ext.asm`) on the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D (RDNA2, `gfx1036`, driver 26.10.1) and an RTX 5080 (driver 616.92); `s_cmovk_i32`, `s_cmov_b64` and the other `s_pack_*` forms have no GPU test on main; the translated shaders were also run on NVIDIA by reviewers (`s_cmov_b32/k_i32`: RTX 5090, RTX 4050 and Radeon 740M in #613; `s_pack_*`: RTX 5090 in #449). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137, SCC kept), and on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1) through `tools/hw_oracle`: all five `salu_cmov_pack` cases and the 14 `s_cmov_b64` cases match the translator in wave32 and wave64 (SCC 0 and 1; an SGPR pair, the inline constants -1/-16/64, the literal 0x89abcdef, `null` and the destination itself; SCC = 1 takes S0, 0 keeps D, SCC is never written), `s_cmov_b32` matches under SCC=0 and SCC=1, and `s_pack_ll/lh/hh_b32_b16` is bit-exact on 500 random programs with 0 mismatches (`--oracle translator,hw`). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137, SCC kept). Also **measured on RDNA2 gfx1036** (Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1, `tools/hw_oracle` HIP launcher, rocm-sdk-core clang): `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --wave both` gives the same words as the translator for all five `salu_cmov_pack` cases in wave32 and wave64, and the 14 `s_cmov_b64` cases of the `salu_cmov_b64` group (`python -I tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --wave both --group salu_cmov_b64`; SCC 0 and 1; source an SGPR pair, the inline constants -1, -16 and 64, the literal 0x89abcdef, `null`, and the destination itself; SCC preset and read back after): SCC preset and read back after) match the translator in wave32 and wave64: SCC = 1 takes S0 (a literal zero-extended, an inline constant sign-extended, `null` gives 0), SCC = 0 keeps D, SCC is never written. Also on `gfx1035` via `int_hw_probe` (SHADER-137, SCC kept), and on `gfx1036` and an RTX 5080 through the fixtures `cs_w32_salu64.asm` (`s_cmov_b32`) and `cs_w32_int_ext.asm` (`s_pack_lh_b32_b16`). `tools/hw_oracle` + `tools/salu_diff.py` verify `s_cmov_b32` under SCC 0/1 and `s_pack_ll/lh/hh_b32_b16` bit-exact on 500 random programs with 0 mismatches. Reviewers ran `s_cmov*` on an RTX 5090, an RTX 4050 and a Radeon 740M (#613) and `s_pack_*` on an RTX 5090 (#449). Measured on native gfx1036 (AMD Radeon Graphics, RDNA2) through tools/hw_oracle and tools/salu_diff.py: s_cmov_b32 verified under SCC=0 and SCC=1 (preserving destination and updating respectively); s_pack_ll/lh/hh_b32_b16 verified bit-exact on 500 random programs with 0 mismatches (--oracle translator,hw). **Not measured on RDNA2.** |
| `v_bfi_b32` | `(S0 & S1) \| (~S0 & S2)` (RDNA2 ISA, VOP3 opcode 330). A literal or known same-block SGPR mask of zero or all ones selects S2 or S1 directly. A contiguous mask uses `OpBitFieldInsert` with S1 shifted down by the field offset, since RDNA2 reads the bits in place. For sparse constant masks the translator precomputes the complement. Runtime masks use the full bitwise expression. Checked by the hand-written `bfi_known_mask.asm` and `tests/semantic/test_bfi_known_mask.py`. |
| `v_bfm_b32` | The low five bits of S0 give the width and those of S1 give the offset. The result is the low 32 bits of `((1 << width) - 1) << offset` (RDNA2 ISA §12.12, VOP3 opcode 867). Literal and same-block SGPR constants are folded in the translator, including zero width and shifts that discard the high bits. Runtime operands keep their low-five-bit masks. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_bfm_known_operands.py` and `test_alu_int_misc.py`. |
| `s_bfe_u32` / `s_bfe_i32` past bit 31 | offset = S1[4:0], width = S1[22:16] (7 bits; S1 bits 23 to 31 are ignored). When offset + width > 32 the field stops at bit 31: width = min(width, 32 - offset), and `s_bfe_i32` sign-extends from the top bit that exists. Example: `s_bfe_i32 s4, 0xd2f90159, 0x7f001c` gives `0xfffffffd` (4-bit field `0xd`), not `0xd`. SCC = D ≠ 0. Source: measured on gfx1150 (Radeon 880M) with `tools/salu_diff.py --oracle hw`, 4000 random control words, all bit-exact, on gfx1201 (Radeon RX 9070, RDNA4) with 10,000 `salu_diff --oracle hw` programs, 0 mismatches (seed 500 with `--bfe-any`), and **on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon 680M, `gfx1035`: `tools/salu_diff.py --oracle hw,translator --bfe-any --programs 500 --seed 1`, 500/500 match, plus `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both` wave32 and wave64, SHADER-137; and RDNA2 `gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1, `tools/hw_oracle` HIP launcher: `tools/salu_diff.py --oracle translator,hw --bfe-any --ops s_bfe_u32,s_bfe_i32 --programs 2000` with seeds 1, 2 and 3, 6,000 of 6,000 programs pass on both sides, plus seed 4 over every opcode with `--bfe-any`, 2,000 of 2,000, and `int_hw_probe.py`'s `salu_bfe_past31` cases in wave32 and wave64); the translator (`bfe_width`) already did this. `tools/salu_diff.py --oracle hw,translator --bfe-any --programs 500 --seed 1`, 500/500 match, plus `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both` wave32 and wave64, SHADER-137); the translator (`bfe_width`) already did this. Source: measured on gfx1150 (Radeon 880M) with `tools/salu_diff.py --oracle hw`, 4000 random control words, all bit-exact, and on gfx1201 (Radeon RX 9070, RDNA4) with 10,000 `salu_diff --oracle hw` programs, 0 mismatches (seed 500 with `--bfe-any`); the translator (`bfe_width`) already did this. LLVM's v_bfe fold (`constantFoldBFE`) is analogous but has a 5-bit width. **RDNA2 is not measured**; the ISA pseudocode does not say. |
| 64-bit scalar data ops (`s_brev_b64`, `s_bfe_u64`/`s_bfe_i64`, `s_bitset0_b64`/`s_bitset1_b64`, `s_flbit_i32_b64`/`s_flbit_i32_i64`) | The SGPR pair is 64-bit data, as for `s_lshl_b64`; a pair that `lane_masks.py` marks as a lane mask (or EXEC) stays `Unsupported` (#210). `s_brev_b64`: D.lo = reverse(S0.hi), D.hi = reverse(S0.lo). `s_bfe_*64`: offset = S1[5:0], width = S1[22:16]; width 0 gives 0; a field past bit 63 stops there (the 64-bit form of the `s_bfe_u32` rule, LLVM `constantFoldBFE`); SCC = D ≠ 0. `s_bitset*_b64`: bit S0 & 63 of the pair cleared / set. `s_flbit_i32_b64`: zeros above the top 1 from bit 63, −1 for 0; `s_flbit_i32_i64`: the same of ~S0 when S0 < 0, −1 for 0 and −1. None but `s_bfe` writes SCC. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_brev_b64.py`, `test_salu_bfe_64.py`, `test_salu_bitset_b64.py`, `test_salu_flbit_64.py` (#723, #726, #729, #733). **Hardware-verified** on an AMD Radeon RX 7900 XTX (gfx1100, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1), one raw wave32 kernel per op through `tools/hw_oracle`: `s_brev_b64` of 0x12345678_0000000F has low word 0x1E6A2C48, `s_bfe_u64(0x00000123_45678000, w16 o28)` = 0x1234, `s_bfe_i64(0x00000001_80000000, w2 o31)` has 0xFFFFFFFF in both words, `s_bitset1_b64(0, 40)` has high word 0x100, `s_flbit_i32_b64(0x00010000)` = 47 and `s_flbit_i32_i64(0xFFFFFFFF_FFFF0000)` = 48. These are the words `runtime/test/shaders/cs_w32_salu64.asm` expects. Also **hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137). |
| `v_bfe_u32` / `v_bfe_i32` past bit 31 | offset = S1[4:0] and width = S2[4:0]: the bits above are ignored, so an offset of 36 reads as 4 and a width of 32 as 0 (a width of 0 gives 0). When offset + width > 32 the field stops at bit 31, as for `s_bfe`: width = min(width, 32 - offset), and `v_bfe_i32` sign-extends from bit 31. Example: `v_bfe_i32` of `0xd2f90159` with offset 28 and width 16 gives `0xfffffffd` (4-bit field `0xd`), not `0xd`, and `0x80000000` with offset 31 and width 4 gives `0xffffffff`. The translator (`bfe_width` for VGPR or SGPR offset and width, the same cut folded at translation time for constants) already did this. Source: **measured on RDNA2**, AMD Radeon 610M (`gfx1036`, Windows 11, AMD driver 32.0.11016.14003), wave32 and wave64 identical, 16 words over VGPR, inline-constant and literal operands: `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_bfe_past31`, 4/4 cases match the translator. The words are pinned on the CPU by the unit test `ValuBfeMeasuredOnRdna2.test_translator_gives_the_gfx1036_words` in `tests/test_int_hw_probe.py`. |
| `s_quadmask_b32`/`_b64`, `s_bitreplicate_b64_b32` | On scalar data (RDNA2 ISA SOP1): `s_quadmask_b32` sets D[i] to the OR of S0[4i+3:4i] for the 8 quads, `s_quadmask_b64` does the same for the 16 quads of the 64-bit S0 (D[63:16] = 0), and both set SCC = D != 0. `s_bitreplicate_b64_b32` copies each bit S0[i] to D[2i] and D[2i+1] and leaves SCC. Built from fixed shift/mask steps, with no loop. A lane-mask source (EXEC, VCC or an SGPR that `holds_lane_mask`) or an EXEC/VCC destination raises `Unsupported`: each invocation holds only its own lane's bit, and these ops read the other lanes of a quad or move bits between lanes. Until 2026-11-22 all three were rejected. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_quadmask_bitreplicate.py` (fixed and seeded values, SCC written or kept, immediates, aliasing, lane masks rejected). **Hardware-verified** on an AMD Radeon RX 7900 XTX (gfx1100, Windows 11, driver 26.10.1) through `tools/hw_oracle`, one raw wave32 kernel per op: `s_quadmask_b32(0xF)` = 1 with SCC 1, `(0xF0000000)` = 0x80, `(0x0F0F0F0F)` = 0x55 (the set quads are 0, 2, 4 and 6), `(0)` = 0 with SCC 0; `s_quadmask_b64` of 0xF = 1 and of 0xF0000000_00000000 (quad 15, bits 60..63) = 0x8000 in the low word with D[63:16] = 0; `s_bitreplicate_b64_b32` of 5 = 0x33 and of 0x80000000 = 0xC0000000_00000000, with SCC left as it was. **GPU-checked** by `runtime/test/shaders/cs_w32_new_ops.asm` (`s_quadmask_b32(0x01020408)` = 0x55, `s_quadmask_b64(0x80000000_00000001)` low word 0x8001, `s_bitreplicate_b64_b32(0x80000001)` high word 0xC0000000, with the expected words from the ISA pseudocode), exact on the Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 9 9950X3D (RDNA2, `gfx1036`, driver 26.10.1) and an RTX 5080 (driver 616.92). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| 64-bit scalar bitwise ops (`s_and_b64`, `s_or_b64`, `s_xor_b64`, `s_andn2_b64`, `s_orn2_b64`, `s_nand_b64`, `s_nor_b64`, `s_xnor_b64`, `s_not_b64`) | Dispatched like `s_cselect_b64`: when destination is `exec`/`vcc` or any source is a lane mask (`holds_lane_mask` or `exec`/`vcc`), uses the per-lane mask path (`mask_src`/`mask_dst` including `null` dst) and votes SCC across the wave (`scc_from_mask`). Otherwise operates on both 32-bit dwords of non-mask 64-bit scalar data (SGPR pairs, sign-extended inline/literal immediates, `null` = 0), reads all source dwords before storing (preserving destination aliasing), accepts `null` destination, and sets `SCC = ((D.lo \ \| D.hi) != 0)`. Until 2026-11-22 these ops always took the lane-mask path, duplicating D.lo into D.hi and voting SCC on D.lo alone. `s_nand/nor/xnor_b64` compute `~(S0 op S1)` on both paths (a lane's mask value is 0 or ~0, so the NOT is per lane); until 2026-11-22 they were rejected as unsupported. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu64_bitwise_data.py` (all 9 ops, 4-corner SCC, immediates, aliasing, stale mask overwrite, wave32/wave64 lane-mask regression, and `--wave64-emulate`); **Hardware-verified** on the same part through `tools/hw_oracle` for the plain-data path: `s_and_b64(0xFFFFFFFF_00000000, 0x0F0F0F0F_0F0F0F0F)` = 0x0F0F0000_00000000, `s_xnor_b64(0xFFFF0000_00000000, 0_FFFFFFFF)` = 0x0000FFFF_00000000, `s_nand_b64(0xFFFF0000_0, 0)` = 0xFFFFFFFF_FFFFFFFF, `s_not_b64(0x0000FFFF_0)` = 0xFFFF0000_FFFFFFFF and `s_andn2_b64(0xFFFF0000_0, 0)` = 0xFFFF0000_0, all with SCC 1, while `s_or_b64(0, 0)` = 0 with SCC 0: SCC = ((D.lo ≠ 0) or (D.hi ≠ 0)). | Constant lane masks (`Gen.mask_src` immediates) | A constant read as a lane mask is one bit per lane: lane L takes bit L. The 64-bit forms (`s_and/or/andn2_saveexec_b64`, `s_andn2_wrexec_b64`, `s_mov_b64`/`s_not_b64` into EXEC or VCC, `s_and/or/xor/andn2/orn2_b64` and `s_cselect_b64` on the lane-mask path) read the 64-bit value, an inline constant sign-extended (`-16` = lanes 4-63) and a literal with a zero high word, as `src64_data` reads 64-bit constants; the b32 forms (`s_*_saveexec_b32`, `s_andn2_wrexec_b32`) read the 32-bit value at bit L mod 32. 0 and -1 still give no lane and every lane, with no per-lane code. `s_bcnt0/1_i32_b64` and `s_ff0/1_i32_b64` of a constant read the constant's 64 bits via `src64_data` (scalar data: inline negative constants sign-extend to 64 bits, so `s_ff0_i32_b64 s0, -1` gives −1 rather than 32, and 32-bit literals zero-extend; CPU-checked in `tests/semantic/test_scalar_bitcmp.py`, **not yet GPU-verified**). `s_bcnt0/1_i32_b64` of a constant counts the constant's 64 bits (scalar data, like `s_ff0/1_i32_b64`). **Before this fix every lane took bit 0 of the constant**: `s_and_saveexec_b32 s7, 0xff00` with all 32 lanes on left none on, `5` left all 32 on, and `s_bcnt1_i32_b64 s0, 0xff00ff` gave 32. llvm-mc rejects a constant as the mask operand of `v_cndmask_b32_e64` / `v_add_co_ci_u32_e64` and decodes one as "Invalid immediate", so those VALU callers never see a constant. **A constant held in an SGPR** (`s_mov_b32 s20, 0x3ffffc00`) is read the same way when it reaches a mask consumer: `mask_src` takes bit L of an SGPR that `holds_lane_mask` doesn't flag (a pair in a wave64 64-bit context: the high SGPR for lanes 32-63). In a wave32 or wave64 program a 32-bit bitwise SALU op (`s_not_b32`, `s_and/or/xor/andn2/orn2/nand/nor/xnor_b32`, `s_cselect_b32`) that writes EXEC/VCC (`exec_lo`/`vcc_lo` in wave32; `exec_lo`/`exec_hi` and mask-holding `vcc_lo`/`vcc_hi` in wave64, including after `s_mov_b64 vcc, 0/-1`) skips scalar constant folding, reads its operands through `Gen._mask_b32_src` / `mask_src` (collecting the opposite half through `wave_mask` if a wave64 op mixes `lo` and `hi` halves), preserves the untouched half of EXEC/VCC in wave64 (`exec_half_write` / `put_mask`), and votes SCC over the wave (wave32) or that 32-lane half (wave64, `scc_any_half`), matching `s_not_b32` and the binary ops on lane-mask SGPRs too; into a plain SGPR with a constant it stays a data read, because `lane_masks.py` classifies that SGPR as data when the other operand is a constant (`s_and_b32 s20, exec_lo, 1` then `s_bcnt1` must count the wave's bits: found by @Pierre-Kookis on #1792, GPU-reproduced), and `s_mov_b32 exec_lo/exec_hi, sN` takes each lane's bit. In a wave32 program a 32-bit bitwise SOP2 (`s_and/or/xor/andn2/orn2/nand/nor/xnor_b32`, `s_cselect_b32`) that writes EXEC/VCC reads both operands through `mask_src` (`Gen.sop2_b32_srcs`); into a plain SGPR it stays a data read, because `lane_masks.py` classifies that SGPR as data when the other operand is a constant (`s_and_b32 s20, exec_lo, 1` then `s_bcnt1` must count the wave's bits: found by @Pierre-Kookis on #1792, GPU-reproduced), and `s_mov_b32 exec_lo/exec_hi, sN` takes each lane's bit. A real mask is unchanged: it holds 0 or ~0 per lane, and bit L of that is the same 0 or 1. Until 2026-11-22 these took the value whole, so `s_and_saveexec_b32 s6, s20` with that constant, or `s_and_b32 exec_lo, exec_lo, 0xff00`, left every lane on (found writing `cs_w32_saveexec_ext`, #1771; CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_mask_src_sgpr_const.py`, and `tools/cf_diff.py` / `tools/salu_diff.py` still pass). `s_not_b32` on lane masks, `s_and/or/xor_b32` with two immediates into `vcc_lo`, and wave64 half-mask bitwise ops (destination half preservation, `exec_hi`, and `scc_any_half`) are CPU-checked in `tests/semantic/test_salu_b32_lane_masks.py` (wave32, wave64, and `--wave64-emulate`, validated with `spirv-val`) and GPU-checked on gfx1036, RTX 5080 (`--wave64-emulate`), and RX 9060 XT (RADV). Not covered here: a b32 op on one half of a wave64 mask, masks read as data (#293), and a literal with bit 31 set in a 64-bit position (zero- vs sign-extension not measured). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_w32_mask_imm.asm` and `cs_w64_mask_imm.asm` on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 (driver 616.92; wave64 with `LEONIDA_W64_EMULATE=1`) and an AMD Radeon RDNA2 iGPU (driver 26.10.1, native 64-wide subgroup): both failed before on both GPUs. Run across every lane in `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py` (`LaneMaskConstants`). |
| Scalar select and pack (`s_cmov_b32`, `s_cmovk_i32`, `s_pack_ll/lh/hh_b32_b16`) | `s_cmov_b32` / `s_cmovk_i32`: D = SCC ? S0 : D (`s_cmovk_i32`'s S0 is the 16-bit immediate, sign-extended); SCC is read, not written; the destination must be an SGPR (a lane-mask destination, and `s_cmov_b64`, stay `Unsupported`, #210). `s_pack_*`: the low half of D from S0, the high half from S1, the letters naming the half of each (l = 15:0, h = 31:16), S0 first; GFX10 has no `s_pack_hl`; SCC not written. Source: RDNA2 ISA SOP1/SOP2/SOPK. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_cmov.py` and `test_salu_pack.py`; the translated shaders were run on NVIDIA by reviewers (`s_cmov*`: RTX 5090, RTX 4050 and Radeon 740M in #613; `s_pack_*`: RTX 5090 in #449). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137, SCC kept). |
| Vector integer additions (`v_mul_hi_u32_u24`/`v_mul_hi_i32_i24`, `v_lshrrev_b64`/`v_ashrrev_i64`, `v_qsad_pk_u16_u8`/`v_mqsad_pk_u16_u8`) | `v_mul_hi_*24`: the high 32 bits of the 48-bit product of the low 24 bits, zero-extended (u24) or sign-extended from bit 23 (i24); the high halves of `v_mul_u32_u24` / `v_mul_i32_i24`. `v_lshrrev_b64` / `v_ashrrev_i64`: the pair (or a 64-bit constant) shifted right by S0 & 63, logically or arithmetically, through the `v_lshlrev_b64` code. `v_qsad_pk_u16_u8` / `v_mqsad_pk_u16_u8`: D.u16[k] = (S2.u16[k] + byte SAD of the 32-bit window S0[8k+31:8k] against S1) & 0xFFFF; the m form skips bytes where S1's byte is 0; `clamp` raises `Unsupported`. Source: RDNA ISA pseudocode. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_mul_hi_24.py`, `test_alu_shift_b64.py`, `test_alu_qsad.py`; run on NVIDIA by reviewers (`v_mul_hi_*24`: RTX 5090 and RTX 5070 in #440; `v_qsad`/`v_mqsad`: 24 cases bit-exact on an RTX 3060 in #623). **Not measured on RDNA2.** **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137), and on native `gfx1036` (AMD Radeon Graphics) with 0 mismatches across signed/unsigned products, shifts, and sliding windows. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137). |
| 64-bit constant vector compares | For `i64`, `u64` and `f64`, V_CMP writes the predicate to VCC or the named scalar mask, with inactive lane bits cleared. V_CMPX ANDs the predicate into EXEC and preserves VCC. Both predicates ignore the source values, including NaNs. The 12 instructions in `tests/semantic/fixtures/vopc_constant_64.asm` assemble for gfx1030 and are CPU-checked with active and inactive lanes in `test_vopc_const_ordered.py`. Measured on native gfx1100 (Radeon RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) as raw compute kernels: with EXEC preset to 0x55555555, `v_cmp_t_i64`, `v_cmp_t_u64` and `v_cmp_tru_f64` write 0x55555555 into the destination mask and the `v_cmp_f_*` forms write 0, so the inactive lane bits are cleared, while the other mask keeps its preset words (`s[10:11]` stays 0xdeadbeef/0xfeedface, VCC stays 0x0f0f0f0f/0xf0f0f0f0); `v_cmpx_t_*` leaves EXEC at 0x55555555 and `v_cmpx_f_*` clears it to 0, with VCC untouched in both; an operand holding a quiet NaN changes nothing. In wave32 only the low 32-bit mask register is written — `s11` and `vcc_hi` keep their previous words for a constant and a real `v_cmp_eq_i64` alike — where the wave64 pair `s[10:11]` receives 0xffffffff in both halves. [AMD RDNA 2 ISA, VOPC](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) |
| Constant and ordered VOPC, `v_cmp(x)_nlg_*`, `v_cvt_off_f32_i4`, `v_pipeflush` | `v_cmp(x)_f_*` is always false, `v_cmp(x)_t_*` (integers) and `v_cmp(x)_tru_*` (floats) always true; `v_cmp(x)_o_{f32,f16}` is true when neither source is NaN, `_u_` when either is (`OpIsNan`), and `_nlg_` when `x == y` or either source is NaN (`OpFUnordEqual`, completing all 16 IEEE-754 RDNA2 float VOPC predicates; LLVM `gfx10_asm_vopc.s`); the result goes through the usual VOPC path (VCC, an SGPR mask, or EXEC for `v_cmpx`; off lanes give 0). `v_cvt_off_f32_i4`: D = sext(S0[3:0]) / 16 (the ISA's CVT_OFF_TABLE, -0.5 .. 0.4375), exact, output modifiers apply. `v_pipeflush` only flushes the VALU pipeline and is translated as nothing, like `v_nop`. Source: RDNA2 ISA VOPC and VOP1. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_vopc_const_ordered.py`, `tests/semantic/test_float_compares.py` (`nlg`; GPU-checked by `ps_cmp_nlg.asm`: f32 and f16 with ±0, quiet and signalling NaN, ±inf and ignored f16 high halves, the e64 form with `abs`/`neg`, and `v_cmpx_nlg` on equal and NaN operands, Tesla V100 (NVIDIA 580.95.05, Linux)), `test_alu_cvt_off.py` (GPU-checked by `ps_cvt_off.asm`, upper bits ignored and `mul:4` with and without `clamp`), `test_valu_pipeflush.py`; the constant/ordered float compares were run on an RTX 5090 (16 draws, quiet and signalling NaNs) and the suite on RTX 4050 / Radeon 740M in #620; `v_pipeflush` on RTX 5090 / RTX 3060 in #735. All 16 f16 predicates (`v_cmp_{f,lt,eq,le,gt,lg,ge,o,u,nge,nlg,ngt,nle,neq,nlt,tru}_f16`, e32 and e64) are GPU-checked on quiet and signalling NaN, +-0, +-inf, denormals and junk high halves by `ps_vcmp_f16.asm` and `ps_vcmp_f16_e64.asm` (RTX 5080 and Radeon gfx1036, 4096/4096 exact), CPU side `tests/semantic/test_vcmp_f16.py`. The 16 `v_cmpx_*_f16` forms (e32 and e64) are GPU-checked the same way by `ps_vcmpx_f16.asm` and `ps_vcmpx_f16_e64.asm` (EXEC result on every case, VCC kept, EXEC only narrowed; RTX 5080 and Radeon gfx1036), CPU side `tests/semantic/test_vcmpx_f16.py`. |
| Relative VGPR moves (`v_movrel*`) | The VGPR file is a Function-scope uint array, so `v_movrels_b32`/`v_movreld_b32`/`v_movrelsd_b32` become dynamic array accesses at `reg + M0` (the whole of M0, as the RDNA2 ISA's opcode descriptions say). `v_movrelsd_2_b32` (VOP1 opcode 72) offsets each side by its own field: VGPR[D + M0[25:16]] = VGPR[S0 + M0[9:0]], other M0 bits ignored (RDNA2 ISA §12.8 opcode description and the RDNA3 ISA; the RDNA2 §6.6 summary table prints M0[7:0] for the source). The write keeps inactive lanes. The SPIR-V register array has 256 entries. An effective index outside this array reads VGPR0 or drops the write, following RDNA2 ISA §3.6.1. A smaller per-wave hardware allocation is not available to this check. The boundary is checked by `tests/semantic/test_relative_vgpr_bounds.py`. Not modelled: the hardware's range check against the wave's VGPR allocation (an out-of-range source reads VGPR0, an out-of-range write is dropped). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_movrelsd_2.py` (including set ignored M0 bits); GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_movrelsd_2.asm` (the ISA's own example, v25 ← v17 with M0 = (20 << 16) \ \| 10) on NVIDIA RTX 4050 Laptop (Ada) and AMD Radeon 740M (RADV). |
| `v_swaprel_b32` | GFX10 VOP1 opcode 104: swap `VGPR[D + M0[25:16]]` and `VGPR[S0 + M0[9:0]]`, the two M0 fields `v_movrelsd_2_b32` uses; the other M0 bits are ignored (RDNA2 ISA opcode description). Both registers are read before either is written. When both name one register it keeps its value. Inactive lanes keep both registers (`OpSelect` on `active`). As for `v_movrel*`, the bounds check uses the 256 entries in the SPIR-V array. Both operands are destinations, so either invalid index suppresses the whole swap (RDNA2 ISA §3.6.1). As for `v_movrel*`, the hardware's range check against the VGPR allocation is not modelled. A non-VGPR operand raises `Unsupported`. Until 2026-11-22 the op raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_swaprel.py` (both fields, ignored M0 bits, a self-swap, a register-file model). **Not GPU-verified.** **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_swaprel`): M0[25:16] & M0[9:0] offsets, ignored upper M0 bits (`0xfc01fc02`), self-swap, and inactive EXEC lanes all match bit-for-bit. |
| Relative SGPR reads (`s_movrels_b32`, `s_movrels_b64`) | `D = SGPR[S0 + M0]`; the b64 form reads the pair at `S0 + M0` and `S0 + M0 + 1` (RDNA2 ISA SOP1). The SGPR file is a Function-scope uint array, so this is a dynamic load, as for `v_movrels`. Every SGPR write stores into that array (`store_reg`), so the load sees the current value. All reads happen before the destination is written, so D may overlap the source. SCC is not written. The index wraps to the 128 array slots, so it cannot reach the lane-mask parking slots past them. An SGPR that `lane_masks.py` marks as a lane mask (a saved EXEC, a `v_cmp` result) holds only this lane's bit, so the whole wave's mask word is collected for each such SGPR (as for `s_bcnt1`) and read instead when the index lands on it; D then holds the mask as data (SHADER-284, CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_movrels_lane_mask.py`, wave32 and wave64). **Not modelled:** the hardware's range check, and reads past s105 landing on VCC and the other special registers. `s_movreld_b32/b64`, the relative writes, stay `Unsupported`, because the translator keeps translate-time knowledge of SGPR values (literal S# words, descriptor caches) that a dynamic write would invalidate. Until 2026-11-22 both reads raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_salu_movrels.py` (M0 from 0 to 5 and from a register, the b64 pair, an overlapping destination, SCC kept, `s_movreld` rejected). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137, SCC kept; lane-mask SGPR collection is CPU-checked only). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| `v_div_fixup_f16`, `v_div_fixup_f64` | Final IEEE-754 special-case fixup for division (VOP3, `S0` quotient, `S1` denominator, `S2` numerator) under the game's `MODE` (`ieee=0, denorm16_64=3, dx10_clamp=1`): `sign_out = sign(S1) ^ sign(S2)`; `NaN(S2)` → quieted `S2`; `NaN(S1)` → quieted `S1`; `NaN(S2)` → `S2` unchanged (sNaN not quieted under `IEEE=0`); `NaN(S1)` → `S1` unchanged; `(0/0)` or `(±inf/±inf)` → canonical negative qNaN (`0xFE00` for f16, `0xFFF80000_00000000` for f64); `(x/±inf)`, `(±0/y)`, or guaranteed exponent underflow (`exp(S2) - exp(S1) <= -25` for f16, `<= -1075` for f64) → `±0` with `sign_out`; `(x/±0)`, `(±inf/y)`, or guaranteed exponent overflow (`exp(S2) - exp(S1) >= 16` for f16, `>= 1024` for f64) → `±inf` with `sign_out`; otherwise `copysign(S0, sign_out)` (including `NaN(S0)` keeping its payload with `sign_out`). `(x/±inf)` or `(±0/y)` (plus `exp(S2) - exp(S1) <= -1076` on f64 only; f16 has no exponent-difference cutoff) → `±0` with `sign_out`; `(x/±0)`, `(±inf/y)`, or `NaN(S0)` → `±inf` with `sign_out` (no exponent-difference overflow cutoff on either f16 or f64); otherwise `copysign(S0, sign_out)`. Computed bit-exactly on integer words (no `OpCapability Float64` for plain `v_div_fixup_f64`); VOP3 input modifiers (`abs`/`neg`, plus `op_sel:[s0,s1,s2,dst]` on f16 with the unwritten half preserved) and `clamp`, which applies in every MODE. `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` are ignored by hardware here: measured by @fiestorra on a gfx1036 iGPU (Radeon(TM) Graphics, drivers `31.0.24002.92` and `32.0.21045.29003` (the same counts on both), raw ISA through `tools/hw_oracle`, `tools/f64_omod_probe.py`, 4 inputs x 5 modifier forms x 5 MODEs for each of the two ops, wave32 and wave64 identical), the f16 and the binary64 result scale only under `IEEE=0` with `denorm_mode_16_64=0`; under `denorm_mode_16_64=3` the scaling is dropped while `clamp` still saturates, which is the `MODE` this row assumes, so only `clamp` is applied and `Float64` is enabled on f64 only when `clamp` is requested. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`V_DIV_FIXUP_F16`, `V_DIV_FIXUP_F64`). **Not measured on RDNA2 hardware** (translated GPU output checked on RTX 5070 by @duudiis). **Hardware-measured on RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, `tools/hw_oracle`, `ieee=0, denorm16_64=3, dx10_clamp=1`, wave32, by @timelery): `NaN(S0)` → `±inf`, f16 has no exponent-difference underflow/overflow check, f64 has underflow cutoff `e2 - e1 <= -1076` with no overflow check, and sNaN passes through unchanged under `IEEE=0`. Checked bit-for-bit on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp`) and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_div_fixup.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| Image instruction without a VGPR data operand | `llvm-mc` accepts `off`, an SGPR or a constant where an `image_*` instruction names the VGPR range of its result or data (`image_sample off, v[1:2], s[16:23], s[24:27] dmask:0xf dim:SQ_RSRC_IMG_2D`); no compiler emits it (the texels would have nowhere to go). The translator raises `Unsupported` (`<op> destination` for `image_op` and `image_get_lod`, `<op> data` for the `image_misc` group: `image_load_mip`, `image_get_resinfo`, `image_store`, the atomics) instead of the `KeyError` it gave when it wrote the result. A probe of about 60,000 DS, MUBUF, MTBUF, MIMG, SMEM and FLAT/GLOBAL/SCRATCH operand and modifier variations (glc, slc, dlc, lds, tfe, d16, a16, unorm, dmask, dim, offset, idxen, offen, addr64, format; as written and as `llvm-mc` prints them; pixel and compute stages) found no other `IndexError`, `KeyError` or `TypeError`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_mem_crash_forms.py`. |
| 16-bit float widening in typed buffer loads | A half NaN in a `buffer_load_format_*` / `tbuffer_load_format_*` load of formats 13 (`16_FLOAT`), 29 (`16_16_FLOAT`) or 71 (`16_16_16_16_FLOAT`) widens to f32 as `sign \| 0x7F800000 \| (payload << 13)`: the sign is kept, the 10-bit payload moves into the f32 mantissa unchanged, and the quiet bit is not forced, so an sNaN stays signalling. Measured natively on RDNA2 (gfx1036, wave32 and wave64, `_x`/`_xy`/`_xyzw`, MUBUF and MTBUF, `tools/f16_widen_probe.py`): 0x7ddb → 0x7fbb6000, 0x7fe6 → 0x7ffcc000, 0x7fbb → 0x7ff76000, 0xffb2 → 0xfff64000, 0x7fcb → 0x7ff96000, 0xff96 → 0xfff2c000, 0x7c01 → 0x7f802000, 0xfc01 → 0xff802000, and the controls 0x3c00 → 0x3f800000, 0xc000 → 0xc0000000. `v_cvt_f32_f16` (what a driver lowers `UnpackHalf2x16` to) differs on an sNaN, which it quiets with the payload kept (0x7ddb → 0x7ffb6000), and `UnpackHalf2x16`'s NaN result is undefined; the mini interpreter canonicalised it to 0x7fc00000. Until 2026-11-23 `Gen.decode_format` used `UnpackHalf2x16` for every half and so lost NaN payloads on all three formats; it now keeps `UnpackHalf2x16` for the non-NaN halves (an exact widening) and builds the NaN word from the bits with integer ops. `tools/mem_diff.py`'s reference was right only on Python 3.14 (its `struct` `'<e'` keeps the payload from 3.14 and canonicalises before); it now uses the same bit-level `f16_to_f32`, so the fuzzer is version independent. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_f16_widen_hw.py` (the ten measured words, MUBUF and MTBUF, 13/29/71, absent MTBUF channels) and `tools/mem_diff.py --self-test`; 10,500 generated `mem_diff` programs across seeds 1, 2, 3, 7, 11, 21, 22 and 23 match. |
| Float-to-int saturation | `v_cvt_u32_f32` and `v_cvt_i32_f32` truncate toward zero, saturate out-of-range values (including infinities) to the destination bounds, and return zero for NaN. The conversion operand is kept in range before emitting SPIR-V `OpConvertFToU`/`OpConvertFToS`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf), §12.8, VOP1 opcodes 7 and 8. The synthetic `cs_cvt_f32_sat.asm` fixture is checked bit-for-bit by `tests/semantic/test_float_to_int.py`. |
| `v_cvt_f16_u16`, `v_cvt_f16_i16`, `v_cvt_u16_f16`, `v_cvt_i16_f16` | Conversions of the low 16-bit half (`op_sel:[src,dst]` picks the high half of the source / of the destination in the VOP3 form where supported; GFX10 does not accept `op_sel` on these opcodes); the other half of the destination is kept, like the other 16-bit-result ops (see *16-bit results*). `v_cvt_f16_u16` / `v_cvt_f16_i16` convert the unsigned / sign-extended half and narrow with the usual f16 rounding (65520 and above give +infinity for the unsigned form). `v_cvt_u16_f16` / `v_cvt_i16_f16` truncate toward zero, saturate to [0, 65535] / [-32768, 32767] (an f16 infinity too) and give 0 for NaN, as the 32-bit conversions do (*Float-to-int saturation*). `clamp` and the output multipliers are rejected as `Unsupported` rather than guessed. Source: the AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) pseudocode. Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_16.py`; **hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137): all four `_e64` forms keep the high half of the destination, `v_cvt_u16/i16_f16` give 0 for ±qNaN and saturate ±inf, and `v_cvt_f16_u16(65520)` gives +inf (`0x7c00`). Not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_cvt_norm_i16_f16`, `v_cvt_norm_u16_f16` | Normalized f16-to-16-bit integer conversions: `round_even(clamp(S0, -1, 1) * 32767)` (`i16`, snorm16) and `round_even(clamp(S0, 0, 1) * 65535)` (`u16`, unorm16), with NaN giving `0`, source `neg`/`abs` and SDWA `WORD_1` supported, and the unwritten half of the destination preserved (matching `v_cvt_i16_f16`/`v_cvt_u16_f16`; GFX10/LLVM does not accept VOP3 `op_sel` on these opcodes). For `\|cl\| >= 0.25`, `cl * (scale + 1)` is an exact even integer in f32, so `cl * (scale + 1) - RoundEven(cl)` rounds the exact product once without binary32 double-rounding ties near `±0.5`. `clamp` and output multipliers (`mul:*`/`div:*`) are rejected as `Unsupported`. VOP3 `clamp`, `mul:2`, `mul:4`, `div:2` (and combinations) and SDWA `clamp` are hardware no-ops on these integer-destination conversions and are accepted without changing output semantics; unsupported modifiers or invalid form/modifier pairs (e.g., `mul:8`, `_sdwa mul:*`, `_e32`/`_dpp` output modifiers) still raise `Unsupported`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`V_CVT_NORM_I16_F16`, `V_CVT_NORM_U16_F16`). **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon RDNA2 iGPU `gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21043.5001`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/hw_oracle`, @Pierre-Kookis): 655,360 / 655,360 exact words across all 65,536 f16 inputs in five forms (`_e32`, `_e32 ieee=1`, `_e64 -v1`, `_e64 \|v1\|`, `_sdwa WORD_1`), including NaN → 0, ±inf clamps, denormals, single-rounding ties near ±0.5, and the untouched destination half; Checked on the CPU and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_norm_f16.py`. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon gfx1036, wave32 and wave64, `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_cvt_norm_f16`): the same 34 inputs through both ops with the unwritten half kept and with `neg`/`abs`, bit-identical to the translator; the RDNA2 assembler takes no `op_sel` on these two opcodes, so only the VOP3P-style `pknorm` forms have an `op_sel` check. Not run on the shader corpus. **output modifiers additionally verified natively on RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21045.29003`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/cvt_norm_mods_probe.py --hw-arch gfx1036`): exhaustive 65,536/65,536 f16 sweeps across VOP3 `plain`, `clamp`, `mul:2`, `mul:4`, `div:2`, `clamp mul:2`, `clamp div:2`, plus `MODE.FP_ROUND=RTZ` (`0xCC`), `IEEE=1` (`0x2C0`), `_e64` high-half preservation, and `_sdwa clamp` (`WORD_0`/`WORD_1`, `UNUSED_PRESERVE`/`UNUSED_SEXT`/`UNUSED_PAD`) all match with 0 mismatches and 0 wave diffs (`1,050,708` total word evaluations across the four probe ops). **GPU-verified through Vulkan** (wave32 synthetic compute shader via `vk_compute_test` with `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` `1.4.363`, 20 `v_cvt_norm_*` output words): bit-exact on `AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT` (driver `26.11.2 (LLPC)`, `driverVersion 2.0.416`, Vulkan `1.4.359`) and `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` (`gfx1036`, driver `26.11.2`, `driverVersion 2.0.353`, Vulkan `1.4.315`), 0 validation errors. Checked on the CPU and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_norm_f16.py` and `tests/semantic/test_cvt_norm_mods.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_frexp_mant_f32`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32`, `v_ldexp_f32` | Computed on the binary32 word, with no float arithmetic. A normal input has a biased exponent of 1..254. `v_frexp_mant_f32` returns the input with its exponent set to 126 (a mantissa in [0.5, 1), sign kept); `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32` returns the biased exponent minus 126, so mantissa × 2^exponent is the input. Zero and denormals (read as zero) give a signed zero and exponent 0; infinities and NaNs give the input unchanged and exponent 0. `v_ldexp_f32` is S0 × 2^S1 with S1 a signed integer: S1 is clamped to [-512, 512] and added to the exponent field; a new field above 254 gives ±infinity, one of 0 or below gives ±0 (no denormal results), and zero, infinities and NaNs ignore S1. Neg/abs, `clamp` and the output multipliers apply as for other float ops (not to the integer exponent result).
