Source: the AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) pseudocode for these three opcodes. **Measured natively** on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, `tools/hw_oracle`): under the MODE inferred for the game (IEEE 0, f32 denormals flushed) the hardware gives the translator's word for all 30 special inputs of each frexp op and 741 ldexp cases (denormal inputs, results in and below the denormal range, every exponent clamp edge, NaN payloads). With f32 denormals kept the hardware normalizes denormal inputs and returns denormal ldexp results, and with IEEE 1 it quiets signalling NaNs; neither is modelled, since the game runs with both off. Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_alu_frexp_ldexp.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_mad_u16`, `v_mad_i16` | `D = S0 * S1 + S2` on the low 16-bit halves (VOP3 only). The product and sum are formed in 32 bits, so nothing overflows, and the low 16 bits are the result. `op_sel` bits 0..2 read the high half of a source and bit 3 writes the high half of the destination; like the other opsel-capable 16-bit VOP3 ops the other half of the destination is kept (see *16-bit results*). `clamp` saturates the full-width result to [0, 65535] (`v_mad_u16`) or [-32768, 32767] (`v_mad_i16`). Source: the AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) pseudocode and Mesa ACO's `can_use_opsel`. Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_alu_mad_u16_i16.py`; **hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137): full-width `clamp`, `op_sel` and the untouched destination half match bit-for-bit. Not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_frexp_mant_f16`, `v_frexp_exp_i16_f16`, `v_ldexp_f16` | The binary16 versions of `v_frexp_mant_f32`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32` and `v_ldexp_f32`, computed on the f16 word of the low half with no float arithmetic (`op_sel` picks the high half of a source; the last `op_sel` bit sends the result to the high half of the destination; the other half of the destination is kept). A normal input has a biased exponent of 1..30. `v_frexp_mant_f16` returns the input with its exponent set to 14 (a mantissa in [0.5, 1), sign kept); `v_frexp_exp_i16_f16` returns the biased exponent minus 14 as a 16-bit integer, so mantissa × 2^exponent is the input. Zero and denormals (read as zero) give a signed zero and exponent 0; infinities and NaNs give the input unchanged and exponent 0. Zero gives a signed zero and exponent 0; infinities and NaNs give the input unchanged and exponent 0. f16 denormals are kept, as in the MODE inferred for the game (`denorm_mode_16_64` = 3): a denormal input is normalized (`v_frexp_mant_f16(0x0001)` = 0.5 with exponent -23, `0x03ff` gives `0x3bfe` and -14). `v_ldexp_f16` is S0 × 2^S1 with S1 the low 16 bits of the source read as a signed integer: S1 is clamped to [-64, 64] and added to the exponent field; a new field above 30 gives ±infinity, one of 0 or below gives ±0 (no denormal results), and zero, infinities and NaNs ignore S1. S1 is clamped to [-64, 64]; a result above the f16 range gives ±infinity, and one below the smallest normal is a denormal rounded to nearest even (1.0 × 2^-15 = `0x0200`, 1.5 × 2^-24 = `0x0002`, 1.0 × 2^-25 = 0, 65504 × 2^-30 rounds up to `0x0400`); zero, infinities and NaNs ignore S1. Source neg/abs apply to the float operand. Under the game's console MODE (`0x1c0`: `IEEE=0`, `DX10_CLAMP=1`, `denorm_mode_16_64=3`) and under `IEEE=1` (`0x3c0`), VOP3 `mul:2`, `mul:4`, and `div:2` are hardware no-ops on `v_frexp_mant_f16` and `v_ldexp_f16` (since `denorm_mode_16_64=3` disables `omod` on f16 results), while `clamp` clamps to [0.0, 1.0] (negative values and NaNs clamp to +0.0, values > 1.0 and +Inf clamp to 1.0, [0.0, 1.0] kept) both alone and combined with `mul:*`/`div:*`; on `v_frexp_exp_i16_f16` (16-bit integer result), `clamp` and `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` are all hardware no-ops under every MODE (`0x1c0`, `0x100`, `0x3c0`). Under the `denorm_mode_16_64=0, IEEE=0` (`0x100`) control, `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` scale `v_frexp_mant_f16` and `v_ldexp_f16` (not modelled because the game runs with `denorm_mode_16_64=3`). Unknown or unmodelled modifiers raise `Unsupported`. Source: the AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) pseudocode for these three opcodes. **Not measured on hardware**: the denormal input and result handling, the NaN payload, and whether `v_frexp_exp_i16_f16` keeps the high half of its destination (it is modelled like the other 16-bit ops here, which keep it) are the translator's choice. **Measured natively** on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, AMD Windows driver 32.0.21045.16010, `tools/hw_oracle`) under IEEE 0, DX10_CLAMP 1, f32 denormals flushed and f16 denormals kept: the translated rule gives the hardware's word for every one of the 65,536 f16 inputs of both frexp ops and of `v_ldexp_f16` under 16 exponents (1,048,576 cases), and the high half of the destination is kept by all three. Until 2026-11-22 the translator read f16 denormals as zero and flushed denormal ldexp results, which the hardware does only with `denorm_mode_16_64` = 0. Not modelled (the game runs with IEEE 0): with IEEE 1 a signalling NaN is quieted (`0x7c01` gives `0x7e01`). VOP3 output modifiers (`clamp`, `mul:2`, `mul:4`, `div:2`, `clamp` + `mul:*`/`div:*`, and source `abs`/`neg`) were additionally measured natively on an RDNA2 iGPU (`AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics`, `gfx1036`, Windows driver `32.0.21045.29003`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/frexp_ldexp_f16_mods_probe.py`): 3,466 / 3,466 exact words (3,360 console-MODE grid words, 22 source-modifier words, and 84 `0x100`/`0x3c0` control words, 0 wave diffs, 0 omod diffs). Checked on the CPU and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_frexp_ldexp_f16.py`, `tests/semantic/test_frexp_ldexp_f16_mods.py`, and `tests/test_frexp_ldexp_f16_mods_probe.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_frexp_mant_f64`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f64` | Binary64 versions of `v_frexp_mant_f32` and `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32`, computed bit-exactly on the `(lo, hi)` dword pair without requiring `OpCapability Float64` or `Int64` unless VOP3 output modifiers are used. A normal input has biased exponent `1..2046` (bits `[62:52]`): `v_frexp_mant_f64` sets the biased exponent to `1022` (`0x3FE00000`, mantissa in `[0.5, 1)` with sign and low mantissa bits kept), and `v_frexp_exp_i32_f64` returns `e - 1022` as a 32-bit signed integer so `mantissa × 2^exponent == input`. Under the default f16/f64 denormal mode (`denorm_mode_16_64` = 3, matching `v_frexp_mant_f16`/`v_frexp_exp_i16_f16`), non-zero subnormals (`e == 0`, 52-bit fraction `≠ 0`) are normalized into `[0.5, 1)` with sign preserved and exponent `msb − 1073` (`-1073..-1022`; e.g. `0x0000000000000001` → `0x3FE0000000000000` / `-1073`, `0x000FFFFFFFFFFFFF` → `0x3FEFFFFFFFFFFFFE` / `-1022`). Zero and denormals give signed zero and exponent `0`; infinities and NaNs pass through unchanged with exponent `0`. VOP3 source `abs`/`neg` flip/clear bit 31 of `hi`; VOP3 output modifiers are gated by the `MODE`, measured for this row by @fiestorra on a gfx1036 iGPU (Radeon(TM) Graphics, drivers `31.0.24002.92` and `32.0.21045.29003` (the same counts on both), raw ISA through `tools/hw_oracle`, `tools/f64_omod_probe.py`, 7 mantissa inputs x 5 modifier forms x 5 MODEs, wave32 and wave64 identical): `clamp` applies to `v_frexp_mant_f64` in every MODE via `clamp_mods_f64` (`OpCapability Float64`), while `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` scale the mantissa only under `IEEE=0` with `denorm_mode_16_64=0` and are ignored under `IEEE=1` and under `denorm_mode_16_64=3`, the mode this row assumes, so only `clamp` needs `Float64` here. All output modifiers are rejected on `v_frexp_exp_i32_f64`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`V_FREXP_MANT_F64`, `V_FREXP_EXP_I32_F64`) and LLVM `VOP1Instructions.td`. **Measured natively** on an AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics RDNA2 GPU (`gfx1036`, not the console's `gfx1013`; Windows driver 32.0.21042.62, `tools/hw_oracle`, wave32 and wave64 identical on every case): under `IEEE=0, denorm_mode_16_64=3, DX10_CLAMP=1` all 71 cases (all 52 single-bit subnormal positions `0..51`, lo/hi boundary-crossing multi-bit subnormals, negative subnormals, `±0`, normals, `±inf`, `qNaN`, `sNaN`, plus VOP3 `abs`/`neg`/`clamp`) matched bit-for-bit. Until 2026-11-22 the translator flushed binary64 subnormals to `±0` / exponent `0`, which the hardware does only under `denorm_mode_16_64=0`. Not modelled (the game runs with `IEEE=0, denorm_mode_16_64=3`): `IEEE=1` quieting signalling NaNs (`0x7FF0000000000001` → `0x7FF8000000000001`), `denorm_mode_16_64=0` flushing subnormals, and `denorm_mode_16_64=3` ignoring `omod`. **Not measured on hardware** beyond that gate: it was measured by @fiestorra with `tools/f64_omod_probe.py` (see the modifiers sentence above), while the pass-through of NaN payloads under `IEEE=0` is part of the 71 cases above and denormal-input handling follows the f32 rule. Checked on the CPU and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_frexp_f64.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_ldexp_f64` | Binary64 load-exponent (`D.f64 = S0.f64 × 2^(S1.i32)`), lowered bit-exactly on the `(lo, hi)` dword pair using only 32-bit integer ops (no `OpCapability Float64` or `OpCapability Int64`, even with `clamp`). `±0`, `±inf`, and both quiet and signalling NaNs (under `IEEE = 0`) pass through bit-for-bit unchanged after `S0` `abs`/`neg`. Subnormal inputs (`denorm_mode_16_64 = 3`) are normalized via `FindUMsb` before adding `clamp(S1, -4096, 4096)` to the exponent field; overflow (`e2 >= 2047`) gives `±inf`, and subnormal outputs (`e2 <= 0`) shift the 53-bit significand right by `sh = 1 - e2` (`1..53`) and round to nearest even (carry into bit 52 yields `±min_norm`; `sh >= 54` flushes to `±0`). VOP3 `clamp` applies bit-exactly in integer ops (`DX10_CLAMP = 1`: `NaN`, `-0.0`, `<0` → `+0.0`; `>1.0` including `+inf` → `1.0`). VOP3 `omod` (`mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2`) is rejected as `Unsupported` because hardware ignores it under `denorm_mode_16_64 = 3` and applies it under `denorm_mode_16_64 = 0`. For immediate `S0` operands, inline integers `-16..64` sign-extend across 64 bits while 32-bit literals occupy `[63:32]` with `[31:0] = 0`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`V_LDEXP_F64`). **Measured natively** on an RDNA2 iGPU (`gfx1036`, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/hw_oracle` under game MODE `IEEE=0`, `DX10_CLAMP=1`, `denorm32=0`, `denorm16_64=3`): 870 / 870 cases bit-exact across all 52 subnormal input bit positions, all 54 subnormal right-shift boundaries and exact half-ULP ties, carry into `min_norm`, `±0`/`±inf`/qNaN/sNaN, `abs`/`neg`/`clamp`, immediate/literal `S0`, and seeded random inputs (not modelled: `IEEE=1` quiets signalling NaNs). Checked on the CPU and assembled by `tests/semantic/test_alu_ldexp_f64.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| `v_min3_f16`, `v_max3_f16`, `v_min3_i16`, `v_min3_u16`, `v_max3_i16`, `v_max3_u16`, `v_med3_i16`, `v_med3_u16` | Three-operand 16-bit min/max/median (VOP3 only). `op_sel` bits 0..2 read the high half of S0..S2, bit 3 writes the high half of D, and the unwritten half of D is kept (see *16-bit results*). `v_min3_f16` and `v_max3_f16` read each selected f16 half through `Gen.f16_in` (`neg`/`abs` apply to the selected half), fold with `NMin`/`NMax` (so a NaN operand yields the other operand and −0 orders below +0, matching `v_min3_f32`/`v_max3_f32` and `v_med3_f16`), then apply VOP3 `omod`/`clamp` and `Gen.f16_out`. The 16-bit integer ops zero-extend (`u16`) or sign-extend (`i16`) the selected 16-bit halves, evaluate `UMin`/`UMax`/`UClamp` or `SMin`/`SMax`/`SClamp`, and write the low 16 bits into the selected half of D; `clamp` is supported on integer min3/max3/med3 as an identity no-op on the 16-bit container (hardware-verified on gfx1036 across wave32 and wave64); non-VGPR `op_sel` destinations raise `Unsupported`. `clamp` and non-VGPR `op_sel` destinations raise `Unsupported`. Until 2026-11-22 all eight raised `Unsupported`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) VOP3 opcodes `0x351`–`0x356`, `0x359`–`0x35A`, and Mesa ACO's `can_use_opsel`. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (gfx1036 iGPU, AMD Windows driver 32.0.21045.16010, `tools/hw_oracle`, IEEE 0 with f16 denormals kept): 6,092 cases (every triple of ±0, a denormal, 1.0, -1.0, 2.0, ±inf, a quiet and a signalling NaN for each f16 op; every triple of 8 edge values for the i16/u16 ops; `op_sel` on each source and the destination; a 0xabcd high half on every source) match the translator's word, including keeping the destination half and returning `src0`'s NaN bits unchanged (`0x7c01` stays signalling under IEEE 0) when all three operands are NaN (`ALL_NAN_SRC0_SIGNALLING` in `tests/semantic/test_alu_min3_max3_med3_16_hw.py`, alongside 540 sampled measured words). `tests/semantic/test_alu_min3_max3_med3_16_hw.py` holds 540 of the measured words. **Not GPU-verified.** CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_min3_max3_med3_16.py`, `tests/test_f16_op_sel.py`, and `tests/test_f16_op_sel_values.py`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_min3_max3_med3_16.py`, `tests/test_f16_op_sel.py`, and `tests/test_f16_op_sel_values.py`; also **hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, Windows 11, driver `26.5.1`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_min3_max3_med3_16`): all eight ops (`op_sel`, untouched destination half, NaN, −0/+0 ordering, and `v_max3_f16` `clamp`) match bit-for-bit. |
| `v_cvt_flr_i32_f32`, `v_cvt_rpi_i32_f32` | RDNA ISA pseudocode: `f32_to_i32(floor(S0))` and `f32_to_i32(floor(S0 + 0.5))`, the `+ 0.5` rounded in binary32 like any f32 add (so 0.49999997 gives 1, where an exact round-half-up would give 0). `f32_to_i32` saturates as in the row above: INT_MAX / INT_MIN at or past ±2³¹ and for ±inf, 0 for NaN. `sat_i32(floor(S0))` and exact `sat_i32(floor(S0 + 0.5))` (computed via `f = Floor(x); (x - f >= 0.5) ? f + 1 : f`, with no binary32 rounding of `S0 + 0.5`: `0x3effffff` → `0`, `0x4b000001` → `0x00800001`, `0x4b7fffff` → `0x00ffffff`). Out-of-range values (±2³¹ and beyond, ±inf) and NaNs all saturate by the sign bit of the operand (`+NaN` → `0x7FFFFFFF`, `-NaN` → `0x80000000`, unlike `v_cvt_i32_f32` which returns `0` for NaN); the sign is read from `x` before `Floor` so driver NaN canonicalization cannot flip it. Source neg/abs apply first. Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_flr_rpi.py`, **not yet GPU-verified**. **Until 2026-11-22 the translator rounded `S0 + 0.5` in binary32 and returned `0` for NaN.** Hardware-verified natively on RDNA2 (AMD Radeon 680M `gfx1035`: 82/82 wave32 and 82/82 wave64 via `tools/hw_oracle`, plus `gfx1036` confirmation in #1746) and CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_flr_rpi.py`. |
| `v_cvt_pknorm_i16_f32`, `v_cvt_pknorm_u16_f32` | D[15:0] from S0 and D[31:16] from S1: the f32 is clamped to [-1, 1] (i16) or [0, 1] (u16), multiplied by 32767 or 65535 and rounded to nearest even **once, from the exact product** (SHADER-363). Mesa ACO emits these opcodes for NIR `pack_snorm_2x16`/`pack_unorm_2x16`, which `nir_opcodes.py` defines as `fround_even(fclamp(x) * 32767.0 / 65535.0)`. Emitted as `FClamp`, `OpFMul`, `RoundEven` and a convert, not `PackSnorm2x16`, whose rounding GLSL leaves to the driver. The f32 `OpFMul` can land exactly on k + 0.5 when the exact product is just below or above it, and `RoundEven` would then pick the even neighbour; the product's error `Fma(c, s, -p)` is exact, so on such a tie the translator takes floor(p) + 1 when it is positive, floor(p) when negative, and `RoundEven` when it is 0 (an exact tie). Away from a tie the two roundings agree, since k + 0.5 is an f32 there. NaN gives 0, as `v_cvt_i32_f32` does: **measured** on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, SHADER-339), 74 of 74 NaN halves (quiet and signalling, both signs, payloads, in S0 and S1) gave 0 in wave32 and wave64 under the game's MODE and with IEEE 1, DX10_CLAMP 0 or f32 denormals kept (`tools/pknorm_probe.py --oracle hw`). **The rounding differs:** the hardware rounds the exact product x · 32767 (or 65535) once, while the translator rounds the f32 `OpFMul` product and then `RoundEven`, so it is one off where the f32 product lands exactly on k + 0.5 (28 of 28 such near-tie inputs, both directions; `docs/shader_validation.md`, "`v_cvt_pknorm_*` NaN and rounding on RDNA2"). On gfx1035 (SHADER-137, `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, wave32 and wave64) ±qNaN → 0, ±inf saturation and the round-to-nearest-even ties at ±0.5 match bit for bit. Not fixed here. NaN gives 0, as `v_cvt_i32_f32` does; that is **not measured on hardware**. Source modifiers apply. VOP3 `clamp` is a hardware no-op on RDNA2 and is accepted without changing the result; other modifiers (`mul:*`/`div:*`, `op_sel:*`) raise `Unsupported`. `clamp` raises `Unsupported`. Until 2026-11-22 both ops were rejected. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_pknorm_f32.py` (ties at the scale steps, ±inf, ±NaN, subnormals, out-of-range values, 40 seeded pairs, abs/neg). **Measured on RDNA2:** on an iGPU (gfx1036, SHADER-339/363, `tools/pknorm_probe.py --oracle hw`) 74 of 74 NaN halves (quiet and signalling, both signs, payloads, in S0 and S1) gave 0 and 28 of 28 near-tie inputs (both directions) gave the single-rounded word, in wave32 and wave64 under the game's MODE and with IEEE 1, DX10_CLAMP 0 or f32 denormals kept; the translator matches all 134 probe words in all ten runs (it was one off on the 28 near ties before SHADER-363; additionally, `tools/cvt_norm_mods_probe.py --hw-arch gfx1036` verified `plain` vs VOP3 `clamp` across 40 edge/near-tie/NaN/±inf/subnormal pairs + 3 source-modifier cases across 6 `HW_REG_MODE` configurations and wave32/wave64 on `gfx1036` driver `32.0.21045.29003`: 2,064/2,064 words exact with 0 clamp diffs and 0 wave diffs). CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_pknorm_f32.py` (the twelve measured near-tie words, ties at the scale steps, ±inf, ±NaN, subnormals, out-of-range values, 40 seeded pairs, abs/neg, `clamp`), `tests/semantic/test_cvt_norm_mods.py`, and `tests/test_pknorm_probe.py`. **GPU-verified through Vulkan** (wave32 synthetic compute shader via `vk_compute_test` with `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` `1.4.363`, 8 `v_cvt_pknorm_*` plain & `clamp` output words): bit-exact on `AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT` (driver `26.11.2 (LLPC)`, `driverVersion 2.0.416`, Vulkan `1.4.359`) and `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` (`gfx1036`, driver `26.11.2`, `driverVersion 2.0.353`, Vulkan `1.4.315`), 0 validation errors. **Not checked through Vulkan on a GPU.** **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-137): ±qNaN → 0, ±inf saturation, and round-to-nearest-even ties at ±0.5 match bit-for-bit (the near-tie rounding above is a separate case). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| f16 rounding (SHADER-55, SHADER-56) | **Narrowing to f16 uses integer ops, not `PackHalf2x16`.** Every f16 ALU result (`v_*_f16` VOP1/VOP2/VOP3, `v_pk_*_f16`, `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_interp_p2_f16`, `v_fma_mix*`) and `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` go through `Gen.f16_bits` (round to nearest even, or round toward zero for pkrtz; f16 denormals kept, f32 denormal inputs give ±0, NaN stays a quiet NaN). After a MODE.FP_ROUND toward-zero write the ALU results take the toward-zero form instead (`Gen.f16_res`; see Known approximations). GLSL.std.450 leaves `PackHalf2x16`'s rounding to the implementation, and drivers fold a `PackHalf2x16` → `UnpackHalf2x16` round trip to a no-op, so the next f16 op saw the unrounded f32: #513 (RTX 6000 Ada, `--wave64-emulate`, `v_pk_add_f16` → `v_pk_mul_f16` 1 ulp off), and pkrtz giving round-to-nearest on llvmpipe. **f16 FMAs round once** (`v_fma_f16`, `v_fmac_f16`, `v_pk_fma_f16`): x·y is exact in f32, TwoSum gives the exact error of the f32 sum, and round-to-odd at f32 then rounds correctly to f16. An RX 6700 matched a correctly rounded f16 FMA in 12,612 of 12,612 cases (#374); `Fma` then narrowing double-rounded. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_f16_int_convert.py` (edges + seeded sweep against Python's binary16), `tests/semantic/test_f16_fma_rounding.py` (exact rational reference; built near-tie cases fail on the old translation) and `tools/valu_fuzz.py --family f16` on an RTX 6000 Ada. Image/buffer d16 packing (#489) and the 16-bit interpolant quantize (#512) are separate. **Widening an f16 ALU operand doesn't trust `UnpackHalf2x16` with denormals** (`Gen.f16_in`): on an RTX 5060 Ti (610.88) it gives 0 for a denormal that an SDWA `BYTE_1` select produced, from an inline constant or a VGPR alike, while the same denormal as a literal or already in a VGPR widens right (a local probe of all four, before and after). It depends on the driver: `ps_sdwa_f16_const` failed this way on `main` with NVIDIA 610.88 (RTX 5060 Ti, RTX 3060 Ti, Windows), 595.99 (RTX 5090, Linux) and 580.173 (RTX 3060 Ti, Linux), and passed with 621.01 (RTX 5080) and 621.24 (RTX 5070, RTX 3080 Laptop) (#3220). So a half whose exponent field is 0 is widened as sign × mantissa × 2^-24 with integer ops. Confirmed by the GPU test `ps_sdwa_f16_const` (word y, the denormal `0x0040` × 1024.0 = `0x1c00`), which gave `0x0000` on an RTX 5060 Ti (610.88) natively and under `--wave64-emulate` before and 4096/4096 exact after, and by `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py` running it with a flushing `UnpackHalf2x16`. The other `UnpackHalf2x16` uses (packed-f16 reads, interpolants, d16 image results) are not changed. `v_fma_mix*` with f32 sources rounds once straight to f16 via round-to-odd in both nearest and toward-zero modes (measured on an RDNA2 iGPU, gfx1036: `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` gave the single-rounded word for all 7 of 7 tie inputs where the two rules differ, SHADER-135; see Known approximations). `v_fma_mix*` rounds once straight to f16 via round-to-odd in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes, matching hardware (7 of 7 tie inputs of `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` on gfx1036, SHADER-135; see Known approximations). `v_fma_mix*` rounds once straight to f16 via round-to-odd in both nearest and toward-zero modes (see Known approximations); the hardware rounds once: 7 of 7 tie inputs of `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` on gfx1036 (SHADER-135). `v_fma_mix*` with f32 sources uses `Fma` then narrows (two roundings, unmeasured) in nearest-even mode; toward zero it rounds once (see Known approximations). 7 of 7 tie inputs of `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` on gfx1036 (SHADER-135; not fixed yet); toward zero it rounds once (see Known approximations). |
| `v_cvt_pknorm_i16_f16`, `v_cvt_pknorm_u16_f16` | VOP3 packed normalized conversion from two f16 sources (`D[15:0]` from `S0.f16`, `D[31:16]` from `S1.f16`): each selected f16 half (`op_sel[0..1]`, with `neg`/`abs` source modifiers and inline f16 constants via `Gen.f16_in`) is clamped to `[-1, 1]` (`i16`) or `[0, 1]` (`u16`), scaled by `32767` or `65535`, and rounded once to nearest even (`+inf → 32767`/`65535`, `-inf → -32767`/`0`, `NaN → 0`, `±0` and f16 denormals `→ 0`). Uses `cl * (scale + 1) - RoundEven(cl)` for `\|cl\| >= 0.25` to avoid binary32 double-rounding at near-tie f16 inputs (e.g. `0x3801` = `0.5 + 2^-11`). **Measured natively on RDNA2** (`AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics`, `gfx1036`, wave32 & wave64 via `tools/cvt_pknorm_f16_probe.py`): across all 65,536 binary16 inputs in `plain`, `clamp`, `op_sel:[0,0,1]`, and `op_sel:[0,0,1] clamp` (1,048,576 half evaluations) plus 33 targeted edge cases (264 words), VOP3 `clamp` is a **hardware no-op**, destination `op_sel[2] = 1` (`op_sel:[s0,s1,1]`) shifts the 32-bit packed result left by 16 bits (`D[31:16] = norm16(S0.f16)`, `D[15:0] = 0x0000`, overwriting prior `vdst` and dropping `S1`), and raw reserved VOP3 `omod` bits `[60:59]` (`1..3`) are ignored by silicon (rejected by `llvm-mc` on `gfx1036`). The translator accepts `clamp` (no-op) and `op_sel[2] = 1` (`OpShiftLeftLogical` by 16), while `mul:*`/`div:*`, VOP3P modifiers (`op_sel_hi:*`, `neg_lo:*`, `neg_hi:*`), `_dpp`/`_sdwa`, duplicate `op_sel:`, and malformed `op_sel` lists raise `Unsupported`. Output modifiers (`clamp`, `mul:*`, `div:*`), `op_sel` destination bit, and VOP3P modifiers raise `Unsupported`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (VOP3 `v_cvt_pknorm_i16_f16` / `v_cvt_pknorm_u16_f16`) and LLVM AMDGPU `VOP3Instructions.td`. Checked bit-for-bit against an exact rational `fractions.Fraction` reference on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp`) and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_pknorm_f16.py` and `tests/semantic/test_cvt_pknorm_f16_mods.py`, by unit tests `tests/test_cvt_pknorm_f16_probe.py`, and through Vulkan (`vk_compute_test` with `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation`, 116/116 exact words, 0 clamp diffs, 0 validation errors) on `AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT` (driver `26.11.2 (LLPC)`) and `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` (`gfx1036`, driver `26.11.2`); not run on the shader corpus. Checked bit-for-bit against an exact rational `fractions.Fraction` reference on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp`) and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cvt_pknorm_f16.py`; **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon gfx1036, wave32 and wave64, `tools/int_hw_probe.py --oracle both --group valu_cvt_norm_f16`): 33 programs of four conversions each over +-0, +-1, 1 +- 1ulp, +-0.5 and the f16 neighbours of the 0.5 and 0.25 ties, an f16 denormal, +-65504, +-inf and quiet and signalling NaNs, with `op_sel:[1,0]`/`[0,1]` and `neg`/`abs`, all bit-identical to the translator. Not run on the shader corpus. **not GPU-verified** and not run on the shader corpus. . **Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle`, 38 cases against the same exact-rational model, all matching: the halves pack in the documented order (`0.3333 \| 0.5+2^-11` and `1.5 \| -0.5` are asymmetric), each result is one round-to-nearest-even of the exact `cl * 32767` or `cl * 65535` (`0x3801` gives `32799` for `u16`), out-of-range values and infinities saturate the same way (`+inf` gives `32767`/`65535`, `-inf` gives `-32767`/`0`, so an infinity clamps to `±1`), and `NaN` (quiet or signalling), `-0` and a denormal f16 input all give `0`. Not measured: the `op_sel` destination bit and the `neg`/`abs` source modifiers, and not run on the shader corpus. |
| `s_round_mode` | SIMM16[3:0] sets FP_ROUND. The translator accepts FP32 nearest-even with FP16/64 nearest-even or toward-zero and writes the same per-invocation state as `s_setreg_imm32_b32`. Other rounding modes raise Unsupported. AMD RDNA2 ISA tables 15 and 24; synthetic positive and negative halfway-neighbour conversions check both modes in one shader (`test_round_mode_switches_half_precision_color_rounding` in `tests/semantic/test_scalar_noop_waitcnt.py`). |
| f16 narrowing size | `Gen.f16_bits` leaves out or shares steps where that can't change the result. In nearest-even mode there is no separate infinity select: infinity is already at or above the overflow threshold (65520), which gives 0x7C00. For `v_add_f16`, `v_sub_f16`, `v_subrev_f16` and `v_pk_add_f16` without omod (`exact_tiny`), the range below 2^-14 is neither rounded nor tested for underflow: a sum of two f16 values is a multiple of 2^-24, so there it is already an f16 value, and clamp keeps that true. With an omod the rounding stays, since `div:2` can create a tie. No range has an underflow test: below 2^-25 the shift is 25, which moves the mantissa (plus the rounding bias) out to 0. The subnormal range rounds like the normal one, by adding half - 1 + the kept low bit before the shift. When the program writes MODE, `Gen.f16_res` makes one pass: toward zero selects a bias of 0, the 65536 overflow threshold and 0x7BFF, and keeps the infinity select. Confirmed by running the emitted integer sequences before and after on all 2^32 f32 inputs (both roundings) and on all 2^32 f16 operand pairs (both roundings, with and without clamp): no difference. Regression test: `tests/semantic/test_f16_sum_narrowing.py`. |
| `v_interp_p2_f16` clamp | The VOP3 `clamp` bit on `v_interp_p2_f16` clamps its f16 result to [0, 1], applied before the narrowing like the other f16 results (Mesa ACO `aco_opcodes.py` declares the op `dst(F16)`, which takes output modifiers; llvm-mc encodes `clamp` on it but no omod). Until 2026-11-22 the translator dropped it. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_interp_f16.py`: attributes 1.5 and -0.25 read with clamp give 1.0 and +0.0, and without it 1.5 and -0.25. No GPU test: `vs_ids` gives attributes in [0, 1) only. |
| 16-bit interpolation (`v_interp_p1ll_f16` / `v_interp_p2_f16`, `high`) | A 16-bit varying shares its 32-bit param channel with another: the producer packs them as low half, high half (Mesa `ac_nir_prerast_utils.c`: `nir_pack_32_2x16_split` by `io_sem.high_16bits`), the PS sets `FP16_INTERP_MODE` (plus `ATTR1_VALID` when the high half is used, RADV `radv_ps_input_type_bits`), and the `high` bit picks the half on both ops of the pair (ACO `emit_interp_instr`, `aco_isel_helpers.cpp`; llvm-mc gfx1013 sets the same bit, byte 5 bit 0, on both: p1ll `v2, v0, attr0.x high` 0x00 → 0x01, p2 `v2, v1, attr0.x, v3 high` 0x02 → 0x03). The translated VS exports the channel's raw bits, so interpolating them as one f32 gives neither half. A location any `*_f16` interp reads is therefore declared `PerVertexKHR` (`vec4[3]`, no `Flat`; it still matches a producer's vec4 output at that location); this needs `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric` (`fragmentShaderBarycentric`) for every such PS, not just the raw-barycentric ones; the index records it like any capability (`features: ["fragmentShaderBarycentric"]`), so `vk_pipeline_check` and `vk_harness` skip such a PS on a device without it. Nothing picks `--bary-fallback` from the device: it is a translation-time flag. Each f16 read decodes its half of each vertex's word (`UnpackHalf2x16` component 0 or 1) and weights them as AMD does, P0 + i(P1 − P0) + j(P2 − P0) with i, j = `BaryCoordKHR`.y/.z, then p2 clamps and narrows as before. f32 reads of such a location (`p1/p2_f32`) interpolate the raw word the same way, and `v_interp_mov_f32` takes vertex 0's. Until 2026-11-22 `high` was ignored and both halves read the smooth-f32 interpolation of the whole word; abilliontokens counted `high` in 4,776 of 12,101 PS (39.5%, 51,684 instructions, always p1ll/p2 pairs, no `p1lv`), and Pipodi found 4,731 of those 4,776 read both halves of the same channel, with p1ll and p2 always agreeing on attribute, channel and `high`. Like the f32 path, the pair's I/J operands are not read: the weights come from `BaryCoordKHR` (213 PS that compute their own barycentrics into v0/v1 first are a separate gap). Confirmed by `tests/semantic/test_interp_f16_high.py` (per-vertex halves 0.5/1.5/2.5 and −4/8/16 at weights (0.25, 0.25, 0.5) give 1.75 and 9.0; fails on the old translation) and the GPU fixture `ps_interp_f16_high` drawn with `vs_f16_pack` (u and v packed in one channel both ways, f32 control) and, through `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`, with the mesh producer `ms_f16_pack` (mesh `exp paramN` stores the same raw words, `Gen.ms_export`): both pass on an RTX 5070 and fail on the old translation (off by 0.984). spirv-val and `vk_pipeline_check` (real pipelines against the vec4 VS stubs, RTX 5070) also accept PS mixing high, low, f32 and flat reads of one location and of several. **Measured natively on RDNA2** (AMD Radeon 680M, `gfx1035`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/vinterp_hw_probe.py --oracle both`, SHADER-430): the VINTRP unit reads `P0`, `P10` and `P20` from LDS at `M0 + 48·attr + {8·c, 8·c + 4, 32 + 4·c}`; `v_interp_p1ll_f16` (and `v_interp_p1lv_f16`, taking `P0.half` from `vsrc2`) widens the selected f16 half (`[15:0]` or `[31:16]` with `high`) to binary32 and writes the fused binary32 `fma(i, f32(P10.half), f32(P0.half))`, while `v_interp_p2_f16` rounds the exact `partial_f32 + j · f32(P20.half)` once to f16 following `MODE.FP_ROUND.16_64` (like `v_fma_mixlo_f16`, whereas the translator narrows the f32 sum) and writes `vdst[15:0]` while hardware keeps `vdst[31:16]`; f16 subnormal halves are preserved with `denorm_mode_16_64 = 3` (`0x0001 + 0.5·0x0200 + 0.5·0x0200 → 0x0201`, flushed to `±0` when `0`), and f16 NaN halves quiet with payload preserved (`0x7e05 → 0x7e05`, `0x7c01 → 0x7e01`). **ALU half measured on AMD hardware** (SHADER-430, `tools/interp_hw_probe.py`, gfx1036, every case matched; `docs/shader_validation.md`): `p1ll_f16` widens the chosen f16 halves exactly and computes fma(h10, I, h0) with one rounding to f32. `p2_f16` computes fma(h20, J, p1ll) and rounds it once, straight to f16: 30 hand-built ties gave that result and never f32-then-f16. It keeps vdst's high 16 bits. The translator used to zero them and now keeps them (`write_lo16`; `test_p2_f16_keeps_the_high_half_of_vdst`). **Still unverified:** what the SPI writes into LDS for a packed channel in a real draw (P10/P20 as f32 differences of the raw words, or per-half f16 differences under `FP16_INTERP_MODE`), which needs a pixel-shader harness; also f16 inf/NaN halves and `p1lv_f16`. **Unverified:** AMD hardware (whether it computes P10/P20 in f16 or rounds the p1ll intermediate differently), f16 denormal and NaN halves. **`--bary-fallback`** has no per-vertex inputs, so it keeps the old smooth-f32 read and cannot honour `high`; `check.py` skips the fixture there. MoltenVK can't compile `PerVertexKHR`: see the next row. |
| 16-bit interpolation without `PerVertexKHR` (`--f16-interp-fallback`) | MoltenVK reports `fragmentShaderBarycentric` but can't create a pipeline with the row above's inputs: its SPIR-V to MSL conversion stops with "PerVertexKHR decoration is not supported in MSL" (MoltenVK 1.4.2 on an Apple A18 Pro and, reported by @DomasUrbonas in #166, an M4 Pro), so `ps_interp_f16_high` failed on every Mac. With the flag no `PerVertexKHR` (and, for these reads, no `BaryCoordKHR`) is used: every VS `exp paramN`, mesh param and `--mesh-compute` vertex (`tools/mesh_compute_vs.py`) also exports the channel's two f16 halves decoded to f32 (`UnpackHalf2x16`), low halves at location 28 − 2N and high halves at 29 − 2N, so nothing goes above location 29: MoltenVK's `maxVertexOutputComponents` is 124 because Metal counts `gl_Position` in its 128 vertex-output scalars, and the Khronos validation layer counts it too, so a VS output at location 30 failed VUID-RuntimeSpirv-Location-06272 on `vs_ids` (SHADER-291); a PS `*_f16` read of attrN.c takes component c of that ordinary smooth input, then p2 clamps and narrows as before. Interpolation is linear, so the device interpolating the decoded halves gives the same P0 + i(P1 − P0) + j(P2 − P0) as the per-vertex path, with the interpolator's rounding instead of explicit f32 arithmetic. f32 and `v_interp_mov_f32` reads of such a location are the raw word as on any location (flat = the provoking vertex's word, exact). A producer leaves out the halves of a param whose locations would land on one of its own params (params 0..P−1 leave room for the halves of params 0..(28 − P) / 2), and when a PS's canonical decoded-half locations collide with an attribute it reads, its halves are packed into free locations instead (dynamic packing, below). The index records `f16_halves` ({param: [low, high location]}) on both sides; a pipeline links when the PS's entries are among its producer's. Chosen like the wave64 mode: `tools/vk_subgroup_info.py` reports `per_vertex_inputs` (false without `fragmentShaderBarycentric` or on driver ID MoltenVK), and `make_game.py` and `run_gpu_tests.py` turn the flag on where it is false (`LEONIDA_F16_INTERP_FALLBACK=1`/`0` overrides the runner; `build.py` takes only the variable, since it may run in WSL). Without the flag the translation is unchanged: all 139 GPU-test modules are byte-identical to main's, with and without `--bary-fallback` and with `--mesh-compute --wave64-emulate`, so NVIDIA and AMD keep the exact per-vertex path. Confirmed on an Apple A18 Pro (macOS 26.6.2, MoltenVK 1.4.2): `ps_interp_f16_high` drawn with `vs_f16_pack` passes natively and with wave64 emulation, every one of its 4,096 pixels bit-exact to f16 of the exact u and v in both halves (it failed before at pipeline creation), and `ms_f16_pack` through `--mesh-compute` (`LEONIDA_MESH_COMPUTE=1`) passes too; `tests/semantic/test_f16_interp_fallback.py` checks the PS reads (1.75 and 9.0 from the decoded halves), the VS exports (one-lane interpreter), the overlap rules and the default being unchanged. **Unverified:** a PS whose producer exports more params than leave room for the halves it reads (it reads that producer's param, not halves: the `f16_halves` entries then don't match); devices with fewer than 31 output locations. CPU check (TEST-398, `FallbackStagePair`): with ordinary VS exports at 28 and 29, the PS reads those values (5 and 7 in the test) in place of attr0's decoded halves (2 and 3). Only the PS lists attr0 in `f16_halves`. With param28 as the last export, location 28 carries its ordinary value and location 29 has no VS output. When the last ordinary export is at 27, both stages list the pair and the PS reads 2 and 3. The move from 30 to 29 (SHADER-291) was checked on an Apple M5 (macOS 27.2.1, MoltenVK 1.4.2, Khronos validation layer 1.4.363): over the 170 GPU-test modules `vk_pipeline_check` reported VUID-RuntimeSpirv-Location-06272 on 6 vertex shaders at location 30 and on none at 29; the same 15 modules list `f16_halves` for the same params, `run_gpu_tests.py` gives the same result per test, and `ps_interp_f16_high` reads back byte-identical pixels. **Dynamic packing and per-pipeline PSO linking (SHADER-378):** when a PS's canonical `28 − 2N`/`29 − 2N` slots collide with its raw attributes (266 corpus PS where f16-read attrs reach `n >= 7` and raw attrs reach 12–14, all with `2·\|F\| <= 29 − max`) or fall below 0, `assign_f16_half_locations` in `tools/rdna2spirv.py` packs all `f16_halves` pairs downward from location 29 into locations disjoint from its raw attributes (keeping the canonical slots byte-identical whenever collision-free, and refusing only genuine `> 30` location budget overflows); `tools/mesh_compute_vs.py` adds `pair_f16_halves` and an optional `f16_halves` override to `vs_spvasm`; and at graphics PSO creation `runtime/src/shader_library.h` loads `f16_halves` while `runtime/src/spirv_f16_halves.h` (called from `runtime/shims/d3d12_vulkan_device.cpp`) fast-paths exact matches, relocates PS/VS `Location` decorations when needed, demotes unneeded VS static half outputs to `Private`, and synthesizes `UnpackHalf2x16` half exports before `OpReturn` for params a high-param producer (`param0..15`) could not emit statically. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_f16_interp_fallback.py`, `tests/semantic/test_interp_p1lv_f16.py`, `tests/test_mesh_compute_vs.py`, `runtime/test/shader_library_test.cpp`, `runtime/test/spirv_f16_halves_test.cpp`, and `runtime/test/d3d12_f16_halves_test.cpp` (real Vulkan 1.3 draw on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4060 Ti, driver 32.0.16.1088, Windows 11: 16-param VS + colliding PS both synthesized and relocated render exact RGBA `(64, 191, 127, 255)`). **Unverified:** native MoltenVK validation run on macOS and full-corpus recount under `--f16-interp-fallback` (needs game copy); native `MeshEXT` runtime PSO specialization (mesh PSOs are not on `main` yet); devices with fewer than 124 vertex output components. Not combined with `--bary-fallback`'s geometry shader. |
| `v_fmac_f16`, `v_interp_p2/mov_f32` output modifiers | VOP3 `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` (applied first) and `clamp` to [0, 1] apply to `v_fmac_f16_e64` (before narrowing to f16, matching `v_fma_f16`, high half preserved) and to `v_interp_p2_f32_e64` / `v_interp_mov_f32_e64` (no-op when no modifier is present). Until 2026-11-22 all three dropped output modifiers. Source: LLVM `llvm/test/MC/AMDGPU/gfx10_asm_vop2.s` (`v_fmac_f16_e64`) and `gfx10_asm_vop3.s` (`v_interp_p2_f32_e64`, `v_interp_mov_f32_e64`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_f16_fma_rounding.py` (`test_fmac_output_modifiers`) and `tests/semantic/test_interp_f32_mods.py`, and on a GPU by `ps_fmac_f16_mods.asm`, `ps_interp_f32_mods.asm` and `ps_interp_mov_mods.asm` (the last two drawn with `vs_interp_consts.asm`, whose params are constant over the triangle; each modifier and the omod-before-clamp order change a word; Tesla V100 (NVIDIA 580.95.05, Linux)). **The hardware ignores the f16 omod in the MODE the translator assumes:** on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036) `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` change an f16 result (`v_fmac_f16`, `v_add/mul/fma_f16`, `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_exp/rcp_f16`) only with IEEE 0 and `denorm_mode_16_64 = 0`, and do nothing with f16 denormals kept; omod comes before clamp, and clamp gives +0 for -0 and, with DX10_CLAMP 1, for a NaN (SHADER-135, docs/shader_validation.md). The translator is not changed yet: which of omod and the kept f16 denormals is wrong for the game is a corpus question. |
| `v_fmac_f16`, `v_interp_p2/mov_f32`, and f16 output modifiers | VOP3 `omod` (`[60:59]`) scales the floating-point result by `2`, `4`, or `0.5` before `clamp` (`[0.0, 1.0]`), gated by RDNA2 silicon on **both** `MODE.ieee == 0` and output denorms for the **destination precision** being flushed (`fp32_denorm` for `f32` destinations; `fp16_fp64_denorm` for `f16`/`f64` destinations). Under Leonida's default shader `MODE` (`IEEE=0`, FP32 denorms flushed, FP16/FP64 denorms kept `denorm16_64=3`), `Gen.clamp_mods(..., allow_omod=True)` applies `omod` then `clamp` on `f32` destinations (including `v_interp_p2_f32_e64`, `v_interp_mov_f32_e64`, and `v_cvt_f32_f16`), whereas `Gen.clamp_f16` (`allow_omod=False`) drops `omod` and keeps `clamp` on all `f16`-destination instructions (`v_add/sub/subrev/mul/min/max_f16`, `v_fma/fmac/min3/max3/med3_f16`, unary/transcendental/exponent `*_f16`, `v_cvt_f16_*`, `v_interp_p2_f16`, `v_fma_mixlo/hi_f16`). Hardware-verified on native RDNA2 (`gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21045.29003`, AMD clang `23.0.0git`, wave32 & wave64 parity, ELF binary disassembly confirming non-zero VOP3 `[60:59]` bits emitted, in-wave `v_add_f32_e64 mul:2` witness proving `IEEE=0` active -> `6.0` vs `IEEE=1` -> `3.0`): across all 4 `f16`-destination classes (binary ALU, 3-op ALU, unary/transcendental, and `v_cvt_f16_{f32,u16,i16}`), `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` are completely ignored under `IEEE=0, denorm16_64=3` while `clamp` still saturates `[0.0, 1.0]`, whereas the `f32`-destination control `v_cvt_f32_f16` scales (`2.0 -> 4.0 / 8.0 / 1.0`); flipping `MODE` to `IEEE=0, denorm16_64=0` (flushed FP16 denorms) re-enables `f16` `omod` scaling (`v_fma_f16 mul:2 -> 6.0`, `v_rcp_f16 mul:2 -> 1.0`, `v_cvt_f16_f32 mul:2 -> 4.0`), confirming the exact hardware gating condition. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_f16_omod_rdna2.py`. **The gate is RDNA2 behaviour, not a general RDNA rule.** `tools/f64_omod_probe.py` (added by this PR) measures the same gate by destination precision on a gfx1036 iGPU (driver `31.0.24002.92`, raw ISA, 175 cases, `mismatches=0`, `wave_diffs=0`): `v_cvt_f64_i32`, `v_cvt_f64_u32`, `v_frexp_mant_f64`, `v_div_fixup_f64` and `v_div_fixup_f16` are f16/binary64 destinations and ignore `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` under `denorm16_64=3`, while `v_cvt_f32_f64` has binary64 sources and an f32 destination and scales under `denorm16_64=3` whenever `denorm32=0`. And @bunbeans2-arch reported in #4371 that RDNA4 `gfx1201` (RX 9070 XT, Windows driver 26.10.1, where IEEE is fixed on) applies the scaling for all 8 binary64 ops with `denorm16_64=3` kept, `v_add_f64 1.5+1.5 mul:2 = 0x4018000000000000`; that run is quoted from the thread and is not reproduced here. The two readings together are why the translator follows the console's RDNA2 MODEs rather than a hardware-agnostic rule, and why no row here should be read as describing RDNA3 or RDNA4. |
| `v_cvt_f16_f32` output modifiers | VOP3 `clamp` and `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` apply to the f16 result (Mesa ACO declares the op `dst(F16)`), in the order an RX 6800 (`gfx1030`) and Radeon Graphics (`gfx1036`) apply them: the value is rounded to f16 first, then scaled, then clamped, so in RNE `65520 div:2` is inf, not 32768. The scaling works on the f16 bits (`Gen.f16_omod`): a zero or denormal f16, before or after it, gives +0, inf and NaN pass through, and a finite overflow gives inf (±65504 under MODE toward zero, unmeasured). The scaling works on the f16 bits (`Gen.f16_omod`): a pre-omod zero or denormal f16 (`e == 0`) gives `+0`, `div:2` underflowing an exponent-1 normal (`e == 1`) gives signed zero (`h & 0x8000`, so negative inputs give `-0.0`, which a trailing `clamp` still turns into `+0`), `inf` and `NaN` pass through, and finite `mul:2`/`mul:4` overflow gives `±inf` in RNE or `±65504` under `MODE` toward zero (`FP_ROUND = 3`), where `±65520` / `±FLT_MAX` with `div:2` also round to `±65504` before halving to `±32752`. Until 2026-11-22 both modifiers were dropped; `Gen.f16_omod` also flushed `e == 1 div:2` to `+0` until the `gfx1036` measurement. Sources: [@shelbykb2's RX 6800 words](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/718#issuecomment-7074230544) (gfx1030, not the console's gfx1013): [@shelbykb2's RX 6800 words](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/718#issuecomment-7074230544) (gfx1030, not the console's gfx1013) and `tools/cvt_f16_omod_probe.py` on `gfx1036` (Radeon(TM) Graphics, Windows 11 driver `32.0.21045.29003`, wave32 & wave64, 2,880 evaluations, 0 diffs across static/dynamic `MODE`): `clamp` applies in every float mode, the omod only with IEEE off and f16 denormals flushed (with `denorm16_64=3` or `ieee=1` omod is ignored while `clamp` still applies), which is the mode a compiler emits an omod for, as the f32 rows above argue; 2^-15 `mul:2` and -0 `mul:2` gave 0x0000 there. That flush is modelled only on results that carry an omod. The f16 ALU results (`v_fma_f16`, `v_fmac_f16`, …) still scale before narrowing, which differs from this only on overflow, denormals and the sign of zero. Checked by `ps_outmods_f16.asm` (`tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`, and on a GPU in both runners): each of its six results changes a word when its modifier is dropped, and two words change when the omod is applied before the rounding. Checked by `ps_outmods_f16.asm` (`tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py`, and on a GPU in both runners), `tests/test_cvt_f16_omod_probe.py`, and `tests/semantic/test_cvt_f16_omod.py`. |
| 16-bit vector compare `op_sel` (`v_cmp(x)_*_{f16,u16,i16}`) | VOP3 `op_sel:[s0,s1]` selects bit `[15:0]` (`0`) or `[31:16]` (`1`) of each source before f16 input modifiers (`neg`/`abs`, via `Gen.f16_in`) or `u16`/`i16` zero-/sign-extension (`OpBitFieldUExtract`/`OpBitFieldSExtract` at bit offset 16 when set, 0 otherwise); VOPC writes a scalar lane mask (VCC/SGPR or EXEC for `v_cmpx`), so there is no destination `op_sel` bit. Measured (TEST-650) with `tools/vcmp16_opsel_probe.py --oracle hw`, which writes `v_cmp_{lt,eq,gt}_{f16,u16,i16}_e64` as raw words with OPSEL set by hand in word 0 [12:11]: llvm-mc rejects `op_sel` on these compares and drops the bits when disassembling, so no listing carries it. On an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036), with the Windows HIP launcher (AMD 26.10.1) and independently with the Linux DRM launcher (#3329), all 50 rows read the low halves in wave32 and wave64, including the 20 where the selected halves would answer differently (f16 with and without `neg`, u16, sign-extended i16). The same hand-set bits on `v_add_nc_u16_e64` do select the high halves, so the encoding is right. The translator still honours `op_sel` here (from the ISA text and Mesa ACO `can_use_opsel`), which only a hand-encoded word can reach: #3487, which made it read the low halves as TEST-650 measured, was reverted because the constant, literal and SGPR-high-half checks below (row 403, measured natively) expect `op_sel` to select; the two measurements disagree and are open. Until 2026-11-22 `Gen.compare` hardcoded the low half and ignored `mods`. Source: AMD RDNA2 ISA VOP3 `op_sel` and Mesa ACO `can_use_opsel`. **Measured natively on RDNA2** (gfx1036, AMD Ryzen 7000 iGPU, driver 26.11.2) across wave32 and wave64: `v_cmp` writes 0 for inactive lanes in `sdst` (`s4` in wave32, `s[4:5]` in wave64); `v_cmpx` kills inactive lanes in EXEC; inline float constants (`1.0`, `-2.0`, `0.5`, `4.0`) read f16 in low half with `op_sel=0` and +0.0 with `op_sel=1`; inline integer constants (`1`, `-1`) read low half with `op_sel=0` and high half with `op_sel=1` (`0` for `1`, `0xFFFF` for `-1`); 32-bit literals read `[15:0]` (`op_sel=0`) and `[31:16]` (`op_sel=1`, including signed negative high halves in `i16`); SGPR high halves as src0 and src1 with modifiers (`neg`, `abs`); and f16 ordered vs unordered relations on `qNaN`/`sNaN`. Probed natively with zero divergences by `tools/probe_gfx1036_vcmp16.py`, and checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_vcmp16_opsel.py` (all four `op_sel` combinations, spirv-val compliance, wave32/wave64 EXEC updates and inactive-lane 0 writes in `sdst`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_vcmp16_opsel.py` (all four `op_sel` combinations across `f16` relations/ordered/unordered/NaN/`neg`/`abs`, `u16`, signed `i16`, and `v_cmpx` wave32/wave64 EXEC updates via `spirv_mini_interp` and `spirv_wave_interp`); Not measured: `v_cmpx`, `abs`, RDNA1. **not GPU-verified**. |
| `v_cmp_class_f16`, `v_cmpx_class_f16`, `v_cmp_class_f64`, `v_cmpx_class_f64` | IEEE-754 floating-point class tests against the 10-bit class mask (`0: sNaN, 1: qNaN, 2: -inf, 3: -normal, 4: -denormal, 5: -0, 6: +0, 7: +denormal, 8: +normal, 9: +inf`), writing VCC/SGPR or narrowing EXEC (`v_cmpx`). Computed bit-exactly on integer words (so `sNaN` is never quieted by float ops and `v_cmp(x)_class_f64` does not require `OpCapability Float64`): `f16` reads both the value operand (`op_sel[0]`) and the class mask operand via `src_h` (so float inline constants like `0.5`, `1.0`, `-2.0`, `0.15915494` take their 16-bit binary16 value over a zero high half before SDWA selection, matching `tools/sdwa_diff.py --oracle hw` on RDNA2 `gfx1036`; until 2026-11-23 `cmp_class` read the mask with `src_u`, turning inline float constants into 32-bit binary32 bits and failing `sdwa_diff --ops v_cmp_class_f16` 12/500) and applies `abs`/`neg` on bits `[15:0]`; `f16` reads the selected half (`op_sel[0]`, inline f16 constants via `src_h`) and applies `abs`/`neg` on bits `[15:0]`; `f64` reads the `(lo, hi)` pair (32-bit literals placed in the upper word with `lo = 0` per RDNA2 ISA) and applies `abs`/`neg` on `hi`. Output modifiers (`clamp`, `mul:*`, `div:*`) and unsupported `op_sel` bits raise `Unsupported`. Source: AMD [RDNA 2 ISA reference](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (VOPC `V_CMP_CLASS_F16/F64`, `V_CMPX_CLASS_F16/F64`). Checked bit-for-bit across all 10 classes on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp`) and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_cmp_class_f16_f64.py`, and across 500 SDWA programs (`v_cmp_class_f16`, 0 mismatches) via `tools/sdwa_diff.py`; **not GPU-verified** and not run on the shader corpus. . **Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) with `tools/hw_oracle`, 85 cases, all matching the operand's own bits: the ten classes on ten `f16` words (`0x7c01` sNaN, `0x7e00` qNaN, `0xfc00` -inf, `0xbc00` -normal, `0x8001` -denormal, `0x8000` -0, `0x0000` +0, `0x0001` +denormal, `0x3c00` +normal, `0x7c00` +inf) and on ten `f64` pairs, each against its own class bit, all ten bits, no bits and the nine other bits (80), plus both `v_cmpx` forms on the launched lane (5). Not measured: `abs`/`neg`/`op_sel`, an inactive lane's `vdst`/`EXEC` under `v_cmpx`, wave64, and the shader corpus. |
| Float-to-byte pack | `v_cvt_pk_u8_f32` rounds to nearest even (the default MODE round mode; the hardware follows the MODE register's f32 round mode, which the translator ignores, see #358), clamps to [0, 255], and gives 0 for NaN and negative values, then inserts the byte at `src1[1:0]` of `src2` and keeps the other three. NaN is replaced by +0 before `FClamp`, which is undefined for a NaN operand. **Measured natively** on RDNA2 (gfx1036, the Ryzen 7 9800X3D iGPU, AMD driver 25.10, the instruction run through inline assembly): all 256 inputs k + 0.5 round to even, 0.5 gives 0, 0x3f000001 gives 1, +inf and 300.0 give 255, -inf, -1.0, ±0, denormals and quiet, signalling and negative NaNs give 0 (#358). `cs_cvt_pk_u8.asm` holds eight of these words, checked by `check.py`/`run_gpu_tests.py` and bit-for-bit by `tests/semantic/test_cvt_pk_u8.py`. |
| Known approximations | `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` is now **exact round-toward-zero** (finite overflow gives ±65504, not inf). The GPU smoke test caught the old round-to-nearest version producing inf in `PS_ImposterShadow`. The other f16 packs (`v_cvt_f16_f32`, d16 results, mix ops) round to nearest, which matches the default mode; the ALU ones follow a MODE toward-zero write (see the f16 rounding row). `v_fma_mixlo/mixhi_f16` round the exact `x*y+z` once in every mode (measured on an RDNA2 iGPU, gfx1036, 7 of 7 discriminating tie inputs, SHADER-135); the translator does that in both modes through `Gen.fma_mix_odd`; the ALU ones follow a MODE toward-zero write (below; `v_fma_mixlo/mixhi_f16` round the exact `x*y+z` once in every mode (measured on an RDNA2 iGPU, gfx1036, 7 of 7 discriminating tie inputs, SHADER-135); toward zero the translator does that already, while in nearest-even mode it still narrows an f32 `Fma`, which is 1 f16 ulp off on ties until #1774 (SHADER-296) lands; the ALU ones follow a MODE toward-zero write (below; ALU sites: see the f16 rounding row). MODE: `s_setreg_imm32_b32 hwreg(HW_REG_MODE, 2, 2)` (f16/f64 rounding) is tracked in a per-invocation variable, the only MODE field the corpus writes (9,417 writes in 6,333 shaders: 6,694 toward zero, 2,723 back to nearest even). While it is toward zero, every VALU f16 result except the transcendentals listed below (`v_cvt_f16_f32`, f16 arithmetic, `v_pk_*_f16`, `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16`, `v_interp_p2_f16`) rounds toward zero exactly, with the integer conversion `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` uses (`f16_bits(rtz=True)`, see the f16 rounding row), and `v_add_f64` steps back one ulp when the TwoSum error shows it rounded away from zero; finite overflow gives the largest finite value. The `v_add_f64` output modifiers apply after that step and round to nearest, so an omod that overflows or lands on a subnormal is not toward zero. **omod versus denormal mode is unmodelled:** the translator applies `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` regardless of the MODE denormal fields (as before this change), whereas hardware ignores omod when denormals are kept (denorm fields = 3) and applies it when they are flushed (both measured on an RX 6800: `v_add_f64 (1 - 2^-60) mul:2` with denormals kept gave 3ff00000:00000000 nearest and 3fefffff:ffffffff toward zero, unscaled; mul:2 was applied with denormals flushed); the tests therefore claim no omod order. CPU-checked by `tests/test_mode_round.py` (the RX 6800 words for f16 add/sub, `v_fma_mix`, the transcendentals, f64 overflow). f16 add and sub (f16 operands) and `v_pk_add_f16` take the exact TwoSum error of the f32 result into account, so they are exact toward zero too (f32 then f16 rounding alone would be wrong: 2 - 2^-24 is an f32 tie that rounds up to 2.0); f16 FMAs need nothing extra, since their round-to-odd f32 value (SHADER-55) narrows exactly in either mode. `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes (measured on AMD RDNA2 gfx1036, finding 7079901079; an RX 6800 gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30, where rounding to f32 first and then narrowing gives 0x4000): `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes (measured on AMD RDNA2 gfx1036 across 7 of 7 discriminating tie inputs with `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw`, SHADER-135, finding 7079901079, and on an RX 6800 where toward zero gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30 vs 0x4000 with double rounding; fixed for nearest mode in SHADER-296 / #1774; see docs/shader_validation.md, "`v_fma_mix*` f16 rounding in nearest mode"): `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, toward zero (an RX 6800 gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30, where rounding to f32 first and then narrowing gives 0x4000): `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes (measured on AMD RDNA2 gfx1036 and verified by `tools/fma_mix_probe.py`; finding 7079901079; toward zero an RX 6800 gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30, where rounding to f32 first and then narrowing gives 0x4000): `Gen.fma_mix_odd` corrects the f32 `Fma` to round-to-odd by the sign of its exact residual (product error by `Fma(x, y, -x*y)`, TwoSum of the sum), which narrows exactly in either direction; the nearest-even path is unchanged (f32 `Fma`, then narrow). `fma_mix_odd` also handles a tiny product ( \| a x*y \| >= < 2^-64: its error underflows or is flushed, so the residual sign is sign(x) xor sign(y), independent of the driver's denormal mode) and an f32 overflow with finite operands (toward zero gives ±FLT_MAX, so f16 ±65504). `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_cos_f16`, `v_exp_f16` and `v_log_f16` ignore the rounding mode on hardware, so they always narrow to nearest even; all seven are measured (an RX 6700 XT gave the same words with FP_ROUND round-toward-zero and round-to-nearest on every input of each; see the f16 transcendentals row). `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_cos_f16`, `v_exp_f16` and `v_log_f16` ignore the rounding mode on hardware (RX 6800 for the first four, plus an exhaustive 65,536-input RNE vs RTZ sweep of all seven in wave32 and wave64 on native `gfx1036` via `tools/mode_round_probe.py`, SHADER-132: 0/65,536 inputs differ on every op; the same probe also confirmed all 17 f16/f64 RNE vs RTZ cases including `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_add/sub/mul/fma_f16`, `v_pk_add/fma_f16`, `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` and `v_add_f64` overflow saturation in both wave32 and wave64), so they always narrow to nearest even. In nearest mode the hardware rounds once as well: `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16`, `v_sin_f16`, `v_cos_f16`, `v_exp_f16` and `v_log_f16` ignore the rounding mode on hardware, so they always narrow to nearest even; all seven are measured (an RX 6700 XT gave the same words with FP_ROUND round-toward-zero and round-to-nearest on every input of each, see the f16 transcendentals row; independently confirmed by an exhaustive 65,536-input RNE vs RTZ sweep of all seven in wave32 and wave64 on native `gfx1036` via `tools/mode_round_probe.py`, SHADER-132: `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16`, `v_sqrt_f16` and `v_sin_f16` ignore the rounding mode on hardware (RX 6800: the same words in every mode), so they always narrow to nearest even; `v_cos_f16`, `v_exp_f16` and `v_log_f16` are unmeasured, assumed like those four and treated the same. The "every VALU f16 result rounds toward zero" sentence above therefore excludes these seven; the f16 results of `v_floor/ceil/trunc/rndne/fract_f16` are exact and need no rounding. `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, SHADER-135) gave the single-rounded word for all seven inputs where the two rules differ by one f16 ulp, as `Gen.fma_mix_odd` produces (docs/shader_validation.md, "`v_fma_mix*` f16 rounding in nearest mode"). In nearest mode the translator still rounds `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` twice (f32 `Fma`, then narrow), but the hardware rounds once there as well: `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` on an RDNA2 iGPU (gfx1036, SHADER-135) gave the single-rounded word for all seven inputs where the two rules differ by one f16 ulp, so the translator, which rounded twice there (f32 `Fma`, then narrow) until #1774, was one f16 ulp off on exactly those ties (docs/shader_validation.md, "`v_fma_mix*` f16 rounding in nearest mode"; the fix is `Gen.fma_mix_odd` in nearest mode too, SHADER-296). Nearest-mode `v_fma_mix*` still rounds twice (f32 `Fma`, then narrow): main's behaviour, unmeasured. `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw` measures it on an AMD GPU, with seven inputs where the two rules differ by one f16 ulp (docs/shader_validation.md). The `v_add_f64` step needs f64 denormals preserved (not flushed) by the driver for subnormal sums. Shaders that never write MODE emit the same code as without this. Writes to other MODE bits (f32 rounding, denormals), directed rounding (+inf/-inf) and register-valued writes are refused, so nothing changes silently; none occur in the corpus. Denormal behaviour is still Vulkan's default. Format conversions in memory and texture units don't follow MODE. `v_mul_legacy_f32` now keeps the `0 × anything = 0` rule like `v_mad_legacy_f32` (GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_legacy_mul.asm` on AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT). \| b \| `, `FastTwoSum`: `bb = r - a`, `e = b - bb`) and steps back one ulp when the rounding error shows it rounded away from zero; finite overflow gives the largest finite value (unordered `TwoSum` `r - x` can overflow to `±inf` and yield `NaN` error for opposite-sign finite operands with ` \| x \| < \| y \| ` near `±DBL_MAX`, e.g. `0x7fdd9bb47daa1333 + 0xffefffffffffffff` found by `@Jolymmiles`; GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/ps_f64_round.asm` on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5060 Ti, 4096/4096 pixels exact). The `v_add_f64` output modifiers apply after that step and round to nearest, so an omod that overflows or lands on a subnormal is not toward zero. **omod versus denormal mode is unmodelled:** the translator applies `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` regardless of the MODE denormal fields (as before this change), whereas hardware ignores omod when denormals are kept (denorm fields = 3) and applies it when they are flushed (both measured on an RX 6800: `v_add_f64 (1 - 2^-60) mul:2` with denormals kept gave 3ff00000:00000000 nearest and 3fefffff:ffffffff toward zero, unscaled; mul:2 was applied with denormals flushed); the tests therefore claim no omod order. CPU-checked by `tests/test_mode_round.py` (the RX 6800 words for f16 add/sub, `v_fma_mix`, the transcendentals, f64 overflow). f16 add and sub (f16 operands) and `v_pk_add_f16` take the exact TwoSum error of the f32 result into account, so they are exact toward zero too (f32 then f16 rounding alone would be wrong: 2 - 2^-24 is an f32 tie that rounds up to 2.0); f16 FMAs need nothing extra, since their round-to-odd f32 value (SHADER-55) narrows exactly in either mode. `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes (measured on AMD RDNA2 gfx1036 across 7 of 7 discriminating tie inputs with `tools/fma_mix_probe.py --oracle hw`, SHADER-135, finding 7079901079, and on an RX 6800 where toward zero gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30 vs 0x4000 with double rounding; fixed for nearest mode in SHADER-296 / #1774; see docs/shader_validation.md, "`v_fma_mix*` f16 rounding in nearest mode"): `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, toward zero (an RX 6800 gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30, where rounding to f32 first and then narrowing gives 0x4000): `v_fma_mix{lo,hi}_f16` rounds the exact fma once, straight to f16, in both nearest-even and toward-zero modes (measured on AMD RDNA2 gfx1036 and verified by `tools/fma_mix_probe.py`; finding 7079901079; toward zero an RX 6800 gave 0x3fff for 1 * 2 - 2^-30, where rounding to f32 first and then narrowing gives 0x4000): `Gen.fma_mix_odd` corrects the f32 `Fma` to round-to-odd by the sign of its exact residual (product error by `Fma(x, y, -x*y)`, TwoSum of the sum), which narrows exactly in either direction; | Denormal flush in `v_mad_f32`, `v_mac_f32`, `v_mad_legacy_f32` (SHADER-118) | The three have no f32 denormal support: a subnormal operand, the rounded product or the result reads as signed zero, whatever the MODE register says. `Gen.flush_f32` emits ` \| x \| < FLT_MIN → copysign(0, x)` on the three operands, after the product rounding and (via `Gen.flush_f32_mods`) both before and after `omod`/`clamp`; inf and NaN compare false and pass through, and 0 keeps its sign. Source: LLVM AMDGPU `SIISelLowering.cpp`, `SITargetLowering::isFMADLegal` (llvm-project `1922c0c`), which forms `v_mad_f32`/`v_mac_f32` only when f32 denormals are flushed. Corpus count with `tools/shader_profile.py` over the 26,503 gfx1013 shaders: `v_mad_f32` in 7,298 shaders (139,287 occurrences), `v_mac_f32` in 5,889 (276,806: 265,901 `_e32`, 10,811 `_e64`, 94 `_dpp`), `v_mad_legacy_f32` in 6,059 (24,765); 9,297 shaders (35.1%) use at least one, 440,858 occurrences (0.99% of the corpus's 44,406,870 instructions). **Measured natively on RDNA1** (`AMD Radeon RX 5600 XT`, `gfx1010`, Windows driver `32.0.21045.29003`, `tools/mad_denorm_probe.py --oracle hw`, SHADER-340): 48 of 48 cases match in wave32 and wave64 across `game` (`IEEE=0`, `denorm32=0`), `dx10_clamp=0` and `denorm32=3` (`v_mul_f32(FLT_MIN, 0.5)` confirms `denorm32=3` keeps subnormals on the GPU while the mad/mac ops still flush them), and all 40 non-`omod` cases match under `IEEE=1` (where hardware ignores `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` as for other f32 VALU ops). Before SHADER-340 the translator ran `flush_f32` only after `clamp_mods`, which gave `FLT_MIN` (`0x00800000`) instead of `+0` (`0x00000000`) on `(1.5*2^-126 - 2^-126) mul:2` (and `0x01000000` on `mul:4`), whereas hardware flushes the sum before `omod` (`+0`) as well as after (`FLT_MIN div:2` → `+0`, `-FLT_MIN div:2` → `-0`). Checked on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_valu_f32_min3_max3_mad.py` (`DenormalFlushTests`: the flush column, pre- and post-`omod` cases, NaN/inf/zero pass-through, and a sabotaged pass-through that fails) and `tests/test_mad_denorm_probe.py`. **not GPU-verified** — a native measurement still needs an RDNA1 part (gfx1010-gfx1013): no RDNA2 part has these opcodes, and on gfx103x the encodings of `v_mad_legacy_f32` (VOP3 0x140) and `v_mac_legacy_f32` (VOP2 0x06) are the fused `v_fma_legacy_f32` / `v_fmac_legacy_f32`, so a raw-encoding run on the gfx1036 oracle would measure a different instruction (see the GFX10.1/GFX10.3 opcode row). **not GPU-verified** — a native measurement still needs an RDNA1 part (gfx1010-gfx1013): no RDNA2 part has these opcodes, and on gfx103x the encodings of `v_mad_legacy_f32` (VOP3 0x140) and `v_mac_legacy_f32` (VOP2 0x06) are the fused `v_fma_legacy_f32` / `v_fmac_legacy_f32`, so a raw-encoding run on the gfx1036 oracle would measure a different instruction (see the GFX10.1/GFX10.3 opcode row). The `omod` order is an assumption: the flush runs after `div:2`/`mul:2`/`mul:4`, so `FLT_MIN div:2` is flushed, as pinned by that test. |
| 16-bit high-half reads (`Gen.u16_half` / `Gen.i16_half`, #1506) | Reading the high 16 bits of a 32-bit word emits `OpShiftRightLogical %uint u 16` (`u16_half`) or `OpShiftRightArithmetic %int (as_int(u)) 16` (`i16_half`) alone, without a trailing `OpBitwiseAnd ... 0xFFFF` or `OpBitFieldSExtract ... 0 16` (used for the low half only). On 32-bit inputs `>> 16` already zero- or sign-extends bits 31..16, and AMD's Windows LLPC compiler (measured on RDNA2, drivers 22.40.84.06 in #1506 and 24.10.24 on Ryzen 5 7600 iGPU) miscompiles both `(u >> 16) & 0xFFFF` and `OpBitFieldSExtract (u >> 16) 0 16` after two partial-half writes to `u` by dropping the `>> 16` when the result feeds `OpIAdd`, `OpIMul`, or `OpShiftLeftLogical` (e.g. `(0x41b1 << 1) & 0xFFFF` became `0x0000` instead of `0x8362` in `cs_f16fuzz_s2_163_w64`, `s_bitreplicate`'s `((0x41b1 << 8) \| 0x41b1) & 0x00ff00ff` became `0xb10000` instead of `0x4100b1`, and `SExtract(0x91b1138b >> 16, 0, 16) + 1` became `0x91b10001` instead of `0xffff91b2`). Applies across all 16-bit half readers (`v_*_b16`/`u16`/`i16`, `v_mad_{u,i}16`, `v_min3/max3/med3_{u,i}16`, `v_cvt_f16_{u,i}16`, `v_pack_b32_f16`, `v_frexp_*_f16`, `v_ldexp_f16`, `s_bitreplicate_b64_b32`, and `buffer_store_format_d16_*`). Checked by the unit tests in `tests/test_u16_high_half.py` and `tools/valu_fuzz.py --family f16 --seed 2 --programs 200 --wave both` on AMD Ryzen 5 7600 iGPU (driver 24.10.24: 400/400 native, up from 399/400; 200/200 wave64 emulated on both AMD and NVIDIA). **The packed 16-bit shifts follow the same rule** (`v_pk_lshlrev_b16`, `v_pk_lshrrev_b16`, `v_pk_ashrrev_i16`, since 2026-11-23): the shift amount comes from `OpShiftRightLogical %uint s0 16` then `& 15`, and the value from `OpShiftRight{Logical,Arithmetic} %uint s1 16`, where the code used `OpBitField{S,U}Extract … 16 16`. A field extract at offset 16 is what an AMD Windows driver 25.10.36 on a gfx1036 iGPU reads with the offset taken mod 16, so both result halves got the **low** half of the source: 5 of 2,000 `tools/valu_fuzz.py --family f16 --programs 2000 --seed 1 --wave 32` programs, all in wave32 and none in native wave64 (@Darkz0rz, reported in the coordination issue as SHADER-777). The same 2,000 programs match 2000/2000 on a second gfx1036 (Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 7 7800X3D, driver 31.0.24002.92), where the field-extract form was already correct, and its SPIR-V does contain the offset-16 extracts, so the change is a lowering that survives both drivers, not a value change: `OpBitField{S,U}Extract(x, 16, 16)` equals `x >> 16` with the matching right shift and `OpBitFieldUExtract(x, 16, 4)` equals `(x >> 16) & 15`, checked for 200,010 inputs including 0, 1, 0xFFFF, 0x10000, 0x80000000, 0xFFFFFFFF, 0x7FFFFFFF and 0x8000. Counted over the 26,503 listings: 1 `v_pk_lshlrev_b16` in 1 compute listing (`op_sel_hi:[0,1]`) and 1 `v_pk_lshrrev_b16` in 1 other (`op_sel:[1,0] op_sel_hi:[0,0]`), no `v_pk_ashrrev_i16` and no `_sdwa` form; those two modules are the only ones whose output changes. Values are checked by the unit tests in `tests/test_pk16_diff.py` and the `ps_ops4` / `ps_ops6` fixtures. |
| `v_mac_legacy_f32` | The GFX10.1 VOP2 form of `v_mad_legacy_f32` with D as the addend: D = legacy(S0 * S1) + D, where 0 times anything (inf and NaN included) is +0, with the same denormal flush and VOP3 modifiers. D is read before it is written, as for `v_mac_f32` (LLVM `VOP2Instructions.td`: `V_MAC_LEGACY_F32` has `$src2` tied to `$vdst`). Until 2026-11-22 it raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `MacLegacyF32Tests` in `tests/semantic/test_valu_f32_min3_max3_mad.py` (a 15x8x6 grid against the `v_mad_legacy_f32` reference, the zero rule, clamp/neg, D aliased as a source). **Not GPU-verified**: like the other mad opcodes it exists on gfx1010-1013 only, so no RDNA2 desktop part runs it. |
| `v_cmp_*_f64`, `v_cmpx_*_f64` | All 16 binary64 vector compare predicates (`f`, `lt`, `eq`, `le`, `gt`, `lg`, `ge`, `o`, `u`, `nge`, `nlg`, `ngt`, `nle`, `neq`, `nlt`, `tru`) for both `v_cmp_` (VCC/SGPR lane mask) and `v_cmpx_` (EXEC narrowing), lowered in `Gen.compare_f64_cond` via pure 32-bit `%uint` IEEE-754 ordering (NaN detection, `-0.0` canonicalized to `+0.0`, sign-magnitude to monotonic unsigned 64-bit key) with no `Float64` capability required and no denormal flushing. Supports VGPR/SGPR pairs, `fimm`, sign-extended inline integers (`-16..64`), 32-bit upper-half literals, and VOP3 `abs`/`neg` sign-bit modifiers on both sources; inactive lanes write 0 for `v_cmp_*_f64` and stay inactive for `v_cmpx_*_f64`. Source: AMD RDNA2 ISA VOPC/VOP3 binary64 compares. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_cmp_f64.py` (all 16 predicates across full IEEE-754 edge grid including subnormals/1-ULP pairs/±0/±inf/sNaN/qNaN, SGPR pairs, constants, `abs`/`neg`, `v_cmpx_*_f64`, inactive lanes, no `Float64` capability, bad-operand rejections). **Not GPU-verified.** Measured on native gfx1100 (single thread, 116 cases): all 16 predicates return IEEE-754 results (`eq` on `NaN` is 0 while `neq`/`nlt`/`nge`/`ngt` are 1, `lg` is 0 for `NaN` with `o`/`u` splitting on it, `eq(+0.0, -0.0)` is 1, `inf` compares equal to itself) and `v_cmpx_*_f64` narrows `EXEC` instead of writing `VCC`. |
| Whole-quad mode (`s_wqm_b64` / `s_wqm_b32`) | A lane mask register holds only this lane's bit, so WQM on one (EXEC, VCC, an SGPR the mask analysis found) ORs the bit of the lane's quad, lanes 4n..4n+3 of the wave: two `OpGroupNonUniformQuadSwap` (horizontal, then vertical; capability `GroupNonUniformQuad`) in the default mode in fragment and compute shaders (quad operations are only guaranteed there; vertex, geometry and mesh shaders shuffle from the quad's four lanes), or the four lanes' values read through `%x_buf` under `--wave64-emulate` (no subgroup operation there). A quad never crosses lane 31/32, so the mask bits are exact per half on a 32-wide subgroup; SCC is a wave vote, so on wave64 it needs a 64-wide subgroup or `--wave64-emulate` (as every wave-wide mask op). The result goes to EXEC, VCC or an SGPR pair as a mask, SCC = the wave-wide any of it (same vote as the other mask ops). A 32-bit form in wave64 (`exec_lo`/`exec_hi`, `vcc_lo`/`vcc_hi`, a half-mask SGPR) writes only its own half (`exec_half_write`) and votes SCC over that half only (`cs_wqm_w64_half`). A source that is not a lane mask (a value loaded from memory, a literal) is data: `((x \| x>>1 \| x>>2 \| x>>3) & 0x11111111) * 15` per 32-bit word and SCC = result != 0, no subgroup operation. Assumptions: in compute and mesh shaders the subgroup lane is `LocalInvocationIndex` mod the subgroup size (as for every cross-lane op); a hardware quad is four consecutive lanes of a wave, and in a pixel shader Vulkan's quad (the 2x2 pixels, helper invocations included) is the same four lanes; GPU-checked on both AMD and NVIDIA with `ps_wqm.asm` (every 2x2 block of a 64x64 target, fully covered). **Helper lanes at a triangle edge, measured on RDNA2** (gfx1036, wave64, raw ISA through `tools/ps_oracle.py` on RADV; `tools/ps_helper_probe.py`, docs/shader_validation.md): a pixel wave's entry EXEC is exactly its covered pixels (helpers off); `s_wqm_b64 exec, exec` turns on exactly the quads with a covered pixel, which are packed from lane 0 (a quad with no covered pixel isn't in the wave); lane % 4 = (x & 1) + 2 (y & 1), so lanes 0-3 are top-left, top-right, bottom-left, bottom-right; a helper's `v_interp` gives its own pixel centre (extrapolated, as precisely as a covered pixel), and its v0/v1 hold its own I/J before `s_wqm` too; without `s_wqm` its VGPRs aren't written; an `exp ... vm` with helpers on in EXEC writes no pixel outside the triangle. Not covered: helper-invocation behaviour at triangle edges is untested, and in native mode an absent invocation in a partial quad (a compute group whose size is not a multiple of 4) gives an undefined value (emulated, the missing lanes of a partial wave read as 0). Before this the instruction was the identity, so helper lanes stayed off (#156 SHADER-34: `s_wqm_b64` appears in 4,285 pixel shaders). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_wqm_w64.asm`, `cs_wqm_w64_half.asm`, `cs_wqm_w32.asm` and `ps_wqm.asm` on AMD Radeon 880M (subgroup 64) and NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Laptop (subgroup 32; `cs_wqm_w64` there needs `LEONIDA_W64_EMULATE=1`), with and without `--wave64-emulate`. |
| `buffer_atomic_{and,or,xor,swap}_x2` | `M = op(M, DATA)` on the 64-bit element at the MUBUF address (two dwords, low first); with `glc` the old 64 bits are returned in `vdata[0:1]`. A bitwise op or an exchange leaves the same memory done one dword at a time, so each dword is its own 32-bit `OpAtomic*` and no Int64 atomics are needed. **Approximation:** the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done. Each dword is its own 32-bit `OpAtomic*`, so no Int64 atomics are needed; a bitwise op leaves the same memory that way, since its bits are independent. **Approximation:** an exchange can tear, since two lanes swapping into one element at once can leave one's low dword with the other's high dword (A.lo, B.lo, B.hi, A.hi), and the returned pair comes from two reads, so another lane's 64-bit op on the same element between them can show up half done. **Bounds:** the check covers the whole 8-byte element, all or nothing, as for a typed 32_32 element (`buffer_address(elem="fmt")`). An out-of-range or inactive lane leaves memory alone and returns 0, as `buffer_atomic` does. How hardware treats a 64-bit atomic that is only partly in range is **not measured**. Still `Unsupported`: `add/sub/min/max_x2` and `cmpswap_x2`. Until 2026-11-22 every `_x2` buffer atomic was rejected. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_atomics_x2.py` (the four ops with and without `glc`, an element ending exactly at `num_records`, half and fully out of range, seeded values, rejected forms). **Not GPU-verified.** |
| Wave64 graphics: partition invariance (SHADER-10) | A wave64 VS/PS/GS on a 32-wide subgroup is exact **iff its observable results do not change when the wave is cut into two 32-lane halves** (the host forms the subgroups of graphics stages, so there is no partner half to exchange with). `tools/wave64_halves.py` decides it from the code (forward taint dataflow over the basic blocks, plus control dependence on wave-wide votes) and the translator records it in `index.json` as `wave64_graphics` (`A`/`B`/`C`), `wave64_graphics_reasons` (tier C) and `wave64_graphics_idioms` (tier B). **A**: only per-lane arithmetic, votes that skip lane-masked work (`s_cbranch_execz/execnz`; `vccz/vccnz` whose run side starts by narrowing EXEC to that VCC and whose other side is the join, with no code of its own: a half that votes no runs that side, the wave does not; the same holds for `s_cbranch_execz/execnz`: the side that runs when EXEC is zero is taken by a half with no active lane, so it must be the join (or only jumps to it) or the exit of a loop the vote closes, else tier C `unsafe-execz-branch`; `s_cbranch_scc*` after a vote is the same kind of skip, any other reader of that SCC, `s_cselect`/`s_cmov`/`s_addc`, is tier C; a vote is on the mask register's value when it was taken, so a new value in that register before the branch (`v_cmp` into the pair or VCC, `s_mov_b64`, `s_cselect_b64`: none of them writes SCC) ends it, and the branch is judged as a plain SCC branch, SHADER-820), whole-quad masks, `ds_bpermute`/`ds_permute`/`v_permlane16`/`v_permlanex16`/`ds_swizzle` (they stay inside a half), a `ds_bpermute`/`ds_permute` address made from the lane id (`v_mbcnt_lo` then `v_mbcnt_hi` of -1) when it is the same modulo 128 bytes in the wave and in a half (the id is 0..63 there and 0..31 here, equal modulo 32 lanes;
