sums, differences and bitwise ops with invariant values keep that, a left shift by c multiplies the modulus by 2^c, and the permute uses address bits 2..6 of its half only: `(id ^ 1) << 2` is exact, `id << 1`, a right shift of the id or the low count alone stay tier C `lane-id`), loops closed by `execnz` with no scalar state carried, and the early exit of a pixel kill: `s_cbranch_scc0/scc1` (or `vccz/vccnz`) on a lane mask's own "not empty" (the last SCC writer is a 64-bit logic op into that mask, e.g. `s_andn2_b64 live, live, exec`, or `s_cmp_lg_u64 mask, 0`, and nothing rewrites the mask before the branch) to a block that only sets EXEC from that mask or 0 (`s_mov_b64 exec`, or `s_and_b64 exec` with it), may then export with that EXEC (an export is allowed, not required), and ends (no other code, store or successor), while the run side narrows EXEC into the mask's whole quads before anything but mask arithmetic (`s_mov_b64`, `s_and_b64`, `s_wqm_b64`) runs, the narrowing itself possibly an `s_and_saveexec_b64` / `s_andn2_saveexec_b64` (or `_wrexec_`) whose source is inside, and keeps it inside on every path from there, loops included (a must-dataflow: an EXEC write keeps it inside only when it is provably a subset: `v_cmpx`, `s_and_b64` with one operand known inside, `s_andn2_b64` / `s_andn2_saveexec_b64` whose first operand is, `s_and_saveexec_b64` while inside or from one that is, `s_andn1`/`s_or`/`s_xor_saveexec_b64` only while inside (the latter two also from one that is), and copies, `s_wqm_b64`, `s_or_b64` and `s_xor_b64` of operands known to be inside (0 counts as inside); a saveexec destination holds the old EXEC and a wrexec one the new, each inside only if that EXEC was), and no `exp ... vm` can run before the vote. A half whose mask is empty then writes nothing and discards its pixels in the exit, and in the wave its lanes are off to the end, so the wave discards them too; an exit shared by several kills reads a mask redefined behind the earlier votes and stays tier C. GPU-checked by `ps_w64_a_discard` (below). `s_cbranch_scc*` after a vote is the same kind of skip, any other reader of that SCC, `s_cselect`/`s_cmov`/`s_addc`, is tier C; a vote is on the mask register's value when it was taken, so a new value in that register before the branch (`v_cmp` into the pair or VCC, `s_mov_b64`, `s_cselect_b64`: none of them writes SCC) ends it, and the branch is judged as a plain SCC branch, SHADER-820), whole-quad masks, `ds_bpermute`/`v_permlane16` (they stay inside a half, not GPU-tested here: no fixture; `s_cbranch_scc*` after a vote is the same kind of skip, any other reader of that SCC, `s_cselect`/`s_cmov`/`s_addc`, is tier C), whole-quad masks, `ds_bpermute`/`v_permlane16` (they stay inside a half, not GPU-tested here: no fixture; `ds_swizzle` and `v_permlanex16` likewise; GPU-checked at required 64 and 32 lanes by `ps_w64_a_bpermute`, `ps_w64_a_permlane` (`v_permlane16` and `v_permlanex16`) and `ps_w64_a_swizzle` in `w64_graphics.py`, TEST-433), loops closed by `execnz` with no scalar state carried. `ds_swizzle` and `v_permlanex16` likewise), loops closed by `execnz` with no scalar state carried. A `vccz`/`vccnz` on a wave-uniform compare is no lane vote and is tier A too: a `v_cmp` made while EXEC is the launch mask from untainted immediates, scalars that are no lane mask and VGPRs written from them (optionally ANDed with EXEC, the LLVM form `s_and_b64 vcc, exec, s[..]`) gives every launch lane or none, so each half votes as the wave does and both sides may have code and scalars of their own (if/else, a uniform loop back edge; the mask itself read as data, e.g. exported, is still tier C `cross-lane`), while the same compare under a narrowed EXEC (a half with no active lane votes no and keeps its own scalars) or on a tainted value (`v_readfirstlane` of a varying VGPR, a lane count, an AND with a lane mask) stays tier C; confirmed on the wave interpreter (`UniformVote` in `tests/semantic/test_wave64_halves.py`: 64 lanes against two waves of 32, equal for the accepted forms with s4 = 9 and s4 = 1, different for each near miss) and by `ps_w64_a_uniform_vote`, which gives its derived words on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5080 (Linux, driver 580.65.06, 32 only). **B**: also a waterfall loop (`v_readfirstlane` of a varying value, `v_cmp_eq` against the same VGPR, `saveexec`, body, `s_xor exec`, `execnz`; the scalar only used inside the body), a wave-aggregated atomic (`s_bcnt1` of EXEC, `v_mbcnt` rank of that same EXEC, one `buffer_atomic_add` of that count (the count of the mask the rank ranks) by the rank-0 lane only, `v_readfirstlane` of the result with EXEC restored, base + rank as a store address, the rank counting the very mask the atomic's count was taken of (a rank of the entry EXEC or of a saved copy next to a base of the narrowed one is tier C `lane-rank`); the rank alone or the base alone is not a slot: exact as a set of slots, **the order of the slots within a wave is not preserved**, a 64-lane wave takes one run of slots, two halves take two), `v_readfirstlane` / `v_readlane` of a VGPR written from scalars and constants while EXEC was the launch mask (the same in every lane), a `v_writelane` / `v_readlane` spill pair on one constant lane below 32. **C**: anything else that reaches an export, a store's data, a branch or EXEC: lane count / rank / first-lane index / raw lane id (`v_mbcnt` of -1), `v_readlane` of a varying value at any lane (on a wave64 every lane gets hardware lane N, in halves each half gets its own lane N, so lane 7 is as wrong as lane 40; the research note's row 9 is too generous here); an analysis failure is tier C with reason `analysis-error` (the translation does not fail), `v_writelane` to a non-constant or >= 32 lane, a 32-bit access to half of a lane mask (`exec_hi`, `vcc_hi`, a mask pair's odd register), a mask read as data (including a mask combined by `s_and/or/andn2_b64` with a register that is not one: a user value, a loaded mask), a scalar defined behind a wave-wide vote and read where it may not have run (`s_add s5, s5, 1` after `s_cbranch_vccz`; a trip count in an SGPR of a divergent loop: the halves vote differently), a branch on a count, an elected-lane side effect that is not a count, `ds_append`/`ds_consume`. The check is conservative: unknown means C. `--wave64-graphics=warn` (default) marks tier C and counts it in the summary line, `error` refuses it (`Unsupported`), `off` skips the analysis; `python tools/wave64_halves.py --census <spirv>/index.json` prints tier, reason and idiom counts (no names); it complements `tools/wave64_graphics_scan.py` (#1132), which counts candidate cross-half opcodes in the disassembly before translation and decides nothing. **Corpus census** (1.0.2.1, all 26,503 shaders translated at `main` `f847748c` with `--wave64-emulate`, `--census` over that index): 13,405 wave64 graphics shaders (2,236 VS, 10,974 PS, 195 GS, the FSSM header's wave size, see *Wave size*); tier A 4,033 (30.1%), B 85 (0.6%), C 9,287 (69.3%): VS 1,455/79/702, PS 2,383/6/8,585, GS 195/0/0. Tier C reasons (a shader can have several): unsafe-vote-branch 8,285, tainted-branch 6,547, tainted-exec 5,189, cross-lane 4,764, stale-scalar 3,415, mask-half 759, unsafe-execz-branch 583, first-lane 353, waterfall-scalar 161, lane-id 152, lane-count 1. Idiom tags, counted on every shader that carries one whatever its tier (`--census` prints them as "tier B idioms"; 85 shaders are tier B): readfirstlane-uniform 626, lane-spill 287, waterfall 35. (Earlier censuses counted 11,405 shaders, from when the wave size came from code markers: TEST-319 had A 1,919, B 17, C 9,469 with all 195 GS in C; its re-run with the GS ring rule below and #1982 had A 2,117, B 17, C 9,271 with all 195 GS in A.) The lane-id bpermute rule moves no corpus shader. (The TEST-319 census, from when the wave size came from code markers and before the GS ring-address rule below, counted 11,405 shaders: A 1,919, B 17, C 9,469, with all 195 GS in C.) The lane-id bpermute rule moves no corpus shader. **Corpus census** (TEST-319, 1.0.2.1, `--census` over the translated index, re-run with the GS ring rule below and #1982): 11,405 wave64 graphics shaders (1,179 VS, 10,031 PS, 195 GS); tier A 2,117 (18.6%), B 17 (0.1%), C 9,271 (81.3%): VS 479/14/686, PS 1,443/3/8,585, GS 195/0/0. Tier C reasons (a shader can have several): unsafe-vote-branch 8,285, tainted-branch 6,550, tainted-exec 5,189, cross-lane 4,760, stale-scalar 3,415, mask-half 758, unsafe-execz-branch 583, first-lane 353, waterfall-scalar 161, lane-id 152, lane-count 1. 11,405 wave64 graphics shaders (1,179 VS, 10,031 PS, 195 GS); tier A 1,919 (16.8%), B 17 (0.1%), C 9,469 (83.0%): VS 465/14/700, PS 1,454/3/8,574, GS 0/0/195. Tier C reasons (a shader can have several): unsafe-vote-branch 8,175, tainted-branch 6,582, cross-lane 6,111, tainted-exec 4,959, stale-scalar 3,162, mask-half 758, unsafe-execz-branch 583, first-lane 353, lane-id 277, waterfall-scalar 167, lane-count 1. Tier B idioms: readfirstlane-uniform 558, lane-spill 287, waterfall 35. The lane-id bpermute rule moves no corpus shader. **Corpus coverage is not measured here**: it needs a run over the game's shaders by someone who has them. **Rewrite (the only one the emission needs)**: `v_readlane_b32` of a proven-uniform VGPR reads the lane's own copy instead of shuffling from lane N, because a subgroup shuffle from lane >= 32 of a 32-wide subgroup is undefined (143 of 144 test shaders translate byte-identically to origin/main, only `ps_w64_b_readlane_uniform` changed). Everything else tier B needs is what the translator already emits per subgroup: the ballot/count/rank/first-lane words of `wave_mask` are the subgroup's own (the high word is 0), `v_readfirstlane` is the subgroup's first active lane, a waterfall runs once per distinct value of the subgroup. GPU-checked on AMD Radeon 880M (driver 26.11.2; `requiredSubgroupSizeStages` has FRAGMENT, size 32..64) with `runtime/test/shaders/w64_graphics.py`: each `ps_w64_*` fixture drawn with the fragment subgroup size required to 64 (one hardware wave) and to 32 (two halves). `ps_w64_a_skip`, `_b_waterfall`, `_b_readfirstlane`, `_b_readlane_uniform` and `_b_ballot_atomic` (counter 1024 and the 1024 slot words as a set; slot order differs) match each other and the words their headers derive; `_c_bcnt_export`, `_c_lane_id`, `_c_readlane`, `_c_vote_stale`, `_c_vote_else`, `_c_scc_vote`, `_c_execz_sgpr` (an SGPR written on the exec-zero side and exported) and `_c_waterfall_count` differ between 64 and 32 (the detector is right). `ps_w64_a_one_lane` is the case where only one pixel votes yes (so one half votes and the other does not, in the wave and in the halves): its skip regions (`execz`, `vccz`, `vccnz` with a jump-only fall-through) give equal images at 64 and 32; `_c_vote_else` (code on the vote's no side) and `_c_scc_vote` (SCC of a vote as data) differ. `ps_w64_a_discard` (three kills, two of them dropping whole regions so that some halves lose every pixel while the other half of their wave lives) was drawn on an AMD Radeon RX 580 (RADV, Mesa 26.0.8, required size 64, saved with `W64G_SAVE_REF`) and an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 (32 only, driver 620.73.04, compared with `W64G_REF`): both equal the derived words, discarded pixels keeping the clear value, and each other. NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 Laptop (32 only, driver 617.42): tier A/B fixtures equal the derived words and the 880M@64 images (`W64G_SAVE_REF` / `W64G_REF`), tier C fixtures differ from the 880M@64 images. `ps_w64_a_permlane`, `_a_swizzle` and `_a_bpermute` (TEST-319: permlane16 neighbour selects, permlanex16 there and back, swizzle quad-permute, swap and broadcast modes, bpermute from lane-id addresses; every read stays in the pixel's 2x2 quad or comes back, so the words do not depend on how pixels are packed into lanes) give the derived words on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070 (Linux, driver 620.73.04, 32 only). With an AMD Radeon RX 580 (Polaris, RADV Mesa 26.0.8) as the 64-lane reference (`W64G_SAVE_REF`; GCN has one subgroup size, 64, in every stage but no FRAGMENT in `requiredSubgroupSizeStages`, so `vk_harness` counts a requested size equal to a device's only size as applied), the RTX 5070 run of every fixture (`W64G_REF`) equals the reference for all 13 tier A/B fixtures and differs from it for all 11 tier C ones, pixel and vertex; the same reads in a vertex shader run on the wave interpreter (64 lanes against two waves of 32, equal) because a GPU's partial vertex waves leave the lanes they would read empty. Vertex shaders: the VS stage cannot be forced on the 880M (no VERTEX in `requiredSubgroupSizeStages`), so VS is covered by the strict wave interpreter (`tests/semantic/test_wave64_halves.py`: 64 lanes against two waves of 32; tier A/B equal, tier C differs); geometry shaders are not tested (the corpus ones are wave64, see *Wave size*; no fixture). 64 lanes against two waves of 32; tier A/B equal, tier C differs), and on RADV also on the GPU (TEST-251): RADV runs the VS stage (NGG) as wave64 by default and as wave32 with `RADV_PERFTEST=gewave32` (GFX10+, seen in the `RADV_DEBUG=shaders` dump: 64-bit EXEC operations against `exec_lo`), so `w64_graphics.py` draws each `vs_w64_*` fixture both ways, 960 zero-area vertices (`VKH_VERTICES`) that store four words each to the heap, with `vs_w64_c_lane_id` as the probe that the wave size changed (largest lane id 63, then 31). RADV packs 62 or 64 vertices into a wave64 and 30 or 32 into a wave32, so the 32-lane run repacks the vertices rather than halving the waves; tier A/B words are keyed on the vertex index, which makes this the stricter test. `vs_w64_a_skip`, `_b_waterfall`, `_b_readlane_uniform` (readfirstlane and readlane 40/63 of uniform VGPRs) and `_b_ballot_atomic` (240 slots as a set) give the derived words at both sizes; `_c_bcnt_export`, `_c_readlane` and `_c_lane_id` differ (the detector is right), on an RX 9070 XT (GFX1201) and a Ryzen 9800X3D iGPU (GFX1036, RDNA2), RADV Mesa 26.3.1; geometry shaders are not tested (no fixture; the corpus has 195 wave64 geometry shaders, all tier C, see the census). `_c_bcnt_export`, `_c_readlane` and `_c_lane_id` differ (the detector is right), on an RX 9070 XT (GFX1201) and a Ryzen 9800X3D iGPU (GFX1036, RDNA2), RADV Mesa 26.3.1; geometry shaders likewise (TEST-251): RADV runs the GS stage (NGG) as wave64 by default and as wave32 with `gewave32`, and `w64_graphics.py` draws each `gs_w64_*` fixture after `vs_ids` (960 vertices, 320 triangles, `VKH_GS`), each invocation finding its triangle from `vs_ids`'s vertex number and storing four words to the heap, with `gs_w64_c_lane_id` as the probe (largest lane id 41, RADV puts at most 42 GS invocations in a wave64, then 31). **LDS writes of a legacy GS:** `rdna2spirv.gs_ds` maps one to an output slot from its offset alone and drops the VGPR address (the lane's GSVS area), so `wave64_halves.py` checks only the data a GS `ds_write` writes, not its address; before, a lane id that only formed the GSVS base made the shader tier C `lane-id`. Confirmed three ways: a hand-written GS with the base `lane id * 96` translates, after `spirv-opt -O`, to the same instructions as without it; `gs_w64_a_ring` (that shape) gives the derived records at wave64 and wave32 on both GPUs; and `tests/semantic/test_wave64_halves.py` (`GeometryRing`) keeps the lane id in the written data, or in a memory store's address, tier C. The corpus GS were that false tier C: all 195 contain `v_mbcnt_hi`, all 195 were tier C in the TEST-319 census, and `wave64_halves.py --census` on the corpus translated at `f847748c` puts all 195 in tier A. `_c_bcnt_export`, `_c_readlane` and `_c_lane_id` differ (the detector is right), on an RX 9070 XT (GFX1201) and a Ryzen 9800X3D iGPU (GFX1036, RDNA2), RADV Mesa 26.3.1; geometry shaders are not tested (the corpus ones are wave64, see *Wave size*; no fixture). The corpus GS form their GSVS base from `v_mbcnt_lo/hi` (all 195 contain `v_mbcnt_hi`): they were all a false tier C, and the census above has all 195 at tier A. `wave64_halves.py --census` on a translated corpus shows it. `_c_bcnt_export`, `_c_readlane` and `_c_lane_id` differ (the detector is right), on an RX 9070 XT (GFX1201) and a Ryzen 9800X3D iGPU (GFX1036, RDNA2), RADV Mesa 26.3.1; geometry shaders are not tested (the corpus ones are wave32; no fixture). Not checked: helper lanes of partial quads; `VK_KHR_shader_maximal_reconvergence` is assumed for waterfall and election loops. Tiers A and B assume subgroups of at least 32 lanes: a fragment stage may run fewer live lanes than the subgroup size it reports (Intel UHD 770: gl_SubgroupSize 32, ballot count 16, a shuffle from lane L^16 returns garbage; measured by kapitche, TEST-433), so `w64_graphics.py` skips the tier A/B pixel fixtures on a device whose minimum subgroup size is below 32 when neither the 64 nor the 32 run took effect; deriving the skip from a measured live fragment width (ballot count) in `vk_harness` is a follow-up. **ADD_TID_ENABLE** (SHADER-833, finding by @shdann in #4571): an access with no `idxen` through a V# whose word3 is a literal (`s_mov_b32` earlier in the block, the notion `rdna2spirv` uses for `sgpr_imm`) with bit 23 set takes the wave lane as its index (`idxen` ignores the bit, see *Swizzled buffer addressing*), so its `buffer_load*`/`tbuffer_load*` result and its `buffer_store*`/`tbuffer_store*`/`buffer_atomic*` address carry the raw lane id: tier C `lane-id`. A literal with the bit clear, and every access with `idxen`, are unchanged. **Assumption**: through a run-time V# (loaded from memory or user data) tiers A and B take ADD_TID_ENABLE as 0 for an access without `idxen`; the code cannot say otherwise. The literal is tracked with the same folds `rdna2spirv` applies to `sgpr_imm` (mirrored in `Analysis.literal_sgpr`: `s_mov_b32/b64`, `s_movk_i32`, `s_pack_*`, `s_bfm_b32`, `s_not_b32`, `s_and/lshl/lshr_b32`, `s_mul_i32`; other folded ops count as run-time). The corpus census was not re-measured with this rule: its tier counts above are from before it and may change. Follow-up, needs the game: count the bound V#s with word3 bit 23 set at run time, and re-run `--census`. |
| GFX10.1 (console gfx1013) / GFX10.3 (gfx1030, gfx1036 oracle) opcode differences | Found by disassembling every opcode of every GFX10 encoding (SOP1/SOP2/SOPK/SOPC/SOPP, SMEM, VOP1/VOP2/VOPC/VOP3/VOP3P, DS, FLAT/GLOBAL/SCRATCH, MUBUF/MTBUF, MIMG) with LLVM 21 `llvm-mc` for `-mcpu=gfx1013` and for `gfx1030`/`gfx1036` (the two gfx103x lists are identical). Every other opcode slot decodes to the same mnemonic on both, so the hardware-oracle rows measured on gfx1036 are about the same instructions the console runs (the microarchitectures still differ, which no opcode table shows). **On gfx1013 only:** `v_mac_f32`/`v_mad_f32` (VOP2 0x1f, VOP3 0x11f/0x141) and `v_mac_legacy_f32`/`v_mad_legacy_f32` (VOP2 0x06, VOP3 0x106/0x140), translated (rows above; not measurable on gfx103x, where 0x06/0x106/0x140 are the fused `v_fmac_legacy_f32`/`v_fma_legacy_f32`); `v_mul_lo_i32` (VOP3 0x16b; `Unsupported` until #3504 translates it); `s_get_waveid_in_workgroup` (SMEM 0x2a), translated in compute and mesh shaders; scalar stores (`s_store_dword*`, `s_buffer_store_dword*`), scalar atomics (`s_atomic_*`, `s_buffer_atomic_*`, 32- and 64-bit), scalar scratch (`s_scratch_load/store_dword*`), `s_dcache_wb`, `s_dcache_discard(_x2)` and the 31 LDS `ds_*_src2_*` forms, all `Unsupported`. **On gfx103x only:** `v_dot2c_f32_f16`, `v_dot4c_i32_i8`, the VOP3P `v_dot2_f32_f16`, `v_dot2_i32_i16`/`u32_u16`, `v_dot4_i32_i8`/`u32_u8`, `v_dot8_i32_i4`/`u32_u4`, `v_fmac_legacy_f32`/`v_fma_legacy_f32`, `global_load/store_dword_addtid`, `global_atomic_csub`, `buffer_atomic_csub`. The console's code can't contain these; the translator handles the integer dot products (rows above, measured on gfx1036) and refuses the rest, so a listing disassembled for the wrong target (VOP2 0x06 printed as `v_fmac_legacy_f32`) fails loudly instead of being translated as a fused multiply-add. Not covered by the sweep: opcodes whose operand template did not decode on either target, and behaviour that differs without an opcode change. |
| Buffer bounds checking | MUBUF `buffer_load*`, `buffer_store*`, and `buffer_atomic*` enforce `num_records` (V# word 2). Out-of-bounds stores/atomics skip memory access completely and out-of-bounds loads return 0 without a load. **The RX 6800 measurement behind RAW loads** (`buffer_load_dword`/`x2`/`x3`/`x4`, stride 0, no `idxen`, OOB_SELECT 3; the per-mode rule below covers every mode, stride and `idxen`): the byte offset O = inst_offset + soffset + (offen ? vaddr : 0) is exact (nothing truncated, and the address keeps base & 3), and dword i is data when O + 4·i + 4 ≤ num_records, else 0, measured on AMD Radeon RX 6800 (gfx1030, Windows driver 32.0.21045.29003) by running the instructions as a raw compute kernel, wave32 and wave64 (155 runs, every result either 0 or the stored data, wave32 = wave64 in every dword), for x1/x2/x4 with V# word3 0x31016fac (OOB_SELECT 3) at offsets 0..63 per lane, NUM_RECORDS 17..23 and 0x1000, base & 3 = 0 and 3: a dword at 0xf with NUM_RECORDS 18 is 0 (a start-only check reads it); x4 at 3 with 18 gives three data dwords and 0 for the fourth, which starts at 15 < 18 (so neither a whole-load nor a per-dword start check), and the zero is a whole 0, not partial bytes; x4 at offset:13 with 20 gives data, 0, 0, 0; inst_offset, soffset and offen are summed before the check; base & 3 = 3 flips at the same offsets. `x3` gets the rule without a measurement. **Per-mode check (V# OOB_SELECT, word3 bits [29:28])**, for every MUBUF/MTBUF access, as measured natively on RDNA2 (gfx1036, 732 cases, `docs/buffer_bounds.md`; Mesa's GFX11 column, not its GFX10 one). `idx` = `vindex` (0 without `idxen`), `off` = inst_offset + vaddr offset (offen), `offs` = `off` + soffset. A dword at byte 4·k of the access is out of range when: **0 STRUCTURED_WITH_OFFSET** `idx >= num_records` or `off + 4k + 4 > stride`; **1 STRUCTURED** `idx >= num_records` (no `idxen`: idx 0, so only `num_records == 0`); **2 DISABLED** `num_records == 0`; **3 RAW** `offs + 4k + 4 > num_records` (`idx * stride` is in the address, not in the check). A literal word3 (`s_mov_b32` earlier in the block) folds the mode, a run-time one selects with `OpSelect`. Multi-dword accesses, measured on the Radeon 880M (gfx1150, a valid proxy: gfx1036 follows the same column and gfx1150 matched it bit-exactly): in modes 0 and 3 **`buffer_load_dwordx2..x4` and `buffer_store_dwordx2..x4` are per dword**, for loads and stores alike (x4 at off 20 of 32 in RAW loads/stores 3 dwords, at 28 one, at 32 none; mode 0 likewise at the stride); modes 1 and 2 check once and always take the whole access. **A typed element (`buffer_load_format_*`, `buffer_store_format_*`) is all or nothing and ends at its format's size** (32_32_32_32 at off 20 of 32 reads or writes nothing), and an atomic checks its dword's end like a dword access. The translator does the same: per-dword guarded loads and stores for modes 0 and 3 (a store that straddled the edge used to write past it), the element size from the instruction's format, a literal word3 or the V#. The same oracle confirmed the terms (504 `buffer_load_dword` probes, idxen/offen/inst_offset/soffset at the stride and record edges, 0 disagreements): mode 0's `off` has `inst_offset` and no soffset, mode 1 reads nothing but the index, mode 3 adds inst_offset and soffset. `s_buffer_load` (SMEM) is separate: it keeps the `num_records` bound of the Scalar/buffer row and ignores OOB_SELECT (the same pattern for 0 to 3). **Swizzle**: SWIZZLE_ENABLE (V# word1 bit 31, Mesa `gfx10-rsrc.json` line 326; word1 [31:30] on GFX11, `gfx11-rsrc.json` line 259) and ADD_TID_ENABLE (word3 bit 23, line 337) change the address and the bounds in every mode and are modelled; see the Swizzled buffer addressing row. SWIZZLE_ENABLE and ADD_TID_ENABLE are modelled; see the Swizzled buffer addressing row. SWIZZLE_ENABLE (V# word1 bit 31, Mesa `gfx10-rsrc.json` line 326; word1 [31:30] on GFX11, `gfx11-rsrc.json` line 259) and ADD_TID_ENABLE (word3 bit 23, line 337) change the address and the bounds in every mode and are not modelled: a literal V# with either set is `Unsupported`, a run-time V# with them is translated unswizzled, which is silently wrong (known gap). Until 2026-11-22 the translator ignored OOB_SELECT and checked by stride alone (`offset < num_records`, `index < num_records`, or `offset < num_records * stride`): a V# with word3 = 0 (mode 0, stride 0) was fully readable and is now always out of range, as on hardware, so the test harnesses' output V# is `0x31016fac` now (`vk_compute_test`, the interpreter tests, `cs_bounds.asm`, `cs_fmt_1010102.asm`, `cs_store.asm`). **How confirmed**: the native RDNA2 measurement (gfx1036) and the gfx1150 oracle (RDNA3.5, same column); GPU test `cs_oob_select.asm` (every mode, literal and run-time V#, words derived from the rules) on an NVIDIA RTX 5070 and an AMD Radeon 880M, which checks the translation, not RDNA2; `tests/semantic/test_buffer_oob_select.py` (a hand-written table of 33 cases over the four modes, loads and stores, both V# forms, plus the multi-dword stores and typed elements); `cs_oob_tail.asm` pins the end-based edge, per-dword x4 loads and stores and the all-or-nothing element, where Mesa's GFX10 column gives other words. **RDNA1 (gfx1011, Navi 12) follows the same RDNA2/GFX11 column**: `tools/buffer_bounds_probe.py` ran 12 one-lane 4-byte `buffer_load_dword` cases at wave32 and wave64 through `tools/hw_oracle` (24 groups, 0 wave32/64 differences); all matched the measured column and 8 disagreed with Mesa's GFX10 column (SHADER-341, results in `docs/buffer_bounds.md`). **Unmeasured**: gfx1013/GFX10.1 itself (see `docs/buffer_bounds.md`), swizzle, unaligned addresses, and the mode 1/2 boundary of `buffer_load_dwordx2..x4` on RDNA2 hardware proper (the gfx1150 proxy shows all dwords in). Checked by `tests/semantic/test_value_semantics.py` (`RawBufferTailBounds`: the RX 6800 rows, values and the addresses loaded) and the GPU tests `runtime/test/shaders/cs_bounds.asm` (AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT, RX 6800), `cs_buf_tail.asm` (RX 6800, Vulkan, driver 26.11.2; aligned offsets only, since the translator's `Aligned 4` loads at byte-exact addresses were not checked on a Vulkan driver) `cs_oob_select.asm` and `cs_oob_tail.asm`. `cs_fmt_xy_xyz.asm` shows the whole element deciding for `_xy` and `_xyz` too, measured natively on a Radeon 680M (gfx1035, RDNA2): components that end inside num_records, in an element that doesn't, read 0 and store nothing. |
| 8/16-bit MUBUF d16 loads and stores | (The plain `buffer_load_{u,s}byte`/`{u,s}short` are `Gen.buffer_load_sub`'s; `tests/semantic/test_buffer_sub_dword.py` checks them too.) The `_d16` loads (`buffer_load_{u,s}byte_d16[_hi]`, `buffer_load_short_d16[_hi]`) write one 16-bit half of `vdata` (`_hi` the high one; a byte zero- or sign-extended to 16 bits) and keep the other. `buffer_store_byte`/`short` write the low 8/16 bits of `vdata` (the `_d16_hi` forms its high half). The address is `buffer_address`'s, and the bounds check covers the access's own size, 1 or 2 bytes, as for a typed element of that size (`elem="fmt"`, format 1 or 7). Out of range, a load gives 0 (in the half it writes, for d16; **not measured** for d16) and a store writes nothing. Each byte is its own access, so a 16-bit one that crosses a dword is exact; in a swizzled buffer the next dword is `step` bytes on. A store merges each byte into its dword with atomic and/or, as buffer byte format stores do. Inactive lanes neither load nor store, and the first-read analysis counts a d16 load's `vdata` as read. Until 2026-11-22 these ten raised `Unsupported`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_sub_dword.py` (every op at byte offsets 0 to 7, the edge of `num_records`, an inactive lane, rejected forms). GPU fixture `cs_w32_buffer_sub16` (the six d16 loads with their kept half and the sign extension, the two `_d16_hi` stores taking bits 23:16 / 31:16), passes on llvmpipe (Mesa 26.0.8) and on an NVIDIA RTX 5090 (595.99.02, @tylerstraub on #1864), all eight words; GPU fixture `cs_w32_buffer_store_sub` for the plain `buffer_store_byte`/`buffer_store_short` (Box16Rehydration's blocker, @YaBulochka on #1864), including a short at byte 3 that writes its high byte into the next dword, passes on llvmpipe with LLVM 21 and 23, and on an Apple M4 (MoltenVK 1.4.2) once the second dword of a 16-bit load is indexed from the first one's pointer instead of a second pointer converted from the same address, which Apple's compiler read as 0 (@sovljanskii on #1864). |
| Swizzled buffer addressing (V# SWIZZLE_ENABLE, ADD_TID_ENABLE) | MUBUF/MTBUF `buffer_load*`, `buffer_store*`, `buffer_atomic*`, `buffer_load_format_*`/`buffer_store_format_*` and `tbuffer_load/store_format_*` follow the rules measured natively on RDNA2 (`AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics`, gfx1036, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 and wave64; `tools/buffer_swizzle_probe.py`, SHADER-343). MUBUF/MTBUF `buffer_load*`, `buffer_store*`, `buffer_atomic*`, `buffer_load_format_*`/`buffer_store_format_*` and `tbuffer_load_format_*` follow the RDNA2 ISA ("RDNA 2" Instruction Set Architecture, 8.1.5 Buffer Addressing, "Swizzled Buffer Addressing" and tables 33, 35 and 37). Fields, Mesa 24.0.6 `src/amd/registers/gfx10-rsrc.json` (`SQ_BUF_RSRC_WORD1`, `SQ_BUF_RSRC_WORD3` and its `_gfx103` twin, the same bits): `SWIZZLE_ENABLE` word1 bit 31, `STRIDE` word1 `[29:16]`, `INDEX_STRIDE` word3 `[22:21]` (`IS = 8 << n`, i.e., 8, 16, 32 or 64), `ADD_TID_ENABLE` word3 bit 23; element size is fixed at 4 bytes (`word3[20:19]` and `word1[30]` do not alter addressing or stepping). **Effective index:** `index = idxen ? vindex : 0)`, where `lane` is the wave lane (`thread_id[5:0]` within the wave, wrapping at 32 in wave32 and 64 in wave64; `Gen.wave_lane`); **`idxen = 1` completely ignores `ADD_TID_ENABLE`** (linear or swizzled, address and bounds, loads/stores/typed/atomics). **Activation:** `SWIZZLE_ENABLE` activates (`swz_active`) only when `idxen = 1` or `ADD_TID_ENABLE = 1`; with neither, addressing is linear (`base + soffset + off`) and `OOB_SELECT 3` stays RAW even when `stride ≠ 0`. **Address formula (when `swz_active`):** `off = uint32((offen ? vaddr : 0) + inst_offset)` (32-bit wrap), `buffer_offset = (index / IS · stride + (off & ~3)) · IS + (index % IS) · 4 + (off & 3)`, `address = base + soffset + buffer_offset` (confirmed byte-exact for IS 8/16/32/64, non-4-multiple strides, stride 0, and unaligned offsets). **Two swizzle sub-modes:** (1) **Scratch swizzle** (`SWIZZLE_ENABLE = 1`, `idxen = 0`, `ADD_TID_ENABLE = 1`): raw multi-dword `buffer_load/store_dwordx2..x4` step by **`+4 · IS`** bytes per dword (and unaligned 4-byte raw accesses crossing a dword boundary split across `+4 · IS` on hardware), while **typed formats > 4 bytes** (8/12/16-byte MUBUF and MTBUF loads/stores, regardless of component count) and **8-byte `buffer_atomic_*_x2`** are **suppressed** (loads return 0, stores/atomics are no-ops, even in `OOB_SELECT 2`); typed formats ≤ 4 bytes work normally. (2) **Indexed swizzle** (`SWIZZLE_ENABLE = 1`, `idxen = 1`, regardless of `ADD_TID_ENABLE`): raw `x2..x4`, typed formats (8/12/16 bytes), and `buffer_atomic_*_x2` step **linearly by `+4` bytes** from the swizzled base address, even across `IS` boundaries, and typed formats > 4 bytes execute normally. **Bounds (`OOB_SELECT`):** modes 0, 1 and 2 follow the Buffer bounds checking row with `idx = index`; **`OOB_SELECT 3` with `swz_active` and `stride ≠ 0`** checks **`uint64(index) + uint64(soffset) >= num_records` or `off + payload > stride`** (per-dword for raw `x1..x4`, whole element for typed), while inactive swizzle or `stride = 0` keeps the RAW check `uint64(off) + uint64(soffset) + payload > num_records` (no 32-bit wrap on `off + soffset`, whereas `vaddr + inst_offset` wraps at 32 bits before bounds checking). A literal word1 / word3 (`s_mov_b32` earlier in the block) folds each bit; a run-time V# selects dynamically with `L = swz_u · (3 + INDEX_STRIDE)` (IS = 1 gives the linear address). Until 2026-11-22 the translator added `vindex + lane` when `idxen = 1` and `ADD_TID_ENABLE = 1`, swizzled even when both were 0, stepped all swizzled multi-dword/typed accesses by `4 · IS`, allowed > 4-byte typed/atomic_x2 accesses in scratch-swizzle mode, omitted `soffset` from swizzled mode 3's index check, and wrapped `off + soffset` at 32 bits in RAW mode 3. **How confirmed:** native RDNA2 measurement on `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` (gfx1036, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 & wave64) via `tools/buffer_swizzle_probe.py --oracle both` (22/22 cases across 8 groups match bit-exactly, 0 unexpected mismatches, 0 unexpected wave32/64 diffs, 64-thread `ADD_TID_ENABLE` wave-lane wrap verified at both wave sizes); CPU semantic tests `tests/semantic/test_buffer_swizzle.py` (literal and run-time V#, both sub-modes, mode 3 `soffset`, 32-bit wrap, typed/`atomic_x2` suppression, and `cs_buf_swizzle.asm`); `tests/test_buffer_swizzle_probe.py`. **Remaining approximations / unmeasured:** unaligned raw accesses splitting across `+4 · IS` in scratch-swizzle mode (the translator uses `Aligned 4` PSB loads/stores), general typed-format / `buffer_atomic_*_x2` natural alignment suppression (`16_UINT` at odd offsets, `32_*` at non-4B offsets, `atomic_*_x2` at non-8B addresses, observed on gfx1036 in both linear and swizzled modes), and GFX11's two-bit `SWIZZLE_ENABLE` (`word1[31:30]`). SWIZZLE_ENABLE word1 bit 31, STRIDE word1 [29:16], INDEX_STRIDE word3 [22:21] (IS = 8, 16, 32 or 64 indices), ADD_TID_ENABLE word3 bit 23; there is no ELEMENT_SIZE field, the element is fixed at 4 bytes (ISA 8.1.5; LLVM `SIInstrInfo::getScratchRsrcWords23` sets it only up to GFX8). `index` = (idxen ? vindex : 0) + (ADD_TID_ENABLE ? lane : 0), where the lane is thread_id[5:0] within the wave (`Gen.wave_lane`), swizzled or not: table 33 adds the thread id with no swizzle condition and table 36's ring column is linear with add_tid. `offset` = (offen ? vaddr : 0) + inst_offset, without soffset. Swizzled: buffer_offset = (index / IS · stride + offset / 4 · 4) · IS + index % IS · 4 + offset % 4, address = base + soffset + buffer_offset, so dword k of a multi-dword access (and of a typed element over 4 bytes) is 4 · IS bytes after dword k − 1, not 4. Bounds as in Buffer bounds checking, with `idx` = `index` (the lane included), except that OOB_SELECT 3 with SWIZZLE_ENABLE and stride ≠ 0 checks `index >= num_records` or `off + 4k + 4 > stride` (table 35), like mode 0; stride 0 keeps the raw check. A literal word1 / word3 (`s_mov_b32` earlier in the block) folds each bit. A run-time word1 makes IS = 2^(SWIZZLE_ENABLE · (3 + INDEX_STRIDE)): IS = 1 is the linear address, so one formula serves both and nothing is selected but the mode 3 check; a run-time word3 multiplies the lane by ADD_TID_ENABLE. Cost, measured on the 116 shaders of `runtime/test/shaders` that both versions translate (all `spirv-val` clean before and after): the 56 with a MUBUF access through a run-time V# grow by 15.1 % in SPIR-V lines (+10.4 % over all 116), 30 to 45 instructions per access (address, lane, 4 per extra dword, a select per dword of the bounds check); a literal linear V# gives the same SPIR-V as before. Outside compute and mesh shaders the lane is `SubgroupLocalInvocationId`, so a VS, PS or GS with a run-time V# now declares `GroupNonUniform`. The offset arithmetic is 32-bit, like the linear address. Until 2026-11-22 a literal V# with either bit was `Unsupported` and a run-time one was translated unswizzled. **How confirmed**: Mesa/LLVM and AMD's ISA reference (fields and formula), not measured on hardware; `tests/semantic/test_buffer_swizzle.py` (hand-worked addresses for IS 8/16/64, index msb/lsb, soffset after the swizzle, the mode 3 rule with and without a stride, per-dword x2/x3/x4 loads and stores, a 16-byte typed element, ADD_TID on lane 5 linear and swizzled, each with a literal and a run-time V#); GPU test `runtime/test/shaders/cs_buf_swizzle.asm` (check.py, `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`), which checks the translation, not RDNA2. **Unmeasured**: the swizzled layout on RDNA2 hardware, offsets that are not dword aligned (the ISA requires alignment for swizzled accesses; the translation adds offset % 4), wrap of the 32-bit offset, and GFX11's two-bit SWIZZLE_ENABLE (word1 [31:30]). **Unmeasured** (the layout itself is measured: `docs/shader_validation.md`, SHADER-343, gfx1036, 22 of 22 cases bit-exact): offsets that are not dword aligned (the ISA requires alignment for swizzled accesses; the translation adds offset % 4), wrap of the 32-bit offset, and GFX11's two-bit SWIZZLE_ENABLE (word1 [31:30]). In the corpus `SCRATCH_EN` is 0 in every CS, so a swizzled V# can only come from a descriptor the game writes itself. |
| `v_permlane16_b32` / `v_permlanex16_b32` with the source lane off in EXEC (SHADER-74) | The VOP3 modifier `op_sel:[FI, BOUND_CTRL]` decides what a lane gets when the lane it reads is off in EXEC. **FI=1** (`op_sel` bit 0, encoding 0x0800): the source lane's value, as before. **FI=0** (the default): the destination lane is not written and keeps its old value when BOUND_CTRL=0, and gets 0 when BOUND_CTRL=1 (`op_sel` bit 1, 0x1000). A source lane that is on gives its value whatever FI / BOUND_CTRL are, and a lane that is itself off is never written (normal EXEC rule). The translator shuffles the active bit with the same selector as the value (also in `--wave64-emulate`, where it is a second exchange). On a native subgroup the two shuffles are combined with bitwise masks, not a scalar select: absent source lanes are redirected to the calling lane first (as `ds_lane_read`), the value and the activity bit are fetched through the redirected index, and `ok` masks the shuffled value over the old one (over zero with BOUND_CTRL=1) and the destination write is masked too, so the ballot and both shuffles run before lane predication — MoltenVK 1.4.2 and MoltenVK main at SPIRV-Cross aa217aeb lowered the scalar select to a short-circuiting Metal ternary and the FI=0 lanes read the source lane's value instead, which `cs_w32_permlane_fi` caught on an Apple M1 Max (#5895); `v_permlanex16_b32` pairs the rows 0↔1 and 2↔3 of a wave64 as before. Until SHADER-74 every case returned the source value (right only for FI=1). Source of the bit mapping: LLVM `llvm.amdgcn.permlane16/permlanex16 <old> <src0> <src1> <src2> <fi> <bound_control>` (IntrinsicsAMDGPU.td) is selected in `VOP3Instructions.td` (`PermlanePat`) with `fi` as the `src0` op_sel bit and `bound_control` as the `src1` one, and the CodeGen tests `llvm.amdgcn.permlane.ll` print `op_sel:[1,0]` for FI and `op_sel:[0,1]` for BOUND_CTRL; `llvm-mc -mcpu=gfx1013` encodes `op_sel:[1,0]` as 0x0800 and `op_sel:[0,1]` as 0x1000 in the second byte pair of the first word (0xD777 / 0xD778). Behaviour: hardware measurement on RDNA2 (Ryzen 7950X3D iGPU, raw kernel, wave64 and wave32 consistent; the same on RDNA3.5) by aslan2142 in [#374](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/issues/374#issuecomment-7071597516). Pinned by `runtime/test/shaders/cs_w32_permlane_fi.asm` (EXEC 0x55555555, every active lane's source is off; out[0..7] = 1000 0 4 1 1000 0 52 49) in `check.py` and `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`, and on the CPU by `tests/semantic/test_permlane_fi.py`. |
| Null mask source | The scalar register `null` supplies zero when read as a lane mask. The wave64 fixture in `tests/semantic/fixtures/null_mask_source.asm` checks a high-bit test, a zero-bit count, a first-set-bit search and a masked vector write after `s_and_saveexec_b64`. LLVM 21.1.8 assembles each instruction for gfx1030. [LLVM GFX10 null operand](https://releases.llvm.org/15.0.0/docs/AMDGPUOperandSyntax.html#null) |
| Wave64 mask ballot | `tools/lane_masks.py` tracks per-lane masks (including 32-bit `MASK_LO`/`MASK_HI` halves, `vcc_lo`/`vcc_hi`, `s_bfm_b64` bitfields, `_b64` copies of immediates into `vcc`, and `_b64` bitwise/select ops combining a lane mask or `vcc` destination with immediates; 32-bit mask + non-0/-1 immediate into an SGPR stays plain data, #1792). Split wave64 SGPR pairs whose two dwords hold separately written half-masks or a half-mask paired with a 0/-1 `EITHER` constant (`include_either=True`) are recognized by `holds_lane_mask` and read `s[r]` for lanes 0–31 and `s[r+1]` for lanes 32–63 in `mask_src`, and `--wave64-emulate`'s `x_mcache` keys `wave_mask` on every dword of an SGPR operand so rewriting `s[r+1]` invalidates a cached 64-bit exchange. `s_bcnt1_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_bcnt0_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_ff1_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_ff0_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_bitcmp0/1_b32/b64`, and `v_mbcnt_lo/hi_u32_b32` reconstruct the 64-bit wave mask via `OpGroupNonUniformBitwiseOr` (`GroupNonUniformArithmetic`), restricting 32-bit half ops (including `v_mbcnt_lo/hi_u32_b32` on `vcc_hi`/`vcc_lo`/`exec_lo` via `mask_half`) and `s_cmp_eq/lg_u32` (`wave_equal`) to the known 32-bit half in wave64. `tools/lane_masks.py` tracks per-lane masks (including 32-bit `MASK_LO`/`MASK_HI` halves, `vcc_lo`/`vcc_hi`, and `s_bfm_b64` bitfields). `s_bcnt1_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_bcnt0_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_ff1_i32_b64`/`b32`, `s_ff0_i32_b64`/`b32`, and `s_bitcmp0/1_b32/b64` reconstruct the 64-bit wave mask via `OpGroupNonUniformBitwiseOr` (`GroupNonUniformArithmetic`), restricting 32-bit half ops and `s_cmp_eq/lg_u32` (`wave_equal`) to the known 32-bit half in wave64. Opposite-half `s_cmp_*_u32/i32` comparisons (lo vs hi), ordering comparisons on mask halves, and `s_cmp_*_u32/i32/u64` comparing a lane mask against an arbitrary scalar or immediate reconstruct the exact 32/64-bit mask words via `wave_mask`. A compare with 0 keeps the vote, but a compare with -1 (all ones) does not: lanes that were never launched (a group smaller than the wave) don't vote, while their bits are 0 in the real mask, so until 2026-11-22 `s_cmp_eq_u32 vcc_hi, -1` read 1 in a 40-thread wave64 for `vcc_hi` = 0xff. GPU-checked with `cs_w64x_partial_scmp.asm` (40-thread wave64, native and `--wave64-emulate`) and `cs_w32_partial_scmp.asm` (20-thread wave32) on an AMD Radeon RX 7800 XT (RADV, Mesa 26.2.6): all 8 words as the headers derive, and 4 of 8 wrong in each with the old translator. GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_wave64_ballot.asm` and `cs_wm_*.asm` on AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT (64-wide subgroup); semantic test coverage in `tests/semantic/test_scalar_bitcmp.py`, `tests/semantic/test_scalar_alu_shifts.py`, `tests/semantic/test_loop_ballot_barrier.py`, and `tests/test_lane_masks.py` (split SGPR pairs, dual-dword `x_mcache`, `_b64` mask+imm, and `v_mbcnt` half selection CPU-checked, not yet GPU-verified). GPU-checked with `runtime/test/shaders/cs_wave64_ballot.asm` and `cs_wm_*.asm` on AMD Radeon RX 9070 XT (64-wide subgroup); semantic test coverage in `tests/semantic/test_scalar_bitcmp.py` and `tests/semantic/test_scalar_alu_shifts.py`. |
| 64-bit mask equality (SHADER-551) | `s_cmp_eq_u64` / `s_cmp_lg_u64` compare both exact 32-bit words and set a uniform SCC. Canonical full-wave masks (`exec`, mask-valued `vcc`, or a wave64 `MASK_LO`/`MASK_HI` SGPR pair) compared with another full mask or zero may use a whole-wave equality vote. Other SGPR pairs materialize each mask half separately and retain scalar data and constant words, including split, swapped, duplicated, mixed mask/data and wave32 pairs. Comparing with all ones also materializes words, so unlaunched lanes remain zero bits in partial waves. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_scmp_u64_mask_pairs.py` with full and partial wave64, wave32, and wave64-emulation cases; independent synthetic Vulkan readback is reported in [PR #3007](https://github.com/ItsNotRexed/Leonida/pull/3007). No native RDNA2 instruction oracle was run for this rule. |
| VOP3 FP modifiers on constants | `tools/shader_ir.py` keeps VOP3 FP source modifiers (`neg(...)`, `\|...\|`, `-\|...\|`, `abs(...)`, and nested combinations) as `abs`/`neg` flags on `imm` and `fimm` operands so `abs` clears the sign bit first and `neg` flips it second (matching LLVM `AMDGPUInstPrinter.cpp::printOperandAndFPInputMods` and `llvm/test/MC/AMDGPU/vop3-modifiers.s`). Plain signed literals (`-1`, `-1.0`) are unchanged. Until 2026-11-22, `-\|1.0\|` folded `-` into the float before `FAbs` (yielding `+1.0`), `neg(0x3f800000)` / `neg(1)` applied two's-complement integer negation (`-4.0f` / `0xffffffff` NaN instead of `-1.0f` / `0x80000001`), and `\|0x...\|` / `-\|0x...\|` dropped `abs`. `tools/shader_meta.py` applies the same bit operations in `_imm_u32`. Checked on the CPU by `tests/test_shader_ir.py`, `tests/test_shader_meta.py` and `tests/semantic/test_vop3_const_mods.py` (f32, f16, `v_fma_mix_f32`, `v_cmp_class_f32`, f64 SPIR-V structure); GPU-checked for `-\|-2.0\|`, `neg(0x3f800000)`, `\|0xbf800000\|` and f16 `-\|0x3c00\|` by `ps_vop3_const_mods.asm` (Tesla V100 (NVIDIA 580.95.05, Linux)). |
| `v_cmp_class_f64`, `v_cmpx_class_f64` | IEEE-754 binary64 numeric class test against the 10-bit mask (`0` sNaN, `1` qNaN, `2` `-inf`, `3` `-normal`, `4` `-subnormal`, `5` `-0`, `6` `+0`, `7` `+subnormal`, `8` `+normal`, `9` `+inf`), lowered in `Gen.cmp_class_f64` using pure 32-bit `%uint` bit extraction on `(lo, hi)` with no `Float64` capability required. Supports VGPR/SGPR pairs, float inline constants (`fimm`), sign-extended inline integers (`-16..64`), 32-bit literals placed in bits `[63:32]` (as on RDNA2), and VOP3 `abs`/`neg` sign-bit modifiers on `src0` (`abs` clears bit 63, `neg` flips bit 63 without quieting sNaN). Inactive lanes produce 0 for `v_cmp_class_f64` and stay inactive for `v_cmpx_class_f64`. Source: AMD RDNA2 ISA VOPC/VOP3 `V_CMP_CLASS_F64` / `V_CMPX_CLASS_F64`. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_cmp_class_f64.py` (all 10 classes with low- and high-word payload bits, mask combinations and high-bit ignores, SGPR pairs, `fimm`/inline/literal constants, `abs`/`neg`/`-\|x\|`, `v_cmpx_class_f64` EXEC narrowing, inactive lanes, no `Float64` capability, bad-operand rejections). **Not GPU-verified.** Hardware-measured natively on `gfx1100`** (RX 7900 XTX, driver 26.10.1) through `tools/hw_oracle`: the ten classes as `f64` pairs (`0x7ff0000000000001` sNaN, `0x7ff8000000000000` qNaN, `0xfff0000000000000` -inf, `0xbff0000000000000` -normal, `0x8000000000000001` -denormal, `-0`, `+0`, `0x0000000000000001` +denormal, `0x3ff0000000000000` +normal, `0x7ff0000000000000` +inf) against their own bit, all ten bits, no bits and the nine other bits (40), and `v_cmpx_class_f64` on the launched lane true and false (2), all matching the same CPU model. Not measured: `abs`/`neg`, inactive lanes and wave64. **Not GPU-verified.** |
| Mesh/compute input registers (historical measurement) | An early read-before-write analysis over all compute and mesh shaders found **compute** v0 (880), v0–v1 (614), v0–v2 (50), consistent with AMD's local thread ID x/y/z in v0/v1/v2, and scalar inputs centred on a pointer at s[4:5] and s6/s7. **Mesh:** every one of 2,969 reads exactly **v5**, plus s3 (always arithmetic), s[16:17] and s19–s23. The early pass also read FSSM 0x13C as the group width and compared the first SGPR multiply against it (82 match, 151 don't, 1,447 no simple multiply) and wrongly concluded that 0x13C was not the group width; the matching register varied by shader (s6/s10/s7/s9…). PSV0 gave the real thread-group sizes (32×1×1 for 1,253, 8×8×1 for 416, …). **Superseded:** 0x13C/0x140/0x144 are the thread-group size, `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC2` at 0x138 gives the per-shader user-SGPR count and enabled workgroup IDs, and mesh takes NumThreads, limits and topology from PSV0 with `v5 = linearGroupIndex * NumThreads + LocalInvocationIndex`. All 2,689 CS and 9,282 mesh shaders translate now (see the Compute shaders and Mesh shaders rows). |
| Formerly untranslated | Mesh (9,282), geometry (195) and compute (2,689) now translate; `s_swappc_b64` is replaced by Vulkan vertex inputs (see *Vertex fetch*) and the `tbuffer_load_format_*` forms use the instruction's format. Packed `tbuffer_load_format_d16_{x,xy,xyz,xyzw}` load two 16-bit components per VGPR, with the same final partial write as MUBUF. Native gfx1036 wave32/64 confirmed all four widths for RGBA8_UINT and RGBA8_UNORM with a conflicting 32_UINT V# format. Both packed and ordinary MTBUF loads ignore the V# channel selectors and use identity for the instruction format, zero-filling missing channels. Native gfx1036 measurements compared multiple V# permutations with MUBUF as a control. An INVALID V# format (0) suppresses the MTBUF fetch even at a valid address inside its bounds. Native gfx1036 wave32/64 returned zero components at all four widths and preserved the untouched half of an odd D16 destination, as specified for unbound resources in the [RDNA2 ISA, §8.1.4](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf). A translated vertex fetch on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics also followed a change to the instruction format between two draws with identical vertex records and V#. Fixed UNORM and SNORM loads share the exact integer quotient for each magnitude width across instructions and channels (checked against rationally rounded binary32 values on RTX 5080 and Radeon Graphics). The gfx1013 disassembler omits `format:` for the [GFX10 MTBUF default](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUModifierSyntax.html#ufmt), `BUF_FMT_8_UNORM` (code 1). A few rare ops are still reported by name rather than guessed. |
| `v_cubema_f32` signed zero | Whenever the selected major axis is ±0.0, `v_cubema_f32` returns `+0.0` (`0x00000000`), never `-0.0` (`0x80000000`), while preserving NaNs and signed non-zero major-axis values (`2.0 * m`). Emitted as `OpFOrdEqual %bool m %f0` selecting `%f0` over `OpFMul m 2.0`, so `-0.0` maps to `+0.0`, NaN compares false and propagates, and non-zero finite/infinite values double with sign preserved. Until 2026-11-22 both `tools/rdna2spirv.py` and `tools/f32_fuzz.py` multiplied `m` by `2.0` directly and returned `-0.0` when `z = -0.0` on `(±0.0, ±0.0, -0.0)` (or when `-0.0` was selected alongside NaN axes). **Measured natively on RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 & wave64, all four MODE combinations `flush`/`keep`/`no_dx10`/`ieee` via `tools/hw_oracle/`): all 8 `(±0.0, ±0.0, ±0.0)` triples, `-v` source negation of `+0.0`, `mul:2`, negative denormals, and `(-0.0, qNaN, qNaN)` returned `0x00000000`, while `(-qNaN, 1.0, 1.0)` returned `0xffc00000` and `(-1.0, qNaN, qNaN)` returned `0xc0000000`. Checked by `tests/semantic/test_valu_cube.py` (`test_signed_zero_triples_and_nan_cubema`), `tests/test_f32_fuzz.py` (`test_cube_major_axis`), and `tools/f32_fuzz.py --oracle hw --ops v_cubema_f32,v_cubeid_f32,v_cubesc_f32,v_cubetc_f32`. |
| `v_dot2c_i32_i16`, `v_dot8c_i32_i4` | VOP2/VOP3 accumulating forms of `v_dot2_i32_i16` and `v_dot8_i32_i4` (`D = sum(S0.elem[k] * S1.elem[k]) + D`, signed 2×16-bit and 8×4-bit). Wraps at 32 bits by default and saturates to `[INT32_MIN, INT32_MAX]` with VOP3 `clamp` (sharing the `v_dot2_i32_i16`/`v_dot8_i32_i4` signed-carry path); `op_sel`, `neg`/`abs`, and DPP/SDWA are refused. Registered in read-before-write VGPR tracking alongside `v_dot4c_i32_i8`. Source: LLVM AMDGPU `VOP2Instructions.td` (`HasDot4Insts` / `HasDot3Insts`, `gfx908` VOP2/VOP3 syntax; not in the default `gfx1013`/`gfx103x` feature set). **Not measured on AMD hardware** (translated Vulkan lowering checked on RTX 5070 by @duudiis: 40,960/40,960 exact words across wave32 and emulated wave64). Checked bit-for-bit on the CPU (`spirv_mini_interp`) and with `spirv-val` by `tests/semantic/test_alu_dotc.py`; not run on the shader corpus. |
| Out-of-bounds typed buffer loads and `DST_SEL=1` | When a MUBUF `buffer_load_format_{x,xy,xyz,xyzw}` or `buffer_load_format_d16_{x,xy,xyz,xyzw}` is out of bounds (`OOB_SELECT` 0–3, `num_records == 0`, partial-element spill, or swizzled OOB), memory is not accessed and raw data channels (`DST_SEL` 4..7) read 0, while the V# `DST_SEL` swizzle still runs: `DST_SEL=0` returns 0 and `DST_SEL=1` returns `1.0f` (`0x3F800000`, `d16` `0x3C00`) iff V# `FORMAT[6:0]` is a valid float, normalized or scaled buffer format (`1..77` ending in `_UNORM`, `_SNORM`, `_USCALED`, `_SSCALED`, or `_FLOAT`), and integer `1` (`0x00000001`, `d16` `0x0001`) for `UINT`/`SINT`, `INVALID` (`0`), and reserved codes (`78..127`). `d16_x` and `d16_xyz` preserve the unaddressed upper 16 bits of the final destination VGPR, and MTBUF `tbuffer_load_format_*` ignores V# `DST_SEL` on both out-of-bounds and `FORMAT=0` loads (returning 0 in addressed channels). Until 2026-11-22 `Gen.buffer_load_format` clamped the post-swizzle result to 0 when `in_b` was false (`SHADER-429`), dropping `DST_SEL=1` on out-of-bounds loads and returning `1.0f` instead of `1` for `DST_SEL=1` with `FORMAT=0`. **Hardware-verified on RDNA2** (`gfx1036`, Windows 11, driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 and wave64, 746 kernels via `tools/hw_oracle`: all 41 `Gen.FORMATS` across `xyzw`/`d16_xyzw`/`d16_xyz`/`d16_x`, all 128 7-bit `FORMAT` codes `0..127` for both `f32` and `d16`, `OOB_SELECT` 0–3, partial spill, swizzled OOB, MTBUF controls, and inactive EXEC lanes), Vulkan GPU-checked across all 128 7-bit `FORMAT` codes (`xyzw`, `d16_xyzw`, `d16_xyz`, `d16_x`) and the `cs_fmt_*`/`cs_oob_*`/`cs_bounds`/`cs_buf_*` fixtures on NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5090 (driver `32.0.16.1088`) and AMD Radeon Graphics (`gfx1036`, driver `32.0.21042.62`), and CPU/`spirv-val`-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_oob_dst_sel.py`. |
| `v_div_fixup_f32` | Measured natively on an AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics RDNA2 GPU (`gfx1036`, not the console's `gfx1013`; Windows driver 32.0.21042.62, `tools/hw_oracle`, wave32 and wave64 identical on every case: 256 seeded triples across 4 MODEs plus 73 targeted special-case/modifier rows, 0 mismatches). Computes `div_fixup_f32(S0=quotient, S1=denom, S2=numer)` with pure integer SPIR-V ops under the game's inferred MODE (`IEEE=0`, `denorm32=0`, `DX10_CLAMP=1`): VOP3 `abs` (clear bit 31) then `neg` (flip bit 31) apply bitwise to `S0`/`S1`/`S2` before classification (including NaN payloads); `S1`/`S2` subnormals flush to signed zero (`exp == 0`) while `S0` subnormals are preserved; priority order is (1) `NaN(S2) → S2`, (2) `NaN(S1) → S1` (signalling NaNs pass unchanged under `IEEE=0`, quieted only when `IEEE=1`), (3) `0/0` or `±inf/±inf → 0xFFC00000` (`-qNaN`), (4) `zero(S2) ∨ inf(S1) → ±0.0`, (5) `zero(S1) ∨ inf(S2) → ±inf`, (6) `exp(S2) − exp(S1) ≤ −151 → ±0.0` (pure biased-exponent check, beats `NaN(S0)` and `inf(S0)`; no overflow check), (7) `NaN(S0) → ±inf`, (8) `copysign(S0, sign(S1) ⊕ sign(S2))`. Output modifiers `mul:2`/`mul:4`/`div:2` apply before `clamp`, and `clamp` maps NaN to `+0.0` (`DX10_CLAMP=1`) before `FClamp` to `[0, 1]`. Not modelled: `IEEE=1` sNaN quieting, `denorm32=3` keeping `S1`/`S2` subnormals, and the hardware `omod` `-0 → +0` quirk (same as the other f32 ops). Checked bit-for-bit against 73 measured `gfx1036` words in `tests/semantic/test_alu_div_fixup_f32.py`. |
| Atomic natural alignment, 64-bit bounds, and `buffer_atomic_{fmin,fmax}` | Measured natively on RDNA2 (AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics, `gfx1036`, Windows driver `32.0.21042.62`, wave32 and wave64 via `tools/hw_oracle/`): **natural alignment** is enforced on every 32-bit and 64-bit buffer/global atomic (`buffer_atomic*`, `buffer_atomic*_x2`, `global_atomic*`, `global_atomic*_x2`), even under `OOB_SELECT = 2` (DISABLED). A 32-bit atomic requires `(addr & 3) == 0` (`offset = 1, 2` and `base + 2, offset = 0` are dropped; `base + 2, offset = 2` succeeds) and a 64-bit `_x2` atomic requires `(addr & 7) == 0` (`offset = 2, 4` and `base + 4, offset = 0` are dropped; `base + 4, offset = 4` succeeds). When dropped (OOB or unaligned), memory is untouched, an active `glc` lane receives `0` (32-bit) or `(0, 0)` (64-bit) in `vdst`/`vdata`, and an inactive lane (`EXEC = 0`) preserves `vdst`/`vdata`. Independently confirmed on a second `gfx1036` (driver `24.10.24`): without the alignment guard, an unaligned SPIR-V `OpAtomicAdd` corrupts the aligned dword (`100 -> 0xDEADBEF3`), whereas with the guard memory stays untouched and `0` is returned. `Gen.atomic_aligned` guards all six atomic emitters on `addr & 3 == 0` / `addr & 7 == 0`, merges `0` via `OpPhi` on skipped paths, and writes through `Gen.dst` so inactive lanes keep their prior register value while active unaligned/OOB lanes receive `0`. **64-bit partial bounds (`buffer_atomic*_x2`)**: the same `gfx1036` wave32/wave64 measurements confirm the all-or-nothing 8-byte bounds rule (`fixed_fmt = 62`) across all four `OOB_SELECT` modes (answering the unmeasured note in the `buffer_atomic_{and,or,xor,swap}_x2` row): mode 3 (RAW) requires `offs + 8 <= num_records` (`0/8` and `8/16` succeed; `0/7`, `0/5`, `0/4`, `8/15`, `8/12` drop cleanly); mode 0 (STRUCTURED_WITH_OFFSET) requires `idx < num_records` and `off + 8 <= stride` (`stride = 8, off = 0`, `stride = 16, off = 8`, `stride = 12, off = 0` succeed; `stride = 7, off = 0` and `stride = 12, off = 8` drop); mode 1 (STRUCTURED) checks `idx < num_records`; mode 2 (DISABLED) checks `num_records != 0`. **`buffer_atomic_{fmin,fmax}`**: lowered via `Gen.buffer_atomic_float` sharing `Gen._emit_atomic_fminmax_cas` (relaxed Device-scope `OpAtomicLoad` seed + `OpAtomicCompareExchange` + `GLSL.std.450` `NMin`/`NMax` CAS loop, no float-atomic extension required), enforcing V# bounds, 4-byte alignment, and `glc` return (`lds` and non-1-VGPR `vdata` raise `Unsupported`). On `gfx1036`, normal floats, `±inf`, `-0` vs `+0` (`fmin(-0, +0) = -0`, `fmax(-0, +0) = +0`), and `qNaN` (returns the non-NaN operand) match `NMin`/`NMax`; **known approximation**: the `gfx1036` L2 atomic unit quiets `sNaN` (`0x7f800001 -> 0x7fc00001`) regardless of `MODE`, while f32 subnormal handling follows the wave's `MODE.denorm32` and preserves sign (independently verified on a second `gfx1036`, wave32/wave64: under `denorm32 = 0`, subnormals flush to signed zero — `fmax(0x80000001, 0x80000002) -> 0x80000000 (-0)`, `fmax(0x00000001, 0x00000002) -> +0` — whereas under `denorm32 = 3`, subnormals are preserved and `NMin`/`NMax` is exact); `NMin`/`NMax` does not model `sNaN` quieting or `denorm32 = 0` subnormal flushing. CPU-checked by `tests/semantic/test_buffer_atomic_fminmax_and_align.py` (value grid, multi-lane CAS contention, OOB/partial-bounds matrix, inactive vs active-unaligned distinction across all six atomic families, and SPIR-V structural checks). |

### System values confirmed on the corpus (SHADER-4)

Counted with `scripts/audit_shader4_regs.py` on the disassembled game shaders (counts only):

- **Compute shaders:** Confirmed by corpus counts across all 2,689 CS (`TIDIG_COMP_CNT` 0/1/2/3 = 724/1,786/179/0; `TGID` X/Y/Z 100/110/111 = 724/1,786/179; `TG_SIZE_EN` = 0, `SCRATCH_EN` = 0, `TRAP_PRESENT` = 0; RBW VGPRs: `v0` in 2,602, `v1` in 893, `v2` in 57) + GPU tests `cs_lds.asm` and #186's `cs_workgroup_yz.asm` (Y/Z `WorkgroupId`).
- **Mesh shaders:** Confirmed by corpus counts (all 9,282 MS are 128×1×1 in PSV0, 100% read `v5` once, through `v_readfirstlane_b32` followed only by `s_lshr_b32 D, sX, 7`, 100% read `s3` exclusively via `s_bfe_u32 D, s3, 0x40018` for bits [27:24]; 0 read `s3` [31:28], [23:16], or [15:0]; audited with `scripts/audit_shader4_regs.py`) + GPU tests `ms_tri.asm`, `ms_wave_info_s3.asm` (`s3[27:24]` across both waves), and #219 (mesh `v5` workgroup index on a 2 × 3 × 4 grid).
- **Geometry shaders:** Confirmed by corpus counts (all 195 GS read only `v0` and `v1`; 0 read `v2`, `v3`, or `v4`) + GPU test `gs_tri.asm`.

**Main blocker at the time (superseded): descriptor loads.** On a 3,000-shader sample, `s_load_dwordx4`/`x8` stop over half of them. Shaders
load texture, buffer and sampler descriptors through user-data SGPR pointers, and how the game's root signatures
map onto those SGPRs is set up by game code. Reading that mapping from the exe (root signature creation plus the
FSSM user-data metadata) before building the binding layer was the next step; guessing it would give silent
rendering bugs. The translator now handles those loads against the push-constant user-data contract (*User data*,
*Scalar/buffer memory* and the descriptor rows); the D3D12 layer still has to supply the root-signature mapping.
The other blockers listed then (mesh, the vertex-fetch call, geometry, compute and compressed exports) are closed
for the corpus: all 26,503 shaders translate (see *Translator status*). The remaining fidelity gap is wave64
cross-half code on 32-wide subgroups.

## Day one, in order

1. `scripts\first_look.ps1 -Exe ...` checks the exe is plain, lists the imports to shim, and does a dry run.
2. Read the RPF9 table of contents only where it is already plain on disk. Five archives have tables that look packed
   or encrypted; under the ground rules above they stay unread and are recorded, not decrypted. Where a table is
   readable, a reader in `rpf9_carve.py` gives carved blobs real names and types, and the remaining unread data shows
   what it is.
3. Find the texture and mesh resource structures in the exe (their loaders give exact field layouts), which turns
   carved blobs into named textures and meshes.
4. Shader translation: already complete for all 26,503 shaders (see *Translator status*); the day-one work is
   validating them through the runtime.

## Disassembling shaders

The AMDGPU disassembler needs LLVM 21+ built with the AMDGPU target (`llvm-mc`; on Linux the `llvm-21` package
installs it at `/usr/lib/llvm-21/bin/llvm-mc`). The official Windows LLVM build has no AMDGPU target and ships no
`llvm-mc.exe`. On Windows, either build a native one or run it from WSL.

Native: `scripts\build_llvm_mc.ps1` fetches the LLVM sources the tool needs (sparse, shallow), builds `llvm-mc` alone
with `LLVM_TARGETS_TO_BUILD=AMDGPU`, static CRT and the DIA SDK off (its headers need ATL, which the C++ build tools
don't always include), checks it disassembles an `s_endpgm` for `gfx1013`, keeps only `llvm-mc.exe` (by default in
`%USERPROFILE%\.leonida\llvm-mc`) and sets `LLVM_MC` for the user. It needs git, Python and Visual Studio with the C++
x64 tools, about 2 GB of temporary space and 20-40 minutes. `-Dest`, `-NoEnv`, `-Keep` and `-Version` change the
defaults. Keep `-Dest` out of AppData: `python` from the Python install manager (the `WindowsApps` alias) doesn't see
folders that other programs create under `%LOCALAPPDATA%` or `%APPDATA%`, so `make_game.py` reports `llvm-mc` missing
and the fuzzers stop with `WinError 2`, although `LLVM_MC` names the file; `py`, or the same `python.exe` started by
its full path, sees it (measured with Python 3.14.7 on Windows 11).

```
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts\build_llvm_mc.ps1
python -I make_game.py --game "D:\path\to\your\package" --stages shaders
```

WSL: install `llvm-21` and give `LLVM_MC` the whole command:

```
set LLVM_MC=wsl -d Ubuntu -e /usr/lib/llvm-21/bin/llvm-mc
python -I make_game.py --game "D:\path\to\your\package" --stages shaders
```

The same form works in every tool that reads `LLVM_MC`. `tools/mc_command.py` splits it for `shader_disasm.py`,
the tests, `runtime/test/shaders/build.py` and `scripts/run_gpu_tests.py`; the assembler callers pass the assembly
on stdin, so a WSL command never receives a Windows file path.

`shader_disasm.py` disassembles one `s_endpgm` before starting, so a missing tool or AMDGPU target stops the run
with that reason instead of writing empty `.s` files. To disassemble by hand inside WSL:

```
LLVM_MC=/usr/lib/llvm-21/bin/llvm-mc python3 -I <repo>/tools/shader_disasm.py <work>/shaders/fssm
```

Code starts at the u32 at FSSM offset 0xC0 (the code-section offset) plus 0x28 bytes into that section, and ends at the
last `s_endpgm`. Use `--mcpu gfx1013`. On 300 random shaders it decoded 284 cleanly (134 invalid words in total),
against 201 clean and 6,560 invalid for gfx1030 and 283 clean for gfx1010–1012.

Some blobs end with a constant-data block after the real code: `s_endpgm`, a dword count N, N dwords of data, a
closing `s_endpgm`, then `s_code_end` padding. The data is not code, so the tool ends the range at the count
(`data_block_start`) and no longer reports those words as invalid. Over a 26,503-blob build, 1,473 blobs had such a
block (N from 0 up to several hundred); with the block excluded all 1,473 decode with no invalid word, and none of
the other 25,030 blobs matches the pattern.
## PAL stage descriptors

The FSSM header starts a table of 0x88-byte hardware-stage records at offset 0x100. A record's first qword is
`0x000000C0_0002_AAHH`: `HH` (byte 0) is the PAL hardware stage and `AA` (byte 1) the API stage. Record k
starts at `0x100 + 0x88*k`; the table ends at the first zero or non-tag qword. On the 26,503-blob corpus
26,308 blobs carry one record and 195 (all GS) carry two, the second at 0x188 being the same shader's VS
copy. API ids: 0 VS (2,236), 1 PS (12,101), 2 GS (195), 3 CS (2,689), 6 MS (9,282), each equal to the carve
index's stage. Hardware ids: 2 ES (the first tag-0x0D payload of the 195 two-payload VS containers), 3 GS
(195), 4 VS (2,431, the 195 GS copies included), 5 PS (12,101), 6 CS (2,689) and 7 (9,282, every mesh
shader: the NGG/primitive path). Unused record fields are zero in 25,643 records; 1,055 records (860 blobs,
the 195 GS pairs included) carry paired `0xDEADBEEFDEADBEEF` qwords, a lone 32-bit `0xDEADBEEF` never
occurs, and those 860 are the blobs whose 0x120/0x12C/0x130 directory words are `0xDEADBEEF`. The hardware
ids 0/1 and the API ids 4/5 are not in the corpus; the rest of a record is not decoded.
`tools/pal_stages.py` prints the records of an FSSM or a container (every tag-0x0D payload);
`tests/test_pal_stages.py` covers the layout with synthetic blobs.
`scripts/vs_layout_census.py` checks these hardware ids against the code of every payload: every stage-7
payload carries the NGG markers (`exp prim`, `s_sendmsg MSG_GS_ALLOC_REQ`, `v_mbcnt`), every stage-4 payload
is a legacy non-NGG VS, and the stage-2 ES payloads have no `exp` (they write the vertex outputs to LDS for
the geometry stage) — see *VS input layout, per PAL hardware stage* and *VS as ES* above.

## Pixel-shader inputs

`tools/fssm_header.py` decodes the FSSM input table: 8-byte records from 0x138 while the first byte is 6.
Byte 1 is the semantic id, byte 2 the component mask, byte 3 the running index, byte 6 the parameter slot,
and byte 7 holds flags (bits 0–1 = the **read method**: 0x01 read through `v_interp_mov_f32`, 0x02 through
32-bit `v_interp_p1/p2`; values 0 and 3 never occur; bit 3 marks a Float16-typed parameter; bit 4 = 16-bit
interpolant). The record's position is the `v_interp attr` number. `read_methods()` returns those bits by
position (the #1246 accessor was named `interpolation_modes`; #1301 renamed it) — they are the read method,
not the D3D interpolation mode (the mode is PSV0's; see the "PS interpolation modes (SHADER-181)" row and the
"PS `v_interp` read methods" row). `interpolants()` returns the count and a fp16 bitmask by position;
`rdna2spirv.py` uses it for the pixel-input declarations. `wave_size()` decodes the compiled wave size from
the header flags word `u32[0xD4]` bit 5 (`0x20` = wave32, `0` = wave64), consumed by `rdna2spirv.py` for
every stage (see *Wave64 handling*). Measured on the 26,503-blob corpus: every blob starts with `FSSM` and
is at least 0xD8 bytes long, 2,662 are wave32 (1,535 CS, 1,127 PS) and 23,841 are wave64 (1,154 CS, 195 GS,
9,282 MS, 10,974 PS, 2,236 VS) with 0 contradictions against GFX10 code-proven wave32/wave64 instructions;
25,643 satisfy `u32[0x120] + u32[0x12C] == u32[0x130]` (the 860 that do not store 0xDEADBEEF at 0x130); 12,101 are PS,
10,307 with a non-empty table (55,791 records, 11,199 of them 16-bit). The count equals the number of
distinct `v_interp attr` numbers in 11,316/12,101 PS (93.5%); 300 more declare one unread input, 475
declare two or more, and the 10 undercounts are one family whose first record at 0x138 has kind 7. A
200-PS spot check agreed on 187/200. Flag bit 4 matches the `*_f16` interpolation opcodes in
12,067/12,101 (99.7%).

`tools/ps_interp_modes.py` decodes the interpolation mode of each PS input from the DXBC container
carved next to the FSSM, and runs the census. The mode is not in the FSSM: no FSSM word stores
`SPI_PS_INPUT_ENA`/`ADDR` (every u16 in bytes 0x00–0x138 of all 12,101 PS was compared against the
value reconstructed from the DXBC signature; only constants coincide). The PSV0 chunk
(writer: dxc `lib/DxilContainer/DxilPipelineStateValidation.{h,cpp}`) holds, after the runtime info,
resource bindings, string table and semantic-index table, a u32 element size (16) and then one
16-byte input element per row: u32 semantic-name offset (0 = system value), u32 semantic-index
offset, u8 rows, u8 start row, u8 cols[3:0] | start col[5:4] | allocated[6], u8 semantic kind
(`DXIL::SemanticKind`: 0 Arbitrary, 3 Position, 6 ClipDistance, 7 CullDistance, 13 IsFrontFace),
u8 component type (`DxilProgramSigCompType`: 1 UInt32, 3 Float32, 6 Float16), u8 interpolation mode,
u8 dynamic mask | output stream, u8 reserved. The ISG1 chunk (the repo's 32-byte elements) supplies
the read mask that marks live rows. The FSSM record flags decode as the read method — bit 0 (0x01)
`v_interp_mov_f32`, bit 1 (0x02) 32-bit `v_interp_p1/p2`, bit 3 (0x08) Float16-typed parameter,
bit 4 (0x10) `*_f16` opcodes — and the disassembly supplies the I/J operand of every interpolation.
Per shader the code reads one barycentric pair (v0/v1 in 10,259 PS, none in 1,841, one v14 copy),
and the pair's class follows the read rows: perspective-centre when a perspective generic is read,
linear only in the 4 PS that interpolate SV_Position through the parameter cache (D3D forces
noperspective on SV_Position), flat otherwise. Synthetic tests: `tests/test_ps_interp_modes.py`.

## Baseline already recorded

- `reports/manifest.json`: SHA-256 of all 108 files in `Documents\GTA6` (208.5 GiB). Run
  `python -I tools\manifest.py verify <game>` to check a copy against it.
- `reports/rpf_probe.json`: all 22 archives are **RPF9**. Five have tables that look packed or encrypted (`levels`,
  `movies`, `core_bvh4`, `navmeshes`, `data`).
- `reports/pe/`: the 16 bundled DLLs are plain x86-64 PE with no encrypted sections. Through the loader their
  `xmem.dll` imports and two `d3d12_xs.dll` root-signature calls bind to shims. Every other import resolves on
  desktop Windows. The proxy covers the measured device slots and forwards the rest in PC vtable order; the Xbox
  queue/fence/command-list extensions and `PresentX` remain unknown.
